# Changelog — 1.0.19

Archived release section of [docs/CHANGELOG.md](../CHANGELOG.md). The current work and the
release the server is running stay in that file; every earlier release is one file here.

## 1.0.19 — 2026-09-27

### Removed

- **A yes/no question left unanswered no longer refuses the save.** This is the half of switches-to-radios
  that is not about appearance: a switch has no unanswered state, so an untouched question was saved as
  **No** — a claim that the check had been made and failed. A radio pair can be genuinely unanswered, but
  these columns are optional booleans, so the honest way to say "not answered" is to leave the field out;
  the empty string the form holds for one would have the server refuse the whole save. Client Storage's
  form and its status dialog, and the **pre production shoot form** — which had this from the day its own
  switches became pairs, and spreads its whole form into the payload the same way — now all drop an
  unanswered yes/no before saving
  ([yesNoField.js](../../frontend/src/constants/yesNoField.js),
  [PreProductionFormPanel.js](../../frontend/src/components/project-management/PreProductionFormPanel.js)).

- **A submitted Qua Qua CRD now shows 100% in Pre Production, not a low percentage.** Pre
  Production marked every CRD document against the Wedding questions, so a Qua Qua document -
  which never asks about a groom or a bride - read as partly complete even once submitted. It now
  uses the document's own vertical, as the CRD Documents page does, and a Qua Qua project shows no
  Sides Received ([summary.js](../../backend/src/modules/sales/crd-documents/summary.js)).

- **Pre Production's success messages now show the green tick.** Saving the General panel, saving
  a shoot, adding a follow-up, marking one done and downloading the CSV used to show a plain notice
  with no icon; they now show the green success tick. "No rows available to export" stays a plain
  notice ([PreProductionPage.js](../../frontend/src/components/project-management/PreProductionPage.js)).

- **A read-only viewer can no longer type into fields that will never save.** Opening a CRM lead on
  `READ` gave a fully editable form with no way to save it — the work of filling it in, then
  nothing. Every field in the lead panel is now genuinely un-editable, with the refused cursor over
  each control and the reason on hover, while the record stays on screen and readable: a panel with
  its fields removed is an empty record rather than a read-only one, and reading the lead is the
  whole reason it opens.

  The pattern is now one shared component,
  [ReadOnlyFieldset](../../frontend/src/components/shared/ReadOnlyFieldset.js), rather than something
  each panel remembers for itself. It wraps its children in a `fieldset` whose `disabled` follows
  `canWrite`, which reaches every input, select, textarea and button inside — including ones added
  later by somebody who never heard of it. `SchemaFormPanel` takes a `readOnly` prop that uses it,
  so every schema-driven form is covered without its author doing anything; the Roles dialog and
  Image Selection's Selection Settings rail both moved onto it. The one thing it cannot reach is a
  control that hides its real input behind a label, as `ToggleSwitch` does, which still needs
  `disabled` passed as a prop.

- **The Pre Production board got its refresh control back.** It was lost when the board moved from
  shoot rows to project rows, and it matters more here than on most boards: the CRD document state
  and the Client Storage answers are read live from other modules, so this page can go stale through
  no action of its own
  ([PreProductionPage.js](../../frontend/src/components/project-management/PreProductionPage.js)).

- **Client Storage's "Shoot Status Pending" now follows the project's shoots instead of a hand-ticked
  box.** It decides whether a delivered disc finishes or goes back round for the next shoot, and it
  was a checkbox somebody had to remember — so a project that gained a shoot after its footage was
  delivered silently rested at "Delivered", which is the exact case the loop exists for. It is now
  derived on every read: true while any of the project's shoots is still ahead (judged from the
  start of today, so a shoot happening today counts). The same derivation feeds both the value shown
  and the moves the row menu offers, so they cannot disagree, and the status-change endpoint asks it
  rather than trusting the stored column — which is now deprecated and read by nothing
  ([client-storage.controller.js](../../backend/src/modules/assets/client-storage.controller.js)).

- **Editing a shoot no longer wipes its event times.** `ProjectShoot.eventStartTime` and
  `.eventEndTime` have existed since the shoot table did, and all three write paths — Add Shoot, the
  inline shoot editor, and the shoot PATCH — stamped them to `null` on every save, so nothing could
  ever hold a value. Now that Pre Production is the screen that enters them, that null would have
  erased a coordinator's work every time somebody edited the shoot from Start Project
  ([AddShootDialog.js](../../frontend/src/components/project-management/AddShootDialog.js),
  [ProjectShootList.js](../../frontend/src/components/project-management/ProjectShootList.js),
  [projects/controller.js](../../backend/src/modules/sales/projects/controller.js)).

- **A duplicated role could not be deleted because a closed account still carried it, and nobody
  could reach that account to unassign it.** Deleting a role is refused while somebody still holds
  it, which is right — but the check counted every assignment, including accounts whose status is
  not `ACTIVE`. The Users screen lists active accounts only, so a role held by a closed one was
  stuck: the refusal said "1 user still carries this role" and there was no user on screen to take
  it from. It read as the wrong role being checked, because the copy looked identical to the
  original that genuinely was in use. The guard now counts only accounts that can sign in — a closed
  account is not using what a role grants — and its `UserRole` row goes with the role when it is
  deleted, which the schema already cascades.

  Two things make it legible rather than just unblocked. The refusal **names the people** instead of
  counting them, so a copy refused on somebody else's behalf is obvious at a glance. And the Roles
  table gains a **Users** column, with the same count in the detail panel: a role and its "(copy)"
  were otherwise indistinguishable until a delete failed. See
  [admin/routes.js](../../backend/src/modules/admin/routes.js) and
  [RoleManagement.js](../../frontend/src/components/admin/RoleManagement.js).

- **A phone number from outside India can now actually be saved.** The picker beside every phone
  field offers 185 countries, but the number itself was capped at exactly ten digits — India's
  length — in the field and in three separate server validators. Most countries are not ten digits,
  and the two failures were not the same: an eight-digit Singapore number or a nine-digit UAE one
  was **refused on save**, while an eleven-digit Chinese mobile was **silently cut to ten** by the
  field before it was ever sent, then stored as a valid-looking number belonging to nobody, with
  nothing to flag it.

  The length is now E.164's — a national number of 4 to 14 digits, with the dial code and number
  together never over 15 — except behind `+91`, which stays exactly ten on purpose: nearly every
  number the studio holds is Indian, and a loose range would accept the nine- and eleven-digit
  typos a strict one catches. The rule lives once per side, in
  [services/phoneNumber.js](../../backend/src/services/phoneNumber.js) and
  [lib/phoneNumber.js](../../frontend/src/lib/phoneNumber.js), replacing the copy of
  `/^\+(\d{1,4})\s*(\d{10})$/` that each of the three modules had been carrying. The 5-5 grouping
  the field displays is Indian, so it is now shown only for Indian numbers rather than imposed on
  every country.

- **The record view can be opened on a phone.** Reading a record in full was bound to a double-click
  and nothing else, and on a touch screen a double-tap is the browser's zoom gesture rather than
  something a table row ever receives — so the record view, and the Emp Chart tab inside it, were
  desktop-only. Every table's ⋮ menu now leads with **View**, which opens the same panel and works
  the same way with a mouse or a finger. Double-click still works where there is a mouse.

  Three tables hid the ⋮ menu entirely from anyone without write access — Assets, Vendors and CRD
  Documents — so a read-only reader had no way in at all; those menus are now always shown, with
  **View** always present and the actions that change something still gated as before. On Projects,
  **View** replaces the "Project details are available in the Info Panel" note, which pointed at a
  panel rather than opening anything.

- **Users and Role Management have a footer.** Both tables ended at their last row with nothing
  under them, so neither said how many accounts or roles there were nor how fresh the list was.
  Each now carries the same **Last updated** and **Total rows** strip as every other table, with
  the timestamp stamped when the load finishes rather than when the page opened.

- **Quotation and CRD Documents answer a click like every other table.** Neither had an info panel
  at all: their rows could not be selected, and neither single- nor double-click did anything, so
  the only way to read a quotation or a received CRD document in full was to open it for editing.
  Both now fill a panel beside the table on a single click and read the record back in full on a
  double-click, from new `quotationInfoSections` and `crdDocumentInfoSections` in
  [infoPanelConfig.js](../../frontend/src/constants/infoPanelConfig.js).

- **Double-click opens the record on Accounts Manager and on Extra Quotations, as it does
  everywhere else.** Single click selects a row and fills the info panel beside it; double-click
  reads the record back in full. Two tables were not answering. Accounts Manager had the panel
  built and the handler wired, but `AccountsTable` never listed `onRowDoubleClick` among the props
  it accepts, so it was dropped in the wrapper and never reached the table — the panel behind it
  could not be opened by any gesture. The Extra Quotations table on Payment Requests had no
  double-click at all, and now reads its record back through the sections its info panel was
  already using. See [AccountsTable.js](../../frontend/src/components/accounts/AccountsTable.js) and
  [PaymentRequestsPage.js](../../frontend/src/components/accounts/PaymentRequestsPage.js).

- **The demonstration services and deliverables are gone, and Settings → Project holds only what
  the studio types.** `scripts/seedDemoServices.js` and `scripts/seedDemoDeliverables.js`, and the six
  `npm run demo:*` commands that ran them, are removed. Nothing on startup, deploy, restart or
  migration writes a service or a deliverable: an empty table stays empty until somebody adds a row
  in Settings, and the Quotation Generator reads those rows and nothing else. The seeds refused to
  run against production, so no production data changes; a local database that holds their
  "Demo …" rows loses them by deleting them from Settings, or by removing every row whose code
  begins `SERVICE_DEMO_` or `DELIVERABLE_DEMO_`.

### Changed

- **The Quotation Generator's Category is found by typing instead of by scrolling.** Each brand's
  Categories tab is the studio's own list and grows with it, so reaching one entry meant scrolling
  past the rest. The field now filters as you type, the same control Assigned Branch beside it
  already uses, and it stays a **closed** list: typing narrows the brand's own categories and
  cannot write a new one, so the answer is still one of them and never free text. Nothing else
  moves - the same list per brand, the same `religion` key on the payload, the same mandatory
  check that is waived while a brand's tab is still empty, and Offer Type beside it is deliberately
  left as it was, since its list is short and fixed. The flag is the field's own rather than every
  dropdown's ([quotationVerticalConfig.js](../../frontend/src/components/sales/quotationVerticalConfig.js),
  [QuotationVerticalFields.js](../../frontend/src/components/sales/QuotationVerticalFields.js)).
  No permission change: the screen stays on `SALES / Quotation Management`.
- **The Signature Album's Design panel loses its two switches.** "Show highlights section" and
  "Show image numbers" are gone: the Our Favourite Moments section now shows whenever the album has
  starred photos, and running numbers on the photos follow the studio-wide Settings → Signature
  Album → Show Image Numbers switch alone, which stays. Migration
  `20260926140000_signature_album_design_switches` drops the two album columns behind them; theme,
  cover style, layout, spacing, fonts, colours and the logo choice are untouched.
- **The Signature Album tab's header is the album's own, and the rail no longer repeats it.** With
  the Signature Album tab open, the workspace header reads the album's last write and its status
  (Draft / Ready / Published / Expired, the chip the album already draws) instead of the project's
  timestamp and the Photo Selection publication badge, which stay on the other tabs untouched; it
  follows every album answer, so publishing moves it to *Updated: just now · Published* on the
  server's reply and never before. A chapter or photo write now moves the album's `updatedAt`
  ([signatureAlbum.service.js](../../backend/src/modules/image-selection/signature-album/signatureAlbum.service.js)),
  so the header is true after adding photos, not only after editing the cover. The rail's ALBUM
  section is the publish switch alone - the Album Status row and the Connected Content section
  (project name and chapter counts) are gone; the chapters, their counts and the photo references
  behind them are unchanged and still drive the Chapters card and the client album. The "Updated"
  wording is now one helper, `updatedAgo` in
  [relativeTime.mjs](../../frontend/src/lib/relativeTime.mjs), shared by both tabs.
- **The Signature Album's rail is down to what it needs, and the client downloads the whole album
  as one ZIP.** The rail's ALBUM section is the publish switch and the status - Album Theme, Cover
  and Last opened are gone (the theme and the cover still drive the album; they are simply not
  repeated here) - and PERMISSIONS is two switches, Allow Download and Allow Social Share: the
  download dropdown (Original / Preview / Disabled), Allow Favourite and Allow Selection have left,
  with the ♡ on each photo, the "Select photos" link and the per-photo download in the viewer, and
  Settings → Signature Album loses the three matching defaults. With Allow Download on, the client's
  album shows one **Download Album** control (Preparing… / Downloading… / Retry Download; a second
  press while one is under way is ignored) that saves `<title>-signature-album.zip`: the cover,
  then every chapter as a numbered folder in the album's order with its photos in the studio's
  order, each the web preview the album shows - never an original. The ZIP is streamed as it is
  built, one photo at a time from the R2 object or the local processed file, so the server never
  holds the album in memory
  ([signatureAlbum.archive.service.js](../../backend/src/modules/image-selection/signature-album/signatureAlbum.archive.service.js));
  the session first asks for a minutes-lived download ticket, and both that step and the archive
  route refuse without the switch, so Allow Download is enforced by the server and not only by the
  page. Migration `20260926130000_signature_album_download_cleanup` drops the favourite table and
  the columns that served only what left (`download_mode`, `allow_favourite`, `allow_selection`,
  the two opened-at stamps and the three defaults); existing albums, covers, chapters, links and
  PINs are untouched, and Photo Selection, Culling and the client gallery are not touched. Adds
  `archiver` to the backend. No permission change: the tab still answers to
  `IMAGE_SELECTION / Signature Album`.
- **The client gallery's People are a tab of their own: All Photos | People | Selected.** The
  face-recognition result used to sit as a strip above a folder's photos; it is now the **People**
  tab on the gallery home, and nowhere else - All Photos shows the folder cards as before and a
  folder's own view shows no people. The tab lists everyone found across the whole gallery (All with
  the gallery's photo count, then each person with their photo count), and beneath them the same
  folder cards All Photos shows, counted for the chosen people - *Total: 10 | 2 Selected* is how
  many of that folder's photos every chosen person is in together - Person 1 and Person 2 means the
  frames holding both, never either of them alone; up to ten people at once - and how many of those are picked; a folder with none of their photos is not a card, and with All the cards
  are All Photos' own. See Photos opens the same folder view, narrowed to those people's photos in
  it, and Back returns to People with the same people chosen. Pressing people is instant: nothing
  reloads or remounts, the cards on screen dim until the next answer replaces them in place, and a
  combination already seen comes back without a round trip. One selection throughout - a photo
  ticked there is ticked in All Photos and counted on Selected at once, and back. Before anyone is found the tab says why:
  *Organizing people...* while photos are still being looked at, *No people found in this gallery*
  once they have all been, or that People is not available when the filter is off. Nothing about
  selection, limits, retouches, submission, expiry, folders, storage or the face processing itself
  changes, and there is no migration. Backend, additive: `GET /:token/folders?personId=` counts the folders for the chosen people (a filtered read no longer counts as the client opening the gallery), the
  folder listing's counts follow `?personId=`, `folders/all/images` lists every folder at once
  (`controller.folderScope`), and the People response carries `processing`. Files:
  [PeoplePanel.js](../../frontend/src/components/image-selection/client-gallery/PeoplePanel.js),
  [FolderCardList.js](../../frontend/src/components/image-selection/client-gallery/FolderCardList.js),
  [PublicGallery.js](../../frontend/src/components/image-selection/PublicGallery.js),
  [FolderCards.js](../../frontend/src/components/image-selection/client-gallery/FolderCards.js),
  [FolderGrid.js](../../frontend/src/components/image-selection/client-gallery/FolderGrid.js),
  [clientGalleryPeople.mjs](../../frontend/src/lib/clientGalleryPeople.mjs),
  [public-gallery/controller.js](../../backend/src/modules/image-selection/public-gallery/controller.js),
  [faces/service.js](../../backend/src/modules/image-selection/faces/service.js).
- **A WhatsApp group name says the branch as a short code.** `Erode` → **ERD**, `Tiruppur` → **TPR**,
  `Coimbatore` → **CBE**, `Bangalore` → **BLR**, `Hyderabad` → **HYD**, `Vizag` → **VSKP**. The name
  is read in WhatsApp's own chat list, where it is truncated to a phone's width, so five characters
  spent on "Erode" are five the couple and the date lose off the end. All three shapes use it —
  `WED Premium VSKP Arun & Meena`, `QQTPR Asha`, `CORP CBE Acme`. The register spells a branch with
  its state after it and the sheets do not, so "Erode TN", "Visakhapatnam AP", "Bengaluru" and
  "Tirupur" all reach the right code; a branch with no code is left exactly as typed rather than
  dropped. **The list is held in the code, not read from the branch table**, and is deliberately
  separate from the one in `backend/src/services/identifiers.js`: that one mints enquiry ids and
  writes Tiruppur as `TUP` and Vizag as `VIZAG`, so changing it would rename the branch segment of
  every id minted afterwards and split one studio's records across two spellings.
  [whatsappGroupNameUtils.js](../../frontend/src/components/sales/whatsappGroupNameUtils.js),
  [group-name-branch-code.test.mjs](../../frontend/test/group-name-branch-code.test.mjs).

- **A linked sheet no longer loses a whole lead over one unreadable cell — it blanks the cell and
  keeps the row.** A row was refused outright when any single field failed to parse, and the fields
  that fail are the ones a person answers in their own words: `feb 10 th and 11 th 2027` in *When is
  the Event Planned*, `Immediate` in *When Can You Join*, a state name where a date was expected.
  Each of those took a whole enquiry with it — the name, the number, the campaign that paid for it —
  so a studio could sync ten new leads and import none of them. Now the unreadable cell is stored as
  **null** and every readable column on the row is kept as it is. It applies to both linked
  integrations and to every field that can fail: name, email, lead status, all six date columns and
  all six phone columns. **A row with no name imports too**, with the name blank — a lead is a lead,
  and a wholly empty spreadsheet row never reaches the importer anyway. Cells are blanked *before*
  the row is matched, so a phone the importer could not read is not a phone it tries to find an
  existing lead by. Deduplication is untouched: a readable phone that already belongs to a lead is
  still recognised and the row still skipped. The summary reports **"6 fields left blank"** on a line
  of its own rather than as rows skipped — nothing was skipped — and names each one by column and
  row (`row 12 — eventPlannedDate: Invalid date value`) so the sheet can be corrected. The import
  preview counts them under **Fields Left Blank** in place of *Invalid Rows*, which is now always 0.
  [googleSheetsService.js](../../backend/src/modules/sales/crm-leads/googleSheetsService.js),
  [sheetImport.js](../../backend/src/modules/hrm/interview/sheetImport.js),
  `hrm_interview_candidate.name` had to be widened to nullable to honour this — it was the one
  column that could not, so a nameless row was being stored as `''`, which on this schema is exactly
  what a blank must never be
  ([20260926090000_interview_candidate_name_nullable](../../backend/prisma/migrations/20260926090000_interview_candidate_name_nullable/migration.sql);
  `sales_crm_lead.name` has always been nullable). One trade-off worth stating: a **phone** that
  cannot be read is blanked like anything else, and the phone is the deduplication key. A re-sync of
  the same sheet row still matches on its source id or position, but a first sync against a lead
  somebody typed in by hand that shares only that malformed phone will now import beside it rather
  than being refused — losing the row was the previous answer, and this is the one that keeps it.
  [sheet-import-blank-bad-fields.test.js](../../backend/test/sheet-import-blank-bad-fields.test.js).

### Added
- **The client's quotation now links to where to pay it: For Payment, under the price, opens the
  Assigned Branch's payment details.** A small gold link on the navy pricing card, between the GST
  line and the validity line and set like the terms link, opens `/q/<token>/payment` on the same
  sheet: the branch's account holder, bank and branch, account number, IFSC, UPI ID and Payment QR
  as Settings → Organization → Branches holds them, with **Copy** on the account number, the IFSC and
  the UPI ID that says Copied for a moment. Each section prints only where the branch holds
  something for it — no UPI ID, no UPI section; no QR, no picture — and a branch with no account
  number, UPI ID or QR prints no link at all, so such a quotation opens exactly as before. Nothing is typed into
  the Quotation Generator: the Assigned Branch is the source — its resolved branch, or failing that
  the branch whose exact name the quotation holds — never the head office, the first branch, a
  branch guessed from a city, or a branch that has since been closed. The details are
  read **live** off the branch, like the download answer and unlike the words and the price, because
  an account changed after a quotation went out has to reach the client still paying it. The
  terms link and page, the price, the GST line, the PDF and the register are untouched. New public
  endpoint `GET /api/public/quotations/:token/payment` answers the seven payment strings and nothing
  else off the branch row, behind the quotation's own token and 404 / 410, with a rate limit of its
  own; the record gains `paymentDetailsAvailable`. No new permission. See
  [PublicQuotationPaymentPage.js](../../frontend/src/components/sales/PublicQuotationPaymentPage.js) and
  [quotations-public/controller.js](../../backend/src/modules/sales/quotations-public/controller.js).
- **A branch can now carry its UPI ID, in the QR Code section of Settings → Organization → Branches.**
  An optional **UPI ID** box sits above the Payment QR, so the address the picture encodes is also
  held as text. Typed on a new branch or added to an existing one, edited or cleared later, and a
  branch saves without it exactly as before; every branch already on the register opens and saves
  unchanged. It is trimmed, stored as written, and checked only for the `address@handle` shape every
  UPI ID shares — `name@okaxis`, `9876543210@upi`, `business@paytm` all pass — with nothing
  provider-specific to refuse a real one. Editing only the UPI ID leaves the Payment QR alone, and
  replacing the QR leaves the UPI ID alone: each is its own column and the form sends both back as
  they are. The client's quotation payment page (below) prints it beside the QR; no invoice,
  receipt or QR generation reads it. Reached, like the rest of the register, with `SETTINGS /
  Organization`; no new permission. Ships
  with the hand-written migration `20260926170000_branch_upi_id`, which only adds the nullable
  `branch.upi_id` column — `prisma migrate deploy`. See
  [BranchesPanel.js](../../frontend/src/components/settings/BranchesPanel.js) and
  [validation.js](../../backend/src/modules/admin/branches/validation.js).
- **The screening form, the onboarding invite and a new joiner's login can now be sent on WhatsApp,
  from the sender's own paired account.** All three screens already showed the finished wording in an editable box with a
  Copy button, and the studio then left the application, opened WhatsApp and pasted it. **Send
  WhatsApp** sits in the dialog's footer beside Close and Send Invite, in WhatsApp's own green with
  its mark, and sends what is on screen — including a line somebody added before
  pressing it, which is why the box is editable and why the message travels from the browser rather
  than being rebuilt on the server. The **recipient's number never does**: it is read off the
  invite or the employee record, so neither screen can reach anybody but the person it is about,
  and there is deliberately no "send WhatsApp to this number" endpoint anywhere. The interview register's **Send Onboarding
  Invite** needed nothing of its own: it opens the employee onboarding dialog, which carries the
  button already. Sending the
  credentials asks for `ADMIN / User Management` Read / Write — the same permission that creates
  them, and a stronger one than the rest of that screen, because the message carries the temporary
  password; the onboarding invite rides the `HRM / Employee Management` Read / Write the router
  already applies, and the screening form the `HRM / Interview` its own router does. No new
  permission and no migration. All three go through the WhatsApp module's own
  send path, so they keep every habit that makes the studio's paired number look like a person
  writing to one person — the pacing between messages, the check that the number is on WhatsApp at
  all, the typing indicator — and both the studio-wide and per-user kill switches stop them. They
  are behind the **same per-user rate limit** as every other send, deliberately sharing one budget
  with the CRM rather than being given a second one: it is one account, and WhatsApp watches the
  account. Somebody who has not linked their own WhatsApp sees the button greyed with the reason,
  rather than not at all — the three reasons it can be greyed (the studio's switch, their own
  switch, no phone linked) are told apart, because only the last is one they can fix. Nothing is
  recorded, which is what the invite's email does too: there is no sent stamp on an invite and no
  screen that shows one.
### Fixed

- **A Qua Qua WhatsApp group name has a space before the branch.** It read "QQ TPR Asha" on the other two
  verticals' pattern and "QQTPR Asha" here — the branch ran straight onto the prefix, which reads as one
  word and hid where the prefix stopped. The three names are read side by side in WhatsApp's own list, so
  only this one was the odd shape. Fixed in the browser's preview and in the name the server writes, which
  are required to stay in step
  ([whatsappGroupNameUtils.js](../../frontend/src/components/sales/whatsappGroupNameUtils.js),
  [whatsapp-group-names/controller.js](../../backend/src/modules/sales/whatsapp-group-names/controller.js)).

- **HRM → Events is in the server's copy of the sidebar, so a saved sidebar layout keeps it.** The
  screen was added to the canonical registry in `navigation.mjs` but not to the mirror the server
  validates a saved layout against
  ([sidebarStructure.js](../../backend/src/modules/user/preferences/sidebarStructure.js)), which is
  the one relationship that file exists to hold. Anybody who had arranged their own sidebar would
  have had Events treated as a screen that does not belong to HRM. It is added in the registry's
  own position, after Request, because the order here is also the default order a layout that has
  never been arranged falls back to. No permission or route changes; the mirror is a list of ids
  and nothing reads it for access. `user-preferences-sidebar.test.js` is the invariant that caught
  the drift.
- **The sheet importer's test agrees with it again about "When Can You Join".** That column is free
  text on purpose - the studio's screening form writes "Immediate", "15 days" or "Negotiable" into
  it - so the importer stopped judging it as a date and keeps the word. An older assertion still
  expected the cell to be emptied as unreadable, and failed on every run
  ([sheet-import-blank-bad-fields.test.js](../../backend/test/sheet-import-blank-bad-fields.test.js)).
  The test now checks the word survives, which is the behaviour the column was changed for. No
  application code changed.
- **The Interview register deletes a whole selection at once, instead of refusing more than 2,000.**
  `removeBulk` called the single-candidate delete in a loop — two statements and a transaction per
  row — so the request was capped at 2,000 to keep that loop short enough not to time out. Select-all
  answers up to **20,000** ids, so the one path that makes a large selection could not be acted on:
  a studio clearing a register had to do it two thousand at a time. It now batches the way the CRM
  lead board does — de-duplicated, sliced 20,000 at a time, the live ids read first so the screen can
  still report what was found, and the invites removed with their candidates in the same transaction
  so no form is left naming somebody who no longer exists. The cap is now the CRM's 200,000 and
  bounds the request rather than the loop. The reply is unchanged, so the screen reports exactly what
  it did before. A slice that fails is logged and the rest still run, so one bad row costs that
  batch rather than turning the whole call into a 500 the screen reads as "nothing happened" while
  earlier slices have already committed. Bulk **status** keeps the 2,000 cap, since that path still
  works a row at a time.
  [service.js](../../backend/src/modules/hrm/interview/service.js),
  [controller.js](../../backend/src/modules/hrm/interview/controller.js).

- **A linked CRM sheet no longer refuses a lead because "When is the Event Planned" is words.** That
  question is answered by the client in their own words on a Meta lead form, and a wedding is two
  days as often as one — "feb 10 th and 11 th 2027", "March 10,11 2027". The column is `TEXT` and has
  always held exactly that, but the importer validated it as a single day, and one bad cell refuses
  the **whole row**: a sheet of new leads imported the people who left it blank and dropped the rest,
  name and number with them. `eventPlannedDate` is out of the validated list, the same way
  `addedTime` already was. It is still folded to `YYYY-MM-DD` where the cell **is** a day — that
  happens through `LEAD_DATE_COLUMNS` on the way to the database, which leaves anything else exactly
  as written — so a real date still sorts and filters as one and the client's own answer is kept.
  The other five date columns are unchanged and still refused when they are not days.
  [googleSheetsService.js](../../backend/src/modules/sales/crm-leads/googleSheetsService.js).

- **A linked Interview sheet no longer refuses a row because "When Can You Join" is a word.** The
  importer validated `availableFrom` as a date, but that column is free text and always has been:
  it is `VARCHAR(40)`, and the studio's own screening form asks whether somebody is an immediate
  joiner and writes a **word** into it — "Immediate", "15 days", "Negotiable". So the sheet import
  refused rows for carrying exactly what the application form puts there, and because one bad cell
  fails a whole row, a sheet of ten candidates could import none of them. `availableFrom` is still
  folded to `YYYY-MM-DD` when the cell **is** a day; it is simply no longer a reason to reject the
  row. The other five date columns are unchanged and still refused when they are not days — "next
  week" in Interview Date is a note in the wrong column, and filing it as a day would put the
  candidate in no tab. [sheetImport.js](../../backend/src/modules/hrm/interview/sheetImport.js).

### Docs
- **A ux-heuristics-reviewer subagent checks every new or reshaped screen for usability.** Rule 6
  in [CLAUDE.md](../../CLAUDE.md) now asks that a screen say what is happening, let the user leave
  and undo, prevent errors before reporting them, use the studio's words, show only what the moment
  needs and fill in what the app already knows. The checklist in
  [ux-heuristics-reviewer](../../.claude/agents/ux-heuristics-reviewer.md) maps Nielsen's 10
  heuristics, Shneiderman's 8 golden rules, Gerhardt-Powals' cognitive engineering principles and
  the interaction and perception laws (Hick's, Fitts's, Miller's and Jakob's laws and the Gestalt
  principles), plus accessibility to WCAG 2.1 AA, content design, Norman's design principles,
  Tognazzini's first principles, the Peak-End rule, the Fogg behaviour model and Cialdini's
  principles of influence (honest use only, no dark patterns), and the house SAGE checklist -
  first-time user, trust and cognitive load, scored per screen - to the components that already do each job (`FloatingNotice`, `ConfirmDialog`,
  `useUnsavedGuard`, `useSubmitOnce`, `Loader`, the status pills), so it reports what a change
  broke rather than restating theory. Further principles will be added to the same file.
- **A security-reviewer subagent looks for the ways the code can be attacked.** Access by
  permission was reviewed; injection, token strength, unauthenticated webhooks, unbounded
  uploads, SSRF, secrets in code or logs, XSS, mass assignment, missing rate limits and
  vulnerable dependencies were not. [security-reviewer](../../.claude/agents/security-reviewer.md)
  carries that checklist, grounded in how this codebase works: values through `?` in the raw SQL
  the CRM uses, tokens from `crypto`, public routers behind `rateLimit`, scoped public JWTs,
  `multer` size limits, no `dangerouslySetInnerHTML`. It states the current posture (open CORS,
  no `helmet`, a 50 MB JSON body, open `npm audit` findings) so it reports a regression rather
  than the status quo. CLAUDE.md's review step and its secrets constraint point to it.
- **A backend-api-reviewer subagent reviews routes, controllers and services.** The frontend had
  a reviewer and the schema had one; a route could still ship with `protect` alone, a role check
  in a controller, a branch call that named no permission, or a helper that already existed in
  `src/services`. [backend-api-reviewer](../../.claude/agents/backend-api-reviewer.md) carries the
  backend checklist - the guard on every router and nothing else deciding access, branch reach
  and `dataScope` by permission, validation and JSON status codes in the controller, reuse of the
  services that exist, no trusted request ids, no secrets, a test for the refusal path - and hands
  schema work to `db-schema-reviewer`. CLAUDE.md's Rule 4 backend row and the review step point
  to it.
- **The changelog no longer repeats itself.** Merge conflicts resolved with "accept both" had
  pasted whole blocks of entries two, three and up to six times: 54 extra copies in this file and
  52 in the archived [1.0.1](1.0.1.md), plus 30 group headings left holding nothing but a
  copy. Each entry now appears once, in the first place it appeared; nothing was reworded and no
  line was added. The Release Information dialog is unaffected, since it reads only the top of
  the file.
- **An abandoned attempt is reverted, not left beside the fix.** When a problem was misread and
  edits were made on that reading before the real cause was found, the earlier edits used to stay
  in the diff. [CLAUDE.md](../../CLAUDE.md) now carries it as a hard constraint: before a change is
  finished the whole `git diff` is read and every hunk that exists only because of an earlier guess
  is removed - code, changelog line, README sentence, migration, test or seed row - and the review
  step checks that the diff holds only the final fix.
- **Rule 1 says what "reuse" means in practice, and a frontend-ui-reviewer subagent checks it.**
  Raw markup typed into a page or module component for something the codebase already has - a
  `<button>`, `<input>`, `<select>`, `<textarea>`, `<table>` or a hand-rolled dialog - is now
  named a defect in [CLAUDE.md](../../CLAUDE.md) Rule 1, with the procedure: list the shared folders
  and grep the module first, extend the nearest component when it nearly fits, create one reusable
  component in the right folder when nothing does, and let the page compose it. The new
  [frontend-ui-reviewer](../../.claude/agents/frontend-ui-reviewer.md) carries the inventory of what
  exists (the raw element, the component to use instead), the ~360px checks of Rule 2 and the
  frontend layer boundaries, so a session reviews UI work without reading the README's Rule 1
  table. CLAUDE.md also states the stance every change is made from: the senior Next.js and Node
  engineer who owns the codebase, inside the frontend, backend and database layers.
- **Rule files are read by section, not whole, and the changelog keeps only the current work.**
  Every session read the whole of a README and IAM.md before starting - some 60k tokens of
  reference before any work - and this changelog had grown to 1.2 MB with every release ever
  shipped in it. [CLAUDE.md](../../CLAUDE.md) now carries a Token Optimization section: it is the one
  file read whole; the READMEs, `docs/project-state/` and this file are read by heading and
  section, and an entry here is placed from the first 40 lines, at the top of the first group of its
  name. Releases before the current one are archived one file per version under
  [docs/changelog/](./) (the Release Information dialog reads only the top section, so it
  is unaffected). [scripts/doc-sizes.sh](../../scripts/doc-sizes.sh) reports what each rule file costs
  a session to read, and the new [token-optimizer](../../.claude/agents/token-optimizer.md) subagent
  says which text moves to an agent or docs file and which large file is worth splitting. Nothing
  is enforced; the point is that a session reads only what its change needs.
- **The database rules live in a subagent, and CLAUDE.md gains Rule 5.** Every link is an id with a
  key; a name is only a copy. The full rules - keys and their delete behaviour, the copies in
  `nameCopies.js` and the reads in `resolvedNames.js`, `""` never meaning "none", how a migration is
  written and rehearsed, what a test fixture must create - are in
  [.claude/agents/db-schema-reviewer.md](../../.claude/agents/db-schema-reviewer.md), a read-only
  reviewer to run before and after any schema, migration, query or raw-SQL change. CLAUDE.md carries
  one paragraph and the pointer, so a session that never touches the database never loads them.
  [.claude/agents/README.md](../../.claude/agents/README.md) says how to add the next one.

### Added
- **A Signature Album chapter's cover can be positioned, with Adjust Cover in Edit Chapter.**
  The same Adjust dialog the album's cover uses - drag, zoom, Fit / Fill, the quick positions -
  opens on the chapter's own 21:9 band, and Apply stages the placement in the chapter form; Save
  Chapter stores it with the chapter (`cover_display_mode`, `cover_focal_x`, `cover_focal_y`,
  `cover_zoom` on `signature_album_chapter`, nullable, the album cover's four values). The
  chapter card, the studio's preview and the client's album all draw the band from that one
  placement, so what is positioned is what is seen everywhere. A chapter from before draws as it
  always did; replacing the cover photo starts the new one from the default; changing the photo
  layout leaves the placement alone. The photo file and its previews are never written. Migration
  `20260926100000_signature_album_chapter_cover_positioning`, applied with `prisma migrate deploy`.
  [ChapterDialog.js](../../frontend/src/components/image-selection/signature-album/ChapterDialog.js),
  [signatureAlbum.service.js](../../backend/src/modules/image-selection/signature-album/signatureAlbum.service.js).
- **The AI Feature guest gallery has a phone layout of its own.** Under 768px the page is a
  gallery app rather than the desktop page squeezed: a translucent header with the mark and Leave,
  the event's name large and the guest and dates under it, padded for a notch; the tabs as a
  segmented control (All Photos · Find Me · My Photos); the host's cover, when there is one, as a
  short strip naming the kind of event and its dates; the RSVP answer as a small card with a tick
  and the guest's name; and the photos in an editorial two-column grid - the first across the
  width, portraits tall, landscapes short, one landscape in seven full width - each tile a fixed
  box the photo covers, so nothing moves as pictures load. Find My Photos loses its card on a
  phone and leads with a full-width Take selfie; My Photos says "Your Photos - we found N photos
  with you", and with nothing matched offers Try another selfie. The footer is centred and padded
  for the home indicator. The desktop page, and every request, session, matching and upload path,
  are as they were. [GuestPhotoGrid.js](../../frontend/src/components/ai-feature/guest/GuestPhotoGrid.js),
  [GuestEventHeader.js](../../frontend/src/components/ai-feature/guest/GuestEventHeader.js),
  [guestGalleryLayout.mjs](../../frontend/src/lib/guestGalleryLayout.mjs).
- **Take Selfie and Upload Selfie on a guest's Find My Photos each have their own switch under
  Event Default Settings → Guest Access → Guest experience.** `Allow Take Selfie` (the device
  camera) and `Allow Upload Selfie` (an existing photo from the device) sit under `Enable guest
  selfie` and are inert while it is off, keeping their answers; at least one must stay on while
  the guest selfie is on, or the save is refused with "Enable at least one selfie option". They
  are not `Enable guest uploads`, which is guests adding event photos and is unchanged. The guest
  page draws only the allowed button, a lone button filling the row, and the selfie endpoint
  refuses a way the event turned off (the page says which way it used), so hiding the button is
  not the only guard. Both are stored on the event's own settings copy - the wizard and an event's
  Settings offer them too - and default on, so every existing event keeps both buttons. An
  event's own Settings and the wizard hold Save shut while a rule is broken and say which, and a
  save the server still refuses names the field rather than reading "Invalid settings." alone. An
  event's own Settings dialog now says that the event holds its own copy of the defaults and offers
  **Use studio defaults**, which copies the studio's current Event Default Settings into the form
  to apply with Save; a change to the defaults still never reaches an existing event on its own,
  and the defaults panel's saved notice now says so.
  [settingsDefaults.js](../../backend/src/modules/ai-feature/shared/settingsDefaults.js),
  [public/service.js](../../backend/src/modules/ai-feature/public/service.js),
  [GuestAccessSettings.js](../../frontend/src/components/ai-feature/settings/GuestAccessSettings.js),
  [FindMyPhotos.js](../../frontend/src/components/ai-feature/guest/FindMyPhotos.js).
- **HRM → Request: leave, permission and comp-off requests on one screen, and comp-off as a new
  request type.** A new sidebar entry under HRM
  ([AttendanceRequestsPage](../../frontend/src/components/hrm/AttendanceRequestsPage.js)) with
  **Waiting for me** — the requests whose employee names this login as an approver on their Login
  Credentials, with **Approve** and **Reject** in the ⋮ at the right end of each pending row, as on
  every other module table (each opens the decision dialog; a rejection needs a reason); nobody's own request is ever in it, including an Attendance
  Approval holder's — and **My requests**, every request the person has raised in any status, to raise, withdraw, cancel and apply
  again. The switch between the two shows a count on each, and the page opens on My requests
  whenever nothing is waiting for the person's answer. The request tables no longer say who
  approved or rejected: the Approved By / Decided By column is gone and Approvals reads only as a
  count ("2 of 3 approved"), on this screen and on Employee Attendance. One click on a request
  opens it in the info panel beside the table and a double click opens it in full in the read-only
  record view (`DataTableInfoPanel` / `RecordViewPanel`, fields in
  `attendanceRequestInfoSections`), with the Period narrowed to the request's own kind and the
  Reason and Remarks in full — on both screens. Requests are raised from one **Raise Request**
  button that opens a form
  ([AttendanceRequestFormPanel](../../frontend/src/components/hrm/AttendanceRequestFormPanel.js),
  fields in `attendanceRequestConfig`): the kind first (Leave, Permission, Comp-off), then the
  dates that kind asks for, the reason, and a read-only **Approvers** section naming who it will
  go to — read from the new `GET /api/hrm/attendance/requests/approvers`, the same people the
  server routes it to. With nobody set it says to ask HR and will not send. The checks and payload
  are shared with the attendance screen's dialog (`requestFormProblem`, `requestPayload`). A **comp-off** gives back a working day for a holiday or weekly off the person actually
  worked: the server checks the worked day was a holiday or weekly off, is over, and has a
  check-in, that it has not been claimed already, and that the day off is a later working day not
  already on leave (a leave and a comp-off now block each other); there is no expiry. "Apply
  again" must now re-raise a request of the same type.
  It goes to the same three Leave Approvers as a leave, and an approved one reads as On leave on
  the day taken and hands that day's leads to the person's cover. New column
  `attendance_request.worked_date` (migration `20260925120000`). The request tables and the
  approve / reject dialog moved out of Employee Attendance into
  [attendanceRequests.js](../../frontend/src/components/hrm/attendanceRequests.js), which both
  screens use. **Permission:** the screen answers to a new `HRM / Requests` (migration
  `20260925130000`), which every role holding `HRM / Employee Attendance` receives at the same
  level, and Super Admin; the request endpoints accept either permission. Narrow it per role in
  Role Management if the screen should reach fewer people.
- **Attendance can be read by hand in IST, with a link to each selfie.** `npm run db:attendance-view`
  creates the read-only MySQL view `attendance_capture_ist`: one row per check-in and check-out with
  the employee, `captured_at_ist`, position, address and `selfie_url`
  ([createAttendanceView.js](../../backend/scripts/createAttendanceView.js)). The stored data does not
  change — times stay in UTC and selfies stay as object keys, which is what the application reads.
  The link works only while selfies are in the public bucket, and then for anybody who has it; it
  is NULL once a private bucket is configured. It writes nothing and is safe to run on the VPS; run
  it once there, and again after any `migrate reset`.
- **A Signature Album's logo now matches its background: the dark-mode Global Logo on a dark
  album (Cinematic, Luxury, or any background set by hand), the light-mode Global Logo on a light
  one.** The mark used to be the light-mode logo whatever the theme, and disappeared into the
  black ground of the dark themes. Every album page - the ending, the PIN screen, the workspace's
  live preview, Review & Share and the client's page - now draws the mark the server names for the
  album's own ground, from the same rule, so the preview and the client cannot differ. Where only
  one Global Logo is uploaded the other ground borrows it on a small plate of its own ground, and
  where none is uploaded the bundled mark is drawn in the matching tone; the artwork is never
  inverted, faded or recoloured. Design gains a Studio Logo choice - Auto (the default, and what
  every existing album reads as), Dark or Light - shown only when the studio-wide settings show a
  logo at all. The two Global Logos are the existing ones in Settings → Organization → Branding
  Logo; nothing else about branding, Photo Selection, Photo Culling, publishing or existing albums
  changes. Migration `20260924150000_signature_album_logo_variant` adds the one column
  (`logo_variant`, default `AUTO`); published albums carry both logos on their branding snapshot
  from their next publish and read today's dark-mode logo until then
  ([signatureAlbum.helpers.js](../../backend/src/modules/image-selection/signature-album/signatureAlbum.helpers.js),
  [AlbumLogo.js](../../frontend/src/components/image-selection/signature-album/client/AlbumLogo.js)).
- **Settings → Organization → Branding Logo takes a light-mode and a dark-mode version of both the
  Global Logo and the favicon.** The two Global Logo slots sit side by side (one under the other on
  a phone), and so do the two favicon slots. The **app bar** wears the dark-mode logo in the two
  dark appearances and the light-mode logo in the light one; the **browser tab** wears the favicon
  matching the browser's own light or dark tab strip, which is the ground a tab is actually drawn
  on. Upload only one of a pair and the other borrows it, so nothing changes for a studio until it
  uploads a dark mark - and a dark-mode logo uploaded for the app bar is shown as uploaded, where a
  borrowed light logo is still inverted to read there. Everything else keeps the light-mode logo,
  because everything else is white whatever appearance the viewer has chosen: the sign-in page, the
  public onboarding and interview forms, the client's selection gallery, the album proofing room,
  the AI Feature guest pages, and the client's quotation on screen and printed to PDF. A surface is
  handed the dark mark only by asking for it, and only the app bar asks. Two nullable columns
  ([migration](../../backend/prisma/migrations/20260924140000_studio_branding_dark_marks/migration.sql))
  beside the existing ones, returned by the public branding endpoint with them
  ([StudioBrandingPanel.js](../../frontend/src/components/settings/StudioBrandingPanel.js),
  [studioBranding.mjs](../../frontend/src/lib/studioBranding.mjs),
  [favicon.mjs](../../frontend/src/lib/favicon.mjs), [AppLogo.js](../../frontend/src/components/shared/AppLogo.js),
  [StudioFavicon.js](../../frontend/src/components/shared/StudioFavicon.js)). No permission changes:
  the panel stays on `SETTINGS / Organization` and its routes on `ADMIN / System Settings`.
- **Every Signature Album theme is a full design preset, and a preset is never a lock.** The eight
  themes now each carry a background, text and muted text colour, an accent, heading and body
  fonts, a border tone and the overlay behind cover words, written once in
  `THEME_PRESETS` ([signatureAlbum.constants.js](../../backend/src/modules/image-selection/signature-album/signatureAlbum.constants.js))
  and read by the client page, the preview and the Design panel through the one resolver, so no
  component names a theme colour of its own. Choosing a theme applies its five studio-editable
  values (background, text, accent, heading font, body font) to the album; each may then be
  changed and stays changed across reloads and on the client. Picking a different theme while any
  of the five has been changed asks first - *Apply Theme Defaults* or *Keep My Custom Styling* -
  and **Reset to Theme Defaults** puts the five back to the current theme's without touching
  photos, cover, chapters, layout or positions. A text-on-background pairing under a contrast of 3
  shows *Low contrast* beside the colours, and is never altered. **Existing albums do not change:**
  migration `20260924130000_signature_album_freeze_theme_values` writes onto every album the
  values it renders with today wherever it held none, so the new presets reach only a new album,
  an explicit theme choice or a Reset. Vintage stays styling and type only; no photo is tinted.
- **Signature Album photos keep their shape, and can be positioned in their frame without
  touching the file.** Every photo was already drawn with `object-fit` (never stretched or
  squeezed), but in a designed frame - an Editorial pair, a Grid square, a Showcase 4:5, the cover -
  the crop was always dead centre. Each album item and the album's cover now carry four values
  (`displayMode` FIT or FILL, `focalX` / `focalY` 0-100, `zoom` 1-2), saved by **Adjust Photo** in a
  chapter's ⋯ menu or the highlights strip, and **Adjust Cover** on the cover card. The editor shows
  the photo inside the frame shape the layout draws it in (chosen from the layout's frames, or
  Desktop / Phone / Framed / Story / Split for the cover), with drag by mouse, pen or touch, a zoom
  slider, Fit / Fill, Top / Center / Bottom / Left / Right presets and Reset; nothing is written
  until Apply. The values are structured, never CSS, and read as FILL / centred / 1× where null, so
  every existing album and item draws exactly as it did. The client page, the live preview and the
  editor turn them into CSS through one helper
  ([lib/signatureAlbumTransform.mjs](../../frontend/src/lib/signatureAlbumTransform.mjs)), so what the
  studio positions is what the client sees. Masonry and single rows keep the photo's own ratio and
  need no crop; the upload row, its R2 previews and the original are never written, and Photo
  Selection reads none of this. Migration `20260924120000_signature_album_photo_positioning`
  adds eight nullable columns and no backfill. The designed frames also follow their contents now:
  a pair or trio in Editorial, Story or Mixed, and Showcase's supporting photos, take a landscape
  frame for landscapes, a portrait frame for portraits and a near-square one for a mix
  (`rowAspect`), so the crop is the smallest it can be before the studio moves it; Masonry, a photo
  on a row of its own and the lightbox keep the photo's natural ratio and crop nothing.
- **Signature Album Design gains more of everything, without changing what an album already has.**
  The Design panel now offers eight themes (Romantic, Luxury, Modern and Vintage beside the four
  that shipped), seven cover styles (Centered Hero, Editorial Overlay, Framed Cover and Story
  Cover added), seven gallery layouts (Story, Showcase, Filmstrip and Mixed added), five spacings
  (Tight and Airy either side of the three), fourteen heading fonts and thirteen body fonts, and an
  optional **Font Pairing** preset (Elegant, Luxury, Classic, Modern, Minimal) that sets both fonts
  at once while the two pickers stay for choosing by hand. Every new choice moves the live preview
  at once and reaches the client's album, and Settings → Signature Album's default theme, layout,
  cover style and spacing dropdowns offer the same lists. Nothing was renamed or removed: an album
  saved as Classic / Full Screen / Editorial / Standard, or holding one of the original fonts, opens
  exactly as before, and a new choice applies only where the studio picks it. Each theme is a
  palette and a font pair drawn by the same components (plus a couple of type touches keyed on the
  theme name), each cover style a branch of the one hero, each layout a pattern over the one photo
  frame, so lazy loading and the image pipeline are untouched. The Google Fonts request now names
  only the weights each family ships, so a family with a single weight (Prata) can no longer break
  the load for the other. No migration: the columns are plain text validated against the lists in
  [signatureAlbum.constants.js](../../backend/src/modules/image-selection/signature-album/signatureAlbum.constants.js)
  and [lib/signatureAlbum.mjs](../../frontend/src/lib/signatureAlbum.mjs).
- **The Signature Album: a premium client album built from a project's existing photos, inside
  Image Selection → Project → Signature Album.** The tab, which used to be empty, is now a workspace
  of two sections. **Album** holds the live preview (Cover → Highlights → Chapters → Ending, on a
  desktop or a phone frame), the cover card (cover photo, title, subtitle, event date, text alignment
  and the ending message), the chapters (add, edit, delete, drag to reorder) and the highlights strip;
  **+ Add Photos** adds from the client's Photo Selection picks, from a folder with its subfolders, or
  from photos ticked by hand, and inside a chapter photos are dragged into order, starred as
  highlights (up to 30), moved to another chapter, used as a cover or removed. **Design** offers four
  themes (Classic, Editorial, Minimal, Cinematic), three cover styles, three gallery layouts, heading
  and body fonts, the three album colours, image spacing and two switches, every change moving the
  preview at once. The rail's **Review & Share** carries the link,
  the PIN, the expiry, Copy Link, WhatsApp (the studio's chat with the client, or the project's linked
  group from the signed-in person's own WhatsApp), a QR code of the link alone, and Open Album. The
  rail beside the tab is the album's own - publish switch, status (Draft / Ready / Published /
  Expired), theme and cover, client URL, Require PIN and the PIN, expiry, the four client permissions
  (download with Original / Preview / Disabled, favourite, selection, social share) and the connected
  chapters - and none of it is the Photo Selection rail's publish status, PIN, expiry or Allow Photo
  Selection, which stay exactly where they were. Publishing is refused, and says why, until the album
  has a cover, a title, a chapter, a photo and a future expiry. The client opens `/a/<code>` (the
  album host in production, `SIGNATURE_ALBUM_PUBLIC_URL`), enters the PIN, and scrolls the cover, Our
  Favourite Moments, each chapter in its layout and the ending, with each chapter's photos arriving
  in batches as they scroll; a ♡, a download, full screen and share appear only where allowed, and
  "Select photos" hands them to the published Photo Selection gallery rather than recording anything
  in the album. **Every photo in an album is a reference to the project's existing
  `image_selection_upload` row** - the same R2 preview the gallery serves, originals untouched on the
  NAS - so a photo deleted through Image Selection simply leaves the album (its items cascade, a
  cover set null), and deleting an album deletes its own five tables' rows and nothing else. Settings
  → Image Selection gains a **Signature Album** rail entry with the studio-wide defaults a new album
  starts from (theme, layout, cover style, expiry, branding switches and footer, client permissions,
  Require PIN and PIN length, auto-unpublish after expiry, background and spacing, initial and
  lazy-load batch sizes, download mode, the ending's message and links); an album already made keeps
  every value it holds. One new permission, **`IMAGE_SELECTION / Signature Album`**
  (`ISSIGNATURE_ALBUM`): Read opens the tab and the preview, Read / Write builds, publishes and
  shares; without it the tab is absent. Migration `20260924100000_signature_album` creates the tables
  and the permission and grants it to SUPER_ADMIN, and `20260924110000` hands it to every role that
  already holds `Project Management`, at the level that role holds - the tab is part of the same
  job, and without this it vanished for everybody but a Super Admin. Role Management can narrow any
  role afterwards. The defaults answer to the existing `SETTINGS / Organization`. Backend in
  [signature-album/](../../backend/src/modules/image-selection/signature-album/) (studio API under
  `/api/image-selection/signature-albums`, the client's under `/api/public/signature-albums`, a
  session scope of its own), frontend in
  [components/image-selection/signature-album/](../../frontend/src/components/image-selection/signature-album/).
  Production also needs the album host rewritten onto `/a/<code>` in Nginx, as the gallery's is.
  Found while driving the built album in a browser and fixed before release: a chapter with no
  photos yet is left out of the client's album (and of the live preview) rather than shown as an
  empty heading; an Original download the storage rules refuse tells the client only that the
  original is not available and to contact the photographer, keeping the studio's storage wording
  out of the album; and a design edit made while the previous edit was still saving no longer
  snaps back when that save answers.

### Fixed
- **Take selfie on a guest's Find My Photos now opens the browser's own camera instead of the
  file picker.** The button was a hidden file input marked `capture="user"`, which a phone honours
  and a desktop browser ignores, so on a Mac both buttons opened Finder. Take selfie now asks for
  the front camera through `getUserMedia` - only on that press, never on page load - shows a
  mirrored live preview with an oval guide and Capture, then the frozen frame with Retake and Use
  photo, and hands the captured JPEG to the same upload and match a picked file goes through; the
  frame sent is the camera's own, not the mirrored preview. The front camera is *preferred*
  (`ideal`), never required: a desktop webcam that advertises no facing mode is asked for again
  with a generic request before anything is concluded, so a MacBook's camera opens rather than
  being reported missing. Capture is offered only once the element is showing real frames. A
  camera the browser still cannot open is said by its cause - access blocked, none could be found
  (with what to check: a Mac whose browser is not allowed the camera in System Settings answers
  "not found", so it is never declared absent), in use by another application, could not start,
  not a secure HTTPS page, not supported - with Try Again where the guest can change something and
  Upload selfie always; the picker is never opened in its place, and the browser's actual refusal
  is logged as a console warning for diagnosis. Every way
  out of the camera - Use photo, Retake, Try Again, Cancel, Back, Upload selfie, leaving the page -
  stops the stream first, so the camera light goes off and no second stream is opened over a first.
  Upload selfie is unchanged, and so are the selfie endpoint, matching and storage.
  [SelfieCamera.js](../../frontend/src/components/ai-feature/guest/SelfieCamera.js),
  [FindMyPhotos.js](../../frontend/src/components/ai-feature/guest/FindMyPhotos.js),
  [selfieCamera.mjs](../../frontend/src/lib/selfieCamera.mjs).
- **The SUPER_ADMIN role now holds `PROJECT / Show All Projects`, so an administrator who is not a
  Super Admin *login* can open the whole-studio project register.** The permission was created by
  20260919120000 with no grants, which is right for every other role and wrong for this one: the
  role named SUPER_ADMIN is granted its permissions as rows like any other, and the two blanket
  grant migrations that fill it both ran before this permission existed. A login whose `role` column
  says SUPER_ADMIN was unaffected, because the IAM service short-circuits on that column without
  reading a grant - but an administrator added the way the studio actually adds one, an EMPLOYEE
  login carrying the SUPER_ADMIN role, was refused the register silently while every screen said
  they had full access. The earlier migration is not edited; a new one adds only the missing row,
  idempotently, the way the permission migrations beside it do. Roles reached: SUPER_ADMIN alone -
  no other role, scope or branch is touched
  ([migration](../../backend/prisma/migrations/20260924160000_super_admin_holds_show_all_projects/migration.sql)).
  `iam-super-admin-grants.test.js` is the invariant that named it.
- **A Signature Album's photo links never point at a bare R2 key.** On a machine without
  `R2_PUBLIC_BASE_URL` - CI, a fresh development database - a photo that has an R2 preview was
  handed a relative "/p/1.jpg" that nothing serves, because the R2 helper joins an empty base and
  the key as they are. The album module now uses an R2 address only when it is an absolute one and
  otherwise falls back to its own media route, for the photo URLs and the preview download alike;
  the develop CI run that failed on exactly this passes again
  ([signatureAlbum.helpers.js](../../backend/src/modules/image-selection/signature-album/signatureAlbum.helpers.js)).
- **The Next.js development indicator is switched off.** The "N" button that `next dev` pins to
  the bottom-left of every page (Route / Bundler / Preferences) was showing on the client-facing
  Signature Album and gallery pages during studio reviews and read as part of the product.
  `devIndicators: false` in [next.config.mjs](../../frontend/next.config.mjs) hides it; compile and
  runtime errors still surface, and a production build never had it.
- **The Signature Album preview is the client's page, not a picture of it.** The live preview and
  the Preview Album dialog had drifted from the published album in three ways: preview-only
  styling (a 420px cover band where the client gets the full viewport, smaller titles, tighter
  chapter padding - and a cover that fills its frame is cropped by the frame's height, so the
  crop differed), the admin window's breakpoints (a "phone" preview laid out with desktop rules),
  and a second content builder in the browser. Now one server builder
  (`contentFor` in [signatureAlbum.client.service.js](../../backend/src/modules/image-selection/signature-album/signatureAlbum.client.service.js))
  answers both the client's `/content` and a studio-only `GET /:albumId/preview` behind the album's
  permission, one presentational view
  ([SignatureAlbumView](../../frontend/src/components/image-selection/signature-album/client/SignatureAlbumView.js))
  draws both pages, every preview-only style is gone, and the preview is the real page at
  `/signature-album-preview/<albumId>` inside an iframe sized like the device (1440×900 or 390×844)
  and scaled to fit - so its breakpoints, its `100svh` cover and its crop are the client's own.
  Pixel comparison of the preview page against the client page at both sizes, for all seven cover
  styles, shows the same rendering. The preview refetches after every save in the Design and Album
  panels, so it follows each change without a reload. The desktop frame is 1440 wide and exactly
  as tall as the box it sits in, so it fills the Album tab, the Design column and the Preview
  Album dialog alike - it used to be a fixed 900 tall, which left the bottom of a tall box empty
  and the bottom of a short one unreachable; the phone frame keeps its 390×844 shape, centred.
- **The Signature Album's ending and PIN screen show the studio's mark whenever Show Studio Logo
  is on.** With no Global Logo uploaded yet, the switch was on and nothing logo-like appeared -
  only the name in text - because the album drew the mark only when a logo file existed. Both
  screens now draw it through `AppLogo`, which shows the Global Logo when there is one and the
  application's bundled mark when there is not, the rule every other surface follows; the name
  sits under it on its own switch. The ending's Website and Instagram fields also accept an
  address typed without `https://` (it is put in front) and refuse one with a space in it, since
  a bare `instagram.com/studio` was being linked as a path of the album's own page. The gap
  between the last chapter and Thank You is shorter. The two links are now named after where they
  go - the site's own name (`dontblinkstudioz.com`) and the Instagram handle (`@dontblinkstudioz`)
  - rather than the words "Website" and "Instagram", and the workspace's live preview draws the
  same ending the client gets (mark, name, links, footer line) instead of a stand-in without them:
  the studio's album now carries the branding block the client page reads, built by one shared
  function ([signatureAlbum.helpers.js](../../backend/src/modules/image-selection/signature-album/signatureAlbum.helpers.js)).
- **Pressing a switch far down a scrolling pane no longer scrolls the whole app shell.** The
  Signature Album's Design panel showed it: pressing *Show highlights section* or *Show image
  numbers* dragged the module header and the project header off the top of the screen and left the
  page grey below. The switch's checkbox is visually hidden with `sr-only`, which positions it
  absolutely, and with no positioned ancestor inside the pane its containing block was the app
  shell - so a switch 1500px down a scrolled pane sat 1500px down the *shell's* layout, gave the
  shell scrollable overflow, and the focus a press gives the checkbox scrolled the shell to reach
  it. [ToggleSwitch](../../frontend/src/components/shared/ToggleSwitch.js)'s label is now `relative`,
  so the hidden checkbox is contained by the switch it belongs to and the pane is what scrolls.
  One class on the shared switch; every other switch in the application looks and behaves as
  before, and the Selection Settings rail's own switches were re-checked.
- **The face engine installs itself on every deploy, in an environment of its own.** Production had
  been answering *Face engine offline — ModuleNotFoundError: No module named 'cv2'*: the engine ran
  on whatever `python3` the server had, nothing in the deploy had ever installed its packages, and
  the only remedy was `pip install` by hand after each release. The deploy now runs
  `backend/scripts/face-engine-setup.sh` first — before the backend's own install, so a failure
  leaves the running application untouched — which creates **`backend/.venv-face`** if it is missing,
  installs `backend/requirements-face.txt` into it, and proves the result with `face_engine.py
  --check` (every import, the two model files fetched from the Hub, one detection) before anything
  else proceeds. That environment is the face engine's alone, apart from Node and from the
  call-transcript engine's `.venv`. The requirements file now names every package the engine and
  insightface actually import, with **`opencv-python-headless`** — insightface declares the desktop
  `opencv-python`, so it is installed `--no-deps` and the headless build is what a server gets.
  `/api/health` carries `faceEngine` (`disabled` / `ready` / `starting` / `not-started` /
  `offline`) and the deploy's health check waits for the models to load and **fails on `offline`**,
  with the reason and the `[face-engine]` log lines; `/api/health/face-engine` (behind
  `SETTINGS / Application Monitoring`) adds the interpreter, whether the environment and model files
  exist, and how many face jobs wait in each module. None of this is a dependency of anything else:
  with the engine off or broken the application answers `ok: true`, Image Selection, Album Proofing
  and Project Management run as before, and queued face jobs park and resume by themselves once it
  answers. A server that says offline is repaired with the same script
  ([DEPLOYMENT-WORKFLOW.md](../project-state/DEPLOYMENT-WORKFLOW.md#the-face-engines-python-environment)).

### Changed
- **The nightly deploy no longer rebuilds the face engine's Python environment.** It upgraded pip
  and reinstalled every face-recognition package on every deploy, which took minutes of CPU inside
  the deploy's 10-minute limit and was part of why the 2026-09-24 deploy stopped halfway. The
  environment (`backend/.venv-face`) stays on the server between deploys, so it is now set up by
  hand once per server, and again only when `backend/requirements-face.txt` changes. The deploy
  still checks the face engine is healthy afterwards.
  [deploy.yml](../../.github/workflows/deploy.yml),
  [DEPLOYMENT-WORKFLOW.md](../project-state/DEPLOYMENT-WORKFLOW.md#the-face-engines-python-environment)
- **A guest's join page is one composition on the event's cover.** The cover used to be a
  banner between a white bar with the mark and a white block of details, with the form on grey
  below; now it fills the page behind everything, under a veil light at the top and deeper at the
  foot, with the studio's mark (its dark-surface one), the kind of event in small caps, the name
  large, the dates and place, the description and "Photos by" set over it in white, and the join
  card floating on a pane of glass - white at about three quarters with the photograph blurred
  through where the browser can, near-solid white where it cannot, a little more white on a phone.
  The fields sit on white of their own; the RSVP answer is one two-part control; Join Event is a
  full-width blue button that reads "Joining..." while it works. The schedule follows on light
  panes of the same kind. The words stay white on every cover: the veil's strength follows how
  bright the cover is where the text sits, measured once in the browser (a bright photograph is
  dimmed more, a dark one less, one the browser will not let a canvas read gets the middle veil),
  a soft scrim pools behind the event's words, every line carries a shadow, and the card's white
  is enough on its own that the picture behind it cannot lift the labels. The questions asked, when each is asked, the checks, the PIN, the RSVP
  values and the join request are exactly as before.
  [GuestLanding.js](../../frontend/src/components/ai-feature/guest/GuestLanding.js),
  [GuestJoinCard.js](../../frontend/src/components/ai-feature/guest/GuestJoinCard.js).
- **The guest gallery's photo viewer is a presentation of its own.** Opening a photo now lifts
  a near-black canvas over the whole page - the gallery behind is not readable through it - with
  a muted "1 / 14" and a 44px Close along the top, the picture at its own ratio and as large as
  the room allows, Previous / Next at the edges only when there is somewhere to go, and a small
  floating dock of the actions: Download and Share, and on My Photos, set apart and quieter, Not
  me, which now asks before removing the photo. A phone pages by swipe and gets the dock as a bar
  clear of the home indicator; a keyboard pages by the arrows and closes with Escape; the tile's
  thumbnail holds the canvas while the preview loads, a preview that fails offers Retry, the
  neighbours are fetched ahead, and the viewer fades in and out briefly unless motion is turned
  down. Focus goes back to the tile on close and the gallery is left exactly where it was. Same
  photos, same download, share and "not me" handlers, same paging; the staff media viewer is
  untouched. [GuestPhotoViewer.js](../../frontend/src/components/ai-feature/guest/GuestPhotoViewer.js).
- **A guest's My Photos reads as their own collection rather than a results list.** A summary
  card - "Your Moments", how many photos were found featuring them, Use a different selfie beside
  it (under it on a phone) - replaces the count line and the floating link. One match is shown
  large and centred as a featured photo instead of a lone tile; two to five sit in a curated grid
  with the first given a two-by-two block on a desktop; more use the gallery grid. While the list
  loads a skeleton holds the shape, and with nothing matched a calm card says what to try - light,
  face visible, looking straight, closer - with Try another selfie. The same list, viewer and
  "This is not me" as before; no badge claims a best match, since the guest's list carries no
  such thing. [MyPhotosTab.js](../../frontend/src/components/ai-feature/guest/MyPhotosTab.js).
- **The guest gallery's tabs now read All Photos, Find My Photos, My Photos, then Sub Events.**
  Find My Photos moved ahead of My Photos, the order a new guest works through them. Which tabs an
  event shows and where a guest lands are unchanged.
  [guestActions.js](../../frontend/src/components/ai-feature/guest/guestActions.js).
- **The AI Feature guest gallery has one stable shell: a sticky header, one page scroll, a
  light footer.** The page was capped to a 1152px column, so on a wide monitor it sat in the
  middle with empty page either side and a handful of large tiles, and its header - marked sticky
  - scrolled away with the photos, because the application's `body` carries `overflow-x: hidden`
  and so was the header's scroll container, one that never scrolls. The gallery is now a box of
  the window's height that scrolls itself (still one scrollbar), so the header (mark, event, guest
  and dates, Leave, the tab bar) stays put on solid white while the photos scroll under it and
  keeps one height on every tab; the content sits in one column up to 1600px with a gutter that
  grows from 16px on a phone to 32px on a desktop; the grid draws two photos across on a phone, up
  to five on a desktop and six on a wide one, each a fixed square the photo covers so nothing
  shifts while it loads; the photo count is a small heading and the RSVP answer a slim line. The
  footer is the mark and "Photos by" alone, in the page's flow after the content - the second
  Leave it carried is gone, and the header's Leave is the same action it always was.
  [GuestGallery.js](../../frontend/src/components/ai-feature/guest/GuestGallery.js),
  [GuestPhotoGrid.js](../../frontend/src/components/ai-feature/guest/GuestPhotoGrid.js).
- **AI Feature's Event Default Settings is now a full-size settings workspace instead of one long
  popup.** More → Event Default Settings opens on the same shell as Image Selection's Settings: a
  rail on the left (General, Guest Access, AI & Face Recognition, Face Matching, Processing,
  Privacy & Access), one section at a time on the right, and Cancel / Save Settings fixed along the
  bottom - Save is off until something changes, says Saving... while it writes, reports "Saved
