  successfully", and a refused save keeps the edit on screen. Every setting the popup had is still
  here, under the same stored keys: event types and allowed formats are edited as chips rather than
  a comma-separated line; the face-matching thresholds sit under Face Matching → Advanced tuning,
  each with its range and a sentence on what it does; the medium / low confidence handling choices
  sit above them. Turning face recognition off dims clustering, selfie search, automatic matching
  and the thresholds rather than hiding them, and keeps their values for when it is turned back on;
  the clustering threshold dims on its own when clustering is off, and Selfie search notes when
  Guest Access → Enable guest selfie is still needed. ×, Cancel, Escape and a browser refresh ask
  "Discard unsaved changes?" while an edit is unsaved. Still defaults for *new* events only - an
  existing event keeps its own copy - and still `IMAGE_SELECTION / AI Event Default Settings` on
  both sides (READ sees the values dimmed, READ_WRITE saves); no server or database change.
  [EventDefaultSettingsPanel.js](../../frontend/src/components/ai-feature/settings/EventDefaultSettingsPanel.js),
  [aiEventDefaults.mjs](../../frontend/src/lib/aiEventDefaults.mjs).
- **Attendance check-in and check-out both need a live selfie and live location, taken on the
  spot.** Opening the camera now asks the server for a capture token that expires after two
  minutes and is tied to the login and to check-in or check-out; the capture is refused without a
  live one, so a kept photo or a replayed request is not recorded
  ([captureToken.js](../../backend/src/modules/hrm/attendance/captureToken.js), new
  `POST /api/hrm/attendance/capture-token`, under the same `HRM / Employee Attendance` guard as
  marking a day). The location's accuracy is now required, and **check-out is no longer best
  effort**: it takes the same selfie, fresh GPS reading and token as check-in, and somebody who
  cannot give them closes the day through Request correction. The
  [Attendance Register](../../frontend/src/components/hrm/AttendanceRegisterPage.js) shows a
  check-in selfie column and the full server date and time — day, month, year and seconds — for
  check-in and check-out, in the table and in a day's detail. No role gains or loses access.
- **Employee Attendance and the Attendance Register are back in the HRM sidebar.** They had been
  kept in the registry but taken off the menu; the `hidden` flag is removed from both in
  [navigation.mjs](../../frontend/src/constants/navigation.mjs). Access is unchanged: each still
  shows only to roles holding `HRM / Employee Attendance` or `HRM / Attendance Approval`
  respectively. A saved sidebar layout picks them up at their default position. Employee
  Referral stays off the menu.
- **The nightly deploy no longer rebuilds the face engine's Python environment.** It upgraded pip
  and reinstalled every face-recognition package on every deploy, which took minutes of CPU inside
  the deploy's 10-minute limit and was part of why the 2026-09-24 deploy stopped halfway. The
  environment (`backend/.venv-face`) stays on the server between deploys, so it is now set up by
  hand once per server, and again only when `backend/requirements-face.txt` changes. The deploy
  still checks the face engine is healthy afterwards.
  [deploy.yml](../../.github/workflows/deploy.yml),
  [DEPLOYMENT-WORKFLOW.md](../project-state/DEPLOYMENT-WORKFLOW.md#the-face-engines-python-environment)
- **A lead's bookings, quotations, meetings, CRD documents, saved group names and call transcripts
  are keyed to the lead, and a CRD document to its project.** They named the lead by a plain string
  nothing checked, so a deleted lead left its meetings and quotations pointing at nothing, and five
  screens wrote an empty string for "no lead". Migration
  [20260924150000_lead_children_keys_all](../../backend/prisma/migrations/20260924150000_lead_children_keys_all/migration.sql)
  holds the rules the Delete Lead action already applied: a lead with a booking, a quotation or a CRD
  document behind it cannot be deleted; a meeting, a group name and a call transcript go with their
  lead; a CRD document goes with its project, and one raised by hand is linked to the project it was raised for from the start. A document, a group name or a transcript raised without
  a lead has none rather than "". The sheet-row ledger deliberately gets no key, since it is what
  stops a deleted lead being imported again. A legacy project with a blank or unknown enquiry keeps
  an empty link rather than blocking the deploy, and `npm run report:lead-keys` lists such rows for
  attention (the development database has four test rows).
- **Every screen now shows the linked branch's or person's current name, with the stored text only
  as a fallback.** The text copy of a name (`source_branch`, `project_manager`, `crm`, `handler_name`,
  an employee's designation and the rest) was what every screen printed, so a rename had to be chased
  through every copy and any copy missed showed the old name. The database client now applies one
  reading rule ([services/resolvedNames.js](../../backend/src/services/resolvedNames.js), wired in
  [config/db.js](../../backend/src/config/db.js)): wherever a row carries a reference, the read returns
  the referenced row's name under the very key the screen always read, and where it carries none the
  text stays exactly as typed - "Malaysia" is still "Malaysia", a lead assigned to "Ajith" alone still
  says "Ajith". Projects, quotations, leads, cash entries, payment requests, tasks, client storage,
  candidates, saved group names, referrals, inspections, the sheet assignment rules and the employee's
  list answers are covered; nothing changes at any call site or on any screen, and nothing changes in
  what is stored. The raw-SQL readers follow the same rule in SQL: the CRM board and every other raw
  read of a lead (`RESOLVED_LEAD_SQL` in crmLeadService), the bell's follow-up notices and the call
  report's CRM column.
- **A lead's occasion rows and watch row are keyed to the lead.** Migration
  [20260924130000_lead_children_keys](../../backend/prisma/migrations/20260924130000_lead_children_keys/migration.sql)
  adds the foreign key on the two lead-owned tables whose rows always carry a lead and go with it; the
  studio's data had no blank or dangling row on either. The other seven tables that name a lead by
  `enquiry_id` wait, because they use an empty string to mean "no lead" - every quotation and saved
  group name, most CRD documents, four projects and two meetings - which a key cannot accept; their
  writers have to store nothing instead first.
- **An employee's list answers are stored as references as well as words, and the lists held
  several of become rows.** The Employee Repository offers twelve maintained lists and stored every
  answer as text, so "everyone in Sales" was a text search that also found "Sales Support" and a
  renamed entry had to be chased through every row by spelling. The employee row now carries the
  list row's id beside designation, work type, gender, blood group, marital status, emergency-contact
  relationship and both work locations, and department, skills and languages become
  `employee_department`, `employee_skill` and `employee_language` rows - derived on every save by
  [services/employeeReferences.js](../../backend/src/services/employeeReferences.js) (which also takes
  over the vertical and branch the employees controller resolved on its own) and backfilled by
  migration [20260924120000_employee_master_data_references](../../backend/prisma/migrations/20260924120000_employee_master_data_references/migration.sql).
  The text stays exactly as it was, so no screen changes; Field Settings renames those answers by
  the reference. Two list entries spelt the same resolve to no reference rather than a guess.
- **The schema and the migrations agree again.** `prisma migrate diff` reported forty-odd lines no
  change had asked for: hand-named indexes and keys, `updated_at` defaults, and a folder column on
  the deliverable config that was dropped from the schema without a migration. The names and
  defaults are now declared in schema.prisma; migration
  [20260924110000_reconcile_schema_drift](../../backend/prisma/migrations/20260924110000_reconcile_schema_drift/migration.sql)
  drops the leftover column with its key and indexes, and the folder purge no longer has to clear it
  defensively. A full replay of every migration now diffs clean against the schema.
- **Every "who did this" column is now a real foreign key with an index, and the branch and vertical
  name columns are as wide as the registers they copy.** 103 integer columns - `created_by`,
  `updated_by`, every `*_user_id` and `*_employee_id`, the login's `linked_employee_id`, and the
  `branch_id` on AI events, interview candidates and attendance sessions - held bare ids: a deleted
  login or employee left them pointing at nothing, and every "my records" query on them scanned the
  table. Migration [20260924100000_reference_foreign_keys](../../backend/prisma/migrations/20260924100000_reference_foreign_keys/migration.sql)
  clears the ids that already point at nothing, adds the constraint (`SET NULL` for optional columns,
  `CASCADE` for a login's own usage sessions, Google connection and OAuth state, `RESTRICT` for an
  approval's approver) and indexes each one; `source_branch`, `vertical` and `vertical_name` copies go
  from as little as VARCHAR(10) to VARCHAR(120). Tests that wrote invented user ids into those columns
  now create a real login. Run `npx prisma migrate deploy` locally; nothing to grant.
- **One table component draws every table, the Leads & Followups board and the Calendar's Schedule
  included.** Those two drew their own `<table>` beside the shared `DataTable` — each a second row,
  header, filter row and arrangement hook — and the two had already drifted twice: the cell clipping
  and then the column filters were each corrected on one and not the other, which is exactly why the
  Interview register's filter dropdown behaved differently from the board's. Both draw through
  [DataTable](../../frontend/src/components/data-table/DataTable.js) now. **Nothing on any other screen
  changed:** what only those two did — the server's own sort, a calendar or clock under a date or time
  column, columns sized to their contents until one is pulled, a right-click row menu, a blue hover —
  was added to the table as props that default to what every other table already had, and the
  twenty-six tables already on it were not edited. The board keeps every column arrangement anybody
  saved (the key is the one it always wrote), keeps its Update Details over a selection rather than
  having the header swap in Export and Clear, and keeps its own idea of "select all" over the pages
  not yet fetched. The arrangement hook the board used
  (`useTableArrangement`) is deleted with the last table that used it. The fifteen small lists inside
  forms, dialogs and panels — a lead's call logs and meetings, a project's payment schedule, a
  candidate's invites, the master-data register and the rest — draw through a new
  [SimpleTable](../../frontend/src/components/data-table/SimpleTable.js), each keeping the look it had.
  A test now fails on a `<table>` written anywhere else, so a fourth copy cannot start again. Two
  things read differently on purpose: a hidden column on the Schedule is remembered per source
  against the login rather than forgotten on reload, and the loading line on those two tables is the
  spinner every other table shows. **Four things are now the standard on every `DataTable`**, with
  `SimpleTable` the one exception: columns can be dragged and pulled, the arrangement is remembered
  against the login, cells clip to one line with the full value on hover, and rows are revealed a
  page at a time. The Schedule, the project shoot list and the two lists in Service Creation opted
  out of reordering or resizing, and eleven tables drew every row at once; a table inside a panel
  that scrolls instead of it now reveals its next page on its own until every row is drawn, so no
  list is left stranded at fifty.

### Fixed
- **A role ticked on Login Credentials is now actually saved.** The roles menu only *staged* a
  ticked role until a small Add button beside it was pressed, while still showing it ticked - so
  ticking a role and pressing Save Changes saved the account with no roles and said "Changes saved".
  On that form a tick is now the role, and the Add button is gone
  ([RolePicker](../../frontend/src/components/shared/RolePicker.js) `onChange`). Admin → User
  Management's Roles dialog, where Add is the save, is unchanged.
- **Attendance selfies now load on the Daily login screen and in the Attendance Register.** They
  were put straight into an image tag pointing at the guarded selfie endpoint, which reads only an
  `Authorization` header that an image tag never sends, so every one was refused. They are fetched
  with the staff token through `AuthImage` instead, which moves from `ai-feature/shared/` to
  [components/shared/](../../frontend/src/components/shared/AuthImage.js) now that two modules use it
  and shows a dash rather than an endless placeholder when a picture is refused; `attendanceSelfieUrl` is gone and
  `attendanceSelfiePath` replaces it in
  [attendance.service.js](../../frontend/src/services/hrm/attendance.service.js).
- **The notification bell no longer stalls the whole app when the server is slow.** Every open tab
  asks for notifications once a minute, and the follow-ups, WhatsApp replies and calls it shows are
  heavy queries; on a busy server they piled up, filled every database connection and left pages
  waiting for their data. Those notices are now worked out once a minute per person and shared by
  every tab and every request that arrives meanwhile. Stored notices, and marking them read, stay
  live. No access change: each request still checks its permissions before anything is served.
  [controller.js](../../backend/src/modules/notifications/controller.js)
- **The SUPER_ADMIN role now holds `PROJECT / Show All Projects`, so an administrator who is not a
  Super Admin *login* can open the whole-studio project register.** The permission was created by
  20260919120000 with no grants, which is right for every other role and wrong for this one: the
  role named SUPER_ADMIN is granted its permissions as rows like any other, and the two blanket
  grant migrations that fill it both ran before this permission existed. A login whose `role` column
  says SUPER_ADMIN was unaffected, because the IAM service short-circuits on that column without
  reading a grant - but an administrator added the way the studio actually adds one, an EMPLOYEE
  login carrying the SUPER_ADMIN role, was refused the register silently while every screen said
  they had full access. The earlier migration is not edited; a new one adds only the missing row,
  idempotently, the way the permission migrations beside it do. Roles reached: SUPER_ADMIN alone -
  no other role, scope or branch is touched
  ([migration](../../backend/prisma/migrations/20260924160000_super_admin_holds_show_all_projects/migration.sql)).
  `iam-super-admin-grants.test.js` is the invariant that named it.
- **A Signature Album's photo links never point at a bare R2 key.** On a machine without
  `R2_PUBLIC_BASE_URL` - CI, a fresh development database - a photo that has an R2 preview was
  handed a relative "/p/1.jpg" that nothing serves, because the R2 helper joins an empty base and
  the key as they are. The album module now uses an R2 address only when it is an absolute one and
  otherwise falls back to its own media route, for the photo URLs and the preview download alike;
  the develop CI run that failed on exactly this passes again
  ([signatureAlbum.helpers.js](../../backend/src/modules/image-selection/signature-album/signatureAlbum.helpers.js)).
- **The Next.js development indicator is switched off.** The "N" button that `next dev` pins to
  the bottom-left of every page (Route / Bundler / Preferences) was showing on the client-facing
  Signature Album and gallery pages during studio reviews and read as part of the product.
  `devIndicators: false` in [next.config.mjs](../../frontend/next.config.mjs) hides it; compile and
  runtime errors still surface, and a production build never had it.
- **The Signature Album preview is the client's page, not a picture of it.** The live preview and
  the Preview Album dialog had drifted from the published album in three ways: preview-only
  styling (a 420px cover band where the client gets the full viewport, smaller titles, tighter
  chapter padding - and a cover that fills its frame is cropped by the frame's height, so the
  crop differed), the admin window's breakpoints (a "phone" preview laid out with desktop rules),
  and a second content builder in the browser. Now one server builder
  (`contentFor` in [signatureAlbum.client.service.js](../../backend/src/modules/image-selection/signature-album/signatureAlbum.client.service.js))
  answers both the client's `/content` and a studio-only `GET /:albumId/preview` behind the album's
  permission, one presentational view
  ([SignatureAlbumView](../../frontend/src/components/image-selection/signature-album/client/SignatureAlbumView.js))
  draws both pages, every preview-only style is gone, and the preview is the real page at
  `/signature-album-preview/<albumId>` inside an iframe sized like the device (1440×900 or 390×844)
  and scaled to fit - so its breakpoints, its `100svh` cover and its crop are the client's own.
  Pixel comparison of the preview page against the client page at both sizes, for all seven cover
  styles, shows the same rendering. The preview refetches after every save in the Design and Album
  panels, so it follows each change without a reload. The desktop frame is 1440 wide and exactly
  as tall as the box it sits in, so it fills the Album tab, the Design column and the Preview
  Album dialog alike - it used to be a fixed 900 tall, which left the bottom of a tall box empty
  and the bottom of a short one unreachable; the phone frame keeps its 390×844 shape, centred.
- **The Signature Album's ending and PIN screen show the studio's mark whenever Show Studio Logo
  is on.** With no Global Logo uploaded yet, the switch was on and nothing logo-like appeared -
  only the name in text - because the album drew the mark only when a logo file existed. Both
  screens now draw it through `AppLogo`, which shows the Global Logo when there is one and the
  application's bundled mark when there is not, the rule every other surface follows; the name
  sits under it on its own switch. The ending's Website and Instagram fields also accept an
  address typed without `https://` (it is put in front) and refuse one with a space in it, since
  a bare `instagram.com/studio` was being linked as a path of the album's own page. The gap
  between the last chapter and Thank You is shorter. The two links are now named after where they
  go - the site's own name (`dontblinkstudioz.com`) and the Instagram handle (`@dontblinkstudioz`)
  - rather than the words "Website" and "Instagram", and the workspace's live preview draws the
  same ending the client gets (mark, name, links, footer line) instead of a stand-in without them:
  the studio's album now carries the branding block the client page reads, built by one shared
  function ([signatureAlbum.helpers.js](../../backend/src/modules/image-selection/signature-album/signatureAlbum.helpers.js)).
- **Pressing a switch far down a scrolling pane no longer scrolls the whole app shell.** The
  Signature Album's Design panel showed it: pressing *Show highlights section* or *Show image
  numbers* dragged the module header and the project header off the top of the screen and left the
  page grey below. The switch's checkbox is visually hidden with `sr-only`, which positions it
  absolutely, and with no positioned ancestor inside the pane its containing block was the app
  shell - so a switch 1500px down a scrolled pane sat 1500px down the *shell's* layout, gave the
  shell scrollable overflow, and the focus a press gives the checkbox scrolled the shell to reach
  it. [ToggleSwitch](../../frontend/src/components/shared/ToggleSwitch.js)'s label is now `relative`,
  so the hidden checkbox is contained by the switch it belongs to and the pane is what scrolls.
  One class on the shared switch; every other switch in the application looks and behaves as
  before, and the Selection Settings rail's own switches were re-checked.

- **A lead added by hand showed an Added Time 5 hours 30 minutes behind.** The Add Lead form stamped
  the time from the computer's own clock - on a machine set to UTC, 5:30 behind India - and took it
  when the form was opened rather than when the lead was saved; a lead that arrived with no time then
  fell back to the row's insert time, written in whatever zone the database runs on. The form now
  stamps Added Time and Enquiry Date at save, in India time, and the server fills either one in India
  time when a record brings none ([services/studioTime.js](../../backend/src/services/studioTime.js)),
  for the single form, Bulk Entry and CSV alike. A time a sheet, a file or a placed call brought is
  kept as it came. A lead created from a call now takes the studio's date rather than UTC's, which
  was a day early for calls between midnight and 5:30 am. The Google Sheet sync already took the
  time from the sheet's own timestamp and the date in India time, and is unchanged.
- **Create Project refused a saved WhatsApp group name ("vertical: expected one of WED|QQ|CORP").**
  The group name keeps its vertical as the code the project stores, and the new reading rule was
  rewriting that code to the vertical's name on the way out; older group names also spell the
  vertical out as "Dum Dum". The group name's `vertical` is no longer resolved or renamed by
  reference ([resolvedNames.js](../../backend/src/services/resolvedNames.js),
  [nameCopies.js](../../backend/src/services/nameCopies.js)), and the project form takes the code from
  the group type whatever the row stored (`projectVerticalFor` in
  [saleGroupName.mjs](../../frontend/src/lib/saleGroupName.mjs)). The Client Gifts detail panel now
  shows the vertical as DD like every other project panel, not WED.
- **The face engine installs itself on every deploy, in an environment of its own.** Production had
  been answering *Face engine offline — ModuleNotFoundError: No module named 'cv2'*: the engine ran
  on whatever `python3` the server had, nothing in the deploy had ever installed its packages, and
  the only remedy was `pip install` by hand after each release. The deploy now runs
  `backend/scripts/face-engine-setup.sh` first — before the backend's own install, so a failure
  leaves the running application untouched — which creates **`backend/.venv-face`** if it is missing,
  installs `backend/requirements-face.txt` into it, and proves the result with `face_engine.py
  --check` (every import, the two model files fetched from the Hub, one detection) before anything
  else proceeds. That environment is the face engine's alone, apart from Node and from the
  call-transcript engine's `.venv`. The requirements file now names every package the engine and
  insightface actually import, with **`opencv-python-headless`** — insightface declares the desktop
  `opencv-python`, so it is installed `--no-deps` and the headless build is what a server gets.
  `/api/health` carries `faceEngine` (`disabled` / `ready` / `starting` / `not-started` /
  `offline`) and the deploy's health check waits for the models to load and **fails on `offline`**,
  with the reason and the `[face-engine]` log lines; `/api/health/face-engine` (behind
  `SETTINGS / Application Monitoring`) adds the interpreter, whether the environment and model files
  exist, and how many face jobs wait in each module. None of this is a dependency of anything else:
  with the engine off or broken the application answers `ok: true`, Image Selection, Album Proofing
  and Project Management run as before, and queued face jobs park and resume by themselves once it
  answers. A server that says offline is repaired with the same script
  ([DEPLOYMENT-WORKFLOW.md](../project-state/DEPLOYMENT-WORKFLOW.md#the-face-engines-python-environment)).
- **A Google Sheet or CSV that still says a branch's or a person's old name no longer undoes the
  rename on its next sync.** Renaming in Settings rewrote every copy in the database but could not
  reach the sheet; the stale cell then matched no branch and was written back as text with no link.
  A branch and an employee now remember the names they gave up (`aliases`, migration
  [20260924140000](../../backend/prisma/migrations/20260924140000_branch_and_employee_aliases/migration.sql)),
  written at the moment of the rename, and the matcher behind every sheet, CSV and form write
  resolves a former name to its row and rewrites the cell's text to the current spelling. A former
  name somebody holds now is theirs, and one two rows gave up is nobody's. The branch **code**
  (`ERODE_TN`) is accepted too, so a sheet can hold a value that never changes; and "Tirupur", the
  spelling six leads use for Tiruppur, is taught as an alias.
- **Converting a lead or a quotation to a project no longer translates the branch through names
  typed into the browser.** `projectBooking.js` held a map of the six branch names, so a branch
  renamed in Settings went on being translated to its old name and Create Project's Source Branch
  refused it. The form now resolves whatever text it is handed - the register's spelling, the name
  without its state, or a studio short form like "cbe" - against the branch list it has just loaded
  ([resolveBranchName](../../frontend/src/lib/branchName.js)), and clears only text that names no branch
  or two. The register is the one judge of what a branch is called.
- **Renaming a branch, a vertical, an employee or a login now renames it everywhere it was written
  out.** Projects, quotations, leads, cash entries, payment requests, tasks, candidates, saved group
  names and AI events store the branch's name as text beside `branch_id`; projects, leads, quotations,
  cash entries, client storage, referrals, inspections and the sheet assignment rules store the
  employee's; tasks store the login's. The register changed and the copies did not, so the screens
  went on showing the old name - and the next save of one of those rows looked the old name up,
  found no branch, and dropped the row out of scope. [services/nameCopies.js](../../backend/src/services/nameCopies.js)
  is the one list of those copies; Settings -> Branches, Field Settings (branches and verticals now
  declare their uses, so they are counted and refused for deletion like every other list), the
  Employee Repository and Administration -> Users each rewrite them inside the rename's own
  transaction, by reference where the row has one and by exact text where it does not - people only
  ever by reference, so a namesake's records never change hands. Branches says how many records
  followed. Audit rows (asset status log, task activity) deliberately keep the name that was current
  when they were written. The bell's follow-up notices and lead-assignment notices now find the
  person through the lead's `owner_employee_id` and the employee behind the login rather than by
  matching the login's display name, so two logins sharing a name no longer share each other's
  follow-ups. Frontend: the Branches panel shows the server's rename message.
- **The Interview register accepted the same candidate over and over — three of the four ways in
  never asked.** Only the Google Sheet importer checked; **Add Application**, **Bulk Entry** and the
  **CSV import** wrote whatever they were handed. So a candidate who applied twice, a file imported
  twice, or a name typed in by two people became two records — two stages, two sets of remarks, and
  an interview booked against the copy nobody is reading. All four now hold to **one person, one
  row**, matched on **phone or email** using the sheet importer's own normalised keys, so
  `98765 43210`, `+91 9876543210` and `9876543210` are one person and not three, and an address
  matches whatever it was capitalised as. Names are deliberately not matched: two Priya Sharmas are
  two people. A refusal **names the candidate and their ID**, so the studio can go and open the
  record rather than hunt a register of thousands for it. **Bulk Entry** follows the CRM board
  exactly: the whole batch is answered **before any of it is written**, every clashing row is
  reported rather than the first, the grid marks those rows in place, and two rows of one paste
  carrying the same number catch each other — the case no per-row check can ever see. **Editing** is
  guarded too, but only where the write actually *changes* the number or address: the register
  already holds duplicates from before the rule existed, and refusing an unchanged value would make
  exactly those rows uneditable, so nobody could open one and correct it. A candidate with neither a
  phone nor an email — a referral that arrives as a name — is still accepted, because there is
  nothing to match on and nothing to refuse.
- **Add Application now says "already on the register" while the number is still being typed.** The
  rule above is enforced on the save, and a refusal that arrives after somebody has filled in a
  whole application arrives too late. So both forms that take a contact number — **Add Application**
  and the candidate record's own form — ask the register as it is typed and answer **under the
  field**, naming the candidate and their ID so it can be gone and looked at. It is **amber, not
  red**: a number the register already holds is worth stopping for and is not an error, and the form
  is perfectly valid. Asked only once the number is whole, so nothing appears against the first four
  digits of one; debounced; and every answer is held against the field as it stands, so a reply
  landing after an edit warns about nothing rather than about a number nobody is looking at. A check
  that cannot be made is not a clash — the form stays usable and Confirm is never held up by a
  network having a moment, because the save is refused by the server regardless. The warning and the
  refusal share one definition of "the same person", or a form would warn about what it then accepts.
  Bulk Entry is deliberately unchanged: it marks its rows when the register refuses the batch, which
  is what the CRM's own Bulk Entry does.
- **The Status filter on the Interview register offered an empty menu.** Every other column's menu
  listed its values; Status listed nothing, so the one column whose values a reader already knows by
  heart was the one they could not pick from. The menu query asked for rows where the column "is not
  null", which Prisma refuses outright against a column that is **required** — and Status is the only
  filterable column on the register that cannot be null, which is exactly why it was the only one
  that broke. The read threw, the page turned a failed read into an empty list, and the menu drew
  itself blank with nothing anywhere saying why. Blanks are now dropped after the read instead
  ([service.js](../../backend/src/modules/hrm/interview/service.js)), where a null and the empty string
  a spreadsheet import leaves behind are the same thing and both have to go. Two tests hold the
  shape: every column offered as a filter is a real column, and the register never offers a menu the
  server will not answer for — both routes to the same blank menu.
- **Filtering the Interview register now filters the register, not the hundred rows you have
  scrolled to.** The register is read a page at a time, but its filter row was matched in the
  browser — so a filter only ever saw the rows already fetched. Picking a value the menu plainly
  listed answered **"no records found"** over a stage holding hundreds of them, and the menu itself
  grew as you scrolled because it was built from the rows in hand. Both now go to the server
  ([service.js](../../backend/src/modules/hrm/interview/service.js)): a filter is part of the query, a
  menu asks the column what it holds, and **Select all** resolves to the filtered set rather than
  the whole stage — so a bulk action can no longer reach rows the reader had filtered away. The
  menus also **cascade**, the way Leads and Follow-ups do: opened with a Source filter on, the
  Position menu offers the positions that Source actually holds, so the values on offer are values
  that would find rows. Two things keep that from turning on the reader. A column's own filter is
  left out of the query behind its own menu, or ticking one value would narrow that menu to the one
  value ticked and a filter could never be widened again. And a menu already open **does not
  re-narrow under the hand using it** — these are multi-selects, so the reader is still in the list
  when the filter changes; it settles on the next open instead. The CRM board reached the same two
  conclusions. Typing into a text filter is debounced, and switching stage tab clears the filter
  row along with the selection — a stage is its own set of values, and a filter carried across is an
  empty table over a stage that has rows. Only listed columns can be filtered; anything else in the
  parameter is ignored rather than handed to a query.
- **A candidate added by hand had no Source, so the column was blank for exactly the rows a person
  was responsible for.** The sheet importer stamped "Google sheet integration" and the public
  application form stamped "Application Form", but a candidate typed into **Add Application** or
  **Bulk Entry**, or brought in by **CSV import**, was left with nothing — so the column answered
  "where did this come from?" for everything an importer brought and for nothing anybody entered.
  Those three now stamp **"Manual entry"** and **"CSV import"**, so all four ways into the register
  say which way it was. It is a **fallback, never an override**: a Source somebody names — Naukri, a
  referral, a walk-in — is kept exactly as they named it, which is the same rule the sheet importer
  has always followed for a row carrying its own Source column. Stamped at the way in rather than in
  the service, because the service cannot tell them apart: a CSV row and a typed one are the same
  `createCandidate` call, and only the route knows which it was.
- **Add Lead cannot make two leads out of one double-click any more.** Its Confirm button had no
  in-flight guard at all — a plain button with nothing disabling it — and the enquiry id is minted on
  the **server**, so a second press was not a duplicate anything could refuse: it was a second, real
  lead with its own id for somebody to find and delete. On a slow connection the window was seconds
  wide, which is exactly when people press again. Confirm now stands down and reads **Adding…**
  while the lead is being written, and so does Cancel. The "go on with it anyway" path out of the
  duplicate-phone warning reaches the same save by a second route, so it carries the same guard.
  The **lead popup's** three save buttons got it too, for a different reason: writing the same values
  twice leaves the lead as it was, but **Update and Proceed Next** advances the board, so a
  double-click saved one lead and stepped past two — and the one it skipped is the one nobody then
  works. **Bulk Entry** already disabled its button while saving and was never the one producing
  duplicates; it gained the missing half below.
  The guard itself is [`useSubmitOnce`](../../frontend/src/components/shared/useSubmitOnce.js), and it
  is a **ref** as well as a state flag. `disabled={busy}` is the half everyone writes and it is not
  enough on its own: React applies state on the next render, so two clicks in the same tick — which
  is what a double-click *is* — both read the old value and both fire. The ref is written before
  anything awaits. It is released in a `finally`, so a save that failed leaves the form usable
  rather than locking somebody out of what they typed. A **delay** on the button is deliberately not
  the answer: whatever delay is chosen, a save slower than it is still double-postable, and every
  ordinary save is made to feel sluggish to pay for it.
- **Every button that writes now says the same thing while it is working.** The application had
  three different answers to "this is in flight": some buttons greyed *and* said **Saving...**, some
  only greyed, and a few did neither. Those read as three different things to somebody waiting — and
  a greyed button with no word reads as *refused* rather than *working*, which is precisely the
  reading that makes people press again. It is now one prop on the shared `Button`: **`busy`**
  disables it, flips its label, and tells a screen reader (`aria-busy`) the same thing the colour is
  saying. The verb follows the button rather than being written twice — **Add Lead** → *Adding...*,
  **Send Screening Form** → *Sending...*, **Update and Close** → *Updating...*, **Save Changes** →
  *Saving...* — with `busyLabel` for the cases where the obvious word is not the honest one, and
  *Saving...* as the fallback for a label it cannot read. Three dots, matching the 183 places that
  already wrote it that way rather than the 26 that did not. A **Cancel** beside a running save is
  deliberately left grey and silent: it is disabled because the form cannot be dismissed mid-save,
  not because it is doing something ([Button.js](../../frontend/src/components/shared/Button.js)).

### Changed
- **One linked Google account now schedules for the whole studio.** Booking an interview used to
  demand a connection from whoever pressed the button, so every recruiter had to link their own
  Google account before they could schedule anything — and a studio that had already connected one
  still had to do it again, person by person. Once **any** account is linked under Settings →
  Integrations → Interview, everybody schedules through it.
  Your own still wins where you have one: the interview lands in your own diary and the invite goes
  out in your name. The studio's account is the fallback, not the rule, and it is the **earliest**
  linked account carrying the Calendar consent — earliest so the organiser does not change as other
  people connect their own. A connection Google has since rejected is skipped rather than refusing
  the studio. When the fallback is used the confirmation says so — *"It is on the studio's calendar
  (address), not yours"* — because the first thing that otherwise happens is somebody opening their
  own calendar, not finding the interview, and booking it twice. Cancelling runs as the account that
  **owns** the event, since an interview on the studio's calendar cannot be deleted with a
  recruiter's own token. This is the reading the linked Google Sheets have always used, where the
  connection stored on the *link* does the reading rather than whoever triggered the sync
  ([meetings.js](../../backend/src/modules/hrm/interview/meetings.js)).

### Fixed
- **The Interview table's column filters offered only the values on the page you had scrolled to.**
  The register is read a page at a time, and the filter menus were built from the rows the browser
  held — so Position offered a handful of the 34 the column actually has, and the list *grew* as you
  scrolled. A filter that hides the rows it was opened to find is the one thing a filter must not
  do. Each menu now asks the column: a new `GET /api/hrm/interview/candidates/column-values`, called
  when the menu opens (one per column, and most are never opened), narrowed by the stage being read
  so the list describes the tab in front of you. An answer arriving after you have changed tab
  belongs to the tab you left and is discarded; a column already answered is not asked twice. The
  column is checked against an allowlist on both sides rather than trusted, and a test asserts the
  two lists name the same set. Tables that hold all their rows are untouched — deriving the values
  from the data is the whole answer there, and `DataTable` still does exactly that unless a page
  supplies `filterValues`.

### Reverted
- **Position Applied For reads the designation list again.** It was changed to offer the positions
  the register holds, on the theory that a picker missing 34 of them was why they were not showing.
  It was not: the picker is what somebody **chooses from** when writing an application down, and the
  filter that was actually hiding them is fixed above. Offering the sheet's own ad labels
  ("CRM Static Erode") as positions to assign would have put them on new candidates — the same fault
  the CRM's assignee picker was fixed for. The two questions stay apart: the picker is the studio's
  maintained roles, the filter is what the column holds.
- **"Select all" on the Interview register selected the first 100, not every candidate.** The
  register is read a page at a time, and the header checkbox ticked `table.getRowModel().rows` — the
  rows loaded so far. So it selected 100 of 5,769, and worse, the same gesture meant a different
  number depending on how far somebody had scrolled. It now uses the **same selection model the
  Leads board already had** (`lib/leadSelection`): `allMatching` plus the exceptions somebody
  un-ticked, which is one boolean for a stage of five thousand rather than five thousand strings,
  and lets a row fetched later arrive already ticked. The count reads against the stage's own total,
  so the bar says 5,769 and means it. The ids are resolved when an action runs, from a new
  `GET /api/hrm/interview/candidates/ids` behind the module's existing guard, with the reader's
  exceptions removed — and a stage larger than 20,000 is refused outright rather than silently acted
  on in part. `DataTable`'s selection now takes an optional `model` for a paged table; the sixteen
  client-side tables keep the plain set of ids, which is correct for them because they hold every
  row. Checked all 18 tables with selection: Interview was the only one affected — Leads already
  handled it, and the rest load their rows whole.
- **Position Applied For offered the wrong list, so 34 of the register's positions were unpickable.**
  The picker was built from the studio's **designation** list, on the argument that most applications
  are for a role the studio actually has. That is a different question: a designation is what
  somebody is *employed* as, a position applied for is what an advert asked for, and nobody keeps the
  two in step. The register held **36** positions against a picker offering **32** designations, with
  **34 of the 36 missing** — every one written by the linked Google Sheet, which is where 5,752 of
  the 5,757 candidates with a position came from. The sheet writes the candidate's `position` and
  nothing in the sync path touches the option lists, so the gap only appeared once a sheet was
  linked: with hand-typed candidates the two agreed by construction, because every position on a row
  got there by being picked. The picker now offers the studio's own **Interview → Positions** list
  first, then every position the register already holds (a capped `DISTINCT` over the column). The
  `designation` table is no longer read by this endpoint and is still never written to it.

### Changed
- **A role's branches now apply to the permissions that role grants, and to nothing else.** Somebody
  holding **CRM Leads across all six branches** through one role and **Asset Management in
  Coimbatore** through another reaches six branches *for leads* and one *for assets* — the branch
  picker on each screen offers accordingly, and each screen's save is checked against its own answer.
  Until now there was one answer per person, the union of every branch every role they held happened
  to name, so **the widest role somebody held silently widened all the others**: that person got
  every branch's assets too. Every call into the branch API now names the `module / feature` it is
  asking about, and `checkBranchAccess` refuses to be built without one rather than falling back to
  the union — which would fail open, on a write.
  Two consequences worth knowing before you deploy. **A role that names no branches contributes
  none** — it is not read as "all of them"; Role Management already refuses to save a role without a
  branch, so this only affects roles that predate that rule (`EMPLOYEE`, `TEAM_LEAD`,
  `BRANCH_MANAGER` and two demo roles currently have none). And **a person's own branch is no longer
  added automatically**: it used to be, which is what made a restriction unenforceable — a role
  saying "Coimbatore only" still showed an Erode-based person the whole of Erode. Being narrowed to
  no branch is not being shown nothing: everybody still sees the work their own name is on and their
  team's. Where a screen asks both questions, they take two permissions — *where may you use this
  screen* is the screen's own, *whose work may you see that is not your own* is
  `ADMIN / Branch Administration` ([iam.service.js](../../backend/src/services/iam.service.js),
  [dataScope.service.js](../../backend/src/services/dataScope.service.js),
  [IAM.md](../project-state/IAM.md#branch-reach-belongs-to-the-permission-not-to-the-person)).

### Added
- **Chasing a candidate is a stage now: `Follow Up`, straight after Yet to Connect.** A candidate
  nobody has spoken to gets one of two things done to them — the screening form, or a call — and
  only the first of those was a stage. The second was a remark, or nothing, so the register could
  not tell *"nobody has tried this person"* from *"we have called three times and they have not
  answered"*, which are the two rows that need completely different work. Its own tab with a count,
  and its own dot colour: a deeper sky than Yet to Connect beside it, so the pair reads as one thing
  progressing.
  **Follow Up** sits on the row ⋮ beside **Send Screening Form**, at Yet to Connect and at Follow Up
  itself — the two stages where chasing *is* the work. It is not offered further on, where something
  has already happened that the move would undo: a form out, a conversation in the diary, an answer
  given. On a row already at Follow Up it reads **Log Another Follow-up**, because there it is not a
  stage change at all but a second attempt recorded against the same one. It opens the same dialog
  the three decisions use, plus the one thing they never ask for — **a next follow-up date** —
  because Follow Up is not a decision about the person but about the work: this is worth another
  try, on this day. Selected, On Hold and Rejected still ask for a reason alone and cannot touch the
  date.
  **Every attempt appends.** The remark lands in the candidate's log rather than over the last one,
  carrying the stage, the day it was written and the day it promised — so three unanswered calls
  read as three, with what was agreed each time. The record shows the whole list, in the info panel
  beside the table and in the panel a double-click opens; the section is now called **Follow-ups &
  Decisions**, since that is what it holds. HR's single **Remarks** note is untouched and still its
  own section — that one is written over when it is edited, and these are not. A linked sheet's own
  vocabulary folds onto the stage too: *follow up*, *callback*, *no response*, *not reachable*,
  *did not pick* ([interviewConfig.js](../../frontend/src/constants/interviewConfig.js),
  [candidates.js](../../backend/src/modules/hrm/interview/candidates.js)).

### Changed
- **The Employee Repository now shows everybody in the branches a role reaches.** Ticking branches
  on a role used to grant nothing here: the register answered the ownership rule, so a person saw
  themselves and whoever reported to them and nobody else — not even a colleague sitting in their
  own branch — unless they also held `ADMIN / Branch Administration`, which is a studio-wide grant
  that opens every lead, asset, project and cash entry in those branches at the same time. There was
  no way to say "this person runs HR for Erode" without also handing them Erode's sales. The
  branches on the role now open this register on their own, and ticking every branch shows everybody
  who has one. It is the only screen that works this way, deliberately: "reaching a branch is not a
  claim on the work of the people in it" is about *work*, and a register of **who those people are**
  is the studio's own shape — which is why the employee directory behind the asset and client-storage
  pickers needs no permission at all. Branch Administration is unchanged everywhere else, and here
  it now neither adds nor narrows ([IAM.md](../project-state/IAM.md#the-one-exception-the-employee-repository)).

### Fixed
- **Nobody could see the people waiting to be onboarded.** A hire at **HR Onboarding Pending** or
  **Employee Submission Pending** has no branch yet, and usually no work location and no reporting
  line either — the form that answers those questions is the one still being filled in. That made
  the row reachable by no branch and named on nobody, so it was invisible to every holder of the
  Employee Repository except a Super Admin, and the onboarding it was waiting for could not be done
  by the people whose job it is. Both statuses are now shown to anyone holding
  `HRM / Employee Management`, whatever branch they reach. It is the **status** that is excepted and
  not the missing branch: an Active employee with no branch is still not shown, because "no branch
  yet" must not become a way of being visible to everybody. The exception ends by itself once the
  form is completed and the person has a branch like anyone else
  ([controller.js](../../backend/src/modules/hrm/employees/controller.js)).

- **Ticking Branch Admin now actually makes somebody one.** `ADMIN / Branch Administration` — the
  permission that widens a person from the work their own name is on to every record in the branches
  their roles reach — is carried by the **Branch Admin** tick itself, and can no longer be granted
  any other way. It used to be an ordinary row in the role matrix, so a role could be marked Branch
  Admin and not hold it; the studio ticked the box, saw nothing change, and had no way to find out
  why. The name did not help either — "Administer Branches" reads like the right to edit the branch
  register, which is Settings → Organization and something else entirely. It is now left out of
  `GET /api/admin/permissions`, which closes the role editor **and** the Admin Permission dialog at
  once (one response feeds both), and a role saved from that editor can no longer set or clear it by
  hand. **Nobody loses access**: a role granted it before keeps it, because the matrix is replaced
  whole on every save and anything the editor was never offered is carried across rather than
  revoked. Merged most-permissively at `READ_WRITE`, so it only ever widens
  ([iam.service.js](../../backend/src/services/iam.service.js),
  [migration](../../backend/prisma/migrations/20260923100000_branch_administration_follows_the_tick/migration.sql)).

### Added
- **Every table in the application can now be ticked row by row, and a selection exported or
  deleted in one go.** Leads & Followups and the Interview register have had a column of tick boxes
  for a while; every other register — Assets, Vendors, Client Storage, the Employee Repository,
  Tasks, Projects, Sales Records, Quotations, Accounts, Users, Roles, Contacts, Referrals, Client
  Gifts, CRD Documents, the Schedule, the attendance registers and Payment Requests — was worked a
  row at a time. Ticking any of them now puts **Export (12)** and, where the register has a delete,
  **Delete (12)** in the screen's own title bar — where Add Employee, Add Task or Add Project
  stands, with those stepping aside while anything is ticked and coming back the moment it is
  cleared, and a ✕ to clear it. That is where Leads & Followups and the Interview register have
  always put theirs, and now every register answers the question in the same place rather than in a
  bar of its own over the page. Export writes the ticked rows through the table's own exporter, so
  it is the same columns and the same file the ⋮ menu's Export CSV gives, over fewer rows.

  **The boxes are their own permission: `ADMIN / Table Select All`**, shown in Role Management as
  *Select Table Rows in Bulk*. One row rather than one per table — the alternative asked the studio
  the same question twenty-odd times. **Nobody holds it but a Super Admin until a role is ticked and
  re-saved in Role Management**: the permission ships with the code in a migration
  (`20260923100000_table_select_all_permission`), and neither the seed nor the migration writes
  grants. `SALES / CRM Lead Select All` and `HRM / Interview Select All` are unchanged; each guards
  bulk routes that do more than delete, so folding them in would have widened what one tick grants.

  **A bulk delete asks for this permission *and* the screen's own Read / Write**, so it is never a
  way around a register somebody may only read — on a read-only screen the boxes appear and offer
  Export alone. It also never widens which rows are yours: each `DELETE …/bulk` runs the module's
  own single-row delete once per id, so an asset outside your branches, an employee still holding a
  camera, a project you do not manage, a system role, your own account and the last Super Admin are
  all refused exactly as they are one at a time. Those rows keep their ticks and the message says
  how many were left alone, so a second press retries just them. Seven registers offer Export and no
  Delete — Contacts, Employee Referrals, Client Gifts, CRD Documents, the Schedule, the attendance
  registers and Payment Requests — because they are read from records other modules own, or are
  worked by status rather than by deletion.

  [bulkDelete.js](../../backend/src/services/bulkDelete.js),
  [useTableSelection.js](../../frontend/src/components/data-table/useTableSelection.js),
  [TableSelectionActions.js](../../frontend/src/components/data-table/TableSelectionActions.js),
  [IAM.md](../project-state/IAM.md).

- **A candidate waiting on their screening form can be moved to Form Submitted by hand.** The form
  is one way answers arrive, not the only one — a candidate replies on WhatsApp, emails a CV, or
  reads the questions out over the phone — but the row stayed at *Form Submission Pending* until the
  link itself was used, so somebody the studio had already heard from sat on a tab meaning "waiting
  on them", and HR's note of what they said had nowhere to be read from. **Mark Form Submitted**
  reads first on that row's ⋮, above the entries about the wait itself. It goes straight through
  with no Why dialog: that is for Selected, On Hold and Rejected, which are decisions somebody has
  to answer for, while this is a stage moving forward — the same way sending an onboarding invite
  moves a candidate to Onboarded. The form is left exactly as it is, so a live link still works;
  **Cancel Form** remains the entry for calling one off, rather than this doing it silently.
  [InterviewPage.js](../../frontend/src/components/hrm/InterviewPage.js).

- **A way to undo a mistaken deletion in the Interview register, and to rebuild it from its sheet.**
  `backend/scripts/resetInterviewSheetImport.js`, Interview only, dry-run by default. Deleting a
  candidate is soft, so `--restore` simply clears `deleted_at` and every field comes back as it was
  — status, interviewer, remarks, interview date, follow-up dates — none of which a spreadsheet
  holds. `--flush` is the other case: it permanently removes the deleted candidates and this link's
  rows from `hrm_interview_google_sheet_row`, which are the two things that stop a sync re-importing
  somebody the register has seen, so Sync Now fills it from the sheet again under new candidate
  codes. `--since=YYYY-MM-DD` narrows either to one afternoon's mistake rather than everybody ever
  deleted. Run with no arguments it lists the links with their live, deleted and ledger counts, and
  names nothing to change. Nothing is written without `--apply`; no other module's tables are read.

- **The client gallery can filter a folder by the people in it.** Above a folder's All Photos /
  Selected tabs a new **People** section shows All (with the folder's photo count) and then a face
  circle per person found in that folder, each with their name and photo count beneath; pressing a
  person rings their circle in blue, ticks it, and narrows the same grid to their photos in that
  folder; pressing more people adds them, so the grid shows every photo **any** chosen person is in,
  with an *N selected* badge and a **Clear** action once two or more are chosen; pressing a chosen person again removes them, and All brings the folder back. People chosen
  are never photos chosen - the tick on a photo card and the Selected count are untouched. The section is always one row: All stays fixed on the left and the people
  scroll sideways beside it natively, with edge fades saying there is more and no arrow buttons; the
  strip leads with as many people as its width holds and a **More** item - the next faces stacked with a +N badge - that puts the rest
  into the same strip and scrolls towards them, with **Less** at its end to fold it back. Circles grow with the screen (48, 56, 64px) and the
  active person carries a blue ring, a soft blue background and a small dot. Nothing about selection changed: a photo picked under Person 1 is picked
  under Person 2 and under All, the Selected count stays global, photos with no face detected stay in
  All Photos, and folders, retouch, submit, download, the client link and deletion are exactly as
  before. Faces are found in the background after a photo is already READY - never while anything
  uploads or loads - in the photo's existing preview, by the same open-source face engine the AI
  Feature runs (`AI_FACE_ENGINE_ENABLED`), and grouped into **Person 1, Person 2, …** for the project;
  a photo added later is scanned alone and joined to the people already found. What is stored is only
  which existing photos each person is in (three new tables, migration `20260922130000`, all
  cascading from the photo so the existing delete paths need no knowledge of them; a purged project
  takes its people too); no photo is copied and no crop is written - the circle is cut from the cover
  photo's thumbnail in the browser. **On whenever the face engine is** (`AI_FACE_ENGINE_ENABLED=true`
  in `backend/.env`; set `IMAGE_SELECTION_FACE_RECOGNITION_ENABLED=false` to keep the engine for the
  AI Feature alone), and while the engine is off or down, or a scan fails, the gallery shows its
  ordinary self with no People row. A gallery from before the filter existed is queued in the
  background the first time a client opens one of its folders, so its people appear a little after
  that first look; `POST /api/image-selection/projects/:projectId/faces/queue` does the same by hand
  and `GET …/faces/status` reports progress.
  Both answer to the existing `IMAGE_SELECTION / Project Management` permission - no new permission,
  no role to re-save - and the client routes are token-authenticated like the rest of the gallery.
  Phase 1 guesses no names and has no rename, merge, hide, cover change or selfie search.
  ([faces/](../../backend/src/modules/image-selection/faces/),
  [PeopleFilterRow.js](../../frontend/src/components/image-selection/client-gallery/PeopleFilterRow.js))
- **Visual Curation has a third screen, AI Feature: event galleries that find a guest's own photos
  by selfie.** A studio creates an event through a six-step wizard (details, sub-events, settings,
  guest configuration, media and AI settings, review), uploads its photos and videos, and shares
  one link; a guest joins with their name and phone, browses the gallery by sub-event, and **Find My
  Photos** matches a selfie against that event's faces only. Every uploaded photo is queued to a
  worker that detects faces, describes each with a vector, groups them into **People** and matches
  them against registered guests - so a guest who joined early is matched again, automatically, when
  the photographer uploads more. Staff rename, merge, split, hide and link people to guests, confirm
  or reject matches, retry failed photos and read a real timeline. **More → Event Default Settings**
  holds the studio-wide template every new event is copied from; a later change never reaches an
  existing event. Face recognition runs in a Python sidecar on open-source models (InsightFace's
  SCRFD and ArcFace from the Hugging Face Hub, `backend/requirements-face.txt`, switched on with
  `AI_FACE_ENGINE_ENABLED=true`); until it is on, photos still upload and their face jobs wait.
  The module is entirely its own - `backend/src/modules/ai-feature/`,
  `frontend/src/components/ai-feature/`, fifteen `ai_event_*` tables (migration 20260922110000),
  the `ai-feature/event-<id>/` storage namespace and the `ai-feature-media` queue - and touches
  nothing in Image Selection or Album Proofing. Three permissions, all under Visual Curation and
  written by migration 20260922100000: `IMAGE_SELECTION / AI Feature` opens the screen and runs
  events; `AI Face Management` decides whose face is whose; `AI Event Default Settings` edits the
  template. Only SUPER_ADMIN holds them until the roles that should are ticked in Role Management
  and re-saved. Guest pages live at `/e/<code>` and answer to the link's code, a PIN if the studio
  set one, and a guest session scoped to that one event.
- **Scheduling an interview now books the Google Meet and moves the candidate itself.** Schedule
  Interview used to write fields: HR typed a date, pasted a Meet link created somewhere else, and
  then remembered to move the stage to **Interview Scheduled**. The one most often missed is the one
  the register is read by, so a candidate with a conversation booked for Thursday sat in the Form
  Submitted tab until somebody noticed. Saving now does all of it in one act — the round is written,
  the candidate **moves to Interview Scheduled with the reason recorded**, and a round whose Mode is
  **Google Meet** gets a real Google Calendar event on your own calendar with its link saved onto the
  round. The dialog has **no Status picker any more**: every stage has the act that sets it, and the
  three that are decisions rather than consequences — Selected, On Hold, Rejected — are still taken
  in their own dialog, which asks *why*.
  **There is no Meet Link box at any mode.** For a Google Meet round it cannot be one — the link does
  not exist until Google issues it as part of the event the save is about to create — and for **In
  Person** and **Phone** there is nothing to ask for: somebody walking into the studio has no link,
  and a phone call is a number the record already holds, so the box stood empty on every round of
  both modes. A link a round already carries, written by a linked sheet or an earlier booking, is
  **kept** rather than blanked by an edit that was only moving a date, and the register still shows
  it as the Meet Link column and as "Join the meeting" on the record.
  The dialog gained an **Interviewer** picker, a **Duration**, and a **Guests** field: the candidate
  is seeded as a guest from the email on their record, the interviewer's work address joins the list
  the moment they are chosen, and anybody else is typed straight in. **Editing a round patches the
  event rather than replacing it**, so moving an interview by an hour moves the entry everybody
  already has, keeps the link the candidate was sent, and lets Google send its own "this has moved";
  changing a round from a Meet to a phone call cancels the event. Editing deliberately does **not**
  move the stage — moving a date is not a decision about a person. It uses the **same Google account**
  the Interview sheets already connect under (Settings → Integrations → Interview), whose consent
  already carries Calendar; a Meet is refused with "connect" or "reconnect" **before** anything is
  written, so a refused calendar records no interview at all
  ([InterviewScheduleDialog.js](../../frontend/src/components/hrm/InterviewScheduleDialog.js),
  [meetings.js](../../backend/src/modules/hrm/interview/meetings.js)).
- **The Interview register has tick boxes now, and two things to do with a selection.** A column of
  boxes in front of the table, header and rows alike, exactly as the Leads & Followups board has:
  ticking the header takes in every candidate the current tab and filters are showing. While anything
  is ticked the action bar becomes **Delete (n)** and **Change Stage (n)**, and it goes back to Add
  Application and its ⋮ the moment the selection is cleared — which changing tab also does, so a
  selection can never act on rows nobody can see. **Change Stage** offers the three *decisions* —
  Selected, On Hold, Rejected — and asks for one reason, which is written against each candidate's
  own record rather than once against the batch. It deliberately does not offer Form Submission
  Pending, Interview Scheduled or Onboarded: those are consequences of an act — a form sent, a
  conversation booked, an invite raised — and writing one onto a row in bulk puts a candidate in a
  tab whose whole meaning is that something exists which does not.
  **The boxes are their own permission: `HRM / Interview Select All`**, shown in Role Management as
  **Select Candidates in Bulk**, `READ_WRITE` or nothing. **Nobody holds it until you tick it** — no
  grants are carried across from `HRM / Interview`, deliberately, since inheriting them would leave
  nothing restricted. Until the roles that should have it are ticked in Role Management and re-saved,
  **only a Super Admin sees the tick boxes**; everybody else works the register a candidate at a time
  exactly as before, through the row ⋮. The two bulk routes
  (`POST /api/hrm/interview/candidates/bulk-status`, `DELETE /api/hrm/interview/candidates/bulk`)
  mount the same guard, so hiding the column is never the only protection
  ([migration](../../backend/prisma/migrations/20260921100000_interview_select_all_permission/migration.sql),
  [InterviewPage.js](../../frontend/src/components/hrm/InterviewPage.js),
  [routes.js](../../backend/src/modules/hrm/interview/routes.js)).
- **Every table in the app can carry a selection now, not just the CRM board.** The shared
  `DataTable` grew a `selection` feature — the tick-box column, the header box with its mixed state,
  and the three rules behind them — so a module that wants one passes the ticked ids and what to do
  when they change rather than hand-rolling a table. Select-all means *the rows the table is
  showing*, since the table is the only thing that knows which rows survived its own filter row, and
  rows filtered out of view keep their ticks. Freeze Columns still counts the first column anybody
  can read: the tick box is pinned with it rather than as it. No existing table changes — a table
  that passes no `selection` is built exactly as before
  ([DataTable.js](../../frontend/src/components/data-table/DataTable.js),
  [tableSelection.js](../../frontend/src/components/data-table/tableSelection.js)).
- **Adding equipment to the register is its own permission now: `ASSET / Asset Administration`.**
  It shows in Role Management as **Administer Assets**, and it is what the **Add Asset** button and
  `POST /api/assets` (single and bulk) answer to. `ASSET / Asset Management` — **Manage Assets** —
  keeps everything else and is still meant to be held widely: it is how somebody sees the camera in
  their hands, reports it faulty and raises a request. Until now `READ_WRITE` on that also carried
  Add Asset, so anybody who could ask for a lens could register one and name its manager. Deciding
  what the studio owns is not the same job as looking after a piece of it.
  **Nobody holds the new permission until you tick it**: no grants are carried across from Manage
  Assets, deliberately — copying them would hand Add Asset to every role that has the widely-granted
  one, which is the thing being undone. Until the roles that should administer assets are ticked in
  Role Management and re-saved, **only a Super Admin can add an asset**. Approving an asset request
  still creates the asset it asked for on `Asset Request Approval` alone, which is a second way onto
  the register and a deliberate one
  ([migration](../../backend/prisma/migrations/20260921120000_asset_administration_permission/migration.sql),
  [assets.routes.js](../../backend/src/modules/assets/assets.routes.js)).

### Fixed
- **The Payment Requests title bar no longer stands taller than every other screen's, or shrink as
  rows are ticked.** Its two buttons — **Extra Quotation** and **Add Request** — were handed to the
  module header inside a `<div>` of their own, and the rule that sizes a header's buttons
  (`.module-page-header-actions > button`) is a *child* combinator: wrapped, they were grandchildren,
  missed it, and kept Tailwind's taller default. So the bar sat a step too tall, and then visibly
  shrank the moment a selection replaced those buttons with the ⨉/Export/Delete set, which are the
  header's own children. Passed as a fragment now, like every other module page, so the bar is one
  height on every screen and does not move when rows are ticked. A test walks `src/components` and
  fails on any page that wraps its header actions again.
  [PaymentRequestsPage.js](../../frontend/src/components/accounts/PaymentRequestsPage.js),
  [table-select-all.test.mjs](../../frontend/test/table-select-all.test.mjs).

### Removed

- **The AI Suggestions panel on a lead's Transcripts tab is gone, and so is the route behind it.**
  The model proposed fields for the lead read out of a recorded call — a status, a follow-up date,
  an event type, a budget, a location — each with a tick box and an **Apply to Lead** button that
  wrote them onto the lead as an ordinary edit. The studio does not want a machine transcript of a
  noisy two-language phone call deciding what a lead says, even with a press in between, so the
  panel, its state and `POST /api/sales/crm-leads/transcripts/:transcriptId/apply` have all been
  removed rather than left to be pressed by accident. The tab still shows everything it showed
  about the **call**: when it happened, which way it went, who took it, how long it ran, the
  player, and the model's summary, sentiment, outcome and next action — plus the spoken text behind
  **View Full Transcript**. It now reads and writes nothing.
  [CRMPage.js](../../frontend/src/components/sales/CRMPage.js),
  [crm-leads/routes.js](../../backend/src/modules/sales/crm-leads/routes.js).

### Changed
- **The Projects register reads Dum Dum's vertical as `DD`, not `WED`.** A project stores Dum Dum as
  the code `WED`, and that spelling belongs to exactly one place — the **WhatsApp group name**,
  which somebody types into WhatsApp and has to go on reading "WED". The register printed the same
  code straight into its **Vertical** column and onto the info panel and record beside it, so a
  column describing what kind of project this is answered with an internal spelling of it. Anywhere
  the vertical is a fact *about* the project it now reads `DD`; the group name generator is
  untouched and still builds `WED …`, and nothing about what is **stored** changes. Done in
  `displayValue`, which feeds the cell, the column's accessor — so the filter and the sort — and the
  CSV alike, so all four say the same word. The mapping now lives in one place as `VERTICAL_CODES`
  beside `VERTICAL_LABELS`, and the CRD document tab title, which kept its own copy of the same
  three pairs, re-exports it rather than repeating it.
  [whatsappGroupNameUtils.js](../../frontend/src/components/sales/whatsappGroupNameUtils.js),
  [ProjectDataTable.js](../../frontend/src/components/data-table/ProjectDataTable.js),
  [project-vertical-code.test.mjs](../../frontend/test/project-vertical-code.test.mjs).

- **A candidate can be marked Selected from Yet to Connect.** The row menu offered **Mark Selected**
  only from Interview Scheduled onward, on the reasoning that nobody had spoken to the candidate
  before then — so taking somebody on off a referral, or off a conversation that never became a
  booked round, meant walking them through stages nobody had used. It is now offered at every stage
  a row can act from, including Yet to Connect and Form Submitted, and still moves through the same
  Why dialog as Reject and On Hold, so a selection made early records the reason it was made. Two
  exceptions stay: a row already Selected, which is the rule Reject and On Hold each keep, and
  **Form Submission Pending**, whose menu is about the form that has not come back. **Edit is now
  offered at every stage too**, with no exceptions: it was withheld at Yet to Connect, on the
  reasoning that the record "held nothing anybody had learned yet", and on a row waiting for its
  screening form. Both readings were wrong about what Edit is for — a number arrives mistyped from a
  sheet, a name comes through in capitals, a position is filed against the wrong department, and
  those are corrections to what *arrived*, most needed exactly where nobody has touched the record
  yet. Withholding it made the one stage carrying the most unchecked data the one stage that could
  not be put right. It still reads **Review** on a submitted form, which is the same entry saying
  what that row is for. [InterviewPage.js](../../frontend/src/components/hrm/InterviewPage.js).

- **The Interview register's date columns filter by typing, and Remarks opens with the table.**
  A date column carried no type at all, so wherever the rows in hand held fewer than forty distinct
  days the filter became a **menu of dates to tick** — where what anybody wants is to type one.
  `day` is the type that says so, on Date, When Can You Join, Interview Scheduled On, Date of Birth
  and both follow-up dates. It is deliberately not `date`: that reformats through `new Date()`,
  which is how a day west of Greenwich becomes the day before, so a day is shown exactly as the
  register wrote it and the type only decides that it is never a set to pick from and that it sorts
  as a date. **Remarks** now opens with the table instead of hiding behind Manage Columns — it is
  HR's running note on the person, which is the one thing somebody scans this register for; it was
  already on the record a double-click opens, and still is. **WhatsApp Number** and **Address** were
  on the record and the info panel but had never been offered as columns at all; both are there now,
  off by default like the other fields the hiring sheet does not carry. **Résumé** reads as
  **Resume** everywhere — the column, the record, the candidate's own form and the file field — the
  accented spelling being the one word on the screen nobody could type into a filter. A test now
  pins all 44 fields the studio asks a candidate for against the columns, by the studio's own names,
  which is how the two missing ones were found. On the record, **Remarks** now reads **above
  Application**: what follows it is provenance — which ad brought the candidate in, when they
  applied, when the row was created — read when somebody is checking where a candidate came from,
  while the remarks are read every time the record is opened.
  [interviewConfig.js](../../frontend/src/constants/interviewConfig.js),
  [interview-table-fields.test.mjs](../../frontend/test/interview-table-fields.test.mjs).

- **A long table now draws only the rows on screen.** Memoising the rows stopped a table
  re-rendering itself on every append, but it could not make the rows cost nothing: a thousand of
  them at thirty columns is thirty thousand cells in the document, most carrying a sticky position,
  and a browser asked to paint that while somebody flicks the scrollbar gives up and shows a blank
  band until it catches up — which is exactly what a reader saw. Rows outside the viewport are no
  longer drawn; two spacers stand in for them, sized to the height those rows would have taken, so
  the scrollbar stays the length it would be if every row were there and a position in it means what
  it says. Used only where its assumptions hold: a server-paged table, not an expandable one (whose
  rows are not a uniform height), and only past 120 rows, below which the spacers cost more than the
  rows they save. The row height and the header's offset are **measured** rather than assumed, since
  density, a wrapped cell or a theme's line height all move them. The footer still counts every row
  held, not the handful being drawn.
  [DataTable.js](../../frontend/src/components/data-table/DataTable.js).

- **The Interview register reads the screening forms for the page it is showing, not for all of
  them.** `GET /api/hrm/interview/invites` returned every form ever raised, unbounded, and then
  every candidate those forms named — a second pass over the whole register, paid on opening the
  screen and again after every write, while the screen only needed the hundred candidates in front
  of it. It now takes `?candidateIds=` and the register asks for its own page, merging the answer
  over what it holds so scrolling never drops the menus of the rows above. Both forms of the read
  are capped. View All Invites still asks for the lot, because that is what it is.
  [invites.js](../../backend/src/modules/hrm/interview/invites.js).

- **Every table in the app memoises its rows, so a long one stays usable while it is scrolled.**
  The shared `DataTable` drew its rows inline in the table body's `map` with no `memo` anywhere, so
  appending a page of 100 rows to 1,100 re-rendered all 1,200 — and every cell in them, tens of
  thousands of nodes. That is the jolt a reader feels each time the next page lands, and it gets
  worse with every page because the work grows with the table. The CRM lead board has never had the
  problem for exactly one reason: its `LeadTableRow` is `memo()`d and its handlers are held at a
  stable identity so the memo actually holds. The shared table now does the same. The row is
  compared on the **record** rather than on TanStack's row object — TanStack rebuilds its rows and
  cells whenever the data array changes identity, which is precisely what an append does, so
  comparing those would defeat the memo on the one render that matters. Everything else a row draws
  from is folded into one `rowRenderKey`, and `sizedStyle`, `stickyStyle` and `frozenOffsets` are
  held at stable identities, because a memo handed a fresh function each render never holds.
  Benefits every table that uses the shared component, not just Interview.
  [DataTable.js](../../frontend/src/components/data-table/DataTable.js).

- **Deleting an Interview candidate removes them, the way deleting a CRM lead does.** It was a
  *soft* delete, and the register paid for it twice. `hrm_interview_candidate` grew a permanent
  shadow of every deletion the studio had ever made — a register cleared of 5,880 candidates still
  held 5,880 rows — and a linked sheet went on recognising those hidden rows by phone and email,
  refusing to re-import people nobody could see. The row goes now, and the candidate's screening-form
  invites go with it in the same transaction, so View All Invites can no longer list forms against
