  candidates that do not exist. Deleting a linked sheet removes the candidates it imported the same
  way. What keeps a deleted candidate from coming back is the sheet ledger alone
  (`hrm_interview_google_sheet_row`), exactly as it is for a lead: the row is remembered, the person
  is not — so a sheet re-sorted after a deletion can bring somebody back, which is the trade the CRM
  has always made. [service.js](../../backend/src/modules/hrm/interview/service.js),
  [hrm-interview-register.test.js](../../backend/test/hrm-interview-register.test.js).

- **The Interview register loads a page at a time instead of all of it.** The screen asked for every
  candidate in one response and filtered it in the browser, so it sat on "Loading candidates…" while
  several thousand rows arrived before one could be drawn — and then held all of them. It now reads
  **100 at a time**, the CRM lead board's own size, appending as you scroll; the stage is applied by
  the server, so a tab is a query rather than a filter over everything. The footer reads
  **"Showing 100 of 5,880"** and says "Loading more…" while a page is on its way, in place of a
  "Total rows" that only ever counted what had been scrolled in. Tab counts come from the register
  as one grouped query, so they describe the whole stage rather than the page, and are skipped
  entirely on a scroll append. Paging orders on `created_at` **and** `id`: several thousand rows
  written by one sync share a timestamp to the millisecond, and without a tiebreak a row can appear
  twice while another never arrives. `GET /api/hrm/interview/candidates` takes `offset`, `limit`
  (capped at 500) and `appending`, and answers `{ data, total, counts }`. The shared `DataTable` gained
  `features.infiniteScroll.onEndReached`, and giving it hands the paging over entirely: the table
  draws **every** row it was passed and reveals none of its own, then asks for the next page 400px
  from the bottom — the CRM lead board's own trigger, so the page has usually arrived before the
  reader reaches it. A table either pages through rows it holds or is fed pages by its page, never
  both: two counters over the same rows is what made the footer read "Showing 500" over a table
  showing three hundred, and put a stall on every page. The in-flight guard is a ref rather than
  state, because a scroll fires many times a frame and state lags by one; appends are deduplicated
  by id, so a duplicated request costs a round trip rather than a duplicated row.

- **A table's columns no longer depend on its rows, which is what made a long scroll give out.**
  `buildTableColumns` took the rows for one thing: the serial column's export value was
  `rows.indexOf(row)` — searching the list for the row it had just been handed. Every caller's
  `columns` memo therefore had to list its data as a dependency, so a screen that appends rows as it
  scrolls rebuilt **all** its column definitions on every page and the table rebuilt itself around
  them. At six or seven hundred rows that is a visible stall on each append. The exporter passes the
  row's index instead, which also takes a CSV export from quadratic to linear — 5,880 rows was 34
  million comparisons. Every table in the app gets this: Employee Repository, Tasks, Accounts,
  Payment Requests, Employee Reviews, Employee Attendance and Interview.
  [tableCells.js](../../frontend/src/components/data-table/tableCells.js),
  [tableExport.js](../../frontend/src/lib/tableExport.js). A server-paged table also lays itself out
  **fixed** from the start rather than only once a column has been pulled by hand: `table-auto` asks
  the browser to size columns from their contents, so appending a page re-measured every cell in
  every row — free at a hundred rows, and at eleven hundred the jolt a reader feels each time the
  next page lands, worsening with every page because the work grows with the table. The widths are
  the ones each field already declares, and pulling a column still overrides them.
  [InterviewPage.js](../../frontend/src/components/hrm/InterviewPage.js),
  [DataTable.js](../../frontend/src/components/data-table/DataTable.js).

- **Quotation Settings → brand now has three tabs - Terms, PDF Content, PDF Image - and the
  pictures' category is a row of tabs.** The Pictures section moved out of PDF Content into a tab
  of its own, unchanged: the same cover and Thank You sets, shoots, uploads, Replace and Remove,
  Add set, first-set fallback and stored data, so everything already saved appears under PDF Image
  without being set up again. The Category dropdown over the sets became tabs - one per category
  the brand's Field Settings → Categories list holds, so a category added there appears on its own
  - and each tab shows that category's sets alone. PDF Content keeps Allow Client Download and the
  wording. Each tab saves only its own part of the brand, so saving pictures never rewrites the
  wording or the other way round. Dum Dum, Qua Qua and Corp stay independent; the PDF, old
  quotations, the generator and General are untouched
  ([QuotationSettingsPanel.js](../../frontend/src/components/settings/QuotationSettingsPanel.js)).
- **The CRM Quotation Calculator's Apply to Quotation opens the Quotation Generator on its choices,
  in place of Apply to Lead.** Apply to Lead wrote the calculator's total onto the lead as the
  amount conversion read into Signed Quotation; Apply to Quotation writes nothing. It hands the
  choices to the generator as the generator's own rows - one shoot dated from the calculator, every
  service and deliverable with the same ids, definitions, Settings prices, No. of Count and
  auto-added records the generator would have written itself, the discount, and the lead's enquiry
  id - and opens Sales → Quotations with the generator already filled in as a new quotation, where
  everything can still be edited and only Generate Quotation saves. A base package or extras are
  carried into Additional Notes after a confirmation, since the generator has no line for them; a
  service whose option is not yet chosen, or a variant Settings has removed, stops the handoff and
  is named. In the calculator a chosen service now reads as Service Name - Shoot Type - Session
  Timing - Duration. Nothing changes for the generator opened from Quotations, saved quotations,
  conversion or the lead-quote endpoints; the calculator still reopens on a draft saved earlier.
  Reaches the same roles as before: `SALES / Quotation Management` (Read / Write to apply)
  ([quotationCalculatorHandoff.mjs](../../frontend/src/lib/quotationCalculatorHandoff.mjs),
  [quotationGeneratorDraft.js](../../frontend/src/lib/quotationGeneratorDraft.js),
  [QuotationPage.js](../../frontend/src/components/sales/QuotationPage.js)).
- **The Quotation Generator's cost summary sits beside the shoots and stays in view while they are
  scrolled.** Service Cost, Discount in % and Total Cost used to sit under the last shoot, a long
  scroll away on a quotation with several. On a wide screen the Service Creation section is now two
  columns - the shoots and Additional Notes on the left, at the width they had, and the summary in
  the strip on the right that was empty - and the summary is sticky within the form's own scroll, so
  it never covers the header, the rail or the Generate Quotation footer and stops where the section
  ends. Below the `lg` breakpoint it is ordinary content under Additional Notes. It is the same block
  rendered once, reading the same figures and the same discount box; nothing about the quotation,
  its pricing or its payload changed
  ([QuotationGeneratorPanel.js](../../frontend/src/components/sales/QuotationGeneratorPanel.js)).
- **The CRM Quotation Calculator now picks services and deliverables the way the Quotation
  Generator does, and shows no unit price beside them.** Instead of listing the whole catalogue with
  a price and a stepper against every row, the calculator opens empty with the generator's own
  **Choose Services** and **Choose Deliverables** pickers: the service list has one entry per
  Settings → Project pricing line, named Service Name - Shoot Type - Session Timing - Duration, and a
  pick adds the row with that booking chosen (an Option dropdown on the row changes it where the
  service has more than one); a deliverable is picked from the generator's brand-and-variant list worded the same way.
  Only the chosen rows are listed, each with a quantity stepper, No. of Count where the deliverable
  is counted, and Remove - which also takes off a deliverable a service brought in when it is not
  wanted. A service answered Deliverable Added brings the variants Settings mapped
  onto that pricing line with it and takes them away when it is removed, and the names the generator
  pairs with a service follow it in, through the same code. Every row is still priced at its Settings
  figure and the totals, the discount and Apply to Lead work as before; Apply to Lead also writes the
  priced lines behind the total onto the lead's draft, and drafts saved earlier reopen unchanged.
  Settings prices, the Quotation Generator, saved quotations and the PDF are untouched. Reaches the
  same roles as before: `SALES / Quotation Management` (Read to quote, Read / Write to apply)
  ([QuotationCalculatorLines.js](../../frontend/src/components/sales/QuotationCalculatorLines.js),
  [quotationCalculator.mjs](../../frontend/src/lib/quotationCalculator.mjs),
  [quotation-calculator/controller.js](../../backend/src/modules/sales/quotation-calculator/controller.js)).
- **`npm run dev` restarts the API only for its own source now.** nodemon watched every file under
  `backend`, so installing the AI Feature's Python packages into `backend/.venv` restarted the server
  once per file written. [nodemon.json](../../backend/nodemon.json) narrows the watch to `src`, the
  Prisma schema and `.env`, and ignores the Python environment and the uploads folder.
- **Enter adds a service or a deliverable in Project Settings.** The Service Name and Deliverable
  Name boxes took a name and then waited for the Add button beside them; typing a name and pressing
  Enter did nothing. Enter now adds, the way it already does on every other name-and-Add row in
  Settings, and only when Add itself would - a name typed, nothing already being added
  ([ProjectSettingsPanel.js](../../frontend/src/components/project-management/ProjectSettingsPanel.js)).
- **A candidate whose form has come back is now Reviewed, not Edited — and the review ends in an
  answer.** The **Form Submitted** tab exists because somebody has to read what a candidate sent
  back and decide what happens next, and the row offered **Edit**, which asks a different question:
  "something on this record is wrong" rather than "what do we do about this person". The panel it
  opened ended in **Save Changes**, which is not an answer to anything — so a tab whose whole reason
  is a decision was worked as a tidying job. The row now says **Review** at that one stage (every
  other stage still says Edit, because every other stage really is a correction), and it opens the
  same panel with four answers in the footer instead of three: **Discard** (close and keep none of
  the corrections — named for what it does to the edits, since "Cancel" in front of an application
  reads as cancelling the application), **Resend** (hand the form back on the candidate's own link
  with every answer still in it), **Save** (keep the corrections, decide later) and **Schedule
  Meeting**, which is the primary. Schedule Meeting saves the record first and opens the schedule
  dialog on what was just saved — so the round is booked against the corrections rather than the
  details last written, and it opens as a next round rather than a first if somebody else has
  scheduled one meanwhile
  ([InterviewCandidateFormPanel.js](../../frontend/src/components/hrm/InterviewCandidateFormPanel.js),
  [InterviewPage.js](../../frontend/src/components/hrm/InterviewPage.js)).
- **Add Application no longer asks what stage a new candidate is at, and is one panel instead of
  two.** An arriving application *is* at **Yet to Connect** — nobody has spoken to them, which is
  the whole definition of that stage — so the dialog files every one there and the pills are gone.
  They offered five, and four of them cannot be true of an application being written down as it
  arrives: **Form Submission Pending** means a form has gone out, **Form Submitted** means it has
  come back, and **Selected** and **On Hold** are decisions about somebody nobody has met. A row
  filed under one by a mis-click read afterwards as a step somebody took, and sat in a tab being
  worked for something that never happened. Each of those stages is reached by the act that makes it
  true — the screening form sent and returned, a round scheduled, the decision dialog, the onboarding
  invite. With the pills gone, **Follow-up Details** was a heading over a date and a note, so it is
  folded into the panel above it: one card, eight controls, the same width as before
  ([InterviewAddApplicationDialog.js](../../frontend/src/components/hrm/InterviewAddApplicationDialog.js)).
- **What you can do to an asset now depends on whether it is yours.** The Assets screen used to ask
  one question for the whole page — do you hold *Asset Management* at `READ_WRITE`? — and if you
  did, every row's ⋮ offered you everything: edit it, inspect it, move it to any status, delete it.
  It now asks per row. An asset is **yours to manage** if you are the employee named as its
  **Manager**, or the login that **added** it; there you get Edit, Start Inspection, every legal
  status move and the handovers, but never Delete. Every other asset you can see — the camera in
  your hands, a colleague's kit in your branch — gives you **View and Under Repair**, because the
  person holding a broken lens is the person who knows it is broken, while deciding it is fixed
  again is the manager's call. Importing a spreadsheet and deleting an asset are a **Super
  Admin's**. The creator is in that rule deliberately: approving an asset request creates the asset
  and records the approver as its creator, so somebody who approved one and forgot to name a manager
  can open it later and set one instead of needing the studio owner. Manage Assets itself is granted
  exactly as widely as before; what changed is that holding it no longer means being allowed to
  change every asset you can see ([assets.read.service.js](../../backend/src/modules/assets/assets.read.service.js),
  [AssetManagementPage.js](../../frontend/src/components/assets/AssetManagementPage.js)).

### Changed
- **Employee Referral has left the sidebar and joined the Employee Repository's own switch.** The
  register was a second HRM menu entry for people the first one is already about — what a referral
  pays is set from the repository's Settings, and who is owed it is read about the same staff — so
  it now sits in the control beside the **Employee Repository** heading, with **Users** and
  **Roles**. Four screens, one heading, and the switch says which of them you are on; it carries a
  way back, so the register is no longer somewhere you can arrive and not leave.
  **The switch lists only what your roles open.** A destination you cannot read is absent rather
  than offered and then refused by the route guard — which is new for Users and Roles, shown to
  everybody until now — and where that leaves a single screen its name is simply printed beside the
  heading: a menu of one offers a choice it does not have, and the name still has to be there
  because Users and Roles both read *Employee Repository* above it. The sidebar keeps one
  row for the whole family: **Employee Repository** opens the first of the four you may read, so a
  role granted the referral register and not the repository has a way in from the menu instead of a
  screen reachable only by URL. `HRM / Employee Referral` still opens it and is still granted under
  that name in Role Management — hiding a menu entry is not taking a permission away
  ([AdministrationSwitch.js](../../frontend/src/components/shared/AdministrationSwitch.js),
  [rbacSections.js](../../frontend/src/constants/rbacSections.js),
  [navigation.mjs](../../frontend/src/constants/navigation.mjs),
  [useSidebarLayout.js](../../frontend/src/components/preferences/useSidebarLayout.js)).

### Fixed
- **"My Leads" means the CRM column, and nothing else.** It matched `owner_employee_id` **or**
  `created_by_user_id` — the login that typed the lead in — so somebody adding a lead on a
  colleague's behalf, which is most of how leads arrive here, saw it under My Leads while the person
  actually working it saw it too. A board meant to answer "what is on me today" was answering with
  other people's work. It now matches the CRM alone: whoever is named on the lead owns it, and
  nobody else's My Leads shows it — including a Super Admin who created a hundred leads and is named
  on none of them, who now correctly sees none of them there. A login with no employee record behind
  it can be named as nobody's CRM, so it matches nothing and gets an empty board rather than every
  lead it ever entered for somebody else. **A lead with no CRM shows on the My Leads of whoever sees
  the whole studio, and nobody else's** — it is nobody's work, and appearing on no My Leads at all
  would leave it findable only by turning the switch off and reading the entire board, so it
  surfaces for the people who hand leads out, as work waiting to be given. The moment it is
  assigned it leaves that board and appears on the CRM's, because it is no longer unassigned. A CRM
  reading their own leads is never shown other people's unassigned work. Unchanged: it is still ANDed with the data scope and can
  only ever take rows away, the branch still grants nothing, and the tab counts read the same rule
  so they cannot disagree with the rows.
  [leadQueryService.js](../../backend/src/modules/sales/crm-leads/leadQueryService.js),
  [crm-lead-mine-only.test.js](../../backend/test/crm-lead-mine-only.test.js).

- **Overall Pending Followups counts today's calls too, so it is the whole of what is owed.** It was
  bounded at yesterday, on the reading that a call booked for today is not yet late — so the one
  number meant to answer "how much is outstanding" was short by a whole day's work, every day, and
  only agreed with the board once the day had passed. It now holds everything owed **today or
  earlier**, still including the leads whose follow-up date was never filled in or cannot be read as
  a date. It overlaps both dated tabs by design — a call owed today is in Today Pending and in
  Overall — so the three still do not add up to the board
  ([leadQueryService.js](../../backend/src/modules/sales/crm-leads/leadQueryService.js)).
- **A lead marked Rejected or Invalid no longer keeps the call it was booked for.** Turning a lead
  down left its Next Followup Date standing — often today's, since that is what Add Lead fills in —
  and the Pending Followups tabs read that date, so the lead came back on somebody's worklist under a
  status saying the conversation was over. The date and time are now **cleared to NULL** whenever the
  status is one that ends the conversation, and the rule is enforced where the record becomes columns
  rather than on the form: the bulk Update Details dialog, the record panel, the sheet import and the
  CSV import all reach the same columns, and each of them had to remember separately. NULL rather
  than blank matters — `follow_up_day` is generated from the date column, and Overall Pending treats
  a NULL day as work owed on a day the board cannot name, which is right for a lead still being
  chased and wrong for one nobody is chasing
  ([crmLeadService.js](../../backend/src/modules/sales/crm-leads/crmLeadService.js)).
- **Reports name the studio's own people, and stop hiding the calls they cannot place.** A handset
  is matched to an employee **once, when the call is delivered** — Callyzer's **Employee Code**
  against the repository's own code (`DBS-10`) first, the handset's label against the employee's name
  second — and the id it resolves to is written onto the call. Reports read the name through that id,
  so somebody renamed in the Employee Repository is renamed on every report at once, including on the
  calls they made before, and a handset relabelled in the Callyzer app no longer moves its calls onto
  another person or splits one person into two rows (`Another Employee` and `Another Emplyoee` were
  two rows). The handset **number** is no part of the match: Callyzer reports the company SIM and the
  repository holds the employee's own number, so that half could never match and every handset was
  falling through to its label. A call matched to nobody now **keeps the name Callyzer gave it and is
  shown** — its calls used to be on no row at all, so the studio's totals were short by every call
  such a phone had made — to a reader narrowed by nothing; it stays off a scoped reader's report,
  since it is in no branch and on no team. A match is never re-made once written, so a company SIM
  passed to somebody else cannot re-file the calls its previous holder made, while a handset matched
  to nobody is tried again on every start and picks up an employee added after their first call.
  **Set each handset's Employee Code in the Callyzer dashboard to that person's code** — until then
  the label carries the match exactly as it does today
  ([callLogService.js](../../backend/src/modules/sales/crm-leads/callLogService.js),
  [crm-reports/service.js](../../backend/src/modules/sales/crm-reports/service.js)).
- **Call times read on the studio's clock, instead of five and a half hours into the future.** A call
  Callyzer reported at 12:00 showed on the Call Logs tab and the Calls board as 5:30 pm, disagreeing
  with the `call_time` printed in the row beside it, and a call taken this morning sorted as though
  it were still to come. Callyzer sends its wall clock with no offset on it — `call_date` and
  `call_time` are the handset's own, so Asia/Kolkata — and this read them as UTC, which does not
  mark the instant but moves it. Every call already in the log is **restamped onto the right clock
  on the next start**, recomputed from the date and time Callyzer sent rather than shifted blindly,
  so the tab does not end up with old calls on one clock and new ones on another
  ([callLogService.js](../../backend/src/modules/sales/crm-leads/callLogService.js)).
- **A second test that failed on what another test was doing beside it.** `data-scope-crm` counts the
  whole leads table twice — once as a Super Admin, once with no scope — and asserts the two agree.
  Asking for them together narrowed the window but did not close it: the pair can still straddle
  another suite's commit, and a run failed on a three-lead difference that said nothing about the
  rule it checks. It now reads both again when they disagree, the way `data-scope-backfill` judges a
  suspect row twice; a real disagreement survives the second reading
  ([data-scope-crm.test.js](../../backend/test/data-scope-crm.test.js)).
- **A role can no longer be saved without a branch, which the form has been asking for all along.**
  Branches on the Roles dialog has carried a required mark since it was written, and the mark was the
  only thing saying so: it is a custom field, which the schema form deliberately does not judge, and
  the route read an empty list as a role deliberately parked. So the form asked for a branch, refused
  nothing when none was given, and saved a role that reaches nowhere — and a role reaching nowhere
  grants nothing anywhere, which reads as the permissions being broken rather than as a role nobody
  finished. Both ends now hold the rule: the field reddens with **Select at least one branch** before
  anything is sent, and the server refuses the save whatever sends it. A role is taken out of use by
  deactivating it, which is a switch of its own
  ([routes.js](../../backend/src/modules/admin/routes.js),
  [RoleDialog.js](../../frontend/src/components/admin/RoleDialog.js)).
- **Adding a service or a deliverable under Settings → Project no longer loses or doubles what was
  typed.** Four faults, all met while adding rows: on a new deliverable, "+ Add Another Brand" made
  a second brand under the same internal id as the first, so a name typed into either landed in
  both and Delete Brand on one removed both — every row the form opens on and every row the dialog
  adds is now named apart ([deliverableBrands.mjs](../../frontend/src/lib/deliverableBrands.mjs),
  [DeliverableDefinitionDialog.js](../../frontend/src/components/project-management/DeliverableDefinitionDialog.js));
  a service's booking could be mapped to a deliverable that was only staged on the page, and the
  mapping was dropped without a word on the way to the server because a staged brand or variant has
  no id to name yet — the picker offers saved deliverables only, and says so when there are none
  ([ProjectSettingsPanel.js](../../frontend/src/components/project-management/ProjectSettingsPanel.js));
  a name another service or deliverable already had was refused only at Save, with the row already
  in the table — both dialogs now refuse it under the name box, by the server's own rule for when
  two names are one ([projectDefinitionNames.mjs](../../frontend/src/lib/projectDefinitionNames.mjs));
  and a row whose settings failed to write after its name had been created stayed staged as a
  row to add, so every later Save was refused as a duplicate — it becomes a staged edit to the row
  it made, and Save writes only what is still owed. A brand name longer than its column is also cut
  to fit before it is sent, and deliverables are written before the services that may name them.
  No permission changes: the panel stays on `PROJECT / Projects` and `SETTINGS / Project Settings`.
- **A role holding Role Management and not User Management can open the Roles screen at last.** Both
  admin tabs were guarded on `ADMIN / User Management`, so the route guard let such a role through —
  it reads `/admin/roles` from the route map, which names Role Management — and the page then met it
  with "you do not have access to the admin panel", naming a permission nobody had mentioned. Each
  tab is now guarded on the permission its own route answers to, read from that same map
  ([AdminPage.js](../../frontend/src/components/admin/AdminPage.js)).
- **`npm test` no longer fails on a clean checkout because two suites share one database.** The
  data-scope invariant suite replays every row of every converted table through the approved
  matchers, and it reads *whatever the database holds* — including, for the seconds they exist, the
  fixtures `data-scope-employees` writes on the core beside it. Three of those fixtures break the
  rule on purpose (`by-base` names its branch by Work Base Location alone, with `branchId` left
  NULL) because that is what proves the branch limb reads the text columns, so the invariant failed
  on three employees that were deleted again moments later and the run reported a defect that was
  not there. Rows carrying a reserved fixture prefix are now not that suite's business, judged
  against the same registry the pretest sweep deletes them by; a genuinely wrong row still fails it
  ([data-scope-backfill.test.js](../../backend/test/data-scope-backfill.test.js),
  [clear-test-fixtures.js](../../backend/scripts/clear-test-fixtures.js)).
- **Every employee picker on the asset forms can be typed into now.** Add/Edit Asset offered a
  search box on **Manager** and not on **Assigned Person** — the two fields side by side, drawing
  the same list — and none of the seven employee pickers on the status forms (Assignee 1 and 2,
  Reported By, Received and Reviewed By, Handover To, Assigned To, Manager) had one at all. These
  lists are the studio's whole payroll, so without the box the only way to a name is a scroll. All
  eight now carry `searchable`, and `test/asset-employee-pickers-searchable.test.mjs` fails if one
  is added without it ([AssetFormDialog.js](../../frontend/src/components/assets/AssetFormDialog.js),
  [formPanelConfig.js](../../frontend/src/constants/formPanelConfig.js)).
- **An asset could be moved to any status by anyone who could edit assets at all.** The table of
  which status may follow which — that a camera *in service* cannot go straight to *disposed*, that
  *sold* is the end of the line — lived only in the browser, so it decided what the ⋮ menu drew and
  nothing else. `PATCH /api/assets/:id/status` checked the incoming status against the list of
  statuses that exist and accepted anything on it, from anybody who could write. The table is the
  server's rule now, and the screen renders the copy the list hands it per row, so the menu can no
  longer offer a move the endpoint would refuse or hide one it would allow — there is one table and
  it cannot drift ([assets.controller.js](../../backend/src/modules/assets/assets.controller.js)).
- **A lead nobody got through to is no longer asked what the job is worth, or when the baby is
  due.** Setting a lead's status to **CNA** now excuses **Estimated Value** and, on Qua Qua, **Date
  of Birth (or) EDOD** — the asterisks go and the save stops refusing, in the lead popup and in Add
  Lead alike ([CRMPage.js](../../frontend/src/components/sales/CRMPage.js)). Only the CNA *name* used
  to do this: the status was read as a different case, on the reasoning that the client is known, so
  the form went on demanding both. That is right about the client and wrong about the questions —
  both are things somebody finds out by getting through, so insisting bought an invented number and,
  worse, an invented date that the Newborns and Birthdays tabs then schedule a shoot from. **Those
  two fields and nothing else**: a CNA lead still has a client, a contact and a branch, and is still
  asked for all three. `Invalid` and `Rejected` already excused both and are unchanged — they end
  the conversation, so they keep only who the person is. The status is also now read through
  `normalizeLeadStatus`, so a lead carrying an older spelling from a sheet — `in review`, lowercase
  `rejected` — is treated as the status its own panel shows it as, rather than being refused for a
  date nobody could have known.
- **A Super Admin is no longer asked who they report to.** Reports To is required on the Employee
  form, which left the one person at the top of the org chart having to name somebody who is not
  their manager — and the answer is not decorative: the data scope walks `reports_to_employee_id` to
  decide whose records a person gets, and the org chart drew the Super Admin *underneath* whoever was
  picked. It is now optional for them and demanded of everybody else, the star included, so the form
  and the refusal agree. Still **shown**, and still answerable, for a studio that wants a line drawn
  — it only stops being required. Somebody counts as a Super Admin by either spelling: the legacy
  `user.role` column, or the system role named `SUPER_ADMIN`, which is what an administrator added
  through Login Credentials actually carries (their `role` column still reads `EMPLOYEE`), and a
  deactivated role grants nothing. The employee row carries the answer from the server as
  `isSuperAdmin`, and the **Login Credentials page answers too** — ticking the role there drops the
  requirement immediately, so an administrator can be created in one pass rather than refused over a
  manager they do not have. Nothing loosened but that one field for that one person; every other
  required field is unchanged, and the endpoint already accepted a null `reportsToEmployeeId`.

- **The Employee Repository shows the people who are yours, not the whole studio.** It was the last
  unscoped screen of people: anybody holding `HRM / Employee Management` saw every employee in the
  company, which DATA-SCOPE.md recorded as a decision waiting to be taken rather than a leak. It now
  reads the same rule every other scoped screen does. **A branch administrator**
  (`ADMIN / Branch Administration`) gets the people in the branches their roles reach, plus
  themselves and everybody reporting to them at any depth; **everybody else** gets themselves and
  their own reports; a **Super Admin**, and anybody reaching every branch, is narrowed by nothing.
  Reaching a branch is still not a claim on the people in it — that is what the administration
  permission is for. The branch limb names **three** columns and any one of them is enough:
  `branch_id`, **Work Base Location** and **Current Work Location**. That is the point of it rather
  than belt and braces — `branchId` is derived on write as base-*or-else*-current, so somebody based
  in one branch and posted to another carries the base branch alone, and a filter reading the
  reference would hide them from the branch they actually work in. Editing, deleting and
  reactivating an employee are held to the same rows as the list and answer **404**: hiding somebody
  from the repository is not protection while their id can still be sent to the endpoint that edits
  them, and a person you may not read is not one you should learn the existence of by being refused
  them. The picker at `/employees/directory` is unchanged — name and id only, open to any session,
  as the asset and client-storage screens need. **Grant `ADMIN / Branch Administration` to the roles
  that run HR and re-save them**, or an HR user who manages nobody will open the repository onto
  their own row alone.
- **Nebula and the repository now name the same people.** `hrm/employees/read.service.js` read the
  two work-location texts only where `branch_id` was NULL, so the assistant hid anybody posted into a
  branch from that branch's administrator while the screen showed them. It reads all three columns
  beside each other, the way the screen does.
- **One number is one enquiry within a vertical — on every path now, not just the Add Lead form.**
  The rule was already right and already enforced, but only in two places: the single-lead form and
  the edit. **Bulk Entry**, the **CSV import** and the **Google Sheet sync** never asked the
  question, so a number the form refuses went in without a word through any of them — which is how
  a lead imported from a sheet and a lead typed in by hand ended up on one number, their calls,
  remarks and follow-ups split across two records that neither knew about. What each path does now:
  Bulk Entry refuses the batch and **names every offending row** (the grid is corrected in one
  pass, so reporting one row at a time would mean submitting the same batch five times to find five
  problems), and it checks the batch **against itself** as well — two rows carrying the same number
  are both new, so a per-row lookup finds neither and writes the duplicate it exists to prevent.
  The CSV import **skips** those rows and counts them under *Already on this number*, apart from
  the blank rows, rather than refusing a hundreds-row file over a handful that are already here.
  The sheet sync already held them back; it now says so, naming how many were recognised by their
  number or email rather than by which row they came from. **Editing stays possible**: the check
  now guards the *change*, so a lead keeps its own number without being refused — otherwise every
  pair of duplicates the unguarded paths already wrote would have been impossible to open and
  correct, which is the one action that resolves them. Cross-vertical is still allowed on purpose;
  a wedding client who later books a corporate shoot is one number and two enquiries.
  [controller.js](../../backend/src/modules/sales/crm-leads/controller.js),
  [csvImportService.js](../../backend/src/modules/sales/crm-leads/csvImportService.js),
  A refused batch **names the numbers**, in the message and on the rows themselves: the dialog stays
  open so the batch can be corrected, and the offending row's phone box is marked with the number
  and who already holds it ("9876543210 is already held by DBS-EID-QQ-ERD-000012 in Qua Qua"), which
  clears the moment the row is edited. The sheet sync also recognises a hand-typed lead **whichever
  of the two number columns it went into** — its index read only `phone` while the Add Lead rule has
  always read `phone` and `contact_number` together, so a lead whose number lived in Contact Number
  was invisible to the sheet and a second copy of that person arrived on the next pass.
  [controller.js](../../backend/src/modules/sales/crm-leads/controller.js),
  [BulkImportLeadsDialog.js](../../frontend/src/components/sales/BulkImportLeadsDialog.js),
  [crm-duplicate-phone-guard.test.js](../../backend/test/crm-duplicate-phone-guard.test.js).

- **The referral register shows your referrals, not the studio's.** Everybody holding
  `HRM / Employee Referral` saw every referral in the studio, which is the wrong answer for a record
  about money owed to a named person — what somebody introduced a hire for is their business and
  their manager's. A referral is now yours if **you made it**, if somebody reporting to you made it,
  or if you entered it on their behalf; `ADMIN / Branch Administration` adds every referral whose
  **referrer** works in a branch the role reaches. The branch is read live through the referrer
  rather than copied onto the referral — `EmployeeReferral` has no `branch_id` and deliberately did
  not get one, because a referral's branch is the referrer's and a copy would go stale the day
  somebody transfers. The instalment write is held to the same rows as the list and answers 404, not
  403: hiding a row from the register is not protection while its id can still be sent to the
  endpoint that marks money paid, and a referral somebody may not read is not one they should learn
  the existence of by being refused it. Studio-wide referral *settings* are unchanged and still
  answer to the permission alone.

### Changed
- **The three message templates sit under one Settings entry, Templates.** They were three rail
  entries whose titles all ended in "Template", which reads as three unrelated screens when what
  somebody looks for is "the templates" before they look for which one. Now one parent holding
  **Cash Received**, **Employee Invite** and **Login Credentials** — the children keep their own
  ids, so a link into one of them still opens the same screen and the unsaved-changes guard is
  unchanged. It reverses an earlier split, which had its own reason: the group costs a click to
  reach the one everybody edits. That click is the trade, and a fourth message the CRM learns to
  send now joins the group rather than lengthening the rail.

### Fixed
- **Bulk Entry now marks the client whose number was refused, not whichever row is standing in that
  place.** A batch holding a number its vertical already has comes back refused with a row number
  per clash, and
  [BulkImportLeadsDialog](../../frontend/src/components/sales/BulkImportLeadsDialog.js) put a red mark
  and a sentence on that row. It was reading the number as a position in the grid, and the grid is
  not what the server counted: blank rows are dropped on the way out, so **a row somebody cleared
  and left in the middle pushed every later refusal one client off**, from the very first submit.
  The marks were held by position too, and nothing re-keyed them when a row went — so **deleting the
  offending client moved their error down onto the innocent one below**, or onto the empty row at
  the end, which read as a duplicate number reported on a row nobody had filled in. Both are gone:
  the verdict is resolved against the rows actually sent and held against each row's own key, so a
  mark belongs to a client and leaves with them. Separately, the red Contact box said **"Contact is
  required."** on a field with a number plainly in it — one flag was covering both reasons a box can
  be red — and now says *Already in this vertical*, with the sentence naming the enquiry still under
  the row. Nothing about the duplicate rule itself changed, and the batch is still all-or-nothing;
  only CRM Bulk Entry is affected — the CSV import and the Google Sheets sync do not use this
  dialog.
- **A date field opened on an unreadable value no longer shows a calendar reading "NaN, NaN".** The
  studio's date columns are free text, filled for years from spreadsheets, so a lead's Next Follow-up
  Date can hold `#N/A` or `20sept2026`. [DateField](../../frontend/src/components/shared/DateField.js)
  handed those straight to `new Date()`, which answers an **Invalid Date** — an object, so truthy,
  so it became the calendar's opening month and the header read `NaN, NaN` over an empty grid. There
  was no day to pick, which left the field dead for exactly the lead somebody had opened it to fix.
  It now reads a value before opening on it and falls back to today, so the day can always be
  picked. **`DD/MM/YYYY` was hitting the same wall and is now read properly**, day-first: that shape
  is not junk — `sales_crm_lead.follow_up_day` parses it, so the board and the follow-up tabs were
  already counting those leads as scheduled while the calendar refused to open on them. Choosing a
  day rewrites the value as a `YYYY-MM-DD` key, so a record edited once stops being the odd one out.
  A value that is genuinely unreadable is **left in the field and marked** *Unrecognised date format*
  in amber rather than blanked — it is the record's own data, and it is the thing somebody needs to
  see to know what they are replacing. **No data was changed to fix this**: a migration would have
  been undone by the next spreadsheet import, and nulling the `DD/MM/YYYY` rows to stop their NaN
  would have deleted live follow-ups. Every `DateField` in the app benefits, not only CRM.
- **A Read grant on Message Template now shows the templates, instead of nothing.** Settings hid the
  three wording panels unless the viewer held `SETTINGS / Message Template` at **Read / Write**, so
  the Read level could be granted and did nothing at all — the person held a permission that opened
  no screen. The endpoint behind those panels was never the problem: its GET is deliberately wide
  (`checkAnyPermission` at Read, so the accounts clerk can see the receipt wording they did not
  write), and the rail was refusing what the server would have served. `MessageTemplatePanel` already
  had the read-only rendering it needed — it greys the field and drops the Save on `canWrite={false}`
  — so visibility now answers to Read and editability to Read / Write, which is what the two levels
  mean everywhere else.

### Removed
- **The Administrator tick is gone from the role form.** It settled every module at Read / Write in
  one move, which is now the shared **Admin Permission** set's job — and two one-click ways to hand
  out sweeping access is one more than the screen can explain. A role that needs everything is built
  from the module dropdowns like any other. `Role.isAdmin` itself is unchanged and still derived from
  the matrix on every save, so the WhatsApp gate's administrator exemption and Role Management's Type
  column go on reading what they always did.

### Added
- **Image Selection sends the gallery invite into the project's WhatsApp group, from whoever pressed
  Send.** The studio runs every production project in a WhatsApp group with the client, and the
  project's name here already *is* that group's name — so the Selection Settings rail gains a
  **WhatsApp Group** row. **Link** reads the groups the coordinator's own paired WhatsApp is in, offers
  the one named like the project first, greys out any group another live project already holds and
  says which project that is; **Change** and **Unlink** are one press. ⋮ → **Match WhatsApp Groups**
  does the same for every unlinked project at once — exact names only — and lists what it left alone
  (no group with that name, two groups with that name, a group already taken). The WhatsApp Template
  panel then has **Send to Group** beside the wa.me link, now called **Send to Client**: it publishes
  if it has to and sends the message shown — built once on the server so the panel, the copy and the
  send cannot differ, and with the event date as a day rather than a timestamp — into the linked group
  *from the signed-in person's own WhatsApp*, so a pre-production coordinator's send arrives from their
  number and a post-production coordinator's from theirs. Not linked yet? The dialog shows the pairing
  code, the same one the CRM chat scans, and the list loads the moment the scan lands. One message per
  gallery version however many people press Send, answered "Already Sent" rather than repeated; a
  group send is paced and typed like a message a person writes, goes to nothing but a `@g.us` address,
  and is never written into a lead's thread — the CRM chat still keeps every group out of its threads.
  It is one deliberate press per project, not a broadcast, and it passes the studio-wide and
  per-person WhatsApp switches like every other send. Three columns on `image_selection_project`
  (`20260919160000_image_selection_whatsapp_group_link`); `GET …/projects/:id/whatsapp/groups`,
  `POST`/`DELETE …/whatsapp/group`, `POST …/whatsapp/send-group`, and
  `/api/image-selection/whatsapp/session`, `/session/connect`, `/link-all` — all under
  `IMAGE_SELECTION / Project Management` (Read to look, Read / Write to link, unlink or send). The
  session is read through this module's own endpoints rather than the CRM chat's because a
  coordinator who holds Image Selection and not `SALES / CRM Leads` must not be sent to a route that
  refuses them. No new permission: every role holding Project Management Read / Write reaches it now.
- **A Branch Admin carries one shared permission set, edited in one place.** Role Management has a new
  **Admin Permission** button beside Add Role: the same module and sub-module matrix the role editor
  uses, with the role taken away — no name, no description, no branches, because none of those are
  being decided there. A role ticked **Branch Admin** carries that set on top of what it grants in
  its own right, so changing the set changes what every branch administrator may do at once, without
  a single role being opened; the editor says how many roles that is before it saves. One set for the
  studio rather than one per role, because two branch administrators in two branches do the same job
  in two places — what differs between them is the branch list on their own role, which this does not
  touch. It is merged through the same most-permissive rule as every other grant, so it **only ever
  widens**: a role already holding a permission at Read / Write keeps it when the set names it at
  Read, and a deactivated role grants nothing, the shared set included. Ticking Branch Admin fills
  the modules below with the set rather than hiding it behind a flag — a tick that silently granted
  eleven permissions with nothing on screen would leave the role editor unable to answer the one
  question it exists for — and **editing any module afterwards unticks it and keeps those permissions
  as that role's own**, because the moment somebody's access differs from the shared set they are not
  carrying it any more. Not to be confused with `ADMIN / Branch Administration`, which answers whose
  *rows* a person gets rather than which screens they may open, and which can sit in the set like any
  other permission. New table `branch_admin_permission`
  (`20260919150000_branch_admin_permissions`), empty on arrival so nobody is widened until a set is
  saved; `GET`/`PUT /api/admin/branch-admin-permissions` both under `ADMIN / Role Management`, which
  is the guard this belongs behind because saving it is a strictly larger act than editing one role.

### Changed
- **A correction's Edit and Remove moved onto the bubble's top line, as icons.** Opening a pin on a
  spread in Album Proofing showed the author, the time and the comment, and then **Edit** and
  **Remove** as words in a band of their own behind a rule — so a two-word correction opened a card
  three bands tall, most of it chrome, hanging over the very photograph being judged. They are now a
  pencil and a bin at the right-hand end of the line that already carries the author and the time,
  which costs the bubble no height at all. Each carries its name for a screen reader and as a
  tooltip, and the bin warms on hover, being the one that cannot be undone. A long author name gives
  way before the icons do. Nothing changed about *when* they appear: they are still only on a
  correction that reviewer may still change, in QC review and the client's review alike. **Reopen**
  — which a resolved correction carries during QC review — deliberately keeps its words underneath,
  because there is no symbol for it a reviewer would read correctly the first time. See
  [ProofSpreadCanvas.js](../../frontend/src/components/image-selection/album-proofing/ProofSpreadCanvas.js).
- **Album Proofing's marking tools are always on screen.** The toolbar over a spread used to start
  folded, as a single chevron in the corner, and unfold sideways when pressed — so on the one screen
  whose whole purpose is marking corrections, every tool sat a press behind a control that said
  nothing about what was inside it, and it folded itself again on every spread and every visit. The
  chevron is gone and the bar is simply there: circle, pen, its colours and undo, on the same
  frosted pill in the same corner. It applies to **both reviews**, the QC Lead's and the client's,
  because both render the one component. What each reviewer may *do* is unchanged: the marking tools
  still appear only for whoever may rule on the album, the corrections button only where there is a
  rail for it to open, and somebody who may do neither still sees no bar at all. On a narrow screen
  the bar scrolls sideways within its own width rather than growing over the spread. See
  [ProofToolbar.js](../../frontend/src/components/image-selection/album-proofing/ProofToolbar.js).
- **The deliverable picker on a pricing line can be typed at.** Settings → Project → Services →
  Add / Edit Service: **+ Add Deliverable** now filters as you type instead of offering the whole
  list to scroll. It holds every deliverable the studio has configured, each named the way the
  Quotation Generator names it — "Demo Album Designing - Studio Press - 16x24" — so a dozen of them
  differ only in the last few words of a long line and finding one by eye meant reading the same
  prefix over and over. Typing narrows on anything in the label, so the brand, the size or the
  deliverable all reach it; arrows and Enter work as before, and picking one still clears the box
  back to **+ Add Deliverable** ready for the next. This is the shared dropdown's existing
  `searchable`, the same filter box the CRM, Role and shoot pickers already use — nothing new was
  built for it. See
  [ServiceDefinitionDialog.js](../../frontend/src/components/project-management/ServiceDefinitionDialog.js).
- **Settings → Project → Services: the Size question now says which sizes it means.** The Yes/No
  field on Add / Edit Service reads **Size (Wall or TV etc...)** rather than just **Size**, so it is
  clear it is asking whether the service is priced per size — an LED wall, a TV — rather than asking
  for a size. Only the label changed: the field, its answers, and everything Yes puts on the pricing
  lines below are exactly as they were. See
  [ServiceDefinitionDialog.js](../../frontend/src/components/project-management/ServiceDefinitionDialog.js).
- **Copying the WhatsApp template now says it was copied.** The Copy button on the Image Selection
  WhatsApp Template panel gave no sign it had worked, so the only way to tell was to paste
  somewhere and look. It now answers in the app's own snackbar, and the copy goes through the
  shared clipboard helper — which matters on a studio running the OS over plain http, where the
  browser's clipboard API is unavailable and this button previously did nothing it could explain
  ([WhatsAppTemplatePanel.js](../../frontend/src/components/image-selection/WhatsAppTemplatePanel.js)).
- **A project name the sidebar cuts off now says the whole of itself on hover.** Galleries are named
  after the WhatsApp group they came from, and the part that tells two of them apart — "WED 3t3
  Erode TN groom & Bride" against "WED 3t3 Erode TN Shanmugam …" — was usually the part that ran
  past the edge. Hovering the name shows it in full. Only where it has actually been cut off: a
  name that already fits needs no explaining
  ([ProjectSidebar.js](../../frontend/src/components/image-selection/ProjectSidebar.js)). The same
  sidebar serves Projects and Album Proofing, so both gain it.
- **The client gallery loads the next photos as the client scrolls, with no Load more button.**
  A client opening a folder of several hundred photographs had to find and press **Load more
  photos** for every 40 of them. The grid now reads the next page as they approach the bottom and
  adds it underneath — the waiting tiles it already drew are the only sign anything happened
  ([FolderGrid.js](../../frontend/src/components/image-selection/client-gallery/FolderGrid.js)).
  Selecting, the folder sidebar, the tabs and the photo viewer are unchanged.
- **The workspace gallery loads more photos as you scroll instead of a page at a time.** The
  Previous / Next bar and the "Page 1 of 1" line are gone: the grid now reads the next 24 when you
  approach the bottom and adds them underneath, so a shoot is reviewed in one continuous scroll
  ([Gallery.js](../../frontend/src/components/image-selection/Gallery.js)). Thumbnails still load only
  as they come near the screen, so scrolling past a page costs the tiles, not the photographs.
  Changing folder, search, filter or order starts the grid again from the top. The gallery still
  re-checks every ten seconds for newly processed photos and the client's ticks — it now re-reads
  only the newest page and merges it in, so nothing you have scrolled past moves.

### Fixed
- **Album Proofing's toolbar no longer looks as though two tools are chosen at once.** The
  corrections toggle and the chosen marking tool drew the same blue, so in the ordinary case — the
  corrections rail open with the circle tool in hand — two buttons on the bar read as equally
  selected. They are not the same kind of thing: the corrections button is an on/off toggle for a
  panel, while the circle and the pen are a choice of exactly one. Blue is now reserved for the
  tool, so whatever else is on, exactly one button is blue and it is the one a drag will use; the
  corrections toggle shows itself on in neutral instead, a soft wash pressed into the glass where
  the hover state lifts out of it toward white. A thin rule between the two groups says it a second
  way for anyone who reads shape before colour, and it appears only where there is something on both
  sides of it. Nothing moved and nothing changed what it does. See
  [ProofToolbar.js](../../frontend/src/components/image-selection/album-proofing/ProofToolbar.js).
- **The Image Selection dashboard no longer reports active upload batches when nothing is being
  processed.** A batch is opened with the total the browser says it is about to send and only
  finishes once its counters reach that total, so a closed tab, a dropped connection or a cancelled
  upload left one calling itself active for ever — which is what put "3 active upload batches"
  under a Processing count of 0. Such a batch is now closed as ABANDONED, on two conditions: no
  counter has moved for fifteen minutes **and** it holds no upload still pending or processing, so
  a batch merely waiting behind a backlog is never touched however long that backlog takes
  ([batchRepository.js](../../backend/src/modules/image-selection/uploads/batchRepository.js)). The
  sweep runs when the dashboard reads its storage figures, so batches already stuck close
  themselves on the next load — nothing to run by hand. An abandoned batch reads as finished, so no
  progress bar is left running against work that will never land, and retrying one still reopens it.
- **Two controls showed nothing when they were tabbed to.** The shared close **×** — the first thing
  Tab reaches in any dialog — and the calendar's month and year pickers each switched the browser's
  focus ring off and drew nothing in its place, so a keyboard user could not see where they were.
  Both now draw their own ring, and only for keyboard focus: clicking either with a mouse looks
  exactly as it did. See [CloseButton.js](../../frontend/src/components/shared/CloseButton.js).
- **Deleting a project in Project Management now takes its Image Selection photographs with it, and
  old deleted projects stop appearing in the Image Selection sidebar.** Deleting a booking only
  began hiding its Image Selection project on 9 September 2026, so every booking deleted before
  that left its project fully listed, with a client it no longer had. Those rows are hidden
  immediately by a parent check on the list and on the dashboard counts
  ([repository.js](../../backend/src/modules/image-selection/projects/repository.js)), and cleared for
  good by a one-time script (below). Going forward the delete also purges what it hides: the
  project's derivatives in R2, its photos, folders, selections and client link, through Image
  Selection's own purge reused whole from
  [the Sales delete](../../backend/src/modules/sales/projects/controller.js) — previously it hid the
  project and left the files, which nothing could then reach or reclaim. Album Proofing projects
  are hidden as before and never purged. The client's original photographs on the studio's own
  drive or NAS are never touched.
- **A booking deleted while the Image Selection list is loading can no longer come back as a new
  project.** The list creates projects for newly in-progress bookings, and read its list of them a
  moment before creating each one; a delete landing in that window was handed a brand new project.
  The parent is now re-checked with its row locked inside the same transaction as the create
  ([service.js](../../backend/src/modules/image-selection/projects/service.js)).

### Added
- **Every dialog and settings panel can now be worked through from the keyboard alone.** Opening one
  moves focus into it, Tab and Shift+Tab cycle within it instead of escaping to the page behind, and
  closing it hands focus back to whatever opened it. Until now focus stayed on the button *behind*
  the dialog, so the first Tab walked the page the dialog was covering — on Settings → Project →
  Services → Edit Service every field was a full lap of the page away, and a dialog marked
  `aria-modal` was telling a screen reader one thing while doing another. It is one shared hook used
  by the shared `Dialog` and the full-screen form panel, so every overlay in the app gets it, and an
  overlay opened over another — a confirmation over a dialog, a dialog over Settings — is answered by
  the innermost one. Nothing moves that did not move before: a dialog that already focused a box of
  its own, like Rename Folder, still does. See
  [useFocusTrap.js](../../frontend/src/components/shared/useFocusTrap.js).
- **Dropdowns can be typed at, not just arrowed through.** Pressing a letter jumps to the option that
  starts with it — `y` for Yes, `n` for No, `st` for Studio — letters typed together make a word that
  narrows the list, and pressing the same letter again walks that letter's options, which is what a
  native dropdown has always done. It moves the highlight only: Enter is still what answers the
  field, so a stray keypress cannot change a saved value. Arrows, Enter and Home/End work exactly as
  before, and a searchable dropdown's own box is untouched. See
  [dropdownOptions.mjs](../../frontend/src/lib/dropdownOptions.mjs).
- **A one-time script clears the Image Selection projects left behind by bookings deleted before
  9 September 2026.** `node scripts/one-off/purgeImageSelectionProjectsWithDeletedSalesProject.js`
  lists what it would delete and deletes nothing; `--execute` runs it, project by project, through
  Image Selection's own purge. It touches only projects whose Sales Manager project is already
  deleted, and only that project's own rows and R2 keys.

### Changed
- **An earlier version opens in the real proof viewer, read-only.** *View V1 spreads* showed a
  thumbnail grid: it could show the pages of a finished round but not what was marked on them,
  which is most of what anybody opens an old round to see. It now opens the same panel the album is
  reviewed in — the same spreads, the same correction pins with their source, status and notes, the
  same zoom and paging — against that exact frozen version. The Corrections tab is where a round is
  worked; the Versions tab is where one is looked at, so this entry changes nothing: marking, the
  pen, the correction menus, Reopen and the ruling bar are all off, and only a `READ` endpoint is
  called. They are not switched off one at a time — the panel already had a single `reviewing`
  gate that every one of those controls reads, and history mode makes it false, so none of them
  can be missed ([QcReviewPanel.js](../../frontend/src/components/image-selection/album-proofing/QcReviewPanel.js)).
- **A correction offers one next step, and the upload dock stays shut until it is wanted.** Every
  correction showed *View Spread*, *Start* and *Resolve* at once, whatever state it was in — so one
  could be marked resolved by somebody who had never opened the spread, and a row with one thing to
  do next read as three. A correction waiting to be picked up now offers **Start Fixes** alone;
  opening the spread and resolving appear once it is in progress; reopening once it is resolved.
  The step comes from the status the server holds, so a reload, a second tab or another admin all
  see the same row. A row offers nothing at all until the round is opened: corrections QC or the
  client has just handed over are waiting on **Start Fix Round** / **Create Revision**, which is an
  act on the album, and a per-correction button beside it put two different things under one label
  on one screen. *View Spread* is withheld only from the person who can work that correction and
  has not started it — never from a reader, and never while browsing an earlier version's
  corrections. On the Album tab, the upload dock no longer stands open through a correction
  round: dropping a folder while corrections are outstanding is not the next step, and the album
  already has spreads in it, so an upload there is a revision rather than a first import. It opens
  when **Upload Revised Version** is pressed. *Regenerate Previews* belongs to the fix stage rather
  than to the dock and stays where it was.
  **And the button no longer waits for ever.** Creating the version hung off the upload store's
  completion event, which fires once per batch, only to whoever is subscribed at that instant, and
  is never replayed — so re-subscribing, remounting, or navigating away and back missed it and left
  *Waiting for the upload...* on screen with the button disabled and no way forward. It now reads
  the store's snapshot instead, which can be read again at any moment, so arriving late at the
  answer is the same as arriving on time. A batch that was already finished in the dock when the
  button was pressed is ignored, or the version would be frozen out of the spreads already in the
  album before a corrected one had arrived.
- **The floating controls over an album proof are frosted glass rather than solid.** The zoom pill
  and the marking toolbar sit on top of the spread being reviewed, so an opaque bar is a piece of
  the photograph the reviewer cannot see. Both are now the same frosted surface: the spread carries
  on underneath, blurred enough that `100%` reads the same over a white album page and a black one.
  The blur does the work rather than the transparency — a plain see-through fill cannot promise
  legible labels over an arbitrary photograph — and where a browser cannot blur at all the pill
  goes nearly opaque instead, which is the one combination that stays readable
  ([proofGlass.js](../../frontend/src/components/image-selection/album-proofing/proofGlass.js)).

### Fixed
- **A corrected spread was being thrown away, and the album then waited for ever.** Two faults, one
  on each side. The dock registers a photo once per folder, matched on its path inside that folder,
  so re-picking a folder to add the few new frames in it does not register everything a second time
  — right for a first import and for a gallery, and exactly wrong for a correction round, where the
  corrected spread carries the same name and path *because* it is the same spread. Every one of
  them was answered "already registered" and its pixels dropped, while the batch counted the slot
  and reported itself complete in about two seconds: a fast upload that changed nothing. A revised
  upload now says so, and a corrected spread writes over the row it corrects. It is opt-in, so
  nothing else about uploading changes, and it is safe because every frozen version holds copies of
  its own — what an earlier round was reviewed with cannot be reached from the live row.
  **And the wait now ends.** The album watched for the batch to report itself finished on the
  progress stream, which a two-second batch can lose the end of, leaving *Waiting for the
  spreads...* on screen with nothing coming. The browser's own work — every file posted, none
  pending, none failed — is knowable without asking anyone, so it counts as well.
- **V1, V2, V3 are the client's versions — a QC pass never advances one.** A studio that needed
  four QC passes to get the first album right ended up with a history reading V1, V1, V1, V1, and a
  client who had been sent V1 was told the next thing they saw was V3. QC asking for corrections is
  a pass *inside* the version the client is waiting on, however many times it goes round; only a
  client correction round answered with a revised upload takes the album to the next number. Each
  QC pass still freezes its own immutable snapshot under its own storage keys — the proofs QC
  reviewed on an earlier pass have to stay exactly as they were reviewed, and nothing is ever
  overwritten or deleted — but the version history shows one card per client version, with the pass
  that stands ([the rule](../../backend/src/modules/image-selection/album-proofs/pipeline.service.js)).
  Albums created before this keep every number they already have: those are real frozen snapshots
  the studio may have reviewed or sent, and guessing which of them were QC passes would be rewriting
  history that cannot be verified.
  **And a retried freeze no longer skips a number.** The next version was read before the sweep that
  clears a dead freeze's leftover draft, so the abandoned draft still counted toward the album's
  highest number and the retry landed one past it.
- **A correction round ends with uploading the corrected spreads, and that upload is what creates
  the next version.** Resolving the last correction used to be enough: *Resubmit to QC* froze
  whatever happened to be in the album and called it V2, so there was no step at which the corrected
  spreads were actually uploaded and a round could produce a version nobody had re-exported. Four
  separate acts now — resolve a correction, upload the corrected spreads, create the version, submit
  it — and only the third follows from the second. **Upload Revised Version** appears once every
  correction of that round's source is resolved, opens the album's own upload dock, and creates
  V(n+1) when the spreads have landed; *Resubmit to QC* then sends that version. The two are never
  offered together. A reopened correction takes the upload out of reach again at once, on the server
  and not only on screen, and having had *no* corrections never unlocks it — zero unresolved used to
  be indistinguishable from all resolved. Both rounds work this way, QC's and the client's, for V1 →
  V2 → V3 → V4 without limit, and a client round still goes back through QC rather than inheriting
  the last approval ([the rule](../../backend/src/modules/image-selection/album-proofs/revisionGate.js),
  [the upload](../../backend/src/modules/image-selection/album-proofs/pipeline.service.js)).
  **A revised upload no longer carries the album out of its round.** Uploading during a fix round
  moved the album to PROCESSING, which reconciles to *Ready for QC* — right for a first upload and
  ruinous for a revised one, because the round QC or the client was waiting on simply vanished and
  its corrections were left gating a stage the album was no longer in. A fix round now holds its
  stage while its pages process.
  **And a failed upload creates nothing.** The spreads under review are checked by name before
  anything is frozen, so previews still regenerating or failed refuse the version and say which
  files; the album keeps its stage, its corrections and every version it had. Two admins pressing
  Upload at the same moment get one version — the album's next number is taken behind a unique
  constraint — and a refresh, a back button or a second tab repeats neither the version nor the
  submission.
- **The Album Proofing client link is short, its PIN is four digits, and neither changes when the
  card is reopened.** The URL handed to a client was `/album-proof/` followed by a 43-character
  token — unreadable down a telephone and long enough to wrap in a WhatsApp message. A publication
  now carries a six-character short code as well, from the same alphabet the client gallery uses
  (no `0`/`O`, `1`/`I` or `L`), random and carrying no album, project or database id, so the link
  reads `/p/AB7KX2`. The original token still resolves to the same record, and links already with a
  client keep working on the URL they were sent as. A link published before this is given a code the
  next time staff open its card — safe to do to a live link precisely because it is additive: the
  token goes on resolving, so the URL the client already has never stops working, and the studio
  stops having to read a 43-character one down a telephone. PINs are four digits rather than six,
  zero-padded so `0047` is a PIN the studio can issue rather than one the generator skips, and the
  gate accepts the six-digit PINs of links already shared — an older PIN is never rewritten
  underneath the client holding it, and the card says it is an older one and that Reset replaces
  it. Nothing on this card is generated in the browser or by reading it: the PIN is decrypted from
  what was stored at publish, so a refresh, a new session and a server restart all show the same one ([link and PIN](../../backend/src/modules/image-selection/album-proofs/client.service.js),
  [card](../../frontend/src/components/image-selection/album-proofing/PublicationCard.js)).
  **The card now says what it holds and lets it be changed.** A header carrying the revision and a
  live/revoked badge, the client URL in a read-only field with Copy and Open, the PIN shown large
  with Copy and Reset, the expiry as a date anyone can read (`19 Oct 2026`) that becomes a picker
  when asked, and an information bar along the bottom — opened how many times and when — with
  Revoke as the one danger-outlined control on it. Reset PIN draws a new four-digit PIN, saves it
  before showing it, and leaves the URL, the expiry, the album, the version and the revision round
  exactly as they were — the previous PIN stops working the moment it returns, and a failed reset
  leaves the working PIN on screen. Both are audited.
  **A new link expires six calendar months out, and the date is the studio's to move.** It was 30
  days from a per-album day count, so a link made on the 31st landed on a date nobody could explain.
  Calendar-month addition clamps to the last day of the target month instead, and the expiry can be
  moved to any future date from the card, saved server-side. Shares that already have an expiry keep
  it. Expiry and revocation are still enforced on the server, and an expired link tells the client
  to contact the studio rather than failing silently.
- **An Album Proofing round is a version now, and no earlier version is written over.** Resubmitting
  after QC fixes used to freshen the *reviewed* version's own page rows in place, under a
  round-scoped key: the earlier round's images survived in storage but nothing pointed at them any
  more, so the spreads QC had actually reviewed could no longer be shown. The schema's own promise -
  a version page is "a snapshot, never a live pointer" - was not being kept. A resubmit freezes the
  **next version** instead: new rows, new version-scoped copies, V1 untouched. Every version
  therefore goes on showing exactly the spreads it was sent with, and V1, V2, V3 accumulate rather
  than replacing one another. The freeze is one routine shared by the first send and every resubmit,
  so the two cannot drift; the version number stays `max + 1` behind a unique constraint, a stale
  DRAFT sweep and a live-DRAFT refusal, so a double-click, a reload mid-freeze or a second tab still
  cannot produce two. Only re-uploaded spreads change - the rest are inherited from the album's live
  tree - and `changedFromPrevious` still marks what moved, so "changed spreads only" review is
  unaffected ([freeze](../../backend/src/modules/image-selection/album-proofs/pipeline.service.js),
  [resubmit](../../backend/src/modules/image-selection/album-proofs/qc.service.js)).
  **An outstanding correction now blocks wherever it was raised.** Approve, Request Changes,
  Resubmit and the client revision gate counted only the version under review - so once a newer
  version existed, reopening a correction on an older one blocked nothing and Reopen became a button
  that silently did nothing. All four count the album's, so reopening one locks the next upload
  again at once. Resubmit still names the spreads holding it up and hands back the failed filenames.
  **The two revision buttons wait for their corrections.** *Resubmit to QC* was offered the moment
  the album entered QC fixes and *Revision Ready for QC* the moment a client revision began - both
  regardless of what was still outstanding, so the backend refused them with a 409 and the reader
  got a primary button that could only fail. Each is now disabled until its own corrections are
  resolved, titled with what is holding it up, reading the album's own counts - the same ones the
  service gates on, so the two cannot disagree when a correction is reopened on an earlier version.
  Resolving a correction, uploading the corrected files and freezing the next version stay three
  separate acts; the button is a convenience and the service remains the authority.
  **And an earlier version's spreads can be looked at.** The API always served them; the Versions
  list said how many pages a version held and gave no way to open them, which is what made earlier
  rounds feel lost. Each version now opens its own snapshot, with the spreads it changed marked
  ([viewer](../../frontend/src/components/image-selection/album-proofing/VersionSpreads.js)).
- **Four filters that had quietly stopped matching anything.** A value that became a list was still
  being read as a single one in four places, and in each the symptom was an empty screen rather than
  an error. **Work Hours** bound the employee list as one parameter and guarded it with a plain
  truthiness test, which an empty array passes - so the clause went on every query and the report was
  empty for everyone. **Call Hours / Span** had the same fault, with a `catch` around the query that
  turned it into a silent zero. The **call board's** column filters joined a list with a comma, so
  ticking Incoming and Outgoing asked for `LIKE '%Incoming,Outgoing%'` and returned nothing. Each now
  builds one placeholder per value - `IN` for the reports, an OR of the existing `LIKE` for the call
  board - so a single value behaves exactly as it did and several mean "any of these"
  ([reports](../../backend/src/modules/sales/crm-reports/service.js),
  [call board](../../backend/src/modules/sales/crm-leads/callBoardService.js)).
  **And every table's header filter.** A column whose values are a set is picked from rather than
  typed into, and a picker hands back an array; a column that declared no filter of its own fell to
  TanStack's guess, `includesString`, which stringified it - so ticking two options searched for the
  literal `"a,b"` and the column stopped filtering altogether. User Management, Role Management and
  Employee Attendance were among those affected. The shared table now defaults to a filter that
  reads both shapes: a typed value is contained ignoring case, exactly as before, and a list matches
  a row that is any of the ticked values. A column that wrote its own filter keeps it
  ([DataTable](../../frontend/src/components/data-table/DataTable.js)).
- **The Settings rail scrolls, so the sections below the fold can be reached again.** Settings had
  grown past a laptop's viewport — User Settings down to Holidays filled the screen, and **Login
  Slide** and **Application Monitoring** under them were not merely out of reach but absent, with
  nothing on screen to say the list had been cut off and no way to scroll the page to them. The rail
  is now its own scroll container, so a list longer than the panel scrolls inside it and a list that
  fits shows no scrollbar at all; reaching the end of it no longer starts scrolling whatever sits
  behind the panel. This is the shared full-screen form rail, so every panel drawn from it — Create
  Project, CRD Terms, Excluded Numbers and the rest — is covered by the same fix, and none of them
  changes while its rail still fits. Below `lg` the rail is the same horizontal strip it always was.
  See [SchemaFormSidebar.js](../../frontend/src/components/schema-form/SchemaFormSidebar.js).
- **A nested row's chevron is no longer cut in half.** The expand column is locked to 34px and hides
  what overflows, but it took the same horizontal padding as a cell of text - 12px a side - leaving
  10px for a 24px button. A top-level chevron overflowed that a little and read as centred; a nested
  one, indented as well, ran to 44px and was clipped six pixels into the glyph, which is what a
  deliverable's brand row showed. The column takes no padding now, exactly as the Actions column
  does - both hold a control rather than text - so the whole 34px is the control's: 4px in at the
  top level, 10px nested, and a rule at 20px two levels down. Nothing is clipped at any depth, and
  a brand's chevron now lines up with a pricing line's rule. The top-level chevron in the Services
  table and the Quotation register sits about 12px further left as a result; nothing else moves
  ([DataTable](../../frontend/src/components/data-table/DataTable.js)).

### Changed
- **Every multi-select offers Select all.** A list that has been searched is one somebody narrowed on
  purpose, so "all" means **all of what is on screen** — picking the whole catalogue from behind a
  search would be the control overruling the search; with no search the two are the same thing. It
  is a toggle: once everything shown is chosen the same row clears them, and values the search is
  hiding are left alone by both halves, so clearing a narrowed list cannot silently drop a choice
  off screen. On the table filters it replaces the old **"All"** row, which a multi-select could tick
