  as a value — filtering a column to the empty string, and answering `Invalid query.` because the
  board's query schema named only single strings and refused a list outright. Both schemas now name
  both shapes, and a blank option is dropped by the control wherever it comes from, so the
  single-select filters keep the "All" row they need.
- **A table column filters by several values at once.** The filter row under every table's headers
  was a plain text box per column — "show me the rows containing this", which is the right question
  for a name somebody is typing part of and the wrong one for a column of a dozen repeated values.
  Reading two people's leads meant filtering to one, reading them, then filtering to the other; the
  table could not put them side by side at all. A column whose values are a **set** now offers those
  values as a list to tick, and the rows are those matching any of them; every other column keeps
  the text box it had. Which is which is worked out from the rows actually loaded rather than
  declared per module, so a column becomes a picker the day its values become a set and no table has
  to be kept in step by hand — and the count is abandoned as soon as it passes 40, so a Name column
  on ten thousand rows costs forty reads rather than ten thousand. Unticking the last value clears
  the filter rather than emptying the board. New shared control:
  [FilterMultiSelect](../../frontend/src/components/shared/FilterMultiSelect.js), styled to the filter
  row like `FilterCombobox` beside it, which stays what a column uses when one value is the question.
- **Reports & Analytics filters by several employees at once.** The Employee filter took one name, so
  "how did the team do last week" was asked by running the report once per person and adding the
  numbers up by hand. It is a multi-select now, and every report behind it — the call reports and the
  usage reports alike — matches the whole list. A single `employee=` on a saved link still means one
  person, and a comma-separated value reads the same way, so nothing bookmarked stops working; the
  list is capped at 50 names, de-duplicated and trimmed. Switching between a call report and a usage
  report now keeps the names that mean something on the tab being opened instead of dropping the
  filter entirely, and the "Showing:" line names one or two people and counts more than that.
- **Every table's columns can be resized, not six of them.** Column resizing existed but was
  opt-in, and six tables out of roughly twenty-five had opted in — Projects, Bookings & Converted,
  Assets and most of the rest could not be resized at all. That reads like an oversight repeated two
  dozen times, and it was not: turning the feature on switched a table from the browser's automatic
  layout to a **fixed** one immediately, and the table library answers 150px for every column that
  declares no size — which none in this application do. Enabling resizing therefore made a table
  worse the moment it was enabled, twenty identical 150px columns where the content used to fit, so
  leaving it off was the sensible thing to do.

  The rule the CRM board had already worked out for its own hand-written table now lives in the
  shared one: **stay on the automatic layout until a column is actually pulled**, and on that first
  pull store every column's measured width so only the pulled one moves. A table therefore looks
  exactly as it does today until somebody drags something, which is what makes it safe to have on
  everywhere — and with the switch costing nothing, resizing is now on unless a table turns it off,
  the same way filtering, sorting and column reordering already are. The two small embedded lists
  inside Service Creation still turn it off, because they are form sections rather than grids
  somebody arranges ([DataTable.js](../../frontend/src/components/data-table/DataTable.js)).

### Changed
- **Closing Add Employee asks before it throws the form away.** The × on that panel discarded
  everything typed without a word. The prompt already existed — it began on Review Employee Details,
  and was extended to Edit after somebody lost three salary reviews to the same press — but Add
  Employee was left out on the reasoning that its first page "is not a review of anything". True,
  and beside the point: it is the longest form in the application, and being new is the case where
  the *most* work is lost, because none of it has ever been saved.

  What Save does there differs, and the prompt says so rather than pretending otherwise: an employee
  that does not exist yet cannot be half-written to the register, so it is the same save Add
  Employee's own footer makes and is held to the same required fields. On a form too incomplete to
  save it names what is missing and the panel stays open with the work still in it — which is the
  thing that was actually wanted. The button reads **Save** rather than *Save draft* there, because
  a prompt promising a draft and then asking for eight more fields reads as a button that does not
  work ([EmployeeFormPanel.js](../../frontend/src/components/employees/EmployeeFormPanel.js)).

### Changed
- **Whoever can book a project can record the advance it was booked with.** Payment Received on
  Create Project was shown only to somebody holding `ACCOUNTS / Cash Management`, because the advance
  is a cash entry. That is the right guard for the cash book — recording any entry against any
  project is being trusted with the studio's books — but it is not the question this form asks. The
  person booking a project is typing what the client just handed over, against the project they are
  creating, and holding that behind the whole cash book left two bad options: give project creators
  the books, or book the project with the advance left off for somebody else to type in later.

  **The act keeps a guard of its own rather than losing one.** The advance now posts to
  `POST /api/sales/projects/:id/advance`, which answers to the same `SALES / Sales Manager` that
  created the project and writes through the Accounts module's own recorder — so the row, its
  validation and its branch check are the ones Accounts applies to everything else, and
  `ACCOUNTS / Cash Management` goes on meaning exactly what it meant for every other way of writing
  to the cash book. It is deliberately narrow: the project comes from the URL rather than the body,
  so an advance cannot be filed against a project the caller cannot reach; branch reach stays
  enforced; and the entry type is forced to **Received**, so this cannot become a way to record
  studio spending without the permission for it. `hrm/payment-requests` already writes a cash entry
  this way under its own permission, so this is that pattern rather than a new one
  ([projects/controller.js](../../backend/src/modules/sales/projects/controller.js),
  [CreateProjectFormPanel.js](../../frontend/src/components/sales/CreateProjectFormPanel.js)).

### Changed
- **Date of Birth (or) EDOD is required in the lead popup, and nowhere else.** The rule is about
  *where* it is asked rather than which field it is. Not on the way in: a lead very often arrives
  before the date does — the call ended before anybody asked, the mother has no scan date yet, the
  enquiry came through a planner — so Add Lead and Bulk Entry still take a Qua Qua lead without one,
  because demanding it there buys an invented date, which is worse than an empty one when the
  Newborns and Birthdays tabs schedule shoots off it. But by the time somebody opens that lead to
  work it, the date is what the whole vertical plans from, and a lead that has been worked and still
  has none is the one nobody can schedule — so the popup marks it and refuses the update until it is
  answered. Only on Qua Qua; the other two verticals do not show the field at all.

  **A lead named `CNA` is excused it, exactly as it is excused Estimated Value.** Nobody answered the
  phone, so the baby's date is no more knowable than what the job is worth — both fall away together
  and everything that makes the call findable stays. That is the flow the studio asked for, and it
  is the same list both fields now sit on ([CRMPage.js](../../frontend/src/components/sales/CRMPage.js)).

### Added
- **A task can be corrected, and duplicated.** Both open the same form the task was raised on.
  **Edit** belongs to the person who gave the task, and to a Super Admin — the same rule deleting
  already followed, and for the same reason: whoever wrote a task down is who can say it was written
  down wrong. The receiver is deliberately not among them; they act on a task through Start,
  Complete and Reassign, and rewriting the title or the due date would let somebody restate the job
  they were given. The menu offers Edit only where the server says `canEdit`, answered by the same
  function the write path refuses with, so it cannot offer one that would then be turned down. An
  edit writes **one activity line naming the fields that actually moved** — "changed due date to
  2026-09-25, priority to High" — because a history reading "the task was edited" answers none of
  the questions a history is read for.

  **Duplicate** starts a new task from an existing one, which is what the studio does with anything
  recurring. It carries no id, so it cannot be mistaken for an edit, and it deliberately does not
  copy the original's attachments — those files belonged to that task. **Reassigning stays its own
  verb**: it records the hand-over in the holder chain and tells the new holder, so an edit does not
  offer the assignee at all and the server's update schema drops it if sent. A task was previously
  never editable at all, the reasoning being that the record is what the receiver acted on — but it
  was already being worked around by deleting and re-raising, which loses the history, the
  attachments and the holder chain that argument was protecting
  ([tasks/service.js](../../backend/src/modules/hrm/tasks/service.js),
  [TaskFormPanel.js](../../frontend/src/components/hrm/TaskFormPanel.js)).

### Changed
- **A task has to say when it is due.** Due Date was optional, so a task could be raised with none —
  and one with no due date is in nobody's "due this week" and is chased by nothing: it sits in the
  list being neither done nor overdue. It is required on the form and on the server, on raising a
  task and on correcting one, and the form still refuses a date that has already passed
  ([taskConfig.js](../../frontend/src/constants/taskConfig.js),
  [tasks/controller.js](../../backend/src/modules/hrm/tasks/controller.js)).

### Fixed
- **Renaming somebody changes their name everywhere, whichever screen it is done from.** A person is
  two records — an `Employee` and the `User` that logs in as them, joined by `linkedEmployeeId` — and
  the name lives on both. Two screens wrote it and neither told the other, so a rename in
  Administration → Users left every employee dropdown in the application still showing the old name,
  because all of them read the employee record. It looked like a screen that had not saved. A rename
  in either screen now reaches the other: the Employee Repository writes the login's name inside its
  own transaction, so a half-applied rename cannot leave the two disagreeing, and Users mirrors the
  employee record afterwards — reported rather than refused if that mirror fails, since the login is
  already saved by then and the two can be brought back into step by saving either screen again.
  The same habit `Employee.loginUsername` already followed for the username
  ([employees/controller.js](../../backend/src/modules/hrm/employees/controller.js),
  [admin/routes.js](../../backend/src/modules/admin/routes.js)).

### Changed
- **The WhatsApp group name generator's lists can be searched.** Branch, Tier and Event were closed
  dropdowns you had to scroll, which mattered most on **Event**: it offers forty options, so finding
  "Naming Ceremony" meant reading past thirty others. All three take typing now, and typing filters
  the list rather than becoming the answer — the value is still only ever an option that was chosen,
  so this adds a way to find one and no way to invent one. The same behaviour the Assigned Branch
  picker on the CRM board already had
  ([WhatsappGroupNameDialog.js](../../frontend/src/components/sales/WhatsappGroupNameDialog.js)).

### Changed
- **Converting a lead records it straight away, in the tab you are already in.** Pressing
  **Converted** or **Converted & Followup** used to write nothing: the lead was handed to Sales
  Manager in a second tab, and the project's own save is what marked it — so a tab closed without
  saving left the lead exactly as it was. That is reversed. The decision is made when the pill is
  pressed, so it is recorded then, and Create Project opens **in the same tab** rather than beside
  it.

  The confirmation now offers two ways on. **Convert** writes the lead and opens Create Project on
  it. **Update and Close** writes the lead and leaves you where you are — for a lead that is
  converted but is not being booked yet. Both go through the ordinary save with the status forced
  onto it, rather than a second way of writing a converted lead, so a conversion carries its remark,
  its activity entry and its Last Follow-up Date exactly as any other update does.

  **The required fields are asked for first.** Converting is a write now, so it is held to the same
  line every other write is: the pill marks the empty boxes red and the confirmation does not open
  until they are filled, instead of a lead being marked Converted with the fields that make it a
  lead still blank. And **a refused save does not navigate** — the reader stays on the lead with the
  reason, rather than being taken to Create Project for a lead that never converted, where they
  could book a project against a record still reading Interested
  ([CRMPage.js](../../frontend/src/components/sales/CRMPage.js)).

### Fixed
- **Converting a lead fills in Source Branch again.** Create Project takes the branch from the
  lead's Assigned Branch, and the translation between them only knew bare cities — `Erode` became
  `Erode TN`. Repairing the branch text earlier in this release changed what a lead holds: a
  properly filed one now stores the register's own spelling, so the conversion arrived already
  reading `Erode TN`, which a list of cities does not contain. It answered with nothing, and Create
  Project opened with **Source Branch blank on a required field** — a form refusing to save over a
  box it had failed to fill in itself. The fix that made Assigned Branch right is what made this
  wrong. A name the register already spells in full is now handed back as it came, and the short
  forms are consulted only for the older records that still hold one. The **register stays the
  authority**: Create Project loads the branches and clears anything not among them, so this
  translates without ever inventing a branch — and a branch the studio adds tomorrow works without
  an edit here, which is the whole reason it holds no list of its own. The same path the Quotations
  screen books through was affected identically and is fixed with it
  ([projectBooking.js](../../frontend/src/components/sales/projectBooking.js)).

### Added
- **An "All leads" switch on Leads & Followups, for anybody with a team under them.** The board has
  always shown a manager their own leads *and* their team's, which is what the data scope answers —
  and there was no way to ask for just their own. The switch sits between the date range and the
  Nebula logo: on, the board is everything that reader may see, as it has always been; off, it is
  only the leads assigned to them or created by them. Remembered per person, so somebody who works
  their own queue does not turn it off every morning.

  **It narrows and can never widen.** The server ANDs it with the same data scope it already
  applied, so turning it off can only take rows away from what was allowed — and the ids it narrows
  by come from the signed-in account, never from the request, so it cannot be pointed at a
  colleague. It reaches **every** read the board makes: the rows, the tab counts, each column
  filter's own menu, and the ids a "select all" resolves to — that last one mattering most, since a
  bulk action taken on a board showing one person's leads must not run against their whole team's.

  **Drawn only for people it means something to** — somebody with a team under them, or anybody who
  sees every lead in the studio, for whom it is more use than for anyone else. For everybody else the
  two states are the same board — they are named on their own leads and on nobody else's — and a
  switch that changes nothing invites somebody to flip it, see no difference, and conclude the board
  is broken. It is not offered on the Call Box either, which is answered from the call log and takes
  no such narrowing. Whether it appears is read on a real page read rather than on every scroll
  append, and the board uses the reader's own stored answer until that reply arrives, so a manager
  who works their own queue does not see the whole team's leads flash past first. A reader who has since lost their team is shown everything regardless of what they last
  chose, so a stored answer can never leave a board narrowed by a control that is no longer on
  screen to turn back ([CRMPage.js](../../frontend/src/components/sales/CRMPage.js),
  [leadQueryService.js](../../backend/src/modules/sales/crm-leads/leadQueryService.js),
  [dataScope.service.js](../../backend/src/services/dataScope.service.js)).

### Fixed
- **A long remark no longer draws across the column beside it and out of the table.** On Leads &
  Followups a cell's text was told not to wrap and then not clipped, so anything wider than its
  column ran straight over the Source column and past the right-hand edge of the board — text that
  appeared to belong to a different row, which is the worst way for a table to be wrong. It showed
  only once somebody had dragged a column wider or narrower, because that is what switches the board
  to a fixed layout where a column is the width it was pulled to rather than the width of its
  contents; until then the columns simply grew and nothing overflowed. So it struck one person's
  screen and not the next. Cells are clipped now and end in an ellipsis, and **the full value is on
  the cell's `title`**, so nothing that is cut short is lost — the same thing the shared `DataTable`
  has always done. The filter row, which was missing the clip entirely, is fixed with it.

  `DataTable` had this right all along; the board is a second hand-written table that had quietly
  drifted from it. **The rule now has one name** — `CELL_CLIP`, `HEADER_CLIP` and `FILTER_CLIP` in
  [tableLayout.js](../../frontend/src/components/data-table/tableLayout.js) — which both read, so the
  two can no longer disagree about it. A test fails if either spells the utilities out by hand
  again, which is how they came to differ in the first place. The board also reads each cell's value
  once now instead of three times per status cell, on a table that draws 32,000 rows
  ([CRMPage.js](../../frontend/src/components/sales/CRMPage.js),
  [DataTable.js](../../frontend/src/components/data-table/DataTable.js)).
- **Update and Close no longer loses the update it just made.** The lead popup will not let an
  update through without a remark, so the ordinary way to use it is to type a note and press
  **Update and Close** with that note still in the box. The button committed the note and then
  saved — and a committed note posts itself, so two writes went to the same endpoint at once. They
  do not carry the same lead: a remark's write can only see the lead **as it was last saved**, since
  the popup's edits live in a draft it knows nothing about, so it sends the old status, branch,
  estimated value and follow-up dates. Whichever request the server finished last won. When that was
  the remark's, the edit was reverted a moment after a toast said the lead had been updated, and
  nothing on screen said so — which is why it struck only sometimes, and why it looked like a button
  that did nothing. A note committed *by* an Update is no longer posted separately: it is committed
  for the screen, handed back to the save, and written once, by the save — the only one of the two
  that knows what was edited. **All three paths are fixed**: Update and Close, Update and Proceed
  Next, and the duplicate-number dialog's "go on with the save", which resumes an interrupted press
  and had the same race. **CNA and Proceed Next** was affected too, though rarely — it is exempt
  from the remark rule, so it is usually pressed with an empty box and never raced; it did whenever
  somebody typed a note before pressing it ([CRMPage.js](../../frontend/src/components/sales/CRMPage.js)).

### Changed
- **Last Follow-up Date is stamped by the lead popup's Update buttons, and by nothing else.**
  Pressing **CNA and Proceed Next**, **Update and Close** or **Update and Proceed Next** now writes
  today's date onto the lead, because pressing one of those is a person saying they dealt with this
  lead today — which is exactly what that column is for. It was never written from the popup at all
  before, so a lead worked every day could still read whatever date an import or a bulk update last
  put there. **A posted remark deliberately does not stamp it.** A remark saves itself the instant
  it is written and is very often written while reading a lead nobody is working — skimming a
  thread, noting something off a call that came in elsewhere, fixing yesterday's typo — so stamping
  there would fill the column and the Pending Followups tabs with days nobody rang anybody, which is
  worse than an empty column because it reads as true. It is written on **every** press, not only
  where something changed: a CRM who opens a lead, finds nothing to change and presses Update has
  still worked it. Both hosts of the popup — the board and the Contacts panel — stamp it through one
  shared rule, so the two cannot drift
  ([CRMPage.js](../../frontend/src/components/sales/CRMPage.js)).

### Fixed
- **The seed runs again.** It failed on a fresh database with *"Argument `remarksEnabled`: Invalid
  value provided. Expected String or Null, provided Boolean"*, taking the pipeline down with it.
  `remarks_enabled` on services and deliverables stopped being a Yes/No switch and became a **note**
  the studio types (`20260918100000`, `20260918130000`), but the seed still wrote `true` into it on
  all twenty definitions. It now writes nothing there: a note is the studio's own words, there is
  nothing for a seed to invent, and the column is nullable so leaving it out means "no note yet".
  The backfill beside it went for the same reason — `if (existing.remarksEnabled == null)` used to
  mean "this row predates the switch, give it the default" and now means "this row has no note", on
  which the right action is to leave it alone. ([seed.js](../../backend/prisma/seed.js))
- **Last Converted Date on the lead popup opens a calendar again, instead of a text box.** It was
  missing from `POPUP_FIELD_CONTROLS`, so it fell through to the plain text box at the end of the
  renderer's chain — a free-text box for a date, on the one screen where the value is read back by a
  board that sorts, filters and reports on it, and where "last week" was a saveable answer. It is the
  **second** time that map has silently dropped a field: the first was `assignedBranch` naming a
  control the renderer had no case for, which turned the branch picker into free text. A hand-kept
  list of which columns are dates, sitting beside a renderer that already knows, goes stale — so the
  control is now **derived** from `DATE_FILTER_COLUMNS`, the application's own answer to which lead
  columns hold a day, and any date column the popup draws gets a calendar without being listed.
  `dateOfBirthOrEdod` came out of the map for the same reason: it was the duplicate that made the
  other one's absence look deliberate. The map still wins where a column needs something its type
  would not give it. The popup's existing orphan-control test now covers the derived `"date"` case
  too, which it would otherwise have missed entirely.
  ([CRMPage.js](../../frontend/src/components/sales/CRMPage.js))
- **A candidate's attachments were uploaded and then thrown away.** `submitPublicInvite` stored every
  attachment in R2 and handed the rows to `prepareValues`, which named no key for them — so the
  files existed in the bucket and on no record, and the column was the one thing this module read
  back without ever writing. Now written, and pinned by a test.
- **Scheduling a next round on an untouched form erased the interview already in the diary.** The
  dialog opens on empty date and time by design; saving without filling them appended a round saying
  nothing, advanced the count to Round 2, and — because the flat interview columns follow the latest
  round — blanked the date, time, mode and Meet link the register's table, the linked sheets and the
  CSV importer all read. The dialog now asks for the day, and the server no longer writes those
  columns from an empty round, since a round can arrive from a caller that is not this dialog.
- **The onboarding-invite entries were offered to recruiters the server refuses.** Every one of those
  endpoints answers to `HRM / Employee Management`, but the row menu gated them on this page's own
  `HRM / Interview` write — so a recruiter holding Interview and not Employee Management was shown
  **Send Onboarding Invite**, got a 403 on create, and the candidate never moved to Onboarded. All
  four entries now read that permission, and the invite list is not even fetched without its READ.
- **Cancel Invite was offered on invites the server will not cancel.** "Live" meant only "not
  Cancelled", so a **Submitted** or **Expired** onboarding invite still showed the entry, which
  `cancelInvite` refuses with a 400. It now names the two statuses that are actually live, which are
  the same two the screening form's own rule names.

### Security
- **The dependency audit is clear of everything but the three major upgrades.** `npm audit` had
  seventeen open findings on the backend and five on the frontend, one of them critical: the
  Next.js the app was pinned to had a middleware bypass and a denial of service in the App Router.
  Next moves to 16.3.6 (with `eslint-config-next` to match, both still pinned exactly), and the
  in-range fixes are taken on both sides: `multer` 2.4, `nodemailer` 9.1, `sharp` 0.35.4, `bullmq`
  5.81 and the transitive `js-yaml`, `qs`, `nanoid` and `postcss`. The frontend audit is at zero.
  The backend keeps eleven, all behind major versions of `prisma`, `puppeteer` and `googleapis`,
  each of which is its own change. Lint, both test suites and a production build were run;
  `eslint-config-next` 16.3 brings a new `react-hooks/set-state-in-effect` rule that warns on
  38 existing lines, none of them errors.
- **Signing in, finishing a password reset and refreshing a session are rate limited, and the
  limiter reads the caller's address from where nginx writes it.** `/login` took passwords as fast
  as the server could check them, and `/reset-password` and `/refresh` took tokens the same way,
  while only the forgot-password and OTP routes were throttled. All three now sit behind the same
  sliding-window limiter on the reset window. Sign-in counts **failures** per account from one
  address (ten) and from anywhere (thirty) - a correct sign-in spends nothing, and junk requests at
  somebody's address from elsewhere fill the sender's bucket, not the owner's - and every request
  per address (a hundred). Finishing a reset is ten per token, refreshing thirty per token and
  three hundred per address; tokens are keyed by a hash, never stored raw. A refusal is a 429 with
  `Retry-After`. With `TRUST_PROXY_HEADER=true` the limiter used to read the **first**
  `X-Forwarded-For` entry, which the caller writes, so a caller could mint a fresh address per
  request; it now reads the entry nginx appended at the end (`TRUST_PROXY_HOPS`, default 1, for a
  second proxy of ours). New env vars `LOGIN_MAX_PER_ACCOUNT_WINDOW`,
  `LOGIN_MAX_PER_ACCOUNT_ANYWHERE_WINDOW`, `LOGIN_MAX_PER_IP_WINDOW`,
  `AUTH_REFRESH_MAX_PER_IP_WINDOW` and `TRUST_PROXY_HOPS` tune it; the defaults apply where they
  are unset, so nothing needs setting on the VPS before the deploy
  ([authRoutes.js](../../backend/src/routes/authRoutes.js),
  [rateLimit.js](../../backend/src/middleware/rateLimit.js)).
- **The public application form could put any file type on the studio's R2 domain.** The declared
  MIME went to the bucket as the object's `Content-Type` unchecked, and the stored extension came
  from the candidate's own file name — so a submission could serve `text/html` or `image/svg+xml`
  from the public bucket and have it run as a page rather than download as a document.
  `extensionsByMimeType` is now the allowlist as well as the extension table: a type it does not
  name is refused, and the extension is derived from the **type** rather than the file name.
  Enforced for every caller, because an authenticated screen has no reason to store an unnamed type
  either. **And the public form no longer accepts a link in place of a file** — `storeDocument`
  returns an `http(s)` url untouched, which is right for an authenticated edit echoing back its own
  files and wrong where whoever holds the invite link would choose what the studio's "Résumé" link
  points at; that path now passes `trustUrls: false`.

### Added
- **One seed that may be run on production, and it can only ever add.**
  `npm run seed:permissions` ([production-permissions.seed.js](../../backend/prisma/seeds/production-permissions.seed.js))
  creates the permissions `permission-catalogue.js` describes and refreshes their display names and
  descriptions. That is the whole of it. It **cannot retire, deactivate or delete** anything — the
  sweep in the full seeder, which turns off every active permission the catalogue does not name, is
  deliberately absent, because on production such a permission is far more likely to have been
  deployed from a branch that has not merged yet than to be one the studio wants taken away. It never
  writes `isActive`, so a permission an administrator turned off stays off across re-runs. It touches
  no role, grant, permission scope, user, branch or master data, so a new permission is still held by
  nobody but a Super Admin until a role is ticked in Role Management and re-saved.
  `-- --dry-run` reports what it would create, what it would refresh, and which permissions the
  database holds that the catalogue does not describe — which it lists and then leaves alone. Both
  modes read through the same comparison, so a dry run is a rehearsal of the real thing rather than a
  different code path. This does **not** replace the migration a permission shipping with code needs
  (IAM.md step 10): it is for repairing drift, or catching production up with a catalogue that has
  moved ahead of it, without handing it `prisma/seed.js`, whose blast radius is every seeder in the
  repository.
- **A new permission, `PROJECT / Show All Projects`, so a project can be worked by somebody it is not
  staffed to.** Editing a project belongs to the people staffed to it and deleting it to its Project
  Manager — right for a project with people on it, and a gap on one without: a **Yet to Start project
  is staffed to nobody**, because those seats are filled in on the Start Project form rather than the
  booking form, so a freshly booked project could be started only by a branch administrator. Granting
  `ADMIN / Branch Administration` to fix that also hands over assets, leads, cash, payment requests
  and tasks for those branches. This is the projects-only version of that trust. **Read** stops the
  register narrowing to the projects you are named on and offers nothing else — the row menu is View
  alone. **Read / Write** edits, starts and deletes any project it reaches.
  **It is scoped by the role's branches, never "all".** A role naming some branches sees those
  branches' projects; naming every branch sees them all, because every branch is in the list; naming
  **none** sees nothing, which is the direction an empty list has to fail in — read as "no
  narrowing" it would make the weakest grant in the system the widest. A Super Admin is confined to
  no branch and sees everything. It reads `reachableBranchIds`, the same answer the data scope and
  the branch pickers use, so "which branches" has one meaning in the application. A project whose
  free-text `source_branch` matched no branch carries `branch_id = NULL`, belongs to no branch and is
  reached only by whoever is named on it.
  This deliberately revisits a retired decision: `PROJECT / All Projects` was removed for being a
  second answer to "whose records?", and IAM.md now records why the branch-scoped replacement is not
  the same mistake. On the twelve per-project plan routes it stands beside `PROJECT / Projects`; the
  studio's own vocabulary — service and deliverable definitions, option sets — stays on
  `PROJECT / Projects` alone, because somebody trusted with every project is not thereby trusted to
  rewrite the lists every project is planned from.
  **It ships in a migration, not the seed**
  ([20260919120000_project_show_all_permission](../../backend/prisma/migrations/20260919120000_project_show_all_permission/migration.sql)),
  and no grants are copied — after it runs, only a Super Admin holds it until the roles that should
  have it are ticked in Role Management and re-saved. Covered by a suite that writes real branches,
  roles, role-branches, logins and projects and asserts the rows each one actually gets back
  ([project-show-all-rows.test.js](../../backend/test/project-show-all-rows.test.js)).
- **A candidate who has already sent their application gets their own form back, not a new one.**
  The record panel's footer offered **Send Screening Form** whatever the candidate had done — so the
  one person who had already answered was offered the one thing nobody wants. An application that
  came back with something missing left the register two answers, both bad: ask for the whole thing
  again on a new link, which throws away everything they typed, or correct it on their behalf, which
  puts HR's words in the candidate's mouth. A submitted form now shows **Resend Form** in that
  button's place — a replacement, never both — which reopens **their own submission on their own
  link** so the form comes back to them with every answer still filled in, to correct and resubmit.
  It is the Employee Repository's **Send Back** said for a candidate, down to the confirmation that
  shows the message to hand them *before* anything is sent, with its own Copy
  ([ResendApplicationFormDialog](../../frontend/src/components/hrm/ResendApplicationFormDialog.js)).
  Whether they have answered is read off their latest form rather than off their stage, because a
  stage is not a form. `POST /api/hrm/interview/candidates/:id/resend-form` does the work, behind
  `HRM / Interview` Read / Write like every other write on that router: the newest Submitted invite
  goes back to `Verified` with `submitted_at` cleared so the next submission is accepted, its expiry
  is extended to at least a week out and **never shortened** — a form reopened after its deadline
  would otherwise be a link that refuses the candidate the moment they open it — and the candidate
  moves back to Form Submission Pending **only from Form Submitted**, so anybody since scheduled,
  put on hold or selected keeps the stage somebody moved them to. A form raised before short codes
  is refused rather than quietly reopened: its token is stored hashed, so there is no link left to
  send and the answer is a new screening form. They are emailed a **different template** from the
  invite, for the reason the onboarding send-back has its own — "complete your application" a second
  time reads as a form that swallowed what they typed — and a refused email undoes none of it: the
  form is open either way, so the screen finishes as it would on a success and says in a notice that
  the message did not go, which whoever is sending it by hand already holds. No migration and no new
  permission. ([invites.js](../../backend/src/modules/hrm/interview/invites.js),
  [InterviewCandidateFormPanel.js](../../frontend/src/components/hrm/InterviewCandidateFormPanel.js))
- **A candidate can be put on hold from the row, the way they can already be rejected.** On Hold and
  Rejected are the two stages a candidate stops at, and only one of them could be reached from the
  register: somebody the studio wanted to keep but could not place yet had to be opened, edited and
  saved to say so, while turning the same person down was one press in the row ⋮. The easier answer
  was the harsher one. **Put On Hold** now sits beside **Reject** in every candidate's menu, at every
  stage and in both shapes of the menu — the one a candidate waiting on a screening form gets and
  the plain one — with the gentler of the pair first and both above the destructive entry (Delete,
  or Cancel Form while waiting). Each moves the stage through the same status route with an
  **Undo** that restores the stage it came from, rather than a confirmation asked before anything
  has happened. Each is withheld only from the row it would be a no-op on: Reject from one already
  Rejected, Put On Hold from one already On Hold. A rejected candidate is still offered Put On Hold,
  because reconsidering somebody is a thing the studio does. Frontend only — On Hold has been a
  stage in the catalogue, the tab row and the record's own status list all along, and the status
  route already accepts it
  ([InterviewPage.js](../../frontend/src/components/hrm/InterviewPage.js)).
- **The studio calendar draws the interviews HR has scheduled, and the source switch offers them.**
  A candidate coming in on Thursday is as much the studio's day as a shoot is, and it was nowhere:
  it lived in the Interview register and everybody else found out when somebody walked through the
  door. **Interviews** now sits in the switch beside the Calendar heading, between Meetings and
  Employee Attendance. Choosing it draws the scheduled interviews as a month — violet chips leading
  with the hour the candidate is coming in, the candidate's name beside it, and the mode and branch
  under that — and, in List view, as a table of its own: Candidate, Candidate ID, Position,
  Interview Date, Time, Mode, Interviewer, Branch and Status, sortable, filterable and exportable
  like every other source. They are in **All** too, unlike Employee Attendance: an interview is one
  booking on one day rather than a row per person per day, so it belongs in the view that promises
  the whole diary. Clicking one opens it in the panel beside the grid — when, the position, the
  stage, the interviewer, the phone and email, and a **Join Meet** button for an online interview —
  rather than following it to the register, which has no link to a single candidate. Read-only: a
  date is still set in HRM → Interview.
  [schedule/calendar/service.js](../../backend/src/modules/schedule/calendar/service.js) gains the
  source, guarded by **`HRM / Interview` Read** — the same permission the register's own router
  mounts, so a viewer who may not open the register is never sent an interview to draw, and one who
  may is never offered a narrower view of it than the register gives. No new permission and no
  migration: `HRM / Interview` is already in the catalogue, so the roles that hold it — Super Admin
  and whoever has the Interview screen — reach this the moment it ships, with nothing to re-save.
  An interview is a **day**, never an instant: `interview_date` and `interview_time` are text
  columns filled by hand, by a CSV or by a linked sheet, so the day is read with the CRM's own
  `toIsoDay` and the hour travels as the text the register shows, with a 24-hour twin beside it for
  the Time column to sort on. Sending an instant would move a 10:30 am interview into the previous
  evening for anybody far enough west, and sorting the printed text would file "03:00 pm" above
  "10:00 am".
  ([meetingSources.js](../../frontend/src/lib/meetingSources.js),
  [schedule.js](../../frontend/src/lib/schedule.js),
  [MeetingsPage.js](../../frontend/src/components/sales/MeetingsPage.js))
- **A candidate can attach more than their résumé.** The form took exactly one file, so a
  certificate, an experience letter, an award or a scanned portfolio either went into the portfolio
  link as a Drive folder or was emailed separately and lost from the record. **Other Attachments**
  sits beside Résumé and takes up to five files of the same kinds. They go to Cloudflare R2 through
  the same shared helper the résumé uses, uploaded before the transaction opens so a slow one never
  holds it, and are stored **as they are** — the 5 MB-per-file cap is the guard rather than
  re-encoding, because compressing a PDF or a scanned certificate damages the one thing it is being
  read for; browser-side resampling belongs to the image fields. Only the file's name and its
  returned URL are kept, in a new `attachments` JSON column
  ([20260918200000_interview_candidate_attachments](../../backend/prisma/migrations/20260918200000_interview_candidate_attachments/migration.sql),
  one nullable column, guarded so it can be replayed). HR reads them on the candidate's record and
  in the panel beside the table, each by the name the candidate's own file carried, through
  [interviewInfoFormatters](../../frontend/src/components/hrm/interviewInfoFormatters.js). A
  re-submission that attaches nothing leaves the files already on the record alone. `resume_url` is
  untouched and goes on holding the résumé on its own.
- **The application form asks what a candidate would least accept, and takes up to three links.**
  Two new columns on `hrm_interview_candidate`, in
  [20260918190000_interview_min_salary_and_links](../../backend/prisma/migrations/20260918190000_interview_min_salary_and_links/migration.sql).
  **Least Expected Salary** (`min_expected_ctc`) sits beside Expected Salary and is asked first: the
  ask is what a candidate opens with, the floor is what decides whether a conversation is worth
  having at all, and HR was learning it on a call. It is asked of everybody, freshers included, and
  is on the register's table, its record and the CSV importer. **Portfolio is now up to three links**
  (`portfolio_links`, a JSON array) — one box with a plus beside it that grows a box at a time to
  three, because a photographer applies with a portfolio site, an Instagram and a Drive folder and
  the other two used to go into Remarks. `portfolio_url` keeps its meaning as the **first** of them
  so the register's table column, the linked Google Sheets and the CSV importer go on reading the
  single link they always have. The migration adds two nullable columns and is guarded against
  `information_schema`, so it can be replayed safely. New shared control:
  [RepeatableTextList](../../frontend/src/components/shared/RepeatableTextList.js), for a short capped
  list of one plain value — `RepeatableDetailsField`'s framed card per row is right for a
  qualification and far too much furniture for a URL. No permission changes: these are columns on a
  table that already answers to `HRM / Interview`.

### Changed
- **The Schedule Interview form stops asking for a Last Follow-up date, and the register stamps it
  itself.** The box asked HR to tell the register something the register already knew — and worse, it
  had to be *remembered*: the column means "the day somebody last worked this candidate", so one
  nobody updated said the opposite of the truth rather than nothing at all. It is gone from all three
  modes of the dialog (Schedule, Schedule Next Round, Edit Schedule), which draw one shared field
  list. The server now writes it from the act itself — saving a candidate **is** working them — in
  `updateCandidate`, so the record, the dialog and the row menu's stage moves all stamp it, and it is
  written **last**, over anything posted, so an older tab cannot backdate when somebody was worked.
  Deliberately **not** in `prepareValues`: three other things write a candidate through that and none
  of them is somebody following anybody up — the candidate's own submitted form, the CSV import and a
  linked sheet — and stamping there would put today's date on every row in an imported file and read
  afterwards as a day HR worked them all. That is the same line the CRM board already draws for its
  own Last Followup Date, where a posted remark does not stamp it. The column is still **shown** on
  the register's table and the info panel, since reading when somebody was last worked is the whole
  use of it, and its value still travels with a save because `PUT /candidates/:id` is not partial —
  dropping the key would blank the column on the first save. **Next Follow-up stays a box**: the last
  one is a fact the application can observe, the next one is a decision only a person can make.
  ([interview/service.js](../../backend/src/modules/hrm/interview/service.js),
  [interviewConfig.js](../../frontend/src/constants/interviewConfig.js))
- **The screening form asks where a candidate is from and where they are now, instead of a state and
  a postal address.** A **State** was too coarse to answer what a studio actually wants to know about
  somebody it is considering — can they be on the floor on Saturday, and would the job mean moving —
  and a full **Address** is more than a studio should hold about a person it has not hired, besides
  being the kind of question that makes a candidate close the tab. **Native Place** and **Current
  Location** replace both, as two ordinary one-line boxes; City is untouched, being a different
  question from either. Two new nullable columns
  ([20260919140000_interview_native_and_current_location](../../backend/prisma/migrations/20260919140000_interview_native_and_current_location/migration.sql),
  guarded so it can be replayed). `state` and `address` come out of the form, the schema, the write
  **and** the prefill — all four, because the submission is applied partially, so a key the form
  stops asking about but keeps sending arrives empty and writes over what is on the record. The
  **columns stay**: a linked sheet and the CSV import still fill them, and HR still reads and
  corrects them on the record, because "the form no longer asks" is not "throw away what was already
  collected". The sheet importer's headings moved with them — `Current Location`, `Native Place` and
  `Hometown` used to fold onto **City** for want of anywhere else to go, so a sheet naming both a
  hometown and a current location had one silently overwrite the other; each now goes to the field it
  names. The register's table, info panel, record form and CSV template carry both new fields.
- **Skills and Languages Known say *(comma separated)* in the label, not only in the placeholder.**
  A placeholder is gone the moment somebody types, so the one instruction that decides whether the
  answer is readable afterwards disappeared exactly when it started mattering — and what came back
  was "Photography and a bit of Lightroom, also Premiere", which no column can be filtered or counted
  on. The example stays in the placeholder; the rule is now in the label, where it is still on screen
  while the box is being filled in.
  ([interviewApplicationConfig.js](../../frontend/src/constants/interviewApplicationConfig.js))
- **Written down: the seed is never run on production.** `prisma/seed.js` runs *every* seeder, and
  most of them write rather than only fill gaps — option lists, catalogues, master data. On a live
  database that is not "the new rows appear", it is every one of those tables being rewritten to
  whatever the repository currently says, silently undoing anything the studio has changed in
  Settings since. A change that needs a row in production carries it in a **migration**
  (`INSERT … WHERE NOT EXISTS`); where a seed genuinely is the only way, it goes in a **file of its
  own** that can be run on its own, with the changelog entry saying which command and what it
  touches. Production is never handed a script whose blast radius is "whatever else is in the seed".
  Added to CLAUDE.md's Hard Constraints, beside the rule about `prisma migrate dev` on the VPS.
- **A project belongs to the people working to it: they can change it, and only its Project Manager
  can delete it.** Editing a project's booking details and deleting it both answered to
  `SALES / Sales Manager` `READ_WRITE` — the permission that *books* a project. So the CRM who typed
  a booking could go on rewriting it long after the studio had begun working to it, and could delete
  it outright; making a project and unmaking one were the same act. They are now two questions.
  **Changing** a project is for anybody it is staffed to — its **Project Manager**, **Pre Production
  Coordinator**, **Creative Manager** or **Post Production Coordinator** — because each of them is
  working to it and each has reason to correct what it says. **Deleting** it is for its **Project
  Manager** alone: a coordinator runs a stage of the work, and removing the project out from under
  everybody else working to it is not a stage of anything. A holder of `ADMIN / Branch
  Administration` passes either, which a Super Admin holds along with everything else, so nothing
  names a role — a role test inside a controller sits below the route's guard and can be granted to
  nobody. The two rules read one list of staffed seats, delete taking the first entry of it, so they
  cannot drift apart about which column names the manager. Neither has a **Yet to Start escape**: a
  project that has not started is staffed to nobody, because those fields are asked for on the Start
  Project form and not the booking form, so until somebody is named the branch administrator is the
  only answer. **The plan is unchanged** — shoots, allocations and the start-project pair stay the
  Project Manager's, since a coordinator works a stage of a plan rather than re-planning it. The
  permission layer widened to the sale's write **or** `PROJECT / Projects`' write, because a
  coordinator works the plan and need not sell at all. On screen the Edit and Delete entries are
  **absent** rather than refused, on Project Management's Projects **and** on Sales Manager — one
  endpoint serves both, and tightening one would have left the other offering an Edit the server
  turns away. An owner who may also plan gets a separate **Edit Sale Details** entry, because the
  plan form shows the booking read-only: without it the people who may now change a booking would
  have had no way to reach one. What this costs is deliberate: the CRM who books a sale can no
  longer correct it afterwards. Booking a new project is untouched, and so is **Duplicate**, which
  books a second one. Also fixed a latent fail-open beside it — the row menu read
  `canEditSale ?? true`, so a screen that forgot the prop was handed the sale's actions; a missing
  permission answer now reads as no. ([projects/routes.js](../../backend/src/modules/sales/projects/routes.js),
  [ProjectDataTable.js](../../frontend/src/components/data-table/ProjectDataTable.js))
- **The leads board's switch is now "My Leads", and it reads the way it is asked for.** It was
  labelled *All leads*, on by default, and turning it **off** was how somebody asked for their own
  queue — so the thing people actually reach for was the negative of the control's name. It is now
  **My Leads**: on shows only the leads assigned to the reader or created by them, off shows
  everything they may see. Nothing about what it fetches has changed — the server is still asked the
  same `mine` question, still ANDed with the data scope so it can only ever take rows away, still
  reaching the rows, the tab counts, each column filter's menu and the ids a select-all resolves
  into. `showingAllLeads` stays the single place the control's sense is turned back into the query's,
  so no call site inverts it twice. Remembered under a **new key**, `crm:my-leads`, defaulting to
  off: the old `crm:all-leads` stored `true` for "showing everything", so keeping that key and
  flipping its meaning would have read every stored answer as "My Leads on" and emptied the board of
  the team's leads for everybody the first time they opened it.
  ([CRMPage.js](../../frontend/src/components/sales/CRMPage.js))
- **A linked Google Sheet only ever adds now — it never rewrites a record already here.** Every
  sync re-read the whole sheet and *refreshed* any row whose lead or candidate it could match, so
  the spreadsheet overruled the app every two minutes: a status set, an interviewer put on a
  candidate, a phone number corrected or a remark written were silently undone on the next pass,
  and the record read as though nobody had touched it. A matched row is now **skipped and counted**
  (`skippedExisting`), and the only rows a pass writes are ones it has never successfully written
  before — genuinely new rows, and rows that were rejected or failed, which are never recorded as
  imported and so are retried on their own until they go in or are corrected in the sheet. Applies
  to both linked integrations: CRM Leads
  ([googleSheetsService.js](../../backend/src/modules/sales/crm-leads/googleSheetsService.js)) and HRM
  Interview ([sheetSyncService.js](../../backend/src/modules/hrm/interview/sheetSyncService.js)).
  Deletion is held twice over rather than once: the row ledger keys a row by its source id, which
  for a sheet with no id column of its own is its *row number*, so inserting a row in the
  spreadsheet shifted every row below it onto a key the ledger had never seen and a deleted record
  came back — the deleted records are now matched directly too, on phone, email or source row id,
  which recognises them wherever their row has moved to. If either check cannot be read the pass
  withholds every new row rather than refill a board just cleared. Settings → Integrations names
  the held-back rows in the sync summary ("12 skipped (12 already here)") and the import preview
  labels them **Existing … (skipped)**, so a pass that writes nothing still says why. Because a
  sync can no longer update, it can no longer back-fill an owner either: only a created row spends
  an assignment-rule share. The **older whole-sheet refresh** obeys the same rule now as well
  (`refreshCrmLeadsFromSheets`): it predates the link sync and wrote every row of every active sheet
  unconditionally, and it is reached not only by `POST /api/sales/crm-leads/refresh` but by the CRM
  Leads list itself whenever the lead table comes back empty — so deleting every lead and opening
  the page wrote the whole spreadsheet back with `deleted_at` cleared. Covered by
  [sheet-sync-add-only.test.js](../../backend/test/sheet-sync-add-only.test.js).

- **The scheduled interview's Meet link is readable on the calendar, not only pressable.** The
  details panel already led with **Join Meet** and the row's ⋮ already offered it, both opening in a
  new tab; the link itself was nowhere on screen, so "send me the link" meant leaving the diary and
  opening the candidate's record in another module. It now reads under the button as the link it is
  — selectable, copyable, and opening in a new tab like every other stored link — beside the
  Candidate ID, Position, Status, Date, Time, Mode & Branch, Department, Interviewer, Phone and
  Email the panel already carried. Only an online interview has one: an In Person interview is a
  room, not a link.
- **Fixed: raising an onboarding invite from the Interview register threw away the hand-over
  screen.** Creating the invite closed the dialog immediately, so the screen that follows it — the
  link, and the studio's own wording from Settings → Employee Invite Template to paste into
  WhatsApp — appeared and vanished, leaving somebody holding an invite they had no way to send. The
  dialog now stays open the way it does in the Employee Repository; the candidate's stage moves to
  Onboarded behind it, and the reader closes it when they are done.
- **A row is only offered the answers its stage can actually give.** The ⋮ carried every entry the
  module has on every row, whatever stage it was at — so a candidate nobody had spoken to could be
  marked Selected, and one whose screening form had not come back could have a round booked against
  answers nobody had read. **Mark Selected** now appears once somebody has been seen: from Interview
  Scheduled onward, and on the two stops, since reconsidering a candidate parked or turned down is a
  thing the studio does. It is gone from **Yet to Connect**, **Form Submission Pending** and **Form
  Submitted**, where "Selected" is an answer that does not exist yet. **Schedule Interview** and
  **Edit Schedule** are gone from Form Submission Pending for the same reason one stage earlier: a
  conversation goes in the diary after the form has been read, not before it has arrived. Both come
  back the moment the form does, so that row is left with the waiting itself — hand them the link
  again, correct what the form was raised on, park them, turn them down, or call the form off.
- **View and Edit read together at the top of every row menu.** They are the two things somebody
  does to the *candidate* rather than to their hiring, and they had drifted apart as each stage grew
  its own menu — Edit was second at one stage, fourth at another, and below an invite's three
  entries at a third. There is one Edit now, hoisted beside View, and everything under it is about
  the hiring itself in the order that stage's work happens. It is still withheld in the two cases it
  always has been and no others: a row **waiting on its screening form**, which is about that form
  rather than the record behind it, and one at **Yet to Connect**, which holds nothing anybody has
  learned yet.
- **An Onboarded candidate's row is about the invite that took them on.** Copy Link, Edit Invite and
  Cancel Invite — the same three questions a live screening form gets asked, about the onboarding
  one — then View, Edit, Reject and Delete. What it stops offering is everything about deciding:
  there is no next round to schedule after somebody has been taken on, no stage to mark them at and
  nothing to put on hold, which is what those rows were still showing. Reject stays, because a
  studio can still be told no after the invite has gone. The three invite entries go together when
  there is nothing live to act on — a candidate moved to this stage by hand or by a sheet — rather
  than sitting there refusing, and Cancel Invite asks first, since a cancelled link cannot be
  revived. The invite is matched to the candidate on **email first, then phone**: the two records
  have no id between them, because an onboarding invite is the Employee Repository's, raised from
  here but never owned by here.
- **A Selected candidate's row is about taking them on.** Every other stage is somewhere in a
  conversation; this one is finished, so the menu is the five things left to do about the person —
  **Send Onboarding Invite**, View, Edit, Reject, Delete — and nothing else. A row that still
  offered a screening form to somebody who has been given the job, or a round to schedule after the
  answer, read as a register that had not noticed it had decided. **Send Onboarding Invite opens the
  Employee Repository's own invite dialog**, not a second one of this module's: an onboarding invite
  is the repository's record and always has been, and two screens raising the same invite would be
  two places to fix when it changes. It opens **prefilled** with the candidate's name, email, number
  and the position they applied for — four answers the register has held since the application
  arrived, and retyping them is how a number gets a digit wrong between two screens that both know
  it. Once the invite exists the candidate **moves to Onboarded** and leaves the Selected tab for
  it, with a note saying why, so the stage does not appear to have changed on its own. If that move
  fails the message names the stage rather than the invite: the invite was raised either way, and
  saying otherwise would have somebody send a second one.
- **Selected, On Hold and Rejected now ask why, and Mark Selected is on the row.** The three
  decisions the register exists to reach were a single click that changed the stage and recorded
  nothing about it, so weeks later nobody could say why a candidate was parked, and "we already
  spoke to them and said no" was a thing the register could not tell you. Each opens a dialog asking
  for a remark, kept in a new `status_notes` JSON column
  ([20260918230000_interview_status_notes](../../backend/prisma/migrations/20260918230000_interview_status_notes/migration.sql))
  as one entry per decision — **appended**, never written over the last, because a candidate held in
  March and rejected in May has two reasons and neither explains the other. It is apart from
  `remarks`, which stays HR's running note on the person, and the record reads them back under a
  **Decisions** heading, newest first. **Mark Selected** joins the menu: the answer the whole
  register is worked towards was the one stage the row could not set. The remark is optional — a
  required box on a decision already made is a box that gets "ok" typed into it — and a blank one
  records no entry at all. This replaces the **Undo** those entries carried: Undo stood in for
  deliberation while there was no question to ask, and a dialog somebody opened and typed a reason
  into is already the deliberate act.
- **A new stage, Onboarded, after Selected.** An offer made and an offer taken are different
  answers, and the gap between them is where hiring most often goes wrong — somebody offered a job
  has not necessarily accepted it, and a register that stopped at Selected could not tell an offer
  still out from one already turned up for. That gap was being tracked in Remarks or nowhere. It is
  a tab, a colour (a deeper green than Selected, so the pair reads as a progression), and a stage a
  linked sheet can write: "onboarded", "onboard" and "onboarding" all fold onto it. **"Joined" is
  deliberately still left as typed** — the map has always refused to fold that word, and quietly
  changing it would move rows a studio had put somewhere on purpose; worth a decision rather than an
  assumption. Add Application does not offer the stage, for the reason it does not offer Interview
  Scheduled or Rejected: none of them can be true of an application being written down as it arrives.
- **A second conversation is a round now, not a correction of the first.** The register held a
  single interview — one date, time, mode and Meet link — so a studio that sees somebody twice had
  nowhere to put the second: scheduling it **overwrote the first**, and with it the only record that
  the candidate had already been in. Nobody could say whether a Thursday conversation was a first
  meeting or a third. The rounds are kept in a new `interview_rounds` JSON column
  ([20260918220000_interview_rounds](../../backend/prisma/migrations/20260918220000_interview_rounds/migration.sql)),
  and **the four flat columns stay as the latest round**, so the register's table, the linked Google
  Sheets and the CSV importer go on reading the one interview they always have. The row's ⋮ now says
  what the row is actually asking for: **Schedule Interview** for a candidate nobody has called,
  **Schedule Next Round** for one who has already been in, and **Edit Schedule** beside it — which
  appears only where there is something to correct. A next round opens on empty date and time
  fields, since the last round's are a conversation that already happened and leaving them there
  invites a second round booked for the first one's date. **Which round a candidate is on is a
  column**, shown by default beside the interview date and reading "Round 2" rather than a bare
  count. It is **derived** from the rounds rather than stored beside them, so a count and the list
  behind it cannot drift apart; a candidate scheduled before this existed reads as Round 1, which is
  what they are.
- **The three interview modes read apart, and a Meet is joined from the row.** In Person, Google
  Meet and Phone now carry their own colours in the Mode column, on the same dot the stage beside
  them uses — the mode decides what somebody has to do about the appointment (a room, a link, a
  number), which is worth seeing down a column rather than reading word by word. The three are
  deliberately clear of the stage colours, since the two columns sit near each other. **Meet Link is
  a column now**, beside the mode that explains it: a Google Meet interview lived only on the
  record, so joining a call meant opening the candidate first. It opens in a **new tab**, with
  `noopener noreferrer`, and stops the row's own click, so following it never navigates the register
  away or opens the info panel underneath — and on the record it is a **Join the meeting** control
  rather than 40 characters of URL to select and paste. Mode and Meet Link are both shown by
  default, since a colour nobody has turned on gives no reading; Manage Columns still hides either.
- **Interview Time is a clock, not a box asking for "e.g. 10:30 am".** A typed-out clock face could
  not be sorted on, and "10.30", "10:30AM" and "half ten" were all the same appointment written
  three ways. It is now `TimeField` in the new Schedule Interview dialog — **the shared control every
  other time in the application already uses**, from the meeting dialogs to the attendance request,
  so the studio meets one kind of time box rather than one per screen. What a linked sheet or a CSV
  already wrote is converted on the way in — including the case the "+12" rule gets backwards, where
  12am is midnight and 12pm is noon — and anything no rule reads is kept **as it stands** rather than
  blanked, so a save carries it back instead of writing an empty over it.
- **Scheduling a candidate is its own dialog now, off the row's ⋮.** The stage, the interview date,
  time, mode and Meet link, the two follow-ups and HR's remarks were the last section of the
  candidate's record, which made one screen two jobs — reading back what somebody submitted, and
  deciding what the studio does about them. Different errands, at different moments, and the stage
  (the one field anybody opens a candidate to change) sat below four sections of the candidate's own
  answers. **Schedule Interview** is offered on every row, in both menus, and the record beside it is
  now the candidate's application and nothing else. Both screens still send the whole candidate,
  because `PUT /candidates/:id` is not partial: a body carrying only an interview date would blank
  everything else.
- **The record asks fresher-or-experienced the way the form does, already answered.** The same
  dropdown, read back off what the candidate submitted — rows mean experienced, "Fresher" in the
  Experience column means fresher, anything else leaves it unanswered — and the same four questions
  that presuppose a job (Current Company, Current Designation, Current Salary, Notice Period) hidden
  behind it. Hidden, never blanked: the save writes Experience the way the form does, but keeps the
  column's own text where there are no rows, so a linked sheet's "2-3 yrs" is not destroyed by a
  screen that had nothing to put in its place.
- **The résumé and the attachments are documents on the record, not addresses.** Résumé was a URL in
  a text box, which is the one thing nobody wants from it — a résumé is read, not proof-read. It now
  opens as a document, with the box kept underneath, because this record is also where a row
  imported with the link in the wrong column is put right. Attachments open the same way, through
  the same formatter, so the two read alike.
- **A candidate's record now reads back the application they filled in.** The two had drifted into
  different forms of the same interview: the application asked for education as rows with an
  institution and a year, the record as a one-line "Highest Qualification"; the application banded
  the notice period, the record took free text; the application collected three links, the record
  showed one. Worse, **half of what a candidate submitted could not be seen here at all** — their
  education rows, their work history and their attachments were stored and shown on no screen, so
  nobody could read or correct them. The record's sections, their order and their questions now
  mirror the application form, with the studio's own columns folded into the matching section and
  **Screening** last, since the stage, the interview and HR's remarks are the studio's answers about
  a candidate rather than the candidate's about themselves. Two controls differ deliberately: Notice
  Period and When Can You Join are **comboboxes** offering the form's own answers rather than closed
  lists, because this record has to survive a value a linked sheet or a CSV put there and a closed
  list would show it back as empty. Attachments are read-only — HR replaces a file by asking for a
  new form, not by uploading on the candidate's behalf.

### Fixed
- **Saving a candidate's record erased when they could join.** `availableFrom` is free text and
  always has been — the application writes "Immediate" into it, a linked sheet writes "15 days", a
  CSV writes a day — but the record read it through a date helper that returns nothing for anything
  but `YYYY-MM-DD`. So opening such a candidate showed an empty box, and because an update writes
  every column it is given a key for, pressing Save wrote that blank over their answer. It is now
  kept as stored, and the field beside it shows all three.

### Changed
- **The application form ends with two questions somebody can answer, instead of one that nobody
  could.** "Anything you would like us to know" came back the three ways a shapeless question does:
  empty, holding a covering letter, or repeating a phone number asked for three fields above — a
  column of those answers taught nobody anything, because no two of them answered the same thing.
  In its place: **How do you know us?** and **Anything about us?**, the second being the cheapest
  read there is on whether somebody is applying to this studio or to forty of them. Two new columns
  ([20260918210000_interview_candidate_about_us](../../backend/prisma/migrations/20260918210000_interview_candidate_about_us/migration.sql)),
  and HR reads both on the record under their own **In Their Words** heading. **How do you know us?
  is deliberately not the `source` column**: that one is the studio's own answer to where a row came
  from, set by a linked sheet's rules and filtered on in the register, so letting a candidate write
  into it would overwrite what the import established — the same fault the form avoided by no longer
  sending `department`. **`remarks` is now out of the submission entirely**, off the form and out of
  its schema, so a candidate reopening their link can no longer overwrite HR's own note on them.
- **The candidate's application form now asks when they can join as a yes-or-no, and bands the
  notice period.** **"Are you an immediate joiner?"** is asked of everybody, and only a **No** is
  then asked for an **Expected Joining Date**, which cannot be in the past. Neither is a new column:
  When Can You Join is free text precisely because the answer is as often a word as a day, so a Yes
  is stored as "Immediate", a No stores the date, and a link opened again reads the answer back off
  that same column. **Notice Period is a dropdown** — Immediate, Under 15 days, Under 30 days, Under
  45 days, 60 days or more — where it was a box in which "1 month", "30 days", "one month notice"
  and "30days" were one answer typed four ways that nothing could sort or filter. **Total Experience
  moved up beside the fresher/experienced question** instead of sitting under the work-history rows:
  "Experienced" and "4.5 years" are one sentence, and below the full-width list it read as a
  question somebody had forgotten to answer. Both new questions take half a row, so the field after
  each sits to its right rather than a row below, and the radio is drawn to the size of the boxes
  beside it rather than the shared control's own larger standing-alone size. **The section no longer falls out of
  alignment as fields come and go.** It is a two-column grid that fields flow into in order, so a
  lone field left the next one in the other column and knocked every pair below it out of step: a
  fresher hid Total Experience, the joining question slid up beside the fresher question, and
  Expected Joining Date wrapped underneath a question it has nothing to do with, with the two
  salaries split diagonally across the rows below. Fixed by order rather than by holding empty cells
  open, which only traded a misaligned row for a hole in the form — **Total Experience is now shown
  to everybody** (a readout of what the register will record: the rows' sum, or the word "Fresher"),
  so the question heading the section always has a partner, and **the two salaries sit above the
  joining question**, so the one remaining optional field falls beside the question that summons it.
- **The candidate's application form asks a fresher far less, and asks everyone the right things in
  the right order.** Four changes to the public form at `/interview/apply/[token]`. **Available From
  is gone from The Role** — when somebody can start is a conversation, not a date typed in before
  anybody has spoken to them, and the picker collected a guess that then sat on the register as
  though it were a commitment; Notice Period is the part worth asking at this stage, and the
  register keeps its When Can You Join column for HR, a sheet or a CSV to fill. **Education moved
  under About You**, beside the candidate's name and address, instead of a combined "Education &
  Work History" section at the foot of the form — it is the one part of a CV every candidate has an
  answer to, a fresher most of all, and it had been sitting below questions that do not apply to
  them. **The work history is now asked directly under "Are you a fresher or experienced?"**, the
  question that decides whether it applies, where it used to be a page further down — a candidate
  could answer Experienced and reach Submit without ever seeing the list. **And a fresher is no
  longer asked Current Company, Current Designation, Current Salary or Notice Period**, the four
  questions that presuppose a job; Expected Salary stays for everybody, since a fresher has no
  current salary but does have an expectation. One `isExperienced` rule drives all of it, and the
  required-field check reads that same rule, so a fresher is never refused for a question that was
  not on their screen. Both repeating lists are ordinary `custom` fields of their sections now, the
  way the onboarding form already carries them. The invite prefill is filtered to the fields the
  form still asks, so a register column it has stopped asking about can no longer land in the form
  unseen and be posted back on the candidate's behalf. Nothing changed for a signed-in user:
  [interviewApplicationConfig.js](../../frontend/src/constants/interviewApplicationConfig.js) and
  [PublicApplicationForm.js](../../frontend/src/components/interview/PublicApplicationForm.js) are the
  public token-authenticated form, and the register's own record still offers every column.
- **Interview: a candidate can be rejected from any stage, and Yet to Connect reads only.**
  **Reject** was on the row ⋮ only while a screening form was out, so turning somebody down after
  the form was read, after the interview, or off On Hold weeks later meant opening the record and
  changing the stage by hand. It is now on **every tab**, sitting above the destructive entry in
  either menu (Delete, or Cancel Form while waiting) and still moving the stage with an **Undo**
  back to where it came from; a row already **Rejected** does not offer it, since it would move the
  stage to where it already is. **Edit is gone from Yet to Connect** — nobody has spoken to that
  candidate, so the record is still exactly what arrived and there is nothing learned to write into
  it; **View** still opens it read-only and Edit returns as soon as the candidate moves on a stage.
  Both live in [InterviewPage.js](../../frontend/src/components/hrm/InterviewPage.js); no permission
  changed, so the whole menu remains behind `HRM / Interview` **READ_WRITE** and is absent for a
  read-only viewer.

  The rule the CRM board had already worked out for its own hand-written table now lives in the
  shared one: **stay on the automatic layout until a column is actually pulled**, and on that first
  pull store every column's measured width so only the pulled one moves. A table therefore looks
  exactly as it does today until somebody drags something, which is what makes it safe to have on
  everywhere — and with the switch costing nothing, resizing is now on unless a table turns it off,
  the same way filtering, sorting and column reordering already are. The two small embedded lists
  inside Service Creation still turn it off, because they are form sections rather than grids
  somebody arranges ([DataTable.js](../../frontend/src/components/data-table/DataTable.js)).

  What Save does there differs, and the prompt says so rather than pretending otherwise: an employee
  that does not exist yet cannot be half-written to the register, so it is the same save Add
  Employee's own footer makes and is held to the same required fields. On a form too incomplete to
  save it names what is missing and the panel stays open with the work still in it — which is the
  thing that was actually wanted. The button reads **Save** rather than *Save draft* there, because
  a prompt promising a draft and then asking for eight more fields reads as a button that does not
  work ([EmployeeFormPanel.js](../../frontend/src/components/employees/EmployeeFormPanel.js)).

  **The act keeps a guard of its own rather than losing one.** The advance now posts to
  `POST /api/sales/projects/:id/advance`, which answers to the same `SALES / Sales Manager` that
  created the project and writes through the Accounts module's own recorder — so the row, its
  validation and its branch check are the ones Accounts applies to everything else, and
  `ACCOUNTS / Cash Management` goes on meaning exactly what it meant for every other way of writing
  to the cash book. It is deliberately narrow: the project comes from the URL rather than the body,
  so an advance cannot be filed against a project the caller cannot reach; branch reach stays
  enforced; and the entry type is forced to **Received**, so this cannot become a way to record
  studio spending without the permission for it. `hrm/payment-requests` already writes a cash entry
  this way under its own permission, so this is that pattern rather than a new one
  ([projects/controller.js](../../backend/src/modules/sales/projects/controller.js),
  [CreateProjectFormPanel.js](../../frontend/src/components/sales/CreateProjectFormPanel.js)).

  **A lead named `CNA` is excused it, exactly as it is excused Estimated Value.** Nobody answered the
