  phone, so the baby's date is no more knowable than what the job is worth — both fall away together
  and everything that makes the call findable stays. That is the flow the studio asked for, and it
  is the same list both fields now sit on ([CRMPage.js](../../frontend/src/components/sales/CRMPage.js)).

  **Duplicate** starts a new task from an existing one, which is what the studio does with anything
  recurring. It carries no id, so it cannot be mistaken for an edit, and it deliberately does not
  copy the original's attachments — those files belonged to that task. **Reassigning stays its own
  verb**: it records the hand-over in the holder chain and tells the new holder, so an edit does not
  offer the assignee at all and the server's update schema drops it if sent. A task was previously
  never editable at all, the reasoning being that the record is what the receiver acted on — but it
  was already being worked around by deleting and re-raising, which loses the history, the
  attachments and the holder chain that argument was protecting
  ([tasks/service.js](../../backend/src/modules/hrm/tasks/service.js),
  [TaskFormPanel.js](../../frontend/src/components/hrm/TaskFormPanel.js)).

  The confirmation now offers two ways on. **Convert** writes the lead and opens Create Project on
  it. **Update and Close** writes the lead and leaves you where you are — for a lead that is
  converted but is not being booked yet. Both go through the ordinary save with the status forced
  onto it, rather than a second way of writing a converted lead, so a conversion carries its remark,
  its activity entry and its Last Follow-up Date exactly as any other update does.

  **The required fields are asked for first.** Converting is a write now, so it is held to the same
  line every other write is: the pill marks the empty boxes red and the confirmation does not open
  until they are filled, instead of a lead being marked Converted with the fields that make it a
  lead still blank. And **a refused save does not navigate** — the reader stays on the lead with the
  reason, rather than being taken to Create Project for a lead that never converted, where they
  could book a project against a record still reading Interested
  ([CRMPage.js](../../frontend/src/components/sales/CRMPage.js)).

  **It narrows and can never widen.** The server ANDs it with the same data scope it already
  applied, so turning it off can only take rows away from what was allowed — and the ids it narrows
  by come from the signed-in account, never from the request, so it cannot be pointed at a
  colleague. It reaches **every** read the board makes: the rows, the tab counts, each column
  filter's own menu, and the ids a "select all" resolves to — that last one mattering most, since a
  bulk action taken on a board showing one person's leads must not run against their whole team's.

  **Drawn only for people it means something to** — somebody with a team under them, or anybody who
  sees every lead in the studio, for whom it is more use than for anyone else. For everybody else the
  two states are the same board — they are named on their own leads and on nobody else's — and a
  switch that changes nothing invites somebody to flip it, see no difference, and conclude the board
  is broken. It is not offered on the Call Box either, which is answered from the call log and takes
  no such narrowing. Whether it appears is read on a real page read rather than on every scroll
  append, and the board uses the reader's own stored answer until that reply arrives, so a manager
  who works their own queue does not see the whole team's leads flash past first. A reader who has since lost their team is shown everything regardless of what they last
  chose, so a stored answer can never leave a board narrowed by a control that is no longer on
  screen to turn back ([CRMPage.js](../../frontend/src/components/sales/CRMPage.js),
  [leadQueryService.js](../../backend/src/modules/sales/crm-leads/leadQueryService.js),
  [dataScope.service.js](../../backend/src/services/dataScope.service.js)).

  `DataTable` had this right all along; the board is a second hand-written table that had quietly
  drifted from it. **The rule now has one name** — `CELL_CLIP`, `HEADER_CLIP` and `FILTER_CLIP` in
  [tableLayout.js](../../frontend/src/components/data-table/tableLayout.js) — which both read, so the
  two can no longer disagree about it. A test fails if either spells the utilities out by hand
  again, which is how they came to differ in the first place. The board also reads each cell's value
  once now instead of three times per status cell, on a table that draws 32,000 rows
  ([CRMPage.js](../../frontend/src/components/sales/CRMPage.js),
  [DataTable.js](../../frontend/src/components/data-table/DataTable.js)).

### Added
- **A branch can carry the QR its clients scan to pay it.** Settings → Organization → Branches →
  *Add / Edit Branch* has a **QR Code** section under Registration taking one picture — PNG, JPG or
  WEBP, up to 5 MB — through the same field the studio's logo and an employee's photograph are
  picked with: it compresses the picture in the browser, shows it back, and offers **Replace photo**
  and **Remove photo**. Optional, and Add and Edit behave alike: a branch that already has one opens
  showing it, and an edit that changes only the phone number sends the stored link back rather than
  uploading the picture a second time. The picture goes to `branches/qr-codes` in the same bucket
  every other image in the app goes to, and the branch stores the link; nothing is kept as binary in
  the row. Reached with `SETTINGS / Organization` — the same permission the rest of the branch
  register already answers to, on the same guarded router, so no role gains or loses anything.
  Needs `npx prisma migrate deploy` in `backend/` for the new `branch.qr_code_url` column, which is
  `NULL` on every branch already on the register. See
  [BranchesPanel.js](../../frontend/src/components/settings/BranchesPanel.js).
- **Each quotation brand decides whether its clients may download their quotation.** Settings →
  Quotation Settings → *brand* → **PDF Content** now opens on **Allow Client Download** (Yes/No,
  **No** until somebody says otherwise), held per brand — Dum Dum, Qua Qua and Corp each answer for
  themselves, and a quotation is judged by the brand it was raised under. On **No** the download
  icon is gone from the client's copy *and* `GET /api/public/quotations/:token/pdf` answers `403`,
  so a client who kept the address gets a refusal rather than a file. The answer is read **live**
  off the brand rather than from the version's snapshot — unlike everything else the client's copy
  prints — so switching it off reaches the links already sent, and switching it on lets a waiting
  client simply download. The studio's own **Download PDF** in Sales → Quotations is untouched: it
  moved to a signed-in route of its own, `GET /api/sales/quotations/:id/pdf` (`SALES / Quotation
  Management` READ, scoped to the quotations the caller may reach), so a salesperson can still save
  the proposal for a brand that hands its clients nothing. No quotation, picture, word or snapshot
  is rewritten, and both existing Download controls behave exactly as before once a brand answers
  Yes. Needs `npx prisma migrate deploy` in `backend/` for the new
  `sales_quotation_brand.allow_client_download` column. See
  [quotation-brands/service.js](../../backend/src/modules/sales/quotation-brands/service.js),
  [projects/controller.js](../../backend/src/modules/sales/projects/controller.js),
  [QuotationSettingsPanel.js](../../frontend/src/components/settings/QuotationSettingsPanel.js) and
  [PublicQuotationPage.js](../../frontend/src/components/sales/PublicQuotationPage.js).
- **An audit that says whether the deliverable migration landed whole.** `npm run audit:deliverables`
  in `backend/` issues `SELECT` and nothing else, so it is safe against production and safe to run
  twice. Ten checks: every name `seed.js` knows about is present as an ordinary row (so nothing
  seeded was missed — and since the seed cannot be run on the VPS, that is the only way to know);
  no orphan brands or variants and no deliverable holding the same brand twice; every Yes/No answer
  survived as a **word** rather than as MySQL's own `1`/`0` (a naive `ALTER` would have written `0`,
  which does not start with "no", silently admitting every deliverable answered No into Image
  Selection); every deliverable that described itself with a brand, size, frame, length or price has
  a variant carrying it; the five columns the CRM Quotation Calculator reads still match the first
  variant they are written from; and nothing a project already booked has lost the definition behind
  it. Exits non-zero on any failure, so it can gate a deploy
  ([script](../../backend/scripts/auditDeliverableMigration.js)).

### Changed
- **A branch no longer needs a GSTIN or a PAN before it can be saved.** Both were mandatory on
  Settings → Organization → Branches, which meant a branch that had started trading before its
  registration came through — and any studio not registered at all — could not be added to the
  register in the first place. The asterisks are gone from both boxes and the server's required list
  matches, in **Add Branch** and **Edit Branch** alike, and an edit may now clear either one. What
  is typed is still checked exactly as before: a mistyped GSTIN or PAN is refused, and a GSTIN
  another branch already holds is still a clash — only a *missing* one is now allowed. Nothing else
  moved: the code, name, address, PIN code, phone and the four bank fields are still required, every
  saved GSTIN and PAN keeps working, and invoices, receipts, quotations and head-office handling
  read the same values they always did. See
  [validation.js](../../backend/src/modules/admin/branches/validation.js).
- **A deliverable is sold by as many companies as sell it, in as many variants as they sell it in.**
  Settings → Project → Deliverables → Add / Edit Deliverable asked for one Company / Brand Name, one
  Size, one Frame Inches, one Length and one Price, which is not how deliverables are sold: the same
  album is sold by Canvera and by Pictoo, each in several sizes at several prices. The dialog now
  asks Deliverable Name and Deliverable Type at the top, then a **Brands & Variants** section —
  **+ Add Another Brand**, **+ Add Variant** inside each brand, and a delete beside every brand and
  every variant. What a variant is asked for follows the deliverable's type and nothing else: an
  image's variant takes **Size**, **Frame Inches** and **Price**, a video's takes **Length** and
  **Price**, and neither is ever shown the other's boxes. Nothing is hard-coded — no brand, size,
  frame, length or price is named anywhere in the code. Changing a deliverable's type when its
  variants already hold what the other type asks for now **asks first**, saying how many variants it
  would empty and that nothing is removed until the deliverable is saved; cancelling leaves it
  exactly as it was. Stored relationally as `ProjectDeliverableDefinition → ProjectDeliverableBrand
  → ProjectDeliverableVariant`, replaced whole on each save
  ([dialog](../../frontend/src/components/project-management/DeliverableDefinitionDialog.js),
  [rules](../../frontend/src/lib/deliverableBrands.mjs),
  [migration](../../backend/prisma/migrations/20260918090000_deliverable_brands_variants_and_text_answers/migration.sql)).
  The Deliverables table keeps its Company / Brand Name, Size, Length, Frame Inches and Price
  columns and lists what the brands hold in each — "Canvera, Pictoo", "12x18, 16x24" — so it still
  reads as the form that filled it in. **Every deliverable already describing itself with those five
  fields is carried over** by the migration into the one brand and one variant it describes, so the
  dialog opens on what the studio already typed. The deliverable's own five columns are kept and
  rewritten from the first brand's first variant on every save, because the CRM Quotation Calculator
  quotes one figure per deliverable and reads them — it is untouched by this change, as are the
  Quotation Generator, project bookings and Post Production Allocation.
- **Everything that opens out now points down to open and up to close.** Four disclosures pointed
  **right** when shut and **down** when open, which describes a different gesture: right is a
  direction to travel in — the way a side panel slides away or a submenu flies out — while down is
  "there is more of this below". A row that opens in place has not gone anywhere. The four are the
  expandable table row (Services, the Quotation register, Deliverables), the Image Selection
  **folder tree**, a shoot's **selection status**, and the **sidebar group headings**. They now match
  the pair the rest of the application already used — a multi-select's chevron and a shoot's
  collapse button were both down-to-open already. Arrows that genuinely mean a direction are
  deliberately left alone: the info panel's chevron still points the way the panel slides, the
  Image Selection project sidebar still opens from its own edge, and a photo's submenu arrow still
  points at whichever side the submenu has room on. Pinned by
  `frontend/test/disclosure-chevrons.test.mjs`.
- **The Services table says whether a service brings deliverables, and Remarks is a note you write.**
  A **Deliverable Added** column sits after Price, reading Yes or No — the question the dialog asks
  beside Remarks and Size; only Yes brings anything, so a service nobody has answered reads as No.
  Remarks stops being a Yes/No dropdown and becomes a typed note about the service, opening **empty**.
  **It has stopped being a switch**, the same trap already removed from a deliverable's Remarks: it
  used to decide whether a project's service line carried a remarks box at all, under the rule "an
  answer starting with 'no' means No", so an ordinary note beginning "Not applicable" or "No drone on
  this one" would have taken that box away across every project using the service. A line now always
  keeps its own remarks. Every service but one answered Yes, the one that answered No is
  demonstration data, and no project service line carries a remark today, so nothing that was
  actually held changes. A migration converts the column to text — writing the words `Yes` and `No`
  rather than MySQL's own `1` and `0` — and then clears those two answers so the box opens ready to
  be written in, touching only what the dropdown could ever have written, so a note typed later
  survives and a second run matches nothing. Neither the question nor the note is drawn on a pricing
  line: both belong to the service, and the head row answers them once
  ([migration](../../backend/prisma/migrations/20260918130000_service_remarks_is_a_note/migration.sql)).
- **A counted deliverable is priced by its count, and a complimentary line comes to nothing.** A
  quotation line was quantity × price. Where the deliverable is counted — **No. of Count = Yes** in
  Settings — it is now **quantity × count × price**: "Album Printing" at ₹2,200 with a count of 10
  is ten albums, so two of that line is ₹44,000. A deliverable that is not counted is unchanged, and
  **an empty count is not a count of nothing** — empty, zero, negative, text or a stray `NaN` all
  leave the line on quantity × price, rather than quietly pricing it at nothing until somebody types
  a figure. The deliverable's own answer has to say Yes too, so a count typed before that answer was
  changed to No cannot price a line through a multiplier the cell no longer shows.
  **Complimentary now decides money**, where before it was recorded and shown but charged in full: a
  line marked Yes contributes nothing to the subtotal, whatever its quantity, count or unit price,
  and keeps all three on screen so what is being given away is visible. Services and deliverables
  alike, each independently of the other. Only an explicit Yes is free, so every line raised before
  the question existed is charged exactly as it was.
  **And the master's Complimentary is a permission, not a default.** Settings answering Yes means
  this *may* be given away; it does not mean it is. A quotation line is asked the question only
  where its own master permits it — a service and a deliverable each read their own, so a service
  allowed to be complimentary never makes the deliverables it brings in complimentary — and the
  answer always starts at **No**, for somebody to change on that quotation alone. Where the master
  does not permit it the cell simply reads "-". A line already answered Yes keeps its control even
  if that permission is later withdrawn: it is a fact about a quotation somebody raised, and hiding
  the control would leave an answer on the line with no way to change it. All of it in `quotationTotals`, which turned
  out to be the only place a line is priced — the register, the client's copy and the PDF read the
  subtotal it stamps rather than working one out of their own, so there was no second calculation to
  keep in step ([totals](../../frontend/src/lib/quotationBrand.mjs)).
- **Complimentary: whether a service or a deliverable is given rather than charged for.** Settings →
  Project asks it of both, as a Yes/No beside the settings already there, and both listing tables
  show it. The two are **independent**: a complimentary service does not make its deliverables
  complimentary, and a deliverable a service brings in automatically keeps its own answer rather
  than the service's. A quotation copies the master's answer onto a line **as the line is added**
  and the line's answer is its own from then on — the person building the quotation may change it
  for that quotation alone, it never writes back to Settings, and a Settings change never moves a
  quotation already raised, because the line's answer is stored with the line. Editable on both the
  service and the deliverable rows in the Quotation Generator, and only there: a project records
  work done and prices nothing, so its screens keep the columns they have.
  **Every existing record reads No** — the column is null on all 80 services and 76 deliverables,
  and only Yes is complimentary, so nothing behaves differently until somebody answers it. Nothing
  is read, rewritten or recreated
  ([migration](../../backend/prisma/migrations/20260918140000_service_and_deliverable_complimentary/migration.sql)).
- **A quotation line carries the deliverable's own note beside its name.** A deliverable's Remarks
  in Settings describes the thing — "Sent flat, never rolled." — so a line on a quotation now reads
  `Demo Album Printing - Canvera - 12x18 - 1.5 inch - Sent flat, never rolled.`, in the Choose
  Deliverables list and on the row it adds, whether the variant was picked by hand or brought in by
  a service. **The line's own Remarks box is untouched**: that is a different value, typed for that
  one quotation and stored on that one row, and neither ever writes to the other. The note is asked
  for rather than appended everywhere — the Settings → Services mapping picker identifies a variant,
  where the note would only be noise — and a deliverable with no note reads exactly as it did. The
  Deliverables cell gained a hover title, since a line carrying its note is longer than the column
  ([label](../../frontend/src/lib/deliverableBrands.mjs)).
- **A service's deliverables wait for its Session Timing, and all of them arrive.** Two faults, one
  cause: the booking was resolved from the **shoot's** sessions rather than from the line's own. A
  shoot offering both Half and Full therefore answered with whichever session the service was priced
  for first — so deliverables appeared the instant the service was chosen, before any Session Timing
  had been picked, and they were that guessed row's rather than the chosen one's, which read as
  "only one deliverable" when the other row held different ones. `servicePricingRowForLine` now
  decides: it reads the session off the line's own Session Timing cell and answers **nothing** until
  that cell is answered, so a service added to a shoot brings nothing until it is booked, then
  brings every deliverable on the matched row — changing the session swaps the whole set for the
  other row's. A service priced under no session at all (hourly, or one flat figure) has no Session
  Timing to answer and resolves exactly as it always did.
  **The price is untouched**: `servicePriceForShoot` still answers from the shoot, because a price
  is a starting figure stamped when the service is added and a guess there is harmless, where a
  guess about what a booking brings adds the wrong rows
  ([resolver](../../frontend/src/lib/servicePricing.mjs)).
- **A service brings its own deliverables into a quotation.** Settings → Project → Services → Add /
  Edit Service asks **Deliverable Added** beside Remarks and Size. Answered Yes, every pricing row
  gains a **Deliverables** list naming the exact variants that booking is sold with — listed and
  searched in the same words the Quotation Generator uses, `Acrylic Printing - Canvera - 12x18 - 1.5
  inch`, and a booking never offers a variant it already holds. The mapping belongs to the
  **booking**, not the service: the 12x18 row brings a 12x18 print and the 8x12 row does not.
  In the Quotation Generator, adding that service resolves its booking exactly as the price is
  resolved — `servicePricingRowForShoot`, one reading for both, so the price and what follows it can
  never disagree — and the mapped variants arrive in Quotation Deliverables by themselves, through
  **the same `deliverableRowFor` call a hand-picked variant makes**. An automatic line and a manual
  one are the same row: same stamped price, same ids, same totals, same payload, same PDF. No second
  calculation exists. A service sold by size names its booking only once the size is chosen, so the
  list is worked out again then — changing the size swaps the 8x12 row's deliverables for the
  12x18 row's, and removing the service takes its own away, while anything added by hand is never
  touched. A variant already on the shoot is not added twice, which is the rule a package's rows
  already followed. A mapping whose variant has since been deleted in Settings quietly brings
  nothing ([dialog](../../frontend/src/components/project-management/ServiceDefinitionDialog.js),
  [generator](../../frontend/src/components/sales/QuotationGeneratorPanel.js)).
  **Existing services are untouched.** `deliverable_added` is NULL on every service already here and
  only TRUE brings anything, so all 80 go on behaving exactly as they do today until somebody
  answers Yes; no service or pricing row is read, rewritten or recreated
  ([migration](../../backend/prisma/migrations/20260918120000_service_deliverable_added/migration.sql)).
  The mapping rides on the pricing line itself rather than in a table of its own — a service's
  pricing rows are JSON with no ids, so a mapping keyed to one would detach the moment the row was
  edited; riding on the line, it stays attached. The server checks every id against the deliverables
  that actually exist, brand and variant together, and drops any that name nothing.
- **The Quotation Generator quotes an exact variant, at that variant's own price.** Service Creation
  → **Choose Deliverables** listed a deliverable and nothing else, so one sold by two companies in
  three sizes each was a single line somebody had to price by hand. It lists every brand and variant
  now, one selectable entry each — `Acrylic Printing - Canvera - 12x18 - 1.5 inch` for an image,
  `Cinematic Full Length - In-House Edit - 45 minutes` for a video, which is never shown a size or a
  frame. Choosing one **stamps that variant's price onto the line**, and that is the whole of the
  change to pricing: `deliverableUnitPrice` already read a stamped price before any fallback, so the
  quotation goes on totalling quantity × unit price through `quotationTotals` with no second sum
  introduced, and discount, GST, save, versions and the PDF are untouched. Stamping is also what
  keeps history still: repricing a variant in Settings tomorrow cannot move a version already
  raised. A selection is identified by **deliverable + brand + variant id**, never by its words,
  because two brands sell a 12x18 and two deliverables may share a name — the ids ride in the
  payload as extra fields, which `quotationLineSchema` passes through, so nothing on the server
  changed. The box's own search finds `Canvera`, `12x18`, `Acrylic` or `45 minutes` because the
  label carries them all. A deliverable sold by nobody yet still appears under its own name and is
  priced exactly as it was before, so nothing disappears from the picker
  ([options](../../frontend/src/lib/deliverableBrands.mjs),
  [generator](../../frontend/src/components/sales/QuotationGeneratorPanel.js)).
  **Only the Quotation Generator lists variants.** The picker is shared with Create Project, Start
  Project and the project plan, and a shoot holds one allocation per deliverable — the server
  answers 409 to a second — so listing six variants there would have offered five that cannot be
  added. Those three screens are unchanged, behind an opt-in prop.
- **Service Name and Deliverable Name are typed, and the studio starts from nothing.** Both boxes
  were combo boxes listing every name already configured, so one that existed was picked rather than
  retyped into a second spelling. The list is gone at the studio's request — in the Add row above
  each table and in the Add / Edit dialog — and each is a plain box that takes whatever is written.
  The forty services and thirty-eight deliverables the seed put there are **retired** so both screens
  start empty and the studio's own names are the only ones in the application.
  **Retired, not deleted** — `is_active = 0`, exactly what the ⋮ menu's own Delete does to one row,
  applied to all of them at once. A hard delete would have cascaded through and destroyed the 25
  post-production workflow definitions hanging off services, and cut the 12 project rows loose from
  the definitions they point at; nothing of the sort happens. It is also reversible by design: typing
  a retired name back into Add Service or Add Deliverable finds the row by its `code` and restores
  it, id and every setting intact, so a studio that retypes "Album Printing" gets its own row back
  rather than a duplicate
  ([migration](../../backend/prisma/migrations/20260918110000_retire_seeded_services_and_deliverables/migration.sql)).
- **No. of Count and Image Selection are answered Yes or No again; Remarks is a note you write.**
  The three were briefly all free text. Two of them are questions with two answers — only Yes puts a
  count on a project's line, only Yes admits a deliverable to Image Selection — so both are
  dropdowns again, writing the word itself (`Yes` / `No`) into the text columns that hold them.
  Image Selection offers Yes and No and nothing else. Remarks is the odd one out and is now what it
  reads as: a typed note about the deliverable, opening **empty**, deciding nothing.
  **It has stopped being a switch**, which removes a real trap — it used to decide whether a
  project's deliverable line carried a remarks box at all, under the rule "an answer starting with
  'no' means No", so an ordinary note beginning "Not applicable" or "No frames for this one" would
  have silently taken that box away across every project using the deliverable. A line now always
  keeps its own remarks, which is exactly what every deliverable already did: all 38 answered Yes.
  A migration clears those 38 answers so the box opens ready to be written in, touching only the two
  words the dropdown could ever have written, so a note typed later survives and a second run
  matches nothing
  ([migration](../../backend/prisma/migrations/20260918100000_deliverable_remarks_is_a_note/migration.sql)).
- **The Deliverables table opens out, the way the Services table does.** A deliverable's brands and
  prices were run together into single cells — "Canvera, Pictoo", "12x18, 16x24" — which is a
  sentence, not a table. Each level is a row of its own now: a deliverable opens into the companies
  that sell it, and each company opens into the variants it sells it in, so the size, the frame, the
  length and the price each sit in their own column and a fourth variant is a fourth row rather than
  more text in one cell. The head row answers only what belongs to the deliverable — what it is,
  which companies sell it, and the three questions asked of every deliverable — and prices itself as
  the **range** its variants cover, `₹1,200 – ₹3,500`, exactly as a service's head row prices itself
  across its pricing lines. Size, Length and Frame Inches read `—` on the head row because they
  belong to a variant, and a variant is never shown the boxes its deliverable's type does not have.
  A brand and a variant carry no ⋮: both are edited in their deliverable's own dialog
  ([panel](../../frontend/src/components/project-management/ProjectSettingsPanel.js)). A deliverable
  saved before brands existed and not yet reopened has no chevron and goes on showing its own five
  columns, so nothing disappears from the table.
  **The shared table now lets a nested row keep its chevron** where something hangs under it in
  turn; it used to draw a rule over every nested row's chevron cell, which made a second level
  impossible to open. The rule is now for a nested row that is a *leaf* — which is what a service's
  pricing line and a quotation's earlier version both still are, so neither table changes
  ([DataTable](../../frontend/src/components/data-table/DataTable.js), pinned by
  `frontend/test/data-table-nested-expansion.test.mjs`).
- **No. of Count, Image Selection and Remarks are typed rather than picked.** The three questions
  asked of every deliverable were a Yes/No dropdown each; a studio can now write what it actually
  does — "50 photos", "2 sets", "only the album" — with Remarks as a textarea. They still decide
  things, by one rule written down in
  [deliverableAnswers](../../backend/src/services/deliverableAnswers.js): an answer starting with "no"
  is No, anything else typed is Yes, and a blank one is unanswered. **Every `Yes` and `No` already
  stored goes on meaning exactly what it meant** — the migration writes the words back rather than
  MySQL's own `1` and `0`, so no deliverable enters or leaves Image Selection, no project allocation
  gains or loses its photo count box, and a deliverable nobody has answered about stays out of Image
  Selection as it always did.

### Fixed
- **A service's deliverables no longer lag one change behind.** Changing a Session Timing, removing a
  service, or clearing several at once re-synced the deliverables that service brings against the
  shoot *as it was*, not as it had just become - so the booking's deliverables were always one
  change out of date, and a timing had to be set twice to take effect. The three handlers set state
  through an updater and then read the ref holding the list, but `setServices(fn)` does not run `fn`
  there and then: React runs it at the next render, so the ref still held the previous list. Each
  now works the next list out first and hands that same list to the sync, which is what `addService`
  has always done ([panel](../../frontend/src/components/sales/QuotationGeneratorPanel.js)).
- **A lead holding an impossible date no longer takes a whole migration down with it.** The first
  deploy of this release stopped on production with `Incorrect datetime value: '2026-31-11' for
  function str_to_date`, pointing at a statement that only sets a lead's Assigned Branch and touches
  no date at all. `sales_crm_lead` carries three **STORED** generated columns that parse its
  free-text date fields, and MySQL recomputes every stored generated column on a row whenever that
  row is updated — whatever column the update actually writes to. So one lead holding `11/31/2026`,
  which is no date read either way round, failed every UPDATE that so much as brushed it. It got
  into the table because the application's connection runs a lenient `sql_mode`, where the same
  expression answers NULL and a warning; Prisma's migration engine connects with
  `STRICT_TRANS_TABLES`, where it is a hard error. The migration now clears impossible dates out of
  those three columns before anything touches the table, checking the month and day with plain
  arithmetic rather than by trying to parse them — February counted properly, so `29/02/2024` is
  kept and `29/02/2025` is not. Values that match neither date shape (ordinary text, blanks, NULL)
  never reached `STR_TO_DATE` and are untouched. **The migration is also idempotent now**: its two
  DDL statements are guarded against `information_schema`, so the file can be replayed against a
  database that already ran part of it, which is what recovering a half-applied migration needs
  ([migration](../../backend/prisma/migrations/20260917120000_hrm_tasks_branch_text_and_admin_fixes/migration.sql)).
- **A lead whose branch says `Vizag` is filed under `Visakhapatnam AP` again.** The branch rule was
  tightened so that a branch is matched by its full name, with or without the state keyword, and by
  nothing shorter — and that removed the one nickname it had forgiven. The consequence was not the
  one intended: `Vizag` is not a guess at a branch the way `viza` or `erd` are, it is what the studio
  calls that branch out loud and in every file it exports, so from that deploy on a CSV or a sheet
  saying `Vizag` produced a lead with **no branch at all** — absent from every branch-scoped read,
  and showing an empty Assigned Branch, because the picker is built from the branch register and
  `Vizag` matches nothing in it. The alias is back as a named entry in `BRANCH_ALIASES`, and the
  shape of it matters: it is checked **after** the register, so a branch genuinely called `Vizag`
  would win; it is resolved **through** the register, so removing `Visakhapatnam AP` leaves an alias
  that matches nothing rather than one pointing at an id that is not there; and a test fails if the
  list grows past one, because a few nicknames belong on the branch row rather than in a source
  file. Nothing else is forgiven — `viza`, `vizagg` and `erd` are still refused and still shown as
  they arrived. A migration repairs the leads written while the alias was gone, filing each one and
  taking its Assigned Branch text from the branch it now points at: the board's branch filter reads
  that text, so leaving half the leads reading `Vizag` would split one branch's leads across two
  filter values. It touches only leads that are filed under nothing and whose text is exactly
  `Vizag`, so a lead since corrected by hand is left alone
  ([referenceMatchers.js](../../backend/src/services/referenceMatchers.js),
  [migration](../../backend/prisma/migrations/20260917120000_hrm_tasks_branch_text_and_admin_fixes/migration.sql)).
- **Converted & Followup opens Create Project, the way Converted does.** Converting is not a status
  change — it hands the lead to Sales Manager in a second tab, and it is that project's own save
  that marks the lead converted. **Converted & Followup** is Qua Qua's status for a client who has
  bought and is still being worked, so it needs a project every bit as much as the plain one does;
  it was setting a status and stopping, which made it the one press that should have opened Create
  Project and quietly did not. Both pills now ask the same question and open the same tab, from the
  lead popup and from the info panel beside the table alike — the info panel had only ever known
  about plain Converted. **The lead keeps the status that was pressed**: which of the two was chosen
  travels with the conversion and is applied by the project's save, so Converted & Followup no
  longer ends as a flattened "Converted" with the distinction lost at the last step. It travels in
  the link rather than being written to the lead first, which keeps the promise the confirmation
  makes — a tab closed without saving leaves the lead exactly as it was. The server checks what it
  is sent against the list of converted statuses it already keeps rather than trusting it, so this
  can only ever set a lead to a converted status ([CRMPage.js](../../frontend/src/components/sales/CRMPage.js),
  [SalesManagerPage.js](../../frontend/src/components/sales/SalesManagerPage.js),
  [crmLeadService.js](../../backend/src/modules/sales/crm-leads/crmLeadService.js)).
- **Converting a lead whose event date the CRM stored as text can create a project again.** Convert
  opens Create Project with the lead's date already filled in, and a lead's dates are free text —
  from a CSV out of Excel, a Google Sheet, or somebody typing. That cell was handed to the server
  exactly as it stood, where `new Date()` made an **Invalid Date** of anything it could not read and
  the save came back `mainEventDate: Invalid input: expected date, received Date`. Create Project
  has **no control for the main event date**, so there was nowhere to go and correct it: those leads
  could not be converted at all. Two halves. The conversion now folds the lead's date through
  `toIsoDay` — the CRM board's own reading of a stored date — so the project files the event under
  the day the board shows, and a cell it cannot place sends nothing rather than something broken. And
  the schema treats a date it cannot read as **not decided**, the same answer an empty value always
  gave, instead of refusing the whole request; a caller that insisted the date was known still gets
  the readable "Main Event Date is required when its status is Yes." What `new Date()` already read
  loosely it still reads loosely — narrowing that would refuse project imports that work today
  ([SalesManagerPage.js](../../frontend/src/components/sales/SalesManagerPage.js),
  [controller.js](../../backend/src/modules/sales/projects/controller.js)).
- **The lead popup stops asking a Rejected lead when to call it back.** Marking a lead **Invalid**
  or **Rejected** ends the conversation, so neither a Next Follow-up Date nor a Next Follow-up Time
  applies — Add Lead and Bulk Update had said so for a while, and the popup was the one place still
  asking. Both fields now go as the status is chosen, replaced by the line Bulk Update already uses,
  so two fields that were there a moment ago do not simply vanish. The half that actually cost
  something is the other one: **a lead already carrying a follow-up kept it when it was rejected**,
  unseen, and turned up in somebody's Pending Followups the next morning under a status saying the
  conversation was over. The press that hides the fields now clears them, including an occasion
  lead's own copies of both — that is the copy the Newborns and Birthdays tabs count, so clearing
  only the ordinary pair would have left the date that matters. It applies on both of the popup's
  edit paths, the lead popup itself and the info panel beside the table. Leads rejected before this
  keep whatever date they were stored with; the fix is to the rule, not to the rows
  ([CRMPage.js](../../frontend/src/components/sales/CRMPage.js)).

### Added
- **The screening form asks whether the candidate is a fresher, and works out their total
  experience instead of asking for it.** **Are you a fresher or experienced?** is now asked before
  the work history, and that one answer decides the rest of it: a fresher is shown no Experience
  Details list to scroll past and sends no rows however many they had typed before answering, while
  somebody experienced has to name at least one company — otherwise they arrived on the register as
  experienced with nothing to show for it, which reads as a form that lost their answers. **Total
  Experience** is added up from those rows and shown back **read-only** beside them, and what
  reaches the register's Experience column is that sum, or "Fresher". It was a box asking for a
  number the candidate then stated again row by row in Experience Details: two answers to one
  question, free to disagree, with nothing to say which the studio should believe. A row naming a
  company but no span adds nothing and leaves the total blank rather than putting "0 years" on a
  record, so HR asks instead of reading a wrong number. **Highest Qualification** is gone for the
  same reason — a box beside a Qualifications list that already asks for every qualification with
  its own marks and year — and it is out of the submission's schema and write as well as off the
  form, because a candidate's answers are applied partially and an empty key would overwrite what
  is on the record. Both derived values are read at render rather than kept in state, since a total
  written by an effect on every keystroke is the pattern this codebase keeps out of components. A
  link reopened after HR filled some of it in comes back as it was left: rows on the record mean
  experienced, "fresher" in the Experience column means fresher
  ([interviewApplicationConfig](../../frontend/src/constants/interviewApplicationConfig.js),
  [PublicApplicationForm](../../frontend/src/components/interview/PublicApplicationForm.js)).
- **The candidate's screening form takes their numbers with the phone control, and no longer asks
  which department they would be in.** Both **Phone** and **WhatsApp Number** were plain text boxes
  on this one form and nowhere else in the application, which is how "9876 500 000 (whatsapp)"
  became a number the register could not match anybody by; they are the shared `PhoneField` now, a
  country picker beside a digits-only field on a numeric keypad, held to the national length of the
  code chosen and emitting the `+91 9876543210` shape the register stores. **Department** is gone
  from the form: it was asked as "where you would be working, if you know", which a candidate
  cannot answer about a studio they have not joined, so it came back blank, guessed, or naming a
  team that does not exist. Which department a hire lands in is the studio's answer, set on the
  record, where it still is. The submission no longer sends the key either, and that half matters as
  much as the field: a candidate's answers are applied through `prepareValues` **partially** so they
  cannot blank the half HR filled in, and partial means every key it is handed is written — so a
  field taken off the form but still posted would have arrived empty and wiped a stored department.
  It is dropped from the schema as well as the write, and an older tab still posting it is no
  trouble, since an object schema drops what it does not name
  ([interviewApplicationConfig](../../frontend/src/constants/interviewApplicationConfig.js),
  [invites.js](../../backend/src/modules/hrm/interview/invites.js)).
- **Double-clicking a candidate reads their record instead of opening it for editing.** Every
  other table in the OS answers a double-click by showing the record and never by opening a form:
  the gesture is too easy to trigger by accident, a slow second click on a row somebody meant to
  select, for it to be what puts boxes and a Save on screen. The Interview register was the one
  that did. It now opens the shared `RecordViewPanel`, the same read-only panel the Employee
  Repository shows, with a field the candidate has nothing to say for dropped rather than drawn as
  an empty line; **View** on the ⋮ opens it too, as the repository's View does. A single click
  still opens the info panel beside the table, and editing is asked for by name from the ⋮, which
  is where the rules about who may edit already live
  ([InterviewPage](../../frontend/src/components/hrm/InterviewPage.js)).
- **An interview row's ⋮ now says where the candidate stands with their screening form, and offers
  the one thing worth doing about it.** A candidate holding a **live** form gets **Copy Link**,
  because where the link is, is what that row gets asked. One who has **submitted** gets nothing
  about the form, because **Edit** is the answer to a form that has arrived — and that fixes a real
  wart: a submitted form is not live, so such a row used to fall through and offer **Send Screening
  Form** to the one candidate who had already filled it in. Everyone else — never asked, or a form
  that lapsed or was called off — still gets Send. "Live" means raised or opened and not past its
  expiry, off the latest form per candidate, judged as of the moment the forms were read rather than
  during render, because reading the clock while rendering is impure and the React Compiler refuses
  it. Correcting a form, emailing it again and cancelling one are **not** on the row: they belong to
  **View All Invites**, which lists every form with those three beside it, and they are things
  somebody does while reading the forms rather than while reading the register. It is the
  repository's menu in look as well as in behaviour: one list of plain words, no rules between the
  entries and no second lines, because entries appearing and disappearing with the row's state is
  already the signal. Every path that changes a form re-reads both the register and the forms, or
  the row goes on offering the wrong entry until a reload.
  **View All Invites gained Copy Link** on every row a candidate can still open: the only route to
  a link before was to raise a *second* form, whose hand-over screen carries one. It copies through
  the shared clipboard helper rather than `navigator.clipboard` directly, which is refused over
  plain http — how a studio on the VPS reaches this screen
  ([InterviewPage](../../frontend/src/components/hrm/InterviewPage.js),
  [InterviewApplicationInviteDialog](../../frontend/src/components/hrm/InterviewApplicationInviteDialog.js)).
  No permission changes: every one of these calls an endpoint already behind `HRM / Interview`, and
  without write the ⋮ and its column are absent as before.
- **Position Applied For now offers "General", without making it a designation.** An application off
  a hiring ad that names no role is not for one of the studio's designations, and Position Applied
  For read only that list. The short fix would have been a `designation` row and the wrong one: a
  row there is also a row in every employee's designation picker, on an employee record and in the
  Employee Repository's filters, and nobody is employed as General. So the register has a short list
  of its own — `interview_position`, migration `20260918180000`, seeded by
  [interview-positions.seed.js](../../backend/prisma/seeds/interview-positions.seed.js) — maintained in
  **Settings → Fields → Interview → Positions** beside Sources, with deleting a row counted against
  the candidates who applied under it. The options endpoint merges the two, designations first and
  these after, deduplicated case-insensitively so a studio that later makes General a designation is
  not offered it twice. The value is a row rather than an array for the reason the sources were: a
  studio that needs one more answer should not need a release. **Who reaches it:** no new permission
  — the list is read through `/api/hrm/interview/options` (`HRM / Interview`) and maintained through
  Settings → Fields (`HRM / Employee Management`), exactly as Sources is.
- **The Interview register opens on the studio's own hiring sheet, and knows which ad brought each
  application in.** What the register replaces is a **Meta lead export** read in a spreadsheet, so
  the table now reads left to right the way that sheet does: Date, **Campaign Name**, **Adset
  Name**, **Ad Name**, Years of Experience, Current Salary, Expected Salary, Languages Known, When
  Can You Join, Name, Phone Number, Gender, City, then the candidate's own **Résumé** and
  **Portfolio Link**, then what the module holds besides — Candidate ID, Status, Position,
  Employment Type, Department, **Interview Scheduled On** (was Interview Date), Branch, Source, and
  the two follow-up dates side by side, because what the last conversation was and when the next is
  due is one answer. Six columns that used to open hidden — a salary, a language, a joining answer,
  a gender and a city — are what the studio's ad form asks, so on this register they are the answer
  rather than a detail. The headers are the studio's words where those differ from the module's
  (Current Salary for `currentCtc`, When Can You Join for `availableFrom`), because a column headed
  something else is one somebody has to translate. **Applied On** is gone as a header: it is the
  **Date** column, one column and the studio's word for it, and the record still reads "Applied On"
  where three other dates sit beside it. `Sl. No.` is unchanged — the shared table numbers its own
  rows, which is why a sheet's serial column is the one heading the importer leaves unread.
  **Campaign, ad set and ad are three new columns** on the candidate (migration `20260918170000`,
  nullable text): `source` already said an application came from Meta Ads, while these say which
  push, which audience and which creative, and that is what decides what keeps running. Three
  columns rather than one string, since the table is filtered by one at a time. Only a linked sheet
  or a CSV fills them, they are in the CSV template and on the record so an imported row can be put
  right, and they are written on every sync pass, so a campaign renamed mid-flight reads as its new
  name. The importer maps a Meta header row as it arrives, question marks and all
  ([sheetImport](../../backend/src/modules/hrm/interview/sheetImport.js),
  [interviewConfig](../../frontend/src/constants/interviewConfig.js)). A résumé or portfolio cell
  **offers the file** rather than printing the URL that reaches it: `tableCells` gained a `link`
  type, shared by every module's table, which shows the name the URL ends with, opens in a new tab
  with `noopener noreferrer`, and swallows the click so opening a résumé does not also open the info
  panel. No permission changes: these are columns on a table that already answers to
  `HRM / Interview`.
- **Where an interview application came from is now a list the studio maintains, and Meta Ads is
  one of them.** Settings → Fields → Interview → Sources holds Naukri, LinkedIn, Indeed, **Meta
  Ads**, Google Ads, Social Media, Website, Campus, Referral, Walk-in and Other, in that order,
  each renamable and each removable — and the order is the list's own, so Other stays last rather
  than landing in the middle of an alphabetical sort. It was an array in
  `hrm/interview/candidates.js` and a second copy of the same array in the browser's
  `interviewConfig.js`, which is two faults rather than one: a studio that started advertising
  somewhere new could not say so, and the two copies could disagree about what the picker offered.
  Add Application, Bulk Entry and the candidate's record all read the one list through the module's
  options endpoint, and **fall back to nothing rather than to an array** — Source is an optional
  combobox on all three, so an empty list is simply a box somebody types into, where a hardcoded
  fallback would be the old problem one read failure away. Deleting a source the studio has used
  says how many candidates name it rather than stranding them. The starting rows are
  [interview-sources.seed.js](../../backend/prisma/seeds/interview-sources.seed.js), with migration
  `20260918160000` writing the same rows for every database, since the seed cannot be run on the
  VPS. The two sources the application writes for *itself* — "Application Form" and "Google sheet
  integration" — deliberately stay in the code: they are what the public form and the sheet importer
  stamp on a candidate and compare against, so renaming a row cannot change what an importer writes.
- **HRM has an Interview module: an application pipeline, fed by hand, in bulk, by CSV, by linked
  Google Sheets, and by the candidates themselves.** **Interview** is a new screen under Human
  Resource Management (`/hrm/interview`), built the way the **Employee Repository** is, because it
  is the same kind of screen — a register of people read by stage. The stages are the header's tab
  row with a count on each: **Yet to Connect** (nobody has spoken to them), **Form Submission
  Pending** (the screening form has gone out and not come back), **Form Submitted** (it is back and
  waiting to be read), **Interview Scheduled**, **Selected**, **On Hold**, and **Rejected last**,
  after every stage a candidate can still move on from — the two places a candidate stops sit
  together at the end, so the five before them are the tabs somebody works every morning. There is
  deliberately no **Shortlisted, Interviewed or Joined** stage: a form that has been read is either
  worth a conversation or it is not, "Interviewed" says only that a meeting happened where the
  interview date already records that, and somebody who has joined is an employee read from the
  Employee Repository. A sheet that says one of those three keeps the word and shows the candidate
  under All, rather than being folded onto a stage that remains — reading "Interviewed" as Form
  Submitted would move a candidate backwards, and as Selected would answer a question nobody has. The action bar is
  **Add Application** as the primary button with a ⋮ after it holding **Bulk Entry**, **Import** and
  **View All Invites**, which is the repository's own arrangement.
  **Add Application** is Add Lead's **dialog** asked of a candidate, and it asks nine controls and
  no more: Candidate Name, Contact and Position Applied For as the three required answers, then
  Email, Experience and Source, and in a second panel **under** them the status pills, the next
  follow-up date and the remarks — stacked down one card, so the pills run across the panel instead
  of wrapping four rows deep in a third of it. The pills offer every stage but **Interview
  Scheduled** and **Rejected**, which cannot be true of an application being written down as it
  arrives and are set on the record instead; they are filtered from the list the server sends rather
  than held as a second list, so a stage the studio adds is offered the same day. There is no **Assign To**: the studio has one HR, so
  there is nobody to assign a candidate to, and a linked sheet's assignment rules are the one place
  a name is still chosen (that value is shown on the record and kept through an edit). An application arrives as a name, a number and the job they are
  after; everything else is either not known yet or is the candidate's own to type. Everything the
  register *can* hold — the department and employment type applied for, qualification, skills,
  languages, pay expectations, notice period, portfolio and résumé, the interview date, time, mode
  and Meet link — is on the record a row opens, which is where an imported or submitted application
  is read and corrected. That is the CRM's own split between Add Lead and the lead popup. A
  candidate is `DBS-INTV-000001`. A row's ⋮ offers View, Edit, **Send Screening Form** and Delete,
  each named plainly since every one of them opens something, with View **disabled rather than taken
  out** — reading a candidate is what pressing the row already does, and a menu that quietly loses
  an entry reads as a permission problem. **Bulk Entry**
  is the CRM's own dialog for candidates — seven fields on a line that fits rather than measures, a
  trailing row that grows as it is typed into — and sends no status, so every row lands in Yet to
  Connect. **Import** is the shared CSV
  dialog every module's table uses, on the same two endpoints.
  **A candidate can be sent the screening form to fill in themselves.** Send Screening Form,
  from the row ⋮ or from the record's own footer, raises a secure one-time link
  (`/interview/apply/<code>`) with a hashed token, a short code, an expiry and the candidate's own
  email or phone as the gate — `employee_onboarding_invite`'s design for a candidate rather than a
  new hire — hands over the message to send with its own Copy, and emails it. Sending it moves the
  candidate to Form Submission Pending, submitting moves them to Form Submitted, and cancelling
  moves them back. The candidate's form asks what a hiring form asks and **none** of what the
  onboarding form asks: no Aadhaar, no PAN, no bank details, no family, no emergency contact, since
  none of that is anybody's business until an offer is made
  ([InterviewPage](../../frontend/src/components/hrm/InterviewPage.js),
  [the candidate's form](../../frontend/src/components/interview/PublicApplicationForm.js),
  [backend module](../../backend/src/modules/hrm/interview/)). **Settings → Integrations is a group
  now, with Sales and Interview under it, and they are the same screen**: the Sales integration was
  lifted out of the Settings overlay into one component
  ([GoogleSheetsIntegrationSection](../../frontend/src/components/settings/GoogleSheetsIntegrationSection.js))
  and drawn twice from a config — the cards, the Google account, the Link Google Sheet dialog with
  its assignment rules, Sync Now, Preview Import, Edit and Delete all behave exactly as they did
  for Sales, and the Interview copy writes candidates rather than leads. The Interview sheets live
  on their own tables and their own routes (`/api/hrm/interview/google-sheet-links`,
  `/api/hrm/interview/google-oauth`), poll on the same two-minute interval, and use the one Google
  account per login the CRM already connects; a consent started from Interview returns to the
  Interview screen. A sheet's Status column is folded onto the stages above and a stage the list
  has no name for is kept as typed; a deleted candidate is remembered so the sheet does not bring
  them back. **Who reaches it:** one new permission, **`HRM / Interview`** — the screen, every
  route under `/api/hrm/interview`, and the Settings → Integrations → Interview section (which also
  needs `SETTINGS / Integrations`) — written into migration `20260918100000` with the five tables
  and granted to `SUPER_ADMIN` there; the seed writes no other grants, so **re-save the HR roles
  that should hold it in Role Management**. The application pipeline adds no second permission:
  `20260918130000` renames the two stages on any rows already written, adds the application's own
  columns and the invite table, and everything in it answers to the same `HRM / Interview`. The
  candidate's own form answers by token, rate-limited and never cached, the way the onboarding
  form's does. Nothing changes for the Sales integration's permissions.
- **The CRM's Quotation Calculator prices a lead from Settings, and its total becomes the Signed
  Quotation.** Opened from a lead (Proceed Next → Quotation), the calculator lists every service
  line by line as Settings → Project → Services priced it — "Studio / In-House · Full Session ·
  10-12 Hours (Full) · 55 inch, ₹9,000" — and every deliverable as Settings → Project →
  Deliverables describes it, with its Company / Brand Name, Length / Size, Frame Inches and Price;
  each row takes a quantity, and Qty × price joins the Base Package Price and Extras before the
  discount. The built-in Albums / Frames list, which was priced nowhere, is gone. **Apply to Lead**
  (Quotation Management Read / Write) writes the total onto the lead with the choices it was built
  from, so the calculator reopens where it was left, and converting that lead prefills Create
  Project's **Signed Quotation** with the total ahead of the lead's budget — still editable there.
  Migration `20260917100000_crm_lead_quoted_amount` adds the two lead columns
  ([quotation-calculator/controller.js](../../backend/src/modules/sales/quotation-calculator/controller.js),
  [quotationCalculator.mjs](../../frontend/src/lib/quotationCalculator.mjs),
  [CRMPage.js](../../frontend/src/components/sales/CRMPage.js),
  [SalesManagerPage.js](../../frontend/src/components/sales/SalesManagerPage.js)).
- **A service can be sold by size.** Add / Edit Service asks **Size**, Yes or No (No by default).
  Yes puts a **Size** on every pricing line — typed, with the studio's service size lists under
  Settings → Project → Sizes (LED Wall Sizes, TV Sizes, and any added) suggested as you type — so
  the same shoot type and timing is priced once per size; a size that matches a list entry is
  linked to it, one the lists do not hold is kept as typed. On a project or quotation line the
  Size picker offers every priced size together, and picking one prices the quotation line; until
  a size is picked such a service prices as before. The Services table's Size column reads Yes or
  No. Migration `20260916150000_service_definition_size_enabled` adds the column; every existing
  service reads No and prices exactly as it did
  ([servicePricing.mjs](../../frontend/src/lib/servicePricing.mjs),
  [ServiceDefinitionDialog.js](../../frontend/src/components/project-management/ServiceDefinitionDialog.js)).
- **A deliverable is described in words and priced once.** Settings → Project → Deliverables →
  Add / Edit Deliverable asks Deliverable Name, No.of.Count, Image Selection, Remarks, **Deliverable
  Type** (Image or Video), **Company / Brand Name**, **Length / Size**, **Frame Inches** and
  **Price** — the three new ones typed as words — and the Deliverables table lists the same
  columns in the same order. The per-kind pricing tabs the dialog carried for a day are gone; a
  deliverable that was priced under kinds keeps those prices behind the scenes and its quotation
  lines still read them, while a quotation line for any other deliverable takes the deliverable's
  Price where one is set and prices as it always did where none is. Migration
  `20260917090000_deliverable_definition_brand_length_frame` adds the three text columns;
  `20260916140000_deliverable_definition_kind_pricing` still adds the type column. Nothing filters
  or prices on the words
  ([DeliverableDefinitionDialog.js](../../frontend/src/components/project-management/DeliverableDefinitionDialog.js),
  [ProjectSettingsPanel.js](../../frontend/src/components/project-management/ProjectSettingsPanel.js)).
- **A service is priced per shoot type and session timing.** Settings → Project → Services →
  Add / Edit Service no longer asks for Shoot Type, Session Timing or Duration on their own: the
  **Pricing** section, always on screen, is where they are answered. A service stays offered for
  everything it already was, and a priced line adds its shoot type and timing to that, so a price
  can always be reached and nothing already saved is narrowed. Each line names its own
  booking from two dropdowns — Shoot Type (Studio / In-House or Outdoor / On-Location) and Session
  Timing (Half, Full, or Hours / Minutes, which is offered for Studio only) — shows the duration
  that booking runs — "4-5 Hours (Half)" or "10-12 Hours (Full)" to begin with, and typed over
  where the studio's sessions run differently — and takes its price. On an Hours / Minutes line the duration is **typed**, in
  whole hours, and the price is for that many hours — "Studio, 3 hours, ₹2,800" — so a service may
  carry several hourly lines for different lengths. **Add
  Pricing** adds a line and each line has **Remove**; the lines are the studio's own list rather
  than tied to the service's Shoot Type and Session Timing above, a booking priced twice is refused
  until one line goes, and only complete lines with a figure are saved. The
  Services table's Price cell lists them ("Half ₹5,000 · Full ₹9,000 · ₹1,000/h"). A service added
  to a quotation's shoot takes the row matching that shoot's type and session timing as its unit
  price — an hourly shoot, the line priced for exactly its length in whole hours; where the pricing
  does not cover the shoot the line prices as it always did. Migration `20260916130000_service_definition_timing_pricing` adds
  the column; every existing service keeps its flat price until its pricing is filled in
  ([servicePricing.mjs](../../frontend/src/lib/servicePricing.mjs),
  [ServiceDefinitionDialog.js](../../frontend/src/components/project-management/ServiceDefinitionDialog.js),
  [QuotationGeneratorPanel.js](../../frontend/src/components/sales/QuotationGeneratorPanel.js)).
- **A deliverable can carry a shoot type, session timings and a duration, read by a project's
  Choose Deliverables picker.** Migration `20260916120000_deliverable_definition_shoot_type_timing`
  adds the four columns, the API accepts them on a deliverable, and a project offers a deliverable
  only on a shoot of its type where one is set — the rule it already applies to services. The
  Add / Edit Deliverable dialog does not ask for them: it asks for Price, No.of.Count, Size, Image
  Selection and Remarks as before, and leaves whatever a deliverable holds for the three untouched,
  so every existing deliverable stays offered on every shoot. The timing controls the service
  dialog used to draw live in one shared module
  ([definitionTimingFields.js](../../frontend/src/components/project-management/definitionTimingFields.js),
  [DeliverableDefinitionDialog.js](../../frontend/src/components/project-management/DeliverableDefinitionDialog.js)).
- **DBS Operations OS installs to a phone's home screen and a desktop dock, and opens in its own
  window with no browser chrome.** Chrome, Edge and Android offer **Install**; iOS Safari installs it
  through **Share → Add to Home Screen**. It launches at `/`, which lands on Employees when there is
  a session and on the login screen when there is not, so nothing about who sees what changes — the
  installed window is the same application behind the same sign-in
  ([manifest.js](../../frontend/src/app/manifest.js), [layout.js](../../frontend/src/app/layout.js),
  [public/icons/](../../frontend/public/icons/)).

  Installing is all this adds, and it needs no service worker: Chrome dropped that requirement for
  the menu item in version 108 on Android and 112 on the desktop. Offline behaviour and push
  notifications are deliberately not here and are a later piece of work — a screen with no network
  still shows the browser's own error page, exactly as it did in a tab.

  Where the command lives differs per platform and is worth knowing before anybody reports it
  missing: Android Chrome ⋮ → **Add to Home screen**; Chrome and Edge on the desktop ⋮ → **Cast,
  save and share** → **Install page as app**; iPhone and iPad **Share** → **Add to Home Screen**;
  Safari on macOS **File** → **Add to Dock**. Firefox on the desktop cannot install any site and is
  not a fault in this. It also requires HTTPS, which the production domain has and a plain-HTTP
  staging host would not.

  Both names of the iOS capability tag are sent. Next renders the standard
  `mobile-web-app-capable`, which iOS has honoured only since 17; an iPhone older than that reads
  `apple-mobile-web-app-capable` alone and, without it, opens the home screen icon inside Safari
  with the address bar still there.

  The mark on the home screen is the one the application ships with, not a favicon uploaded in
  Settings: a manifest is read once, when the icon is created, so branding it from the database
  would reach only installs made after the change and would make installing depend on the API
  answering. Android is given its own **maskable** pair — the mark at 70% on a full-bleed white
  square — because a launcher cuts its own circle or squircle out of the icon and keeps the central
  80%, which would otherwise clip the B and the C.

  **No new permission.** The manifest adds no module, no feature and no endpoint, and reads no data,
  so there is nothing to grant in Role Management and nothing to seed or migrate — every screen
  reached from an installed window is guarded exactly as it was from a browser tab. Nothing needs
  re-saving and no role reaches anything new.
- **The WhatsApp usage limits are now set in Settings → User Settings → WhatsApp.** The three
  numbers that decide when a CRM is warned they are messaging too hard — different clients an hour,
  messages an hour, messages a day — lived in the code, overridable only by an environment variable
  on the server. A studio whose CRMs genuinely work at a hundred messages an hour therefore had two
  ways to stop being told off for it hourly: ask a developer, or learn to read past a red banner
  every afternoon. The second is what happens, and a warning people have trained themselves to
  ignore is worse than none, because it is still there when the pattern is finally real. The three
  sit in a card under the studio switch, and **a box left empty means the app's own number**, shown
  in the box as a placeholder so nobody has to clear a field to find out what it was — that is the
  only way back once a number has been typed. Saving needs `SETTINGS / User Settings` Read / Write,
  the same permission the switch beside it and the per-CRM blocks answer to; everybody else sees the
  limits in force as a sentence and no form. Nothing is blocked by any of it and no session is
  touched: the limits decide what a CRM is *told*, and one saved here reaches a panel that is
  already open on its next poll. The screen says outright that **different clients an hour is the
  one to raise last** — WhatsApp bans numbers for looking like a broadcaster, and from the outside a
  broadcast is reach, not volume, so raising the two message counts is a far smaller decision than
  raising that one. Limits are stored per studio on the existing WhatsApp settings row, and a saved
  number beats the environment variable, which still beats the built-in default — so a server
  already carrying those variables does not change behaviour on this deploy
  ([WhatsAppLimitsCard.js](../../frontend/src/components/settings/WhatsAppLimitsCard.js),
  [usage.js](../../backend/src/modules/sales/whatsapp-chat/usage.js),
  [repository.js](../../backend/src/modules/sales/whatsapp-chat/repository.js)).

### Changed
- **A deliverable is asked what its own type needs.** Settings → Project → Deliverables →
  Add / Edit Deliverable asks Deliverable Name, **Company / Brand Name** and **Deliverable Type**,
  then what that type needs — **Size** and **Frame Inches** for an image, **Length** for a video —
  followed by **Price** and, as always, No.of.Count, Image Selection and Remarks. Size and Length
  were one box labelled "Length / Size", so a deliverable switched from Image to Video read its
  print size as a running time and a video could keep a frame size nothing explained. They are
  stored apart now: switching type and back still finds what was typed, and what the type does not
  ask for is neither shown nor saved — the server clears it whatever the form sends. The
  Deliverables table reads in the dialog's own order, with Size, Length and Frame Inches as
  columns of their own. Migration `20260917110000_deliverable_definition_image_size_video_length`
  renames the shared column to the size it held and adds the length beside it
  ([DeliverableDefinitionDialog.js](../../frontend/src/components/project-management/DeliverableDefinitionDialog.js),
  [ProjectSettingsPanel.js](../../frontend/src/components/project-management/ProjectSettingsPanel.js)).
- **The Services table lists a service's bookings as rows, not as a sentence.** Settings → Project
  → Services ran every priced booking together in one Price cell — "Studio Full ₹9,000 · Studio
  Half ₹4,000 · Outdoor Full ₹10,000" — so what a booking was priced for could not be read, sorted
  or filtered apart. The columns are **Services, Remarks, Size, Shoot Type, Session Timing,
  Duration, Size, Price, Remarks** now, and each pricing line is a row of its own folded under its
  service, the way the quotation register folds a chain's earlier versions: a fourth shoot type is
  a fourth row. Every priced service folds, a single booking included, so the head always answers
  for the service — the shoot types it covers, whether it is sold by size, the range its lines are
  priced in — and each booking inside answers for itself, the size it is priced at among them.
  **Size** reads Yes or No on the head; a booking inside names its size, or shows nothing where
  the service is not sold by one. Edit and Delete stay on the service, since a line is edited
  inside its dialog
  ([ProjectSettingsPanel.js](../../frontend/src/components/project-management/ProjectSettingsPanel.js)).
- **Deliverables count "No.of.Count".** The Deliverables table in Settings → Project and the
  Add / Edit Deliverable dialog both head the count as "No.of.Count" rather than "No.of.Photos"
  ([ProjectSettingsPanel.js](../../frontend/src/components/project-management/ProjectSettingsPanel.js),
  [DeliverableDefinitionDialog.js](../../frontend/src/components/project-management/DeliverableDefinitionDialog.js)).
- **A service is a studio service or an outdoor one, and Hours / Minutes is a studio timing.**
  Shoot Type on Settings → Project → Services → Add / Edit Service is a radio now rather than two
  tick boxes, so each service names the one kind of shoot it is for; a service wanted for both is
  entered once for each. Session Timing offers **Hours / Minutes** only while Studio / In-House is
  chosen — choosing Outdoor hides it and unticks it, so a timing the screen does not show is never
  left on the record — and the whole Session Timing row stays disabled until a shoot type is chosen,
  since what it offers depends on that answer. The dialog no longer asks for **Price**,
  **Quantity**, **Timing Options** or **Size**: a service's price and quantity are settled on the
  project that uses it, every session-timed service is booked from the studio's Service Session
  Timing set without being asked, and values a service already holds for any of the four are
  carried through a save untouched. The Services table no longer lists Quantity, Session Timing or Timing Options either; it shows the name, the price lines, the shoot type, the size and remarks. A service saved earlier with both types ticked, or neither, opens with no
  type selected and has to be given one before it can be updated; until then it is offered on
  every shoot exactly as before
  ([ServiceDefinitionDialog.js](../../frontend/src/components/project-management/ServiceDefinitionDialog.js)).
- **Date of Birth (or) EDOD no longer refuses a Qua Qua lead.** It was the one field beyond the four
  every vertical asks for that could stop a save, in Add Lead, in the lead popup and in Bulk Entry
  alike — and a lead very often arrives before the date does: the call ended before anybody asked,
  the mother has no scan date yet, the enquiry came through a planner. Demanding it then bought a
  date somebody invented, which is worse than an empty one, because the **Newborns** and
  **Birthdays** tabs schedule shoots off it. The field is unchanged in every other way — still asked
  on the Qua Qua form and in Bulk Entry, still stored in its own column, still what those two tabs
  read — it is simply filled in when it is known rather than before the lead may be written down.
  What remains required is the same on all three verticals: Enquire Name, Contact, Assigned Branch
  and Estimated Value (and Bulk Entry's shorter line, which asks for a name, a number and a branch).
  The `CNA` rule beside it now waives Estimated Value alone, since the date it also used to waive is
  no longer demanded of anybody ([CRMPage.js](../../frontend/src/components/sales/CRMPage.js),
  [crmBulkImport.mjs](../../frontend/src/lib/crmBulkImport.mjs)).

### Fixed
- **Bulk Entry's note field is labelled "Description" again.** A stray typo had it reading
  "De  scription" on every row of the dialog
  ([crmBulkImport.mjs](../../frontend/src/lib/crmBulkImport.mjs)).

### Fixed
- **Assigned Branch in the lead popup shows the branch the lead is filed under, whatever its text
  says.** The picker is built from the branch register, and `Dropdown` shows its placeholder for a
  value it cannot find among its options — so a lead imported as `Erode`, against the branch
  `Erode TN`, read as a lead with no branch at all even though its `branch_id` was right. The field
  now reads that reference rather than the text: `Erode` shows as **Erode TN**, and it does so
  immediately, without waiting for the migration that corrects the stored text. Text naming no
  branch — `viza`, `erd`, `Out of Branch Location` — shows **nothing**, which reads as the question
  it is: this lead is filed under no branch, choose one. Nothing is written by any of it, so the
  stored text survives until a branch is actually picked, and a branch just chosen wins over the
  reference the lead arrived with, so the field cannot snap back under the hands of whoever chose
  it. The board's rows carry `branchId` and the branches endpoint carries each option's `id` to make
  this possible ([CRMPage.js](../../frontend/src/components/sales/CRMPage.js),
  [leadQueryService.js](../../backend/src/modules/sales/crm-leads/leadQueryService.js),
  [branchOptions.js](../../backend/src/services/branchOptions.js)).

### Fixed
- **An imported lead's Assigned Branch is now the studio's branch, not the file's spelling of it.**
  A CSV's **Nearest Branch** column is typed by whoever exported it, usually as the place without the
  state the register ends the name with — `Erode` for `Erode TN` — and while the `branch_id` beside
  it was always resolved against
  the branch table, the text was stored exactly as the file wrote it. Assigned Branch in the lead
  popup and in Add Lead is a picker built from that same table, so an imported lead matched no
  option in it: a lead filed under the right branch looked like a lead nobody had filed. The import
  now takes the name and the id from **one match against the branch register**, so the two cannot
  disagree, and the same applies to the Google Sheet sync and to every save through the application.
  Nothing is hard-coded — no branch name appears in any of those files, which is what a test now
  holds them to. Text that names no branch the studio has is kept exactly as it arrived rather than
  blanked, because an unrecognised value is something somebody has to look at. Leads imported before
  this are corrected by the migration, reading each one's branch from the reference it already
  carries and leaving the unmatched ones alone. That correction was run once before, in September,
  and could not hold — the import went on storing the file's own spelling, so every import since put
  it back, which is the half fixed here
  ([referenceMatchers.js](../../backend/src/services/referenceMatchers.js),
  [csvImportService.js](../../backend/src/modules/sales/crm-leads/csvImportService.js)).

### Fixed
- **The org chart draws the people working here, and nobody else.** It left out archived rows and
  nothing else — but an employee row exists from the moment HR sends an onboarding invite and stays
  after somebody leaves, so the chart drew candidates who had not filled in their form and colleagues
  who had been relieved, fired or had absconded, as part of an organisation none of them is in. It
  now draws the three working statuses — **Active, in Training and PIP**, the same list HR's own
  Active tab groups and every employee picker offers. Somebody in training holds a real place in the
  structure and people report to them; leaving them out would leave those reports hanging off nobody
  ([repository.js](../../backend/src/modules/hrm/org-chart/repository.js)).

  Three consequences, each deliberate: the **count under a card** is taken over the same people, so a
  node reading "2" opens on two rather than on seven; a **chain running up through somebody who has
