  left stops at the last person still here**, exactly as it already did for an archived manager, and
  is a vacancy rather than a broken chain; and asking for the chart of somebody it does not draw
  answers **"That employee is not on the reporting chart"** — the old "Employee not found" was a
  strange thing to read while looking at that employee's own record, which is where HR reaches it
  from when reviewing or offboarding somebody.

### Fixed
- **Reports & Analytics shows you your own figures and your team's, not the whole studio's.** The
  three usage tabs — **WhatsApp Usage**, **Application Usage** and **Work Hours** — were narrowed by
  nothing: anybody who could open the screen read every colleague's messages, lead edits and measured
  hours, by name, for any range they chose. The call tabs were narrowed by the lead each call was
  against, which is not the same question. All nine tabs now answer the rule the rest of the
  application already uses: **a row is yours if your name is on it, or the name on it belongs to
  somebody who reports to you**, at any depth. `ADMIN / Branch Administration` sees the branches that
  role reaches, and a Super Admin is narrowed by nothing
  ([service.js](../../backend/src/modules/sales/crm-reports/service.js),
  [DATA-SCOPE.md D34](../project-state/DATA-SCOPE.md)).

  The **Employee filter** follows the same rule — it offers only people whose figures you would
  actually be shown, since an option that answers an empty table reads as "this person did nothing" —
  and it now lists **Active employees only**, narrower than the three working statuses every other
  picker in the application offers. Typing a name past the dropdown is resolved under the same scope,
  so a colleague's name no longer resolves to their handset number. Attribution keeps the wider list:
  somebody in training who spent the week on the phone still has their calls counted.

### Fixed
- **The `SUPER_ADMIN` role now actually holds everything: every permission, at Read / Write, in every
  branch.** The role is described as "full access across every branch" and was granted **nothing** —
  no permissions, no branches. It went unnoticed because the one person carrying it also had
  `SUPER_ADMIN` on their *login*, and the server short-circuits on that column without reading a
  grant. Give the role to somebody whose login says `EMPLOYEE` — which is how an administrator is
  added — and a role named SUPER_ADMIN granted them nothing at all: no screens, no branches, every
  guard refusing them. It could not be fixed from Administration → Roles either, which refuses to
  edit a system role.

  Filled twice over, because the two kinds of database need different routes. A migration writes the
  grants into every database that already exists, production included,
  where the seed cannot be run; the seed writes them for a fresh machine, where the migration runs
  before there is anything to grant. Both are re-run safe: they add what is missing and change
  nothing else, so a database where some of this was granted by hand keeps it. A permission added
  from here on is granted to the role in the same migration that creates it — [IAM.md](../project-state/IAM.md)
  step 10 carries the four lines, and `test/iam-super-admin-grants.test.js` fails if the two ever
  drift apart ([iam-roles-permissions.js](../../backend/prisma/seeds/iam-roles-permissions.js)).

  **Apply it on the VPS with `npx prisma migrate deploy`.** Anyone already carrying the role gains
  everything it was always meant to grant the moment it runs.

### Fixed
- **The CNA count in the lead popup follows the save, not the press.** Pressing the CNA pill posted
  an unanswered attempt immediately, so six presses — easily done, since nothing on screen said the
  press had already been written — walked a lead to **Rejected** without anybody saving anything,
  and there was no way to un-choose it. The pill now selects a status like every other one, and the
  attempt is recorded when the lead is saved: **CNA and Proceed Next**, or choosing CNA and pressing
  Update. The limit is unchanged — the sixth attempt still reads `CNA (6)` and the seventh still
  rejects the lead — and the notice now reports what happened rather than which button was pressed
  ([CRMPage.js](../../frontend/src/components/sales/CRMPage.js)).

  It also fixes a quieter version of the same fault: `requestedStatus` is CNA for a lead that is
  *already* CNA, so saving an unrelated edit on one — a number corrected, a remark added — used to
  count another unanswered call. The count now follows the decision, meaning the CNA button or the
  pill pressed in this popup, so opening and updating a CNA lead leaves its tally alone.

  **The board's info panel is deliberately unchanged**: its CNA pill still records an attempt on the
  press. That pane is the quick pass down a list of leads nobody answered, with no form being filled
  in and no Update to reach for.

### Fixed
- **Escape in the Quotation Calculator closed the lead popup underneath instead of the calculator.**
  Opening the calculator from a lead left two overlays on screen, and the calculator was the one
  overlay on the board that never registered with the Escape arbiter — so the press reached the lead
  popup below it, which shut, while the calculator stayed standing over an empty board. It takes its
  turn on the stack now: the first press closes the calculator, the second closes the lead. It
  registers on its `open` flag rather than for the whole mount, which is what puts it *above* the
  popup — the component stays mounted for as long as the popup does, and a child's effect runs
  before its parent's, so registering unconditionally would have pressed the same fault the other
  way round ([CRMPage.js](../../frontend/src/components/sales/CRMPage.js)).

### Changed
- **A lead written down as `CNA` is no longer asked what the job is worth, or for a date of birth.**
  `CNA` typed into **Enquire Name** is how a caller nobody has reached gets recorded: the phone rang,
  the call did not connect, and who they are is the one thing not known yet. **Estimated Value** and,
  on Qua Qua, **Date of Birth (or) EDOD** are both required on every other lead and cannot be
  answered from a call that never connected — so the form demanded two invented answers, or the call
  went unrecorded. Neither is asked for on a lead by that name now, in the lead popup and in Add
  Lead alike. Everything that makes the call findable still is: the name, the contact and the
  assigned branch. Matching is on the name as typed — `cna`, ` Cna ` — and on that alone: `CNA
  Traders` and `cna kumar` are people, and both fields stay required for them. The CNA **status** is
  a different thing and deliberately changes nothing: an unanswered attempt against a lead whose
  client is known still has a value and a date to give ([CRMPage.js](../../frontend/src/components/sales/CRMPage.js)).

### Changed
- **A branch is matched by its full name, with or without the state keyword, and by nothing
  shorter.** `Erode TN` and `Erode` are both the Erode branch; `erd`, `viza` and `Tel` are not
  branches but guesses at one, and are now refused wherever a branch is read off text — the CRM's
  Nearest Branch, a project's or quotation's source branch, a cash entry, a payment request, a task.
  One short spelling used to be forgiven as a hard-coded alias, **`Vizag` for `Visakhapatnam AP`**,
  and it is not any more: a name written into the code that the branch register has never heard of
  is the fault this rule exists to prevent, and a studio that wants `Vizag` to be a branch renames
  the branch. The state keyword is dropped as a word rather than matched against a list of states,
  so a register spelling its states out in full — `Bangalore Karnataka` — needs no edit. A bare name
  two branches would share (`Erode TN` and `Erode KA` both answering to `Erode`) is **ambiguous** and
  matches neither, instead of quietly taking whichever loaded first, which is how a record ends up
  filed in the wrong state ([referenceMatchers.js](../../backend/src/services/referenceMatchers.js)).

  **Nothing loses its branch over the change.** The 3,667 leads typed `Vizag` keep the branch they
  were filed under: the migration takes each record's text *from the reference it already carries*,
  so they read `Visakhapatnam AP` before the stricter rule is ever asked about them, and the same is
  done for every other table that stores a branch beside a branch id. Records whose text names no
  branch the studio has are left exactly as they are.

### Changed
- **A task id reads `DBS-TK-00001`.** Every other business id the studio mints wears the `DBS-`
  prefix — `DBS-BK-DD-ERD-000001`, `DBS-EID-QQ-HYD-000007` — and a task was the one record answering
  to a shape of its own, `TSK-000001`. Unlike a booking or an enquiry it carries no vertical and no
  branch: a task names its department and branch in its own columns, and work is handed across both
  often enough that either in the id would be wrong the first time a task moved. Tasks already
  raised are renamed by the migration so the list is one format rather than two — `TSK-000010`
  becomes `DBS-TK-00010`, the same number. The text quoting a code inside the activity log and the
  notices already sent is deliberately left alone: it records what was said at the time
  ([service.js](../../backend/src/modules/hrm/tasks/service.js)). Files attached before the rename keep
  their existing bucket keys and still download; only new attachments land under the new folder name.

### Changed
- **Bulk Entry leaves a batch in Yet to connect instead of marking it Interested.** Every row typed
  into ⋮ → **Bulk Entry** was saved with the status **Interested**, which said somebody had spoken to
  a name that had only just been written down — and took a batch of twenty names off an event
  straight past the queue of calls still to be made. Those rows now carry no status, which is what
  puts them in **Yet to connect**: that label is not a stored word or a pill anyone presses, it is
  what a lead with an empty status column reads as, so leaving it blank is how a lead is filed there
  and setting a status is what takes it out again. The Next Follow-up Date typed beside a row is
  kept, so a batch waits in that queue with the day it is to be called already on it. **Add Lead is
  unchanged** and still opens on Interested — one client being written up as they are worked is not
  a list still to be rung — and leads entered before this keep the Interested they were saved with
  ([crmBulkImport.mjs](../../frontend/src/lib/crmBulkImport.mjs)).

- **Today Pending Followups is now the follow-up calls owed today, cut by Followup like the other two
  pending tabs.** It was cut by **Yet to connect** instead, on the reasoning that the tab was the
  calls still to be *made* today and a lead already at Followup had been spoken to. That made the
  three pending tabs two different questions wearing one name — Today asked "who has nobody rung",
  Yesterday and Overall asked "what follow-up is owed" — and it lost the case in between: a lead at
  Followup with a call booked for today was owed a call **today**, appeared in none of the three
  tabs, and then surfaced in Yesterday Pending the next morning as though it had been missed. The
  three are one question asked at three dates now: a lead at Followup, due today, due yesterday, or
  older. "Nobody has rung this" keeps its own tab, **Yet to connect**, which is the untouched queue
  and is unchanged ([leadQueryService.js](../../backend/src/modules/sales/crm-leads/leadQueryService.js)).

  Same on all three verticals. On Qua Qua the status read is still the **occasion's** where a lead
  sits inside a newborn or birthday window, so a birthday being worked is chased at that birthday's
  status rather than the lead's own — that override is untouched. Overall Pending still also holds
  the follow-ups whose date was never filled in or cannot be read as a date: they are owed a call on
  a day the board cannot name, and without that they sit in no pending tab at all.

  Expect the counts to move. Leads at Followup dated today now appear in Today Pending where they
  showed nowhere before, and leads nobody has rung that happened to carry today's date drop out of it
  — they are in Yet to connect, where they always were.

### Changed
- **The duplicate leads in the CRM were cleaned up, and the scripts that did it have been removed
  again.** The typed form refuses a second lead for a number already on a vertical, but the CSV, the
  linked Google Sheet and the Callyzer webhook are deliberately outside that rule — so the same client
  re-enquiring over months arrived as a new lead each time, and production held **3,154 clients with
  3,865 leads too many**. That was not only untidy: the duplicate check runs on *every save*, so an
  employee editing any one of those leads was refused with "already holds this contact number" for a
  reason that had nothing to do with their edit.

  Two throwaway scripts did the work on 17 September 2026 — one read-only report, one resolver that
  kept the newest lead per client, set it to Followup and soft-deleted the rest. Both are deleted now
  that the job is done; they are in the history at `8f8dde2` if the same thing is ever needed again.
  **The leads they removed are recoverable**: `deleted_at` was stamped rather than the rows removed,
  one timestamp per run (`2026-09-17 12:48:56` for the first 96, `2026-09-17 13:34:29` for the
  remaining 3,769), so `UPDATE sales_crm_lead SET deleted_at = NULL WHERE deleted_at = '<stamp>'`
  restores a run in full.

  **The import paths are unchanged**, which was the studio's decision: a file arrives as it arrives
  and refusing half of it would lose the half nobody looked at. So duplicates will accumulate again
  from the sheet sync, and the same refusal will come back for the clients they land on.

### Added
- **A column filter's dropdown now offers the values that column actually holds, on the tab being
  read.** Every dropdown filter on Leads & Followups was built from a register rather than from the
  data: the whole Sales roster under **CRM**, every branch the studio maintains under **Assigned
  Branch**, the full shoot-type list under the three columns that use it, and every status the module
  can produce under **Status**. Most of a menu was therefore values no row in front of the reader
  held — pick one and the board empties. They read like a spreadsheet's filter now: open the menu on
  Qua Qua → Yet to connect and you get the CRMs who hold those leads, not the fifty people on the
  roster ([leadQueryService.js](../../backend/src/modules/sales/crm-leads/leadQueryService.js),
  [CRMPage.js](../../frontend/src/components/sales/CRMPage.js)).

  The list is the server's answer, because the board is paged — the browser holds a hundred rows of a
  vertical that has thirty thousand, and a menu built from those would change with every scroll. It
  is narrowed by everything the tab is narrowed by (board, tab, date range, the reader's own data
  scope, the other columns' filters) with one deliberate exception: **a column's own filter does not
  narrow its own menu**, or choosing a value would leave a menu holding only that value and no way
  back. Asked when a menu opens rather than up front, so columns nobody opens cost nothing, and the
  register stays the fallback while the answer is in flight or if the request fails. The Calls board
  answers the same way from the call log.

### Added
- **Leads nobody has been given read `Unassigned`, and have a tab of their own.** A lead with no CRM
  against it — nobody in Assign To — used to wear whatever status it carried, or *Yet to connect*
  when it carried none, so the queue of leads waiting to be handed to somebody was invisible: the
  only way to find them was to sort the CRM column and count. The Status column now reads
  **Unassigned** for those leads, and a tab of that name sits beside Yet to connect on all three
  verticals with its own count. It is read **before** the lead's own status, which is what the
  studio asked for: a lead saying Converted with nobody on it is a lead nobody owns, and showing it
  as Converted is how it stays that way. Like Yet to connect, nothing stores the word and no pill
  offers it — it is worked out from the lead each time it is read, so putting somebody on the lead
  brings its own status straight back, untouched underneath. The Status column's filter offers it
  beside the other two derived labels
  ([leadQueryService.js](../../backend/src/modules/sales/crm-leads/leadQueryService.js),
  [CRMPage.js](../../frontend/src/components/sales/CRMPage.js)).

  **Yet to connect and Unassigned are a clean split.** A lead nobody has been given leaves Yet to
  connect, because that tab lists exactly the rows whose Status column reads Yet to connect and
  theirs no longer does — All still holds both, and the two counts add up to what the one used to be.
  The **pending** tabs are deliberately not split that way: they are cut by the status a lead
  actually holds rather than by the label its column prints, so an unassigned lead at Followup still
  appears under the call it owes. Hiding an owed call because nobody has been put on the lead would
  lose the work twice over.

### Added
- **A task can be deleted, not only cancelled.** Cancelling says a real task was called off — the
  holder is told, and it keeps its place under the Cancelled tab. There was no way to say the other
  thing: that a task should never have been raised at all, being a duplicate, a test row or one
  typed against the wrong person, so those sat in the studio's list for good. **Delete Task** is now
  in the row menu and in the task's own footer, behind a confirmation naming the task, offered to
  **whoever gave the task and to a Super Admin** — the holder is not offered it and the server
  refuses them. It is the one verb no status gates, because a task that should not exist should not
  exist whether or not somebody has started it. **The record survives**: the row is stamped rather
  than removed and keeps its holders, its history — including who deleted it and when — and the files
  handed over with it, so a mistaken delete is a column somebody can clear. Everything else treats it
  as gone: it leaves My Tasks, Tasks Given and All Tasks, its id answers the same 404 to every verb,
  to its files and to a link in the bell as a task that never existed, and notices about it are
  cleared with it ([service.js](../../backend/src/modules/hrm/tasks/service.js),
  [TasksPage.js](../../frontend/src/components/hrm/TasksPage.js)). No new permission: it is
  `HRM / Task Management` Read / Write like every other write on that screen, narrowed to the
  task's giver or a Super Admin — so no role gains the ability to delete other people's tasks.

### Added
- **Update Details now sets the Next Follow-up Date across a selection.** Ticking leads on
  Leads & Followups and pressing **Update Details** offered Lead Status, Last Updated Date and CRM,
  so the one thing a batch of leads usually needs after a round of calls — when to call them back —
  still had to be typed lead by lead. The dialog carries **Next Follow-up Date** beside Last Updated
  Date now, and every field stays independent: a blank one means "leave this as it is", so a
  date-only edit does not touch the status and a status-only edit does not touch either date.
  Invalid and Rejected disable both dates, as they already did on the lead form, because those
  statuses end the conversation. On Qua Qua the next follow-up date follows the status onto the
  newborn or birthday occurrence being worked — which is what the follow-up tabs read — while Last
  Updated Date stays on the lead as the record of when it was last worked. Still one request for the
  whole selection, whatever it holds
  ([CRMPage.js](../../frontend/src/components/sales/CRMPage.js),
  [crmLeadService.js](../../backend/src/modules/sales/crm-leads/crmLeadService.js),
  [occasionLeadService.js](../../backend/src/modules/sales/crm-leads/occasionLeadService.js)).
  No permission change: it is the same `POST /api/sales/crm-leads/bulk-status` behind
  `SALES / CRM Lead Select All` Read / Write, so the roles that could bulk-edit before can set this
  and no others can.
- **The Leads & Followups board can be arranged like every other table, and remembers how you left
  it.** Columns are dragged into the order you want by their headings, pulled wider or narrower by
  the grip on a heading's trailing edge, and a column carried to the edge of the table scrolls it
  along underneath — the same gestures the Employee Repository and every other `DataTable` has had.
  **Reset Table Layout** in the ⋮ menu puts all of it back, and appears only once there is something
  to put back.

  The board draws its own `<table>` rather than rendering through `DataTable` — it carries a filter
  row, inline editing, row selection and infinite scroll over tens of thousands of rows — so it had
  none of this: no reorder, no resize, and nothing kept. Hiding twenty of Qua Qua's thirty columns or
  freezing the first two was plain component state, discarded on reload, on leaving the module and
  coming back, and on every other machine. On a thirty-column board that meant re-hiding the same
  twenty columns every visit, and it contradicted what the rest of the application promises.

  All four — order, widths, hidden columns, frozen count — are now one value under `table:crm-<board>`,
  kept by `PreferencesProvider` against the **login**, so the arrangement built at the desk is waiting
  on the laptop. Each vertical keeps its own: Dum Dum, Qua Qua, Corp and Calls do not share a column
  list, so an order made on one would be nonsense on another
  ([useTableArrangement.js](../../frontend/src/components/data-table/useTableArrangement.js)).

  Nothing about the board's appearance changes until you actually arrange something. The first
  resize stores every column's current width before applying the new one, so the table switches from
  sizing itself to its contents without a single column moving except the one that was pulled.

  The rule that moves a column is now
  [tableLayout](../../frontend/src/components/data-table/tableLayout.js)'s `reorderColumnOrder`, shared
  with `DataTable` rather than written twice, so the two tables cannot drift about what a drop on a
  column's left or right edge means.
- **The onboarding invite and the login credentials are messages the studio words itself now, and
  each message template is its own entry on the Settings rail.** Both were sentences hardcoded in
  their dialogs — the one message a studio most wants in its own voice was the one it could not
  touch. They join the cash receipt in Settings as **Employee Invite Template** and **Login
  Credential Template**, seeded with exactly the wording each dialog printed before, so nothing reads
  differently on the day this ships and each box opens on the real message rather than empty.
- **These are the copy-and-send messages, not the emails.** Both screens still send their own email
  with its own wording, untouched. What these govern is the message somebody copies out of the dialog
  and pastes into WhatsApp — the copy that has to work when the mail quota is spent or the mail never
  arrives, which is the case the Copy button exists for.
- **Each template is now a rail entry rather than a child of "Message Template".** The group cost a
  click to reach the one everybody edits and hid the others behind a title that named a category
  instead of a message. Each has its own wording, its own placeholders and its own screen that sends
  it, so each stands under its own name; a message the CRM learns to send is a fourth entry beside
  them rather than a fourth child
  ([SettingsOverlay.js](../../frontend/src/components/settings/SettingsOverlay.js)).

  **The placeholder list is per message.** It was one list, written for the receipt, shown under
  every template — which under a login handover would have offered `{payment mode}` and left it
  unanswered in the message, the exact mistake the list exists to prevent. The invite offers
  `{candidate name}`, `{onboarding link}` and `{expiry date}`; the credentials offer
  `{employee name}`, `{username}`, `{login email}`, `{temporary password}` and `{login link}`
  ([MessageTemplatePanel.js](../../frontend/src/components/settings/MessageTemplatePanel.js)).

  **A template that is missing falls back to the built-in wording.** A studio that has not written
  one, or a row somebody deleted, still gets a usable message — a dialog whose whole job is handing
  over a link must never answer with an empty box. Rendering happens in the browser, because both
  dialogs already hold every fact the message needs; the helper mirrors the server's fill rules
  exactly, and a test pins the two renderers to the same answers so a message cannot read one way
  when Accounts sends it and another when HR does
  ([messageTemplate.mjs](../../frontend/src/lib/messageTemplate.mjs)).

  RBAC: no new permission. Writing stays `SETTINGS / Message Template` `READ_WRITE`; the read guard
  gains `HRM / Employee Management`, which the routes file already said was the rule — each sending
  module's own permission is named there as it arrives, or the screen is refused the data it exists
  to show ([routes.js](../../backend/src/modules/settings/message-templates/routes.js)). The migration
  inserts each template only where the studio has not already written one, so re-running it is not a
  way to lose an edit
  ([migration.sql](../../backend/prisma/migrations/20260916140000_employee_message_templates/migration.sql)).

### Removed
- **Settings → Overall → Project no longer has a Size Options screen.** The rail lists Services
  and Deliverables only, and the option-value table under Size Options is gone with it. The size
  lists themselves are untouched: every project and quotation line still offers them in its Size
  picker, and a service sold by size still has them suggested as a size is typed
  ([ProjectSettingsPanel.js](../../frontend/src/components/project-management/ProjectSettingsPanel.js),
  [SettingsOverlay.js](../../frontend/src/components/settings/SettingsOverlay.js)).
- **Qua Qua no longer asks for a Date or a Contact.** Both were a second way of saying something the
  board already held: the enquiry's day is **Enquiry Date**, and the client's number is **Phone
  Number**. `Contact` did not even hold a value of its own — it resolved to the lead's phone when it
  was empty, which it always was — and the lead popup asked for it **twice**, once in the first row as
  the enquiry number and again in the baby's row directly below. Both columns are empty on every one
  of the 32,916 leads in the table, so nothing is lost by dropping them.

  Gone from the Qua Qua board's columns, from its lead popup, and from the CSV template it downloads
  ([CRMPage.js](../../frontend/src/components/sales/CRMPage.js)), and from the headings the import
  expects ([csvImportService.js](../../backend/src/modules/sales/crm-leads/csvImportService.js)) — the
  template is 32 columns down to 30. Dum Dum and Corp are untouched; Corp keeps its own **Contact
  Number**, which is a different field and really is separate from its Phone Number.

  An existing Qua Qua file that still carries either heading imports exactly as before: an extra
  column the template does not claim has always been ignored rather than refused. The `date` and
  `contact_number` columns stay in the database — `contact_number` is Corp's and is read by the
  Callyzer number matching — so this removes the questions, not the storage.

### Fixed
- **A lead imported from a Google Sheet or a CSV now shows the remark it arrived with.** Its Remarks
  tab read "No remarks yet.", the Remarks column in the table read "—", and the remark was in the
  database the whole time. Remarks are stored in two columns: the app writes `remarkEntries`, a list
  carrying the time each note was written and the files clipped to it, while the sheet sync and the
  CSV import write the flat `remarks` column and nothing else — a spreadsheet cell is one piece of
  text with no time against it and nothing to attach to it. Every screen asked for
  `remarkEntries || remarks`, which cannot work: the list is **always** an array, because the API
  answers `[]` for a NULL column, and `[]` is truthy in JavaScript — so the flat column was never
  reached. Reading a lead's remarks is now one function that tests emptiness rather than presence
  ([lib/crmRemarks.mjs](../../frontend/src/lib/crmRemarks.mjs)), used by the table cell, the lead popup
  and the info panel's Remarks tab alike, on Dum Dum, Qua Qua and Corp. Posting a note on such a lead
  keeps the imported remark above the new one rather than replacing it. Nothing about how remarks are
  stored or synced changed, and a lead that already had notes written in the app is unaffected — those
  still win over the flat column, which the sync overwrites on every pass. Bulk Entry's **Description**
  was never affected — it has always written both halves — but it built its remark from a copy of the
  entry shape, so it now builds it from the shared function instead of a copy that can go stale
  ([crmBulkImport.mjs](../../frontend/src/lib/crmBulkImport.mjs)). `test/crm-remarks.test.mjs` holds the rule.
- **A Qua Qua lead with an occasion open is read at the status and the follow-up date it actually
  carries.** Inside a newborn or birthday window the board reads a lead's status, attempt count and
  follow-up date from that occurrence in `sales_crm_lead_occasion` — an occasion is worked as its own
  enquiry, and an occurrence that has been given either still wins. But occurrence rows are written
  by [crmLeadService](../../backend/src/modules/sales/crm-leads/crmLeadService.js) alone — Add Lead, the
  lead popup, a bulk status change — so a lead that arrived by **CSV import or sheet sync has none at
  all**, and all three overrides answered for it as though the occasion had been started and left
  blank ([leadQueryService.js](../../backend/src/modules/sales/crm-leads/leadQueryService.js)).

  `createCrmLead` already names this exact fault: a lead added while one of its windows is open
  "starts that occasion at the status it was added with", because "without this the row would go
  straight into Yet to connect on the strength of an empty occurrence, however plainly the person
  adding it had just spoken to the client". It writes that occurrence and so escapes the trap; the
  importer and the sheet sync never did. So this was an omission rather than a decision, and the rule
  now reaches every lead instead of only the ones typed in — including the ones already imported,
  with no backfill.

  Two things were wrong and both are fixed. The **status** overrides answered `''`, so a lead reading
  Followup in its own Status column was counted at no status: out of Today Pending and Yesterday
  Pending, which ask for Followup, and into Yet to connect, the queue for leads nobody has rung. The
  **follow-up date** override had no fallback at all, so it answered NULL — and `NULL = '<today>'` is
  NULL, so the lead dropped out of Today and both dated pending tabs however plainly its Next
  Followup Date read today, then surfaced in Overall Pending, which takes a NULL follow-up day as
  work owed on a day the board cannot name. A call booked for today, filed as backlog. All three now
  fall back to the lead's own column, and the two status overrides fall back together, because the
  Status column is drawn from one and the tabs filter on the other.

  Measured on a 32,881-lead Qua Qua board: **Today 160 → 179**, **Today Pending 88 → 96**, Yesterday
  Pending 5 → 6, Overall Pending 13,143 → 13,428, and Yet to connect 2,175 → 1,388 — the 787 leads
  that left it carry a real status from their last shoot, which is the point: they have been rung.
  Dum Dum and Corp generate no occasion SQL at all and are unaffected.

- **A CSV imports as the number of leads it holds, and a remark typed over more than one line stays
  on its lead.** A 32,880-row Qua Qua export arrived in the table as 33,569 leads with individual
  days short of the file — both halves of the same fault in the import's CSV reader
  ([csv.js](../../frontend/src/lib/csv.js)). The reader is deliberately lenient about quotes nobody
  escaped, and the rule that recovers from a quote which never closes used to fire on a
  row-completeness test: if the fields gathered so far plus the comma-pieces of the value being read
  reached the header's column count, the row was cut at the line break. **Remarks is the
  second-to-last column of all three CRM templates**, so a single comma in a two-line remark was
  enough to cut a good row in two — the half after the break arriving as a lead of its own carrying
  the remark's tail for a name, and the columns after Remarks lost from both halves. A line break in
  the final column needed no comma at all. The same test failed in the other direction on a quote
  that really was unclosed but held few commas: the reader read on through the rows below and merged
  them into itself until the commas happened to add up, and those rows were never imported.

  The reader now answers the only question that matters — does this quoted field ever close? — by
  reading ahead for the closing quote, within a bounded window so the file is still streamed rather
  than held in memory. A multi-line value keeps its line breaks and stays one lead; an unclosed quote
  costs its own row and no other. The reader has moved out of `CRMPage.js` into
  [lib/csv.js](../../frontend/src/lib/csv.js) beside `parseCsvRows`, where
  [csv-stream.test.mjs](../../frontend/test/csv-stream.test.mjs) holds both directions to account.

  **Already-imported files are not repaired by this.** A file imported before this fix has to be
  removed and imported again to come out right.
- **Frozen columns line up now, on every table that is not the Employee Repository.** Freezing two
  columns on Quotations left the second one sitting on top of the first, or a gap beside it with the
  unfrozen rows scrolling through — and which of the two you got changed with the tab, the filter and
  the rows on screen. The offsets were computed from `column.getSize()`, the size the table *model*
  holds, which is only the width the browser draws where a table turns on `columnResize`: that path
  renders `table-layout: fixed` with a `colgroup` pinning each column to exactly that number. Every
  other table renders `w-full` on the browser's default `auto` layout, where a column is as wide as
  its content and then stretched to fill the container, while the model keeps answering a flat 150px
  for any column that declares no `size` — which is most of them. Employee Repository is the only
  screen in the app passing `columnResize`, so it was the only one where freeze had ever been right,
  and the feature had quietly depended on it. The offsets are measured off the header cells instead,
  re-measured when the browser changes its mind — a window resize, Compact view, a column shown or
  hidden — so freeze is correct under both layouts and stays correct
  ([DataTable.js](../../frontend/src/components/data-table/DataTable.js)).
- **"Freeze 2 columns" now means two columns somebody can read.** On a table with sub-rows —
  Quotations, and anything else passing `expandable` — the expand chevron is a real 34px column in
  front of the data, so freezing one column froze the chevron and nothing else, and the first real
  column only pinned on the second notch. The chevron is still frozen whenever anything is, because
  it belongs to its row and one that scrolled away from the row it opens would be worse than one that
  never pinned; it is simply frozen *with* the first column rather than counted *as* it. Tables
  without a chevron count exactly as they did
  ([tableLayout.js](../../frontend/src/components/data-table/tableLayout.js)).

### Added
- **A Google Sheet rule can now be shared between several people, by percentage.** A rule named one
  employee, so a column value two people work between them could not be written down: the studio
  picked one and moved leads by hand afterwards, which is the job these rules exist to end. **+
  Split** adds a person to a rule and spreads the shares evenly — 50/50, then 34/33/33 — and the
  percentages are then whatever you type, as long as they add to 100. The same person may hold a
  share on as many rules as you like; the rules do not interact.

  A lead cannot be split 60/40, only a run of them can, so each lead goes to whoever is furthest
  below their share. That lands on exactly 6 and 4 out of ten rather than drifting, and the running
  count is **stored**: the poller re-reads every two minutes, and a counter that restarted each pass
  would give a sheet gaining three rows at a time all three to the same person, forever. Editing a
  rule keeps its balance — the counts are carried across on the column, value and person — so
  changing a percentage, or saving anything else on the link, does not restart the split.

  Nothing adjusts the percentages on its own, on leave or otherwise; what is typed is what runs. The
  cover chain is unchanged and still redirects whoever's share lands on somebody who is away, so it
  moves the recipient rather than the split. Import Preview names the share a lead came from, so a
  rule sending consecutive rows to different people does not read as one that keeps changing its
  mind ([leadAssignmentRules.js](../../backend/src/modules/sales/crm-leads/leadAssignmentRules.js),
  migration `20260916120000_sheet_rule_percentage_split`).
- **Settings → Employee Referral now asks how many instalments a referral is paid in, and states what
  falls due in each one.** The schedule was `PAYOUT_MONTHS = [3, 6]`, a constant in the referrals
  service, so a studio that wanted to pay in thirds had to have the application changed. It is asked
  for now, beside the figure it divides: **Number of instalments** is a single box, because "how
  many" is the question a studio has an answer to — **one falls due every three months** from the
  referred employee's joining date, so two are their third month and their sixth, which is exactly
  what the register has always paid, and three are 3-6-9. The two fields sit on one form because
  neither means anything alone: an amount without a schedule is not a payment, and a schedule without
  an amount is not a figure.
- **The section answers the question those two are really being asked: how much falls due in each
  month.** It lists every instalment by its month with the money against it, rather than the
  `₹3,000 × 2` it used to print — which was computed by dividing by two and stopped being true the
  moment a studio paid in three. The amount divides **equally**, with any rounding remainder on the
  last instalment so the parts still add back up to what the referrer was promised: 1,000 in three
  instalments is 333.33, 333.33, 333.34, never 999.99. At most 24; `0`, `2.5` or an empty box is
  refused in those words while it is still being typed, and Save stays disabled until it reads. The
  count is what the form sends and the server applies the interval itself, so the schedule that lands
  in the database cannot disagree with the one previewed
  ([ReferralAmountPanel.js](../../frontend/src/components/settings/ReferralAmountPanel.js),
  [referralPayouts.js](../../frontend/src/components/hrm/referralPayouts.js)).

  **Changing either is never retrospective**, and the months took more than leaving old rows alone. A
  referral already recorded keeps the months it was recorded with even when the employee's record is
  saved again later — the write path runs on *every* employee save, so reading the studio's current
  schedule there would have quietly grafted a twelfth-month instalment onto a referral promised as
  two, months after the fact. An existing referral's schedule is therefore rebuilt from its own
  instalment rows and only a new one reads the setting, which is the same principle that already
  stamps the amount onto the row rather than reading it at payday
  ([service.js](../../backend/src/modules/hrm/referrals/service.js)).

  No permission changes: both routes already answered to `HRM / Employee Referral`, Read to look and
  Read / Write to change, and with Read both fields are read-only and Save is absent rather than
  present and refused. The Employee Referral register is untouched and stays a register — its tabs
  are still All, Outstanding and Settled. The migration defaults every studio to `3,6`, so nothing
  pays differently until somebody changes it
  ([migration.sql](../../backend/prisma/migrations/20260916090000_referral_instalment_months/migration.sql)).

### Removed
- **Two people may now cover each other: the rule refusing a mutual pair of alternatives is gone.**
  Naming Vijay as Abi's alternative while Abi was already Vijay's used to be refused on save —
  *"Two people cannot cover only each other"* — on the grounds that a pair has nobody outside itself
  to hand the work to on a day both are away. True, but not worth a refusal: `availableCover`
  already answers **nobody** for that day, which is the same answer the rule was protecting, and the
  rule cost every studio with two people in a role the pairing it had every reason to want. The
  check is gone from both save paths, create and edit alike
  ([controller.js](../../backend/src/modules/hrm/employees/controller.js)), and so are the three graph
  helpers that existed only to serve it
  ([alternativeChain.js](../../backend/src/services/alternativeChain.js)).
- **The employee form's alternative menus no longer ask the server which people to hide.**
  `GET /api/hrm/employees/:id/alternative-candidates` existed only to narrow those two menus by the
  rule above; with it gone the sole id it could still return is the employee themselves, whom the
  picker already leaves out. The endpoint, its route and its service call are removed, and the form
  now offers everybody working here straight from `coverCandidates`, one round-trip lighter
  ([useCredentialForm.js](../../frontend/src/components/employees/useCredentialForm.js)). No permission
  changes: a route was removed, none added, and nothing new is reachable.

  What did **not** change is the part that does the work. Cover resolution is untouched — a lead
  meant for somebody on leave still goes to the nearest ring of their alternatives that has anybody
  free in it, shared between them, and still stops rather than walking outward past a nominee who is
  available. The two faults that are faults in a single row rather than shapes in the graph are
  still refused: an employee cannot be their own alternative, and the two slots must be different
  people. And the walk's `seen` set matters more now than it did — a pair is a loop the resolver can
  genuinely be handed, so termination has never rested on the graph being acyclic. There is a test
  for exactly that.

### Fixed
- **A percentage split no longer skews on a sheet whose leads mostly have owners already.** The
  assignment rules were asked about every row of every pass, including rows whose lead already had
  a CRM — where the answer is discarded, since rules only ever fill a blank. Asking anyway spent a
  turn of the split each time, so a sheet of a thousand assigned leads, re-read every two minutes,
  churned the balance a thousand times a pass; the handful of genuinely new rows in that pass then
  went to whoever the churn left lowest rather than to whoever was actually furthest below their
  share. The rules are now consulted only where there is a blank to fill
  ([googleSheetsService.js](../../backend/src/modules/sales/crm-leads/googleSheetsService.js)). A
  second gap in the same place: only newly created leads were counted toward the split, so a rule
  that back-filled leads sitting unassigned took shares the stored balance never learned about, and
  the next new leads went to that person again to make up a gap that had already been filled. Every
  lead the rules actually give an owner to now counts.
- **The Qua Qua board now shows its Remarks column, which Dum Dum and Corp have always shown.** The
  column exists on the lead — `remarks` is stored, written by the Remarks composer, and read back on
  every vertical — but Qua Qua was the one table whose column list left it out, so the note somebody
  had just typed against a lead was visible on two of the three boards and nowhere on the third. It
  is not only the table: the Info panel builds its fields from that same list, so Qua Qua's panel
  was missing the row too. The column sits where the other two tables keep it, last before Source
  ([CRMPage.js](../../frontend/src/components/sales/CRMPage.js)). Two paths that feed the board were
  short the same column and are fixed with it — Qua Qua's CSV template now writes a **Remarks**
  heading, which the header check pins to the table's own columns
  ([csvImportService.js](../../backend/src/modules/sales/crm-leads/csvImportService.js)), and the
  Google Sheets sync reads that heading, which it previously did for Dum Dum and Corp alone. That
  mapping moved into the shared map rather than being copied into a third vertical, since all three
  tables now carry the column — the CSV path's own aliases were never per-vertical and already read
  it ([googleSheetsService.js](../../backend/src/modules/sales/crm-leads/googleSheetsService.js)). No
  permission changes: the column is part of the CRM board a role already reaches.
- **Closing the enquiry popup with unsaved edits now asks, instead of throwing them away — from the CRM board and from Contacts alike.** Every
  field on that form — the client details, the event rows, the follow-up date, the status — writes
  into a draft that reaches the server only when **Update** is pressed, and both ways out discarded
  it silently: the **×**, and Escape, which is the same door. On the largest form in the module,
  losing a half-filled lead to the gesture people reach for most. It now asks the question the
  Settings rail has always asked, through the same `useUnsavedGuard` hook rather than a second copy
  that could drift from it — *"Unsaved changes will be discarded"*, **Go Back** against **Discard &
  Close**, with dismissing the dialog keeping the work, because the answer that loses nothing should
  need no decision. Only the popup's own close asks: **Update and Close** and **Update and Proceed
  Next** have already saved by the time they close, and asking there would be a dialog that can only
  ever be wrong. The form reaches the screen two ways that are wired separately — the board renders
  the popup itself, Contacts goes through `LeadPopupHost` — and **both** ask now, through one hook
  and one wording rather than two dialogs that could drift apart, and both are raised on the
  `dialogOverPanel` layer so they land *above* the popup — on a dialog's own layer they drew behind
  it, dimming the form and showing nothing, which reads as a button that did nothing at all. Our
  dialog is the whole of the question: no `beforeunload` warning sits on top of it, because two
  prompts for one decision — ours, and then the browser's generic *"Reload site?"* over it — reads
  as the application asking twice and trusting neither answer.

  **Add Lead asks the same question, and no longer keeps a draft.** It used to hold what was typed
  when it closed — close it, open it again, and the half-written lead was still there, which meant a
  lead abandoned on Monday was sitting in the form on Tuesday under whoever opened it next. The form
  is emptied on close now and the question is asked before that happens, from the **×**, **Cancel**,
  **Escape** and a press on the dim behind it. Only what somebody actually typed counts: the form
  opens carrying today's date, the current time, a vertical, a status and one blank shoot row, and
  prompting on those is how a studio learns to click through warnings without reading them. Saving,
  and choosing to open the existing lead from the duplicate warning, both close without asking —
  each has already decided what happens to the form ([CRMPage.js](../../frontend/src/components/sales/CRMPage.js),
  [crmLeadForm.mjs](../../frontend/src/lib/crmLeadForm.mjs)).

### Added
- **Every new permission this release adds now ships in a migration, so production needs no seed.**
  `backend/prisma/seeds/` cannot be run on the VPS, and a permission that exists only in the
  catalogue therefore never reaches the `permission` table there — while the guard naming it refuses
  the entire studio but a Super Admin, silently, from the moment the code ships. `SALES / CRM Lead
  Select All` and `SALES / CRM Lead Import` were in that state and would have closed the CRM's tick
  boxes and its CSV import to everybody; they are created by migration
  `20260915200000_crm_lead_select_and_import_permissions` now, and the thirteen `SETTINGS` rows
  already were. **Deploying is `npx prisma migrate deploy` and nothing else.** Both migrations are
  written `INSERT ... WHERE NOT EXISTS`, so re-running writes nothing and an environment where the
  seed *has* been run matches the rows rather than duplicating them — the catalogue and the
  migrations carry the same `module`, `feature` and `level`, which is what the seed upserts on. The
  two CRM rows deliberately copy no grants, so a studio decides who may rewrite a whole vertical at
  once; the Settings rows carry the retired `ADMIN / System Settings` grants across so nobody loses
  a screen they had. [IAM.md](../project-state/IAM.md) gains this as step 10 of adding a permission,
  and a test fails on a permission added to the catalogue with no migration behind it
  ([crm-lead-rbac.test.js](../../backend/test/crm-lead-rbac.test.js)). `node
  scripts/auditPermissionCatalogue.js` answers the question on any database, production included:
  it is read-only, and names every permission the code guards that the database does not hold —
  the failure that otherwise shows up only as a screen quietly missing for the whole studio
  ([auditPermissionCatalogue.js](../../backend/scripts/auditPermissionCatalogue.js)).

### Fixed
- **Settings was missing from the profile menu for everybody except the seeded admin, whatever
  their role was granted.** Two checks sat above the permission system and quietly overruled it: the
  top bar withheld the **Settings** row from `user.role === "EMPLOYEE"`, and the overlay then
  narrowed its rail to User Preference alone on the same test. `user.role` is the legacy column, not
  a permission — and every account the studio issues through onboarding carries `EMPLOYEE` in it,
  including accounts whose IAM role is Super Admin. So the door was hidden before anything asked
  what was behind it, and granting a role its Settings sections changed nothing anybody could see.
  Both are gone. The menu row is offered to everybody, because everybody has something in there —
  **User Preference** is their own sidebar order and appearance, which is not the studio's to
  withhold — and what each person finds inside is decided by the thirteen `SETTINGS` permissions and
  nothing else. This is the check CLAUDE.md Rule 4 names: it overrules the guard above it and can be
  granted to nobody ([AppTopbar.js](../../frontend/src/components/app-shell/AppTopbar.js),
  [SettingsOverlay.js](../../frontend/src/components/settings/SettingsOverlay.js)).

### Changed
- **My Profile shows the whole record you filled in when you joined, instead of eleven assorted
  facts.** The Personal Information tab stated a name, an ID, a department and seven other things
  in read-only text boxes — a summary rather than a record, so the one place somebody can check
  what the studio holds about them, and spot what needs correcting, was showing a tenth of it. The
  tab is now **My Details** and states the onboarding form in full: Personal, Qualification &
  Experience, Family, Identity, Bank, Emergency Contact, References and Nominees, each as its own
  card, with the qualification, experience and children lists read back as small tables, skills and
  languages as chips, and the Aadhaar and PAN photographs as thumbnails that open full size.
  Anything nobody filled in is left out rather than shown as an empty box — which is also what
  keeps a spouse row off an unmarried employee's page. It is stated as label-over-value rather than
  as text boxes nobody can type in, since none of it is editable here; HR still maintains it in the
  Employee Repository. The page is drawn from the onboarding form's own section list, so a field
  added to that form appears here the same day
  The two identity proofs read as one fact each — the Aadhaar number with a thumbnail of that very
  card beside it, and the same for the PAN — rather than both numbers in one place and both
  photographs three fields away. Fields sit **two to a row**, because the form writes them in pairs
  (a nominee's name then their phone, a father's name then his occupation) and an odd number of
  columns splits every one of them across two rows; the page is filled by putting the short cards
  side by side instead, with a card holding an address or a table taking the whole row
  ([OnboardingDetailsView.js](../../frontend/src/components/profile/OnboardingDetailsView.js),
  [routes.js](../../backend/src/modules/user/routes.js)).
- **A Google Sheet rule's column is typed rather than picked, so the field stops changing shape.**
  It offered a dropdown of the sheet's own headings — but only once that sheet had been read, which
  meant a text box on a new link, a text box on a sheet whose share had been revoked, and a text box
  that turned into a dropdown a second or two after **Edit** opened, as the background read landed.
  One field wearing three faces, and typing into it while it changed was a good way to lose what you
  had typed. It is now always a text box, with the headings offered as autocomplete suggestions
  wherever they happen to be known. A heading that does not exist is still caught, where it always
  was: the import preview names any column a rule asks for that the sheet has not got
  ([SheetAssignmentRulesPanel.js](../../frontend/src/components/settings/SheetAssignmentRulesPanel.js)).
- **A new Google Sheet assignment rule is added at the top of the list, and a split reads as one
  table.** Rules are checked top to bottom and the first match wins, so the rule just written is
  also the first one tried — appended at the bottom it was both the last thing checked and, on a
  long list, off screen. The shares now sit under the rule as a single table with the column
  headings written once, instead of a "Share 3" and a "Percent" label per person, which made a
  four-way split taller than the dialog holding it. Each share's picker also drops whoever the other
  shares already name, so the same person can no longer be put on one rule twice and refused on save
  ([SheetAssignmentRulesPanel.js](../../frontend/src/components/settings/SheetAssignmentRulesPanel.js)).
- **The Quotation Calculator's quantities are typed, and an album cannot be quoted past its sheet
  limit.** The Qty box on the ⋮ → **Quotation Calculator** was a read-only number between a `−` and
  a `+`, and the album rows count in **sheets** — so quoting a Luxury album at 35 sheets meant
  pressing `+` thirty-five times. The number is typed straight in now. Each album row is held inside
  the limit its product sets — **0 to 35** for Luxury, Premium and Standard Plus, **0 to 25** for
  Hard Cover and Soft Cover — and nothing above it can be entered at all: the digits are capped as
  they are typed, so a box cannot accept 400 and silently show 35 a moment later, and `+` goes dead
  once the limit is reached rather than walking past it. Frames are uncapped, because there is no
  stated limit on how many a client orders. What is typed is settled when the box is left, so a
  number is read whole rather than a keystroke at a time. The limits travel with the product in the
  catalogue instead of being read back out of the "(Min 35 Sheets)" in its name, so renaming a
  product is not a silent change to what the studio may quote
  ([quotation-calculator/controller.js](../../backend/src/modules/sales/quotation-calculator/controller.js),
  [CRMPage.js](../../frontend/src/components/sales/CRMPage.js)).

### Added
- **Settings is a module in Role Management now, one tick per entry on its rail.** `ADMIN / System
  Settings` was the whole overlay behind a single permission: a studio that wanted somebody to keep
  the message templates had to hand them the studio's branding logo — read by the top bar, the
  sign-in page, every quotation and the client gallery — along with the branches, the quotation
  brands, the login slide, the holidays and the usage monitoring. Role Management now carries a
  **Settings** block of its own, separate from Administration's user and role screens, holding one
  row per title on the Settings rail: User Settings, Table Settings, Field Settings, Project
  Settings, Exclude Phone Numbers, Integrations, Quotation Settings, Employee Referral, Message
  Template, Organization, Holidays, Login Slide and Application Monitoring. A role sees exactly the
  sections it was given and the rail lists nothing else, so somebody who keeps the holidays opens
  Settings on Holidays alone. **Nobody loses access:** migration `20260915190000` copies every
  existing `ADMIN / System Settings` grant onto all thirteen at the level it was held and retires
  the old row, so a role that could open Settings yesterday opens exactly as much of it today —
  withholding a section is now a decision somebody takes rather than one the upgrade takes for them.
  Every guard that named the retired permission was re-pointed in the same change, including
  Image Selection's own settings panel and the email health check, because a guard naming a
  permission the catalogue no longer holds refuses everybody but a Super Admin and says so to nobody
  ([permission-catalogue.js](../../backend/prisma/seeds/permission-catalogue.js),
  [SettingsOverlay.js](../../frontend/src/components/settings/SettingsOverlay.js), migration
  `20260915190000_settings_module_permissions`).

  Two parts of the split are deliberate and worth knowing when granting. **User Preference has no
  row**: the sidebar order and the appearance are one person's own, and a role that could be refused
  the right to arrange its own screen is not a permission worth being able to grant. And **six
  sections still need the module that owns their data** — Table Settings, Exclude Phone Numbers and
  Integrations also need `SALES / CRM Leads`, Field Settings also needs `HRM / Employee Management`,
  Project Settings also needs `PROJECT / Projects`, Employee Referral also needs `HRM / Employee
  Referral` — because their endpoints belong to those modules and are shared with the CRM board, the
  employee forms, the project register and the referral page. The Settings row can only withhold
  those sections, never open one the server would refuse; each row's description says so.

### Removed
- **Added Accounts is gone from Bank Details and from the Employee Repository table.** The field sat
  under Bank Details on Review Employee Details, Add Employee and Edit Employee as a free-text box
  beside the account the studio actually pays into, and rode along as a column in the repository's
  table — one more of sixty to scroll past, holding nothing anyone reads. Bank Details now ends at
  IFSC Code, which is where the account being asked about ends. **No data is destroyed:** the
  `added_accounts` column and the API field that accepts it are untouched, so anything already
  recorded is still in the database and a decision to drop the column stays a separate, deliberate
  one ([EmployeeFormPanel.js](../../frontend/src/components/employees/EmployeeFormPanel.js),
  [EmployeeTable.js](../../frontend/src/components/employees/EmployeeTable.js)).

### Fixed
- **Bulk Entry's Assigned Branch offered nothing, so no batch could be saved.** Leads & Followups →
  ⋮ → **Bulk Entry** drew the Assigned Branch picker with an empty menu on every row of every
  vertical — and the field is required, so a batch could be typed in full and then refused, with the
  one thing standing in the way being a list that could not be opened. The branches were fetched
  correctly and handed to the row; the row simply never passed them to the field, which had a
  `branchOptions = []` default sitting ready to answer for them. The picker now offers the studio's
  own branches from the branch table, narrowed as everywhere else to the ones the signed-in person's
  roles reach. The default is gone with the fix: a fallback standing in for a list somebody else is
  fetching is how the thread came to be cut with nothing to say so
  ([BulkImportLeadsDialog.js](../../frontend/src/components/sales/BulkImportLeadsDialog.js)).

### Changed
- **Leads & Followups' footer says Last sync, and says it to the minute.** It read "Last updated" and
  a date, which on a board that re-reads itself on a timer and after every write answered "today" —
  true of every sync a reader will ever see, and so no answer to the question the footer is there to
  settle: is what I am looking at current? It now reads *Last sync: 15 Sep 2026, 14:07* off the same
  clock the board already kept, so a board that has quietly stopped refreshing is visible as a time
  that stops moving. The footer wraps rather than overflowing at phone width, and every other module
  keeps `Last updated` with its date — this is the one board that refreshes itself
  ([CRMPage.js](../../frontend/src/components/sales/CRMPage.js),
  [UI-STANDARDS.md](../project-state/UI-STANDARDS.md)).

### Added
- **Selecting leads and importing a CSV are each their own permission now, and Bulk Import is called
  Bulk Entry.** Ticking Read / Write on `SALES / CRM Leads` used to hand out the whole of Leads &
  Followups, including the two acts that reach a whole board at once. Both are separate rows in Role
  Management from now on. **`SALES / CRM Lead Select All`** is the column of tick boxes: a role
  without it has none at all — no header tick, no row ticks — so there is no selection for **Update
  Details** or the bulk **Delete** to run over, and the board is worked a lead at a time. Absent
  rather than greyed out, because a tick box that cannot be ticked reads as a broken table.
  **`SALES / CRM Lead Import`** is ⋮ → **Import**, the CSV, which is gone from the menu without it —
  a file lands as many leads as it has rows, from a source the studio did not type and cannot
  proof-read. ⋮ → **Bulk Entry** is deliberately left on the board's own Read / Write, so a role that
  may not import a file can still type a batch in: that is the entry the menu keeps, and it is why it
  is no longer called Bulk Import — it types leads, it does not take a file, and two things called
  Import in one menu was the confusion the name caused. The server refuses `bulk-status`,
  `bulk-assign`, the bulk delete and both `import-csv` routes to anyone without the matching
  permission; a bulk delete asks for Select All **and** `CRM Lead Deletion`, since it is both acts at
  once, and deleting one named lead still asks only for the second. The whole-board `ids` route is
  deliberately **not** gated — the Quotation Generator's Enquire ID picker reads that same route, and
  those ids are rows the caller may already see; rewriting them all at once is the privileged act,
  which is what the three guarded routes are
  ([permission-catalogue.js](../../backend/prisma/seeds/permission-catalogue.js),
  [crm-leads/routes.js](../../backend/src/modules/sales/crm-leads/routes.js),
  [CRMPage.js](../../frontend/src/components/sales/CRMPage.js),
  [BulkImportLeadsDialog.js](../../frontend/src/components/sales/BulkImportLeadsDialog.js)).
  **Both permissions are new rows and the seed writes no grants, so reseed
  `backend/prisma/seeds/iam-roles-permissions.js` and then re-save every role that should select or
  import** — until that is done only a Super Admin can do either, which is the same handover
  `SALES / CRM Lead Deletion` needed.

### Fixed
- **The sort arrow on table headers showed as a blue emoji on macOS.** The unsorted control is the
  text character ↕, and Safari and Chrome on a Mac draw that character as its emoji unless told
  otherwise, so the Employee Repository, the CRM board and the Meetings page each carried a blue
  square where a thin arrow was meant. The character now carries the text-presentation selector
  and draws as the same thin arrow everywhere; ↑ and ↓ were never affected
  ([DataTable.js](../../frontend/src/components/data-table/DataTable.js)).
- **Album Proofing was missing from the sidebar for the whole studio, and Role Management could
  not grant it.** Its two permissions, `IMAGE_SELECTION / Album Proofing` and `Album Proofing QC`,
  were added to the seed catalogue alone, and the seed cannot be run on the VPS — so on production
  the `permission` table never held them, the sidebar (which lists a screen only where its
  permission is held) left the entry out for everybody including a Super Admin, and no role could
  be given it. Migration `20260916100000_album_proofing_permissions` creates both rows, `INSERT …
  WHERE NOT EXISTS`, so a seeded machine matches them rather than doubling them. No grants are
  copied: after `npx prisma migrate deploy`, tick **Album Proofing** (and **Album Proofing QC** for
  the QC Lead's role) in Role Management and re-save those roles
  ([migration.sql](../../backend/prisma/migrations/20260916100000_album_proofing_permissions/migration.sql)).
- **Employees added or edited between 6 and 15 September have no branch or vertical reference,
  so the attendance geofence cannot find their office and branch scoping cannot see them.** The
  Employee Repository's create and update paths did not stamp `branch_id` and `vertical_id` beside
  the text they saved until the fix of 15 September, and that fix repaired nothing already written.
  Migration `20260916090000_employee_reference_repair` re-runs the employee half of the data-scope
  backfill — work base first, then current location; the vertical by any of its names — on every
  row still holding NULL, and nothing else. `data-scope-backfill.test.js` fails until it has run
  ([migration.sql](../../backend/prisma/migrations/20260916090000_employee_reference_repair/migration.sql)).
- **Any signed-in session could read the whole Employee Repository and every separation.** The
  HRM master-data router lets every `GET` through on a session, which is right for the department
  and branch lists it exists for — but `GET /api/hrm/employees` sits on the same router and answers
  each person's children, qualifications, experiences, reviews and login, and `GET /separations`
  answers why people left. A role holding only Client Storage could read all of it. Those reads,
  the next Employee ID, the import template and the alternative-candidates list now carry
  `HRM / Employee Management` Read (the template, Read / Write). The asset and client-storage
  pickers, whose roles do not hold HRM, read the new `GET /api/hrm/employees/directory` instead —
  id, Employee ID and name for the people working here, and nothing more
  ([routes.js](../../backend/src/modules/hrm/master-data/routes.js),
  [employees.service.js](../../frontend/src/services/hrm/employees.service.js)).
- **The WhatsApp switches in Settings → User Settings were dead for every role but the seeded
  Super Admin, whatever it was granted.** Disconnecting a CRM, switching one off and switching the
  studio off all tested `req.user.role === "SUPER_ADMIN"` inside the controller — the check
  CLAUDE.md Rule 4 names, which sits below the route's guard and can be granted to nobody. They
  answer to `SETTINGS / User Settings` Read / Write now, the row that already decides which logins
  are held to linking WhatsApp; a Super Admin keeps all three by holding every permission, and the
  screen's `canEdit` follows the same answer
  ([controller.js](../../backend/src/modules/sales/whatsapp-chat/controller.js)).
- **Approving a salary advance, or any payment request with no single project behind it, failed
  with "Unable to approve this payment request".** The cash entry the approval writes takes its
  client from the project it is filed against; with no project there was none, the schema stripped
  the counterparty the approver passed, and `client_name` — which cannot be null — failed at the
  database, as a 500, with the decision rolled back. The schema accepts a client name now, and an
  entry with no project uses the one it was given — the employee or vendor being paid — or is
  refused as a 400 that says so ([controller.js](../../backend/src/modules/accounts/cash-entries/controller.js)).
- **Importing cash entries from a spreadsheet failed on every file.** The import validated each row
  synchronously against a schema that has to ask the database which payment modes exist; under zod
  4 that does not fail the row, it throws, outside the per-row catch, and the whole import answered
  500 before its first row. Rows are parsed asynchronously now, for every module's import
  ([tableImport.js](../../backend/src/services/tableImport.js)).
- **Editing a payment request could file it under a stranger's branch.** The edit path passed
  `resolveBranch` four arguments where it takes three, so the caller's id landed in the wrong slot
  and the fallback looked up `user.findFirst({ where: { id: undefined } })` — the first account in
  the table — and used *their* branch for a request with no shoot and a requester with no work base.
  The call passes what the function takes ([controller.js](../../backend/src/modules/hrm/payment-requests/controller.js)).
- **A project's shoots and allocations could be edited and deleted by somebody whose data scope
  does not include the project, with the started-project check skipped on the way.** The guard read
  the project through the caller's scope, found nothing, and waved the request on for "the handler's
  own 404" — but the shoot and allocation writes look their rows up by project id alone and never
  gave one. The guard answers the 404 itself now, so nothing behind it runs for a project the caller
  may not see ([controller.js](../../backend/src/modules/sales/projects/controller.js)).
- **Album Proofing: revoking a client link, or letting it expire, left the album stuck in client
  review with no way to send it again.** The album stayed `CLIENT_REVIEW`, which Send to Client
  refused and nothing else moved; only a database edit recovered it. Revoke now returns the album
  to `READY_FOR_CLIENT`, and Send to Client also accepts an album already in client review — the
  earlier link is revoked and a fresh one issued on the same frozen version, so nothing the client
  marked is lost. A client's second Send or Approve on a version already submitted answers 409
  "already submitted" rather than 500
  ([client.service.js](../../backend/src/modules/image-selection/album-proofs/client.service.js)).
- **Album Proofing: Send to QC failed, then answered "already being sent" for fifteen minutes, when
  any page had arrived through the upload dock.** A dock upload has no `originalPath` — its
  original is the stored upload — and the frozen version page requires one, so the freeze threw
  after writing its DRAFT version row. Such a page is frozen under its storage name now
  ([pipeline.service.js](../../backend/src/modules/image-selection/album-proofs/pipeline.service.js)).
- **Album Proofing, client room: the arrow keys threw away a half-written comment.** Left and
  Right turned the spread even while the comment composer was open, dropping the composer and the
  text with it — and the caret is what an arrow key means in a text box. They are ignored while a
  comment is being written or a dialog is up, as the QC room already did. In both rooms, turning
  the page also clears the strokes and words in hand: a stroke painted on one spread used to follow
  the reviewer to the next and be saved against it
  ([ClientProofViewer.js](../../frontend/src/components/image-selection/album-proofing/client/ClientProofViewer.js),
  [QcReviewPanel.js](../../frontend/src/components/image-selection/album-proofing/QcReviewPanel.js)).
- **Album Proofing, QC room: Edit on a correction opened the comment card in the corner of the
  spread, and every save put "Changed spreads only" back on.** The card's anchor read `x` and `y`
  off a staff correction, which carries `xNormalized` and `yNormalized`; and the reload after each
  correction re-applied the round's default filter, so a reviewer who had switched it off was
  switched back — onto a spread number the shorter list no longer had. The anchor reads the right
  fields, the default is applied when the review opens and not after, and the spread position is
  held inside the list being paged ([QcReviewPanel.js](../../frontend/src/components/image-selection/album-proofing/QcReviewPanel.js)).
- **Payment Requests reopened on an Extra Quotations table a person could no longer see the tab
  for.** The remembered tab was accepted whether or not the viewer still held `PROJECT / Projects`
  Read / Write, which is what lists it; it is a valid remembered tab only for those who do
  ([PaymentRequestsPage.js](../../frontend/src/components/accounts/PaymentRequestsPage.js)).
- **Payment Requests showed a table of empty rows.** The list arrived with the right number of rows
  and every single cell blank — Request ID, Status, Type, Raised By, Amount, all "—" — while the
  Pending, Approved and Rejected tabs all read zero because no row carried a status. The rows were
  never loaded wrong: the endpoint built each one with an async helper and returned
  `data.map(toRow)` without awaiting it, so the API sent an array of *promises*, and a promise
  serialises to `{}`. Three requests became three empty objects. Every other endpoint in the file
  already awaited the same helper. Nothing in the module's data changed — the requests were there
  the whole time ([controller.js](../../backend/src/modules/hrm/payment-requests/controller.js)).

### Added
- **Categories are kept per brand, and every quotation asks for one.** Settings → Overall →
  Fields → Sales & CRM → Quotations → **Categories** now has a tab each for Dum Dum, Qua Qua and
  Corp, and a category added under a tab belongs to that brand alone. The Quotation Generator's
  Client Details ask for **Category** on all three verticals - beside Referred From, closed and
  mandatory, as Dum Dum's already was - and offer only the brand's own list; a brand whose list is
  still empty is not asked until it has entries. Quotation Settings → *brand* → PDF Content shows
  the same **Category** picker above the cover sets for all three brands, so Qua Qua and Corp can
  carry a cover and Thank You pair per category exactly as Dum Dum does.
  Migration `20260916110000_lead_religion_per_vertical` adds the brand column and files every
  existing category under Dum Dum, which is the only tab that could have written it; the table,
  the `religions` resource and the payload key keep their original names, so saved quotations are
  untouched. `GET /api/hrm/religions?vertical=QUA_QUA` reads one brand's rows
  ([VerticalScopedListEditor.js](../../frontend/src/components/settings/VerticalScopedListEditor.js),
  [quotationVerticalConfig.js](../../frontend/src/components/sales/quotationVerticalConfig.js),
  [definitions.js](../../backend/src/modules/hrm/master-data/definitions.js)).
- **A page under `(app)` with no row in the permission map now fails a test.** `/profile` had
  none and nothing noticed, because the drift tests walk the menu and the map and a page in
  neither is invisible to both. `permission-catalogue-sidebar.test.js` walks the pages on disk
  now; the two that are meant to be open to every session — `/profile` and `/sales/meetings` —
  are named there with the reason ([test](../../backend/test/permission-catalogue-sidebar.test.js)).

### Docs
- **The catalogue is `permission-catalogue.js`, not `iam-roles-permissions.js`.** CLAUDE.md and
  the route map's own comment sent a developer to the file that only *requires* the catalogue;
  both name the right file and the migration that has to accompany it. IAM.md no longer promises
  Full and Read Only starter roles per module that no seed has ever created — the seed ships the
  four roles and nothing else.

### Changed
- **"Religion" reads "Category" on every screen.** The Dum Dum quotation's field, the Settings →
  Field Settings → Sales & CRM → Quotations list, and the dropdown above the cover sets in
  Quotation Settings all say Category now. Only the labels changed: the `religion` key on the
  quotation and its cover sets, the `lead_religion` table and the `religions` list resource are
  unchanged, so nothing already saved is affected
  ([quotationVerticalConfig.js](../../frontend/src/components/sales/quotationVerticalConfig.js),
  [moduleFieldLists.js](../../frontend/src/constants/moduleFieldLists.js),
  [definitions.js](../../backend/src/modules/hrm/master-data/definitions.js)).

  Two parts of the split are deliberate and worth knowing when granting. **User Preference has no
  row**: the sidebar order and the appearance are one person's own, and a role that could be refused
  the right to arrange its own screen is not a permission worth being able to grant. And **six
  sections still need the module that owns their data** — Table Settings, Exclude Phone Numbers and
  Integrations also need `SALES / CRM Leads`, Field Settings also needs `HRM / Employee Management`,
  Project Settings also needs `PROJECT / Projects`, Employee Referral also needs `HRM / Employee
  Referral` — because their endpoints belong to those modules and are shared with the CRM board, the
  employee forms, the project register and the referral page. The Settings row can only withhold
  those sections, never open one the server would refuse; each row's description says so.

### Added
- **A lead meant for somebody on leave goes to their alternatives instead, and the cover chain can
  no longer loop.** Every employee may already name two alternatives; nothing used them, and
  nothing stopped Vijay naming Abi while Abi named Vijay. Now, when a Google Sheet assignment rule
  names somebody who is on **approved leave today** — or who has left the studio — the lead goes to
  their alternatives, **shared between whichever of them are free** rather than piled on the first.
  Only the nearest ring is used: if Vijay named Abi and Kishore and only Abi is away, everything
  goes to Kishore rather than reaching past a person Vijay did nominate to one he did not. If the
  whole chain is away the lead arrives unassigned, which is visible and recoverable. The import
  preview says so per row — *"covering for Vijay Kumar (on leave today)"* — so a lead landing on
  somebody the rule does not name never reads as the rule being wrong.

  **Two people can no longer cover only each other.** Saving an employee whose alternatives would
  form that pair is refused, naming it: *"Two people cannot cover only each other: Vijay Kumar →
  Abi → Vijay Kumar."* The alternative menus on the employee form drop whoever already names that
  employee, computed by the server with the very module that enforces the save, so a menu can never
  offer a choice its own save will reject. A longer ring — Vijay → Abi → Kishore → Vijay — is
