  deliberately allowed: it is how three people can all have cover, and **three is the fewest at
  which nobody is left uncovered**, since two people can only cover each other
    ([alternativeChain.js](../../backend/src/services/alternativeChain.js),
  [leadCover.js](../../backend/src/services/leadCover.js)).
- **A linked Google Sheet now assigns its own leads.** A spreadsheet says who a lead is and almost
  never says whose lead it is, so every imported lead arrived with an empty CRM column and somebody
  assigned them by reading a column the sheet does carry — the Facebook page, the form, the city —
  and picking a name per lead, on a sheet the poller re-reads every 2 minutes. Settings →
  Integrations → **Google Sheets** now asks, in the same dialog that links the sheet, for an ordered
  list of rules: a **column**, the **value** to look for in it, and the **employee** whose lead a
  matching row is. Asked while linking because storing a link imports the whole sheet immediately,
  and rules added afterwards would miss that first batch — always the largest one. The match is the
  column holding that value, past differences of case, spacing and punctuation, so `erode-tn` finds
  `Erode TN`; two values going to one person is two rules. The first matching rule wins and
  evaluation stops, so two rules matching one row is answered by dragging them into order rather
  than reported as a clash. The same rules run on **every** sync, so a
  row the 2-minute poller picks up an hour later is assigned exactly as the first import would have
  assigned it. Rules **fill blanks and never overrule people**: the sheet's own CRM column wins, then
  the owner the lead already has, then the rules — a reassignment made in the board is not taken
  back on the next pass, and editing the rules does not re-assign leads already imported. A rule
  naming a column the sheet no longer has is reported rather than silently doing nothing: **Preview
  Import** shows who each row would go to and which rule sent it there, warns about columns the
  sheet does not have, and the sync line counts how many were assigned by rule and how many matched
  none. Reachable by anyone holding `SALES / CRM Leads` at `READ_WRITE` — the permission that
  already governs linking a sheet, so no role needs re-saving
  ([leadAssignmentRules.js](../../backend/src/modules/sales/crm-leads/leadAssignmentRules.js),
  [rulesStore.js](../../backend/src/modules/sales/google-sheet-links/rulesStore.js),
  [SheetAssignmentRulesPanel.js](../../frontend/src/components/settings/SheetAssignmentRulesPanel.js),
  migration `20260915090000_google_sheet_assignment_rules`). The **Link / Update Google Sheet**
  dialog is one size whatever it holds — adding a rule scrolls its body rather than growing the
  dialog and moving **Update** out from under the cursor — and each rule reads as a single line from
  a tablet up, stacking on a phone
  ([SettingsOverlay.js](../../frontend/src/components/settings/SettingsOverlay.js)).

### Changed
- **An employee's photographs are replaced rather than removed.** **Remove photo** is gone from the
  Profile Photo, Aadhar Card Photo and PAN Card Photo on the Add Employee form and on the Onboarding
  Pending submission HR reviews — the same panel serves both. Those are pictures the record is kept
  *for*, so emptying one only left a field that had to be answered again before the record would
  save; **Replace photo** does the whole job in the same single click. The button stays wherever a
  picture is genuinely optional and taking it down is a real decision — the studio's logo, a login
  slide, a quotation's cover, an asset's photo — through a new `removable` prop on the shared field
  that defaults to on ([ImageUploadField.js](../../frontend/src/components/shared/ImageUploadField.js),
  [EmployeeFormPanel.js](../../frontend/src/components/employees/EmployeeFormPanel.js)).

### Fixed
- **Create Project's Payment Mode dropdown was empty, which made the project uncreatable.** The
  **Payment Received** section draws the same fields Accounts records money with, and those fields
  ask their context for `paymentModes` — but Create Project built its context by hand and left that
  key out, so the dropdown opened with nothing in it. Since the advance is required in full, anyone
  holding `ACCOUNTS / Cash Management` — the people who see the section — could not create a project
  at all. It now offers the studio's own list from Settings → Field Settings → Accounts → Payment
  Mode, with the same never-empty fallbacks Cash Received uses, and it also honours what each mode
  asks for: a UPI advance wants a transaction ID and a cash one does not, where before those
  follow-up fields fell back to a list written into the code. Both screens read one hook now, so a
  mode the studio adds or renames reaches them together
  ([usePaymentModes.js](../../frontend/src/components/accounts/usePaymentModes.js),
  [CreateProjectFormPanel.js](../../frontend/src/components/sales/CreateProjectFormPanel.js)).
- **A Google Sheet that has stopped syncing now says so on the row.** The poller runs every 2
  minutes with nobody watching, so a link whose tab was renamed, whose share was revoked or whose
  URL was edited simply stopped importing — and Settings → Integrations → **Google Sheets** went on
  showing a green **Connected** pill beside it, because that pill is about the Google account and
  never was about the sync. Each linked sheet now carries a second pill when the last pass did not
  come back clean, and prints underneath it what went wrong and when: *"The selected tab is no
  longer available"*, *"Change sharing to Anyone with the link → Viewer"*. A clean sheet says when
  it last synced and what it did. A rule pointing at a column the sheet no longer has is named
  there too, since that is a sync that succeeds and still assigns nobody. **The rows that did not
  import are named** — "Row 12 — Lead name is required", "Row 47 — Invalid phone/contact value" —
  so the spreadsheet can be opened at the line and corrected, instead of the count being the only
  thing said; the first five are kept, with the true total after them and Preview Import for the
  rest. The list also re-reads
  itself once a minute while the panel is open — quietly, keeping what is on screen if a read fails
  — so a sync that breaks while somebody is looking at it appears without closing Settings and
  coming back ([SettingsOverlay.js](../../frontend/src/components/settings/SettingsOverlay.js)).
- **An employee in more than one department is a salesperson again.** Departments are a
  multi-select, stored as one Text column joined by `", "` — so somebody set to `Sales, Technical`
  or `Management, Sales` was compared whole against `"Sales"`, matched nothing, and quietly
  disappeared from every list of who works in Sales: the CRM board's **Assign To**, the project
  register's CRM picker, the WhatsApp roster, and the new Google Sheet assignment rules. The three
  readers each wrote that comparison themselves, which is how they came to be wrong together; they
  now share `salesEmployees()`, which tests membership of the list rather than the whole of it — so
  a department named `Salesforce Admin` still is not Sales
  ([employeeOptionLists.js](../../backend/src/services/employeeOptionLists.js)).
- **An optional dropdown can be put back to nothing: its menu now offers a None row.** A picker
  could only ever be moved from one answer to another, so an optional field answered by mistake
  stayed answered — and **Referred By** on an employee is the referral itself, so somebody named
  there in error could not be taken off the Employee Referral register at all. Choosing **None**
  clears the field, and saving with Referred By empty removes that employee's referral row and its
  outstanding instalments with it; a referral with an instalment already **paid** still refuses to
  be removed and says so. The row appears only on fields that are **optional** and only once one is
  answered — a required field cannot be emptied, and an unanswered one already reads "Select" — and
  it is offered by the employee form and the shared schema-form renderer, not by pickers that are
  not a question with a blank answer, such as a phone's country code or a filter switch that
  already offers "All" ([Dropdown.js](../../frontend/src/components/shared/Dropdown.js),
  [dropdownOptions.mjs](../../frontend/src/lib/dropdownOptions.mjs),
  [EmployeeFormPanel.js](../../frontend/src/components/employees/EmployeeFormPanel.js),
  [SchemaFormFields.js](../../frontend/src/components/schema-form/SchemaFormFields.js)). Access is
  unchanged: the fields sit on screens already guarded by `HRM / Employee Repository`, and no route
  or permission was added.

### Added
- **A hire that falls through before the candidate submits is offboarded like anybody else, and the
  row moves to InActive instead of vanishing.** The Employee Submission Pending tab mostly holds
  **invites** rather than employee records — a candidate who has not filled the form in has no
  employee row behind them, which is why those rows show no Employee ID — and all their menu could
  do was **Cancel Invite**, which took the row out of the list and left the studio with no account
  of what happened. Those rows now carry **Start Offboarding**, opening the same offboarding form an
  employee is relieved with: type, reason, dates, remarks. Saving it turns the invite into an
  employee record inside the same transaction — a separation is a row against an employee id, and an
  unsubmitted invite has none — so the row lands in **InActive** and **Move to Active** can bring it
  back to the wait it was in. Cancel Invite stays beside it for the other case: an invite sent by
  mistake or to the wrong address, which is not a departure
  ([EmployeeTable.js](../../frontend/src/components/employees/EmployeeTable.js),
  [SeparationFormPanel.js](../../frontend/src/components/employees/SeparationFormPanel.js),
  `employeeFromInvite` in
  [separations/controller.js](../../backend/src/modules/hrm/separations/controller.js)).
- **Move to Active asks why, and keeps the answer.** The reason is required — the action *deletes*
  the offboarding it reverses, so without it the only trace of a departure that was taken back would
  be a status quietly reading Active again. Stored on the employee with who did it and when
  (`reactivation_reason`, `reactivated_at`, `reactivated_by_user_id`; migration
  `20260915180000_employee_reactivation_reason`).
- **Start Offboarding is on every employee row, including the two onboarding statuses.** It was
  withheld from **HR Onboarding Pending** and **Employee Submission Pending**, on the reasoning that
  there is nothing to offboard somebody from before they have been taken on. A hire falls through at
  exactly that point — the candidate stops answering, or takes another offer — and the row then sat
  in an onboarding tab waiting for a submission that was never coming, with nothing to record what
  happened but deleting the record. Such a row now offboards like any other and lands in InActive.
  **Move to Active** returns it to the onboarding status it actually held rather than promoting a
  candidate who never started to Active, which would hand the studio an employee it never took on
  ([EmployeeTable.js](../../frontend/src/components/employees/EmployeeTable.js),
  `returningStatus` in [employees/controller.js](../../backend/src/modules/hrm/employees/controller.js)).
- **An inactive employee can be put back to work: Move to Active, on the row's own menu.** The
  InActive tab could be reached and never left — an offboarding put somebody on Relieved, Fired or
  Absconded, and the only thing that restored a status was removing the separation, which no screen
  offered; somebody on **Long Leave** had no separation to remove at all. The entry is on every row
  the repository does not count as working, asks before it acts, and in one step removes the
  offboarding record, puts the employee back on the status they held **before** it — their own
  `in Training` or `PIP`, not a promotion to Active — and reopens their login. Reactivating is the
  offboarding taken back rather than a second chapter beside it, so the separation is not kept, and
  that is also what leaves the employee able to be offboarded again later
  ([EmployeeTable.js](../../frontend/src/components/employees/EmployeeTable.js),
  [EmployeeActionDialog.js](../../frontend/src/components/employees/EmployeeActionDialog.js),
  [employees/controller.js](../../backend/src/modules/hrm/employees/controller.js)). It is a write on
  the employee, so it answers to the `HRM / Employee Management` **READ_WRITE** the employee router
  already puts on every non-GET — the same permission Start Offboarding and Delete answer to, held
  today by Super Admin and by whichever roles the studio has granted it. No new permission, and
  nothing to reseed.

### Fixed
- **Offboarding an employee now closes their login, and taking the offboarding back reopens it.**
  A user and an employee are separate records joined only by `linkedEmployeeId`, and sign-in checks
  nothing but `User.status` — so somebody marked **Fired** or **Absconded** could still sign in the
  next morning carrying every role they held, because only *deleting* an employee had ever closed
  an account. Recording a separation now sets the linked account to `INACTIVE` in the same
  transaction as the status, and removing a separation — or Move to Active above — sets it back to
  `ACTIVE`. The roles on the account are left alone in both directions: closing it is what stops the
  sign-in, and stripping the grants would mean rebuilding them by hand for somebody who returns
  ([separations/controller.js](../../backend/src/modules/hrm/separations/controller.js)).

  **Two people can no longer cover only each other.** Saving an employee whose alternatives would
  form that pair is refused, naming it: *"Two people cannot cover only each other: Vijay Kumar →
  Abi → Vijay Kumar."* The alternative menus on the employee form drop whoever already names that
  employee, computed by the server with the very module that enforces the save, so a menu can never
  offer a choice its own save will reject. A longer ring — Vijay → Abi → Kishore → Vijay — is
  deliberately allowed: it is how three people can all have cover, and **three is the fewest at
  which nobody is left uncovered**, since two people can only cover each other
    ([alternativeChain.js](../../backend/src/services/alternativeChain.js),
  [leadCover.js](../../backend/src/services/leadCover.js)).

  **Two people can no longer cover only each other.** Saving an employee whose alternatives would
  form that pair is refused, naming it: *"Two people cannot cover only each other: Vijay Kumar →
  Abi → Vijay Kumar."* The alternative menus on the employee form drop whoever already names that
  employee, computed by the server with the very module that enforces the save, so a menu can never
  offer a choice its own save will reject. A longer ring — Vijay → Abi → Kishore → Vijay — is
  deliberately allowed: it is how three people can all have cover, and **three is the fewest at
  which nobody is left uncovered**, since two people can only cover each other
    ([alternativeChain.js](../../backend/src/services/alternativeChain.js),
  [leadCover.js](../../backend/src/services/leadCover.js)).

  **Two people can no longer cover only each other.** Saving an employee whose alternatives would
  form that pair is refused, naming it: *"Two people cannot cover only each other: Vijay Kumar →
  Abi → Vijay Kumar."* The alternative menus on the employee form drop whoever already names that
  employee, computed by the server with the very module that enforces the save, so a menu can never
  offer a choice its own save will reject. A longer ring — Vijay → Abi → Kishore → Vijay — is
  deliberately allowed: it is how three people can all have cover, and **three is the fewest at
  which nobody is left uncovered**, since two people can only cover each other
    ([alternativeChain.js](../../backend/src/services/alternativeChain.js),
  [leadCover.js](../../backend/src/services/leadCover.js)).

### Added
- **Image Selection uploads can be compressed to ~100 KB or ~50 KB.** ⋮ → Settings →
  **Processing** → Preview quality gains **Light (1000px, ~100 KB)** and **Smallest (800px, ~50 KB)**
  below the three presets already there, for a studio sending galleries to clients on slow or metered
  connections. Each drops the preview's long edge alongside its byte ceiling, because the encoder
  accepts its last quality rung whatever it weighs — a smaller ceiling on a large long edge would
  have quoted a size it never reached. The setting stays per-computer and still applies only to
  uploads, never to a gallery already filled
  ([imageSelectionProcessingSettings.mjs](../../frontend/src/lib/imageSelectionProcessingSettings.mjs)).

### Changed
- **Image Selection's studio-wide settings panel is now called Settings, not Project Settings.**
  ⋮ → **Settings** on the Image Selection title bar, and the panel it opens is headed the same. The
  old name collided with Project Management's own **Project Settings** screen — two unrelated screens
  answering to one label, which the workspace made worse by sending people to *that* one by name from
  inside this module. The message offering a Target Download Path after a download now names the new
  label as well, so it still points somewhere that exists. Labels only: nothing about what the panel
  saves has changed, and the per-project **Selection Settings** rail keeps its own name
  ([ImageSelectionNavigation.js](../../frontend/src/components/image-selection/ImageSelectionNavigation.js),
  [ImageSelectionSettingsForm.js](../../frontend/src/components/image-selection/ImageSelectionSettingsForm.js)).
- **Preview quality uses the application's own dropdown instead of the browser's.** The control was
  the one native `select` left on the panel, so it opened an operating-system menu that looked
  nothing like every other picker in the app; it now reads and behaves like the rest, keyboard
  stepping included, and lists the presets heaviest-first rather than alphabetically
  ([ProcessingSettingsField.js](../../frontend/src/components/image-selection/ProcessingSettingsField.js)).
- **Album Proofing's New Project asks for a saved group name or a manual name.** The Album
  Proofing page now opens its own create form
  ([CreateAlbumProofingProject.js](../../frontend/src/components/image-selection/album-proofing/CreateAlbumProofingProject.js)):
  pick an unused WhatsApp group name from an in-progress Sales Manager project, which links the
  project and reads its client from there, or type a manual name with the client the proof link
  is sent to. The Projects page's manual-only form is unchanged. The group-name rules live in
  [albumProofingGroupNames.mjs](../../frontend/src/lib/albumProofingGroupNames.mjs) with a test.
- **Album Proofing is no longer one of Image Selection's sections.** The section switch in the
  Image Selection header now offers only Dashboard and Projects
  ([ImageSelectionNavigation.js](../../frontend/src/components/image-selection/ImageSelectionNavigation.js));
  Album Proofing is reached from its own Visual Curation entry in the sidebar and its page now
  carries its own **Album Proofing** heading instead of Image Selection's with a dropdown.

- **The comments panel beside a proof reads like the pins on it.** In QC review and in the
  client's proofing room, each correction in the right-hand panel is now the author's initial,
  their name and how long ago, then what they said — instead of a number the reader had to carry
  back to the photograph. Selecting a row still opens that pin on the spread,
  and Edit, Remove and Reopen stay where they were. Both rooms draw the one component
  ([ProofCommentList.js](../../frontend/src/components/image-selection/album-proofing/ProofCommentList.js)).

- **An album can only be approved once every spread has been looked at.** In QC review and in the
  client's proofing room alike, the approve control stays disabled until the reviewer has been
  through the spreads in front of them, with a line saying how many are left; a spread counts once
  it has been on screen for a moment, so holding an arrow key does not tick them off. The Review
  progress panel now counts spreads seen rather than the one being shown, and remembers them per
  album and version in the reviewer's own browser, so a reload does not start the count again. A
  QC round filtered to the changed spreads is judged on those. Both rulings wait, in both rooms —
  sending a version back ends the pass over it just as approving it does
  ([spreadReview.mjs](../../frontend/src/lib/spreadReview.mjs),
  [useSeenSpreads.js](../../frontend/src/components/image-selection/album-proofing/useSeenSpreads.js),
  [QcReviewPanel.js](../../frontend/src/components/image-selection/album-proofing/QcReviewPanel.js),
  [ClientProofViewer.js](../../frontend/src/components/image-selection/album-proofing/client/ClientProofViewer.js)).

- **A correction pin is a comment marker, the way Figma draws one.** In QC review, in the client's
  proofing room and in the corrections centre's spread view, a correction is now a teardrop whose
  point sits on the exact spot and whose face carries the author's initial. Pointing at it opens the
  comment itself — who wrote it, how long ago, and what they said, with the resolution note when
  there is one, and Edit, Remove or Reopen inside the bubble for whoever may still use them. The
  bubble opens on whichever side has room and closes when the pointer leaves; a click pins it open,
  which is what a touch screen has instead of a pointer, and Escape or a click away closes that. A comment still
  waiting on somebody is grey — it is a remark on the work, not an alarm — while a resolved one
  stays green and a reopened one violet; the numbered circles in the lists beside the proof follow
  the same grey. Reading a correction no longer means carrying a number from the photo to a list
  ([ProofSpreadCanvas.js](../../frontend/src/components/image-selection/album-proofing/ProofSpreadCanvas.js),
  [relativeTime.mjs](../../frontend/src/lib/relativeTime.mjs)). Neither room repeats the album and
  client in its left panel any more: they are named once, in the header over the proof.

- **QC review works exactly like the client's proofing room.** The QC Lead now reviews an album
  the way the client does: one spread at a time with swipe and arrow keys, zoom that magnifies
  the photo alone, a double-click or a dragged circle to mark a correction, and pins that are
  the reviewer's own to edit or remove until the ruling is sent — after which they lock, as the
  client's do on submit. This spread's corrections sit beside the proof, the footer count opens
  the per-spread summary, and one primary action switches by itself: Request N Corrections while
  any are open, Approve QC at zero, each behind its confirm and result. A resubmitted round still
  defaults to changed spreads only, and reopening a resolved correction stays QC's alone
  ([QcReviewPanel.js](../../frontend/src/components/image-selection/album-proofing/QcReviewPanel.js),
  [qc.service.js](../../backend/src/modules/image-selection/album-proofs/qc.service.js),
  [album-proofs/routes.js](../../backend/src/modules/image-selection/album-proofs/routes.js)).

- **Album Proofing runs on its own backend, separate from Image Selection's.** Its projects are
  now listed, created, edited, archived and deleted through `/api/image-selection/album-proofs/projects`
  under the Album Proofing grant, and album pages upload through the dock mounted under those
  routes — so an Album Proofing user no longer needs the Image Selection Project Management
  permission, and Image Selection's project routes can no longer see or touch an album project.
  The Album Proofing screen has its own row in the route table for the same grant. Image
  Selection's project files carry no album logic any more beyond hiding album rows from their own
  list; the two modules share a table, not code
  ([album-proofs/projects.service.js](../../backend/src/modules/image-selection/album-proofs/projects.service.js),
  [album-proofs/routes.js](../../backend/src/modules/image-selection/album-proofs/routes.js),
  [uploads/routes.js](../../backend/src/modules/image-selection/uploads/routes.js),
  [album-proofs.service.js](../../frontend/src/services/image-selection/album-proofs.service.js),
  [album-proofing/page.js](../../frontend/src/app/(app)/image-selection/album-proofing/page.js)).
  Archive and Restore on that page now do what they say instead of deleting.

### Removed
- **Image Selection → Settings no longer edits the studio's Global Name and Logo.** The
  panel's "Global Logo" section is gone; both values are edited in **Settings → Organization →
  Branding Logo**, which has always been their home and answers for the same `ADMIN / System
  Settings` permission, so nobody loses the ability to change them. They are studio-wide — the top
  bar, the sign-in page, every quotation PDF and the client gallery all read them — and a second
  editor inside one module's project settings was a place to rename the whole studio by accident.
  The settings record is unchanged: both values are still read by the form and written back exactly
  as they were found, because the API reads a missing field as an instruction to clear it and
  dropping them from the save would blank the studio's branding everywhere
  ([formPanelConfig.js](../../frontend/src/constants/formPanelConfig.js),
  [ImageSelectionSettingsForm.js](../../frontend/src/components/image-selection/ImageSelectionSettingsForm.js)).

### Changed
- **The Employee Repository's Username column moved next to Employee Name.** It already existed
  and was already correct - the login linked to each employee, joined the same way on every row -
  but sat as the 7th of more than 60 columns with nothing frozen, so it was easy to miss. It is the
  value an admin needs on hand while filling in a CRM sheet's owner column with logins rather than
  full names (see the Added entries below), so it now reads off the same row as the name it belongs
  to without scrolling ([EmployeeTable.js](../../frontend/src/components/employees/EmployeeTable.js)).

### Added
- **The studio's holidays are declared on a calendar in Settings, and every employee sees them.**
  Settings → **Holidays** draws the month, and a click on a day declares one, renames it, moves it
  or removes it; the month beneath lists what it holds, which is the reading a year's planning
  actually wants. A day declared here shows as a rose chip on the **Calendar** the topbar icon
  opens — for everybody, whatever else they hold — and attendance goes on counting it as a holiday
  rather than an absence, taking it out of every employee's hours target. The list is the one that
  was already there: nothing has to be re-entered. **Settings → Holidays answers for
  `ADMIN / System Settings`** — Read to look, Read / Write to change — which is a change of hands
  from HRM, so re-save any role that should keep it (the permission itself already exists and needs
  no reseed) ([holidays/controller.js](../../backend/src/modules/settings/holidays/controller.js),
  [HolidaysPanel.js](../../frontend/src/components/settings/HolidaysPanel.js),
  [calendar/service.js](../../backend/src/modules/schedule/calendar/service.js)).
- **Every employee now carries their own login username as a field of its own -
  `Employee.loginUsername`.** Not a second place a username is decided: it is still created only
  by Create Login Credentials and changed only by the account holder's own Settings → Profile, on
  `User` - this is a mirror, written in the same transaction as those two, so it can never disagree
  with the real login. It exists so that anything that only needs to *look up* an employee by their
  login reads one table instead of joining to `User` for it - the CRM's Google Sheet sync foremost
  (see the next entry). Backfilled for every account that already existed
  ([schema.prisma](../../backend/prisma/schema.prisma) - migration `20260912150000`,
  [employees/controller.js](../../backend/src/modules/hrm/employees/controller.js),
  [user/routes.js](../../backend/src/modules/user/routes.js)).

  **This adds a column**, so `npx prisma migrate deploy` is needed on the VPS. No new permission:
  the field rides the two endpoints that already write it.

- **The CRM's Google Sheet sync can now be pointed at each Sales employee's login username,
  not only their full name.** The sheet's `CRM` column has always resolved to an owner through
  `referenceMatchers.js` - the same rule every write path here answers to - but it only recognised
  an employee's id or their exact full name, typed exactly. An admin maintaining that column with
  logins (`dhanush.m`) instead of full names had every row come back unmatched: `owner_employee_id`
  stayed NULL, and the lead never reached anybody's Sales → Leads & Followups board, silently.
  A username is now checked ahead of a name and behind the employee id — it is unique in the
  database, so unlike a name it can never be ambiguous — and the same rule applies wherever a lead's
  owner is written: the sheet sync, the CSV import, and the manual "CRM" write from the board itself.
  **Nothing else changes**: `dataScope` already narrows every CRM screen to "your name is on it, or
  the name on it belongs to somebody who reports to you" ([DATA-SCOPE.md](../project-state/DATA-SCOPE.md)),
  so once the owner resolves, an employee sees their own leads on their own board with no new
  screen, filter, or permission involved
  ([referenceMatchers.js](../../backend/src/services/referenceMatchers.js)).

- **A permission is now decided by one named person, not the leave queue.** Create Login
  Credentials — the page HR → Onboarding → Pending opens, and the same page inside Edit Employee —
  carries a **Permission Approval** section holding one employee. One rather than the three Leave
  Approval takes: a permission is an hour or two out of a single day and is settled the same morning
  it is asked for, so there is nobody to escalate to and no order to ask in, which are the two
  things the leave list exists to express. A permission request then goes to that person, and to
  them alone; leaves and on-duties are untouched and keep the queue of three.
  **Left blank, the leave approvers decide it** — which is what the box says while nothing is in it,
  and what every record starts as, so nothing stalls on the day this ships and a studio that wants
  one approver for everything simply leaves it alone. The menu itself holds people and nothing else:
  the colleagues actually working here, which is the same list the alternatives and the leave
  approvers are picked from. Nobody approves their own permission,
  and the same manager may hold both this slot and a Leave Approval one, which is the ordinary case.
  The approver's own queue shows what they may decide and nothing else: a permission reaches the
  person named for it, or the three where nobody is named
  ([schema.prisma](../../backend/prisma/schema.prisma),
  [employees/controller.js](../../backend/src/modules/hrm/employees/controller.js),
  [attendance/service.js](../../backend/src/modules/hrm/attendance/service.js),
  [employeeCredentialConfig.js](../../frontend/src/constants/employeeCredentialConfig.js)).

  **This adds a column**, so `npx prisma migrate deploy` is needed on the VPS. Access is unchanged:
  the field rides the endpoints that already carry the rest of the page — `ADMIN / User Management`
  write for the credential routes, `HRM / Employee Management` write for the employee edit — and no
  new permission is involved.

### Removed
- **The holiday calendar is no longer on the HRM attendance rules screen.** The date-and-name list
  under HRM → Employee Attendance → rules is gone, with the `POST`/`DELETE
  /api/hrm/attendance/holidays` routes behind it: a company-declared holiday is a studio setting,
  not an attendance decision about one person, and two write paths into one table on two different
  permissions is how one screen comes to be refused what another allows. Declaring one is Settings
  → Holidays now. Attendance is otherwise untouched — it still reads the same table when it judges
  a day ([attendance/routes.js](../../backend/src/modules/hrm/attendance/routes.js),
  [EmployeeAttendancePage.js](../../frontend/src/components/hrm/EmployeeAttendancePage.js)).

### Fixed
- **A column filter on Leads & Followups no longer re-counts every tab on the strip.** The twelve
  tab counts are worked out in one pass over the board, and the column filters sat in the part of
  that pass every tab shares — so typing a name into Client Name narrowed all of them at once: All,
  Converted and Rejected each fell to what that one person holds, and the strip stopped answering
  the only question a number beside a tab label is asked, which is how big that tab is. The filter
  now goes into the open tab's own count, exactly where the date range was already put for the same
  reason. Its partner is that **the filter boxes now clear when you switch tabs** — an unfiltered
  count is a promise about what pressing the tab will show, and a filter that followed you across
  would break that promise on every tab at once (Converted reading 800 and opening on four rows).
  Clearing on a tab change is what every other table in the app already does; this board keeps its
  filters on the server, so it has to say so out loud. The rows on screen were always correct and
  are untouched, as is the date range. Access is unchanged: same endpoint, same
  `SALES / Leads & Followups` guard, no new route or permission
  ([leadQueryService.js](../../backend/src/modules/sales/crm-leads/leadQueryService.js),
  [CRMPage.js](../../frontend/src/components/sales/CRMPage.js)).
- **Save Draft works again on Create Login Credentials.** The draft the page sends carries the
  WhatsApp toggle, and the endpoint validates with a `.strict()` schema that never listed it —
  which refuses an unknown key rather than dropping it, so every Save Draft has been answered with
  a 400 since the toggle joined the payload. The half-made account the button exists to keep was
  not being kept. Found while adding the permission approver to the same schema
  ([employees/controller.js](../../backend/src/modules/hrm/employees/controller.js)).
- **An album's upload hands you back to Album Proofing, not to the Projects page.** Clicking a
  running or finished upload in the floating dock always opened Image Selection → Projects, so an
  upload started in Album Proofing dropped the reader into the other section, hunting for a
  project that is not in that list. The dock now sends each upload back to the section it was
  started in, Album Proofing honours that link the way the Projects page always has, and an
  upload resumed after a reload still remembers which section it belongs to
  ([UploadDock.js](../../frontend/src/components/image-selection/UploadDock.js),
  [album-proofing/page.js](../../frontend/src/app/(app)/image-selection/album-proofing/page.js),
  [imageSelectionUploadStore.js](../../frontend/src/lib/imageSelectionUploadStore.js)).

- **Create Album says what is actually wrong when a project has gone.** Creating an album on a
  project a page still listed but that had since been archived or deleted answered "Active Image
  Selection project not found." — a sentence naming the wrong module, which read as though a
  manually named project were being refused. Both kinds of Album Proofing project, typed by hand
  or taken from a saved WhatsApp group name, have always been accepted and still are; the refusal
  now names the project that has gone and says to refresh. The lookup is also scoped to this
  section, so an album can never be filed under a Projects-page project
  ([album-proofs/service.js](../../backend/src/modules/image-selection/album-proofs/service.js),
  [album-proofs/repository.js](../../backend/src/modules/image-selection/album-proofs/repository.js)).

- **View Spread shows the whole spread instead of scrolling a strip of it.** The corrections
  centre opened a proof at its own size inside a dialog that then scrolled it, so a tall spread was
  never on screen at once and a comment could be scrolled away from the pin it belongs to — the
  height it was given could not resolve against a box that sized itself to the picture. The spread
  is now fitted to the dialog the way both review rooms fit it, so it is whole at any window size
  and the dialog does not scroll, and it sits on the same grey those rooms put behind a proof
  rather than a near-black panel of its own
  ([CorrectionsTab.js](../../frontend/src/components/image-selection/album-proofing/CorrectionsTab.js)).

- **A correction can only be pinned on the spread itself.** Both review rooms give the proof the
  whole pane and fit the picture inside it, which leaves an empty band beside a spread that does
  not match the pane's shape — and a double-click or a dragged circle in that band was accepted,
  putting a pin next to the photograph rather than on it, at coordinates that meant nothing.
  Marking up is now confined to the picture as it is actually drawn, so a pin lands where it was
  put and keeps meaning the same point at any window size or zoom. A circled area is held to the
  same edges: dragging past the side of a spread stops the circle at it rather than drawing a ring
  around the photograph, and a circle already recorded is drawn clamped too
  ([ProofSpreadCanvas.js](../../frontend/src/components/image-selection/album-proofing/ProofSpreadCanvas.js)).

- **Two overlapping Send to QC requests can no longer wreck an album's version.** The draft
  cleanup found drafts and then deleted them by id in a second statement, so a second send
  arriving as the first finished could delete a version that had just been frozen and pointed
  at, cascading its QC round away and leaving "The album's current version could not be found."
  The sweep is now one statement on status and touches only drafts older than fifteen minutes,
  a send that finds a fresh draft is refused as already in progress, and two sends racing past
  that check collide on the version-number unique with the same message. Freezing also copies
  eight pages at a time instead of one, so a hundred-page album freezes in seconds rather than a
  minute ([pipeline.service.js](../../backend/src/modules/image-selection/album-proofs/pipeline.service.js),
  [repository.js](../../backend/src/modules/image-selection/album-proofs/repository.js)).
- **A deleted Album Proofing or Image Selection project no longer blocks its WhatsApp group name.**
  A soft-deleted project kept its Sales Manager link, so the next New Project from the same
  saved group name in that section answered "already has an Album Proofing project" while the
  sidebar showed nothing. The duplicate check now counts only live projects, the delete purge
  releases the link, and a data migration frees rows deleted before this
  ([repository.js](../../backend/src/modules/image-selection/projects/repository.js),
  [migration](../../backend/prisma/migrations/20260903000000_release_sales_link_on_deleted_projects/migration.sql)).

### Added
- **A correction can be painted on the spread, and the tools to mark one are named down its right
  edge.** Circle an area, or take the pen and draw on the photograph — the thing every reviewer
  reaches for, and the one there was no way to do. The pen carries what a pen needs:
  six colours to mark in and an undo for the last stroke, both appearing only while it is the
  chosen tool. The toolbar starts closed, as a single chevron at the corner, and opens on a click —
  most of a review is looking rather than marking, and a column of controls standing in front of
  the photograph the whole time is the tool getting in the way of the work. Closing it changes
  nothing else: the chosen tool stays chosen. Painting opens the comment card against the middle of the drawing, so a stroke is
  still a correction with words to go with it; further strokes join the same one until it is sent,
  and undoing the last of them takes the card back with it.
  The drawing is **part of the correction**, not a passing sketch: the studio sees the exact line
  the client drew, weeks later, redrawn on the same spread beside the pin, and editing a painted
  correction brings its strokes back to be undone or added to. Strokes are stored in the same
  normalized coordinates the pins use — `album_proof_correction.path_normalized`, migration
  [20260907060000](../../backend/prisma/migrations/20260907060000_album_proof_correction_path/migration.sql)
  — so a drawing lands on the same part of the photograph at every pane size, on a phone, and at
  every zoom level. What a drawing may hold is written once in
  [proofPaint.mjs](../../frontend/src/lib/proofPaint.mjs), trimmed to in the browser and enforced
  again by the API, with a test that fails if the two ever disagree.
  Both rooms get it from the one canvas and the one toolbar they share
  ([ProofToolbar.js](../../frontend/src/components/image-selection/album-proofing/ProofToolbar.js),
  [ProofSpreadCanvas.js](../../frontend/src/components/image-selection/album-proofing/ProofSpreadCanvas.js)).
  **One behaviour changed on the way:** a drag now does what the chosen tool says — it circles an
  area under Circle and paints under Pen, where before every drag circled. A room opens on Circle.

### Changed
- **A QC review says how much of the album has been reviewed, on the bar above the proof.** The bar
  carrying the album name now ends with `Reviewed 10 / 54`, so how far the review has got is
  readable without looking away from the proof. A count and nothing else: no bar drawn under it, and
  no tally of what is still open — the ruling bar below already carries that, and the corrections
  rail spells it out. It counts spreads **reviewed**, never the position, which is the figure that
  unlocks Approve QC and Request Corrections; which spread is open is on the page's title bar.

  **The bar itself is shorter** — 57px rather than 78px on a wide screen, against the 48px title bar
  directly above it. The album's name was set at display size (30px), which suited a room that
  opened on it; with the project named on its own bar above, it read as a banner over the thing
  being reviewed rather than a heading for it. At 20px the two bars sit together and the proof keeps
  the ~20px they were spending. The
  bar lays itself out against **its own width rather than the window's**, which is
  the only thing that works here: the app shell, the projects sidebar and the review's own sidebar
  all sit beside this pane, so it is far narrower than the window and a screen-width rule put the
  readout beside the album name at a width that left the name nothing at all. The readout sits to the right where there is room for both and drops under the
  name where there is not. Containment is kept to a wrapper around that bar alone, because
  `container-type` brings layout containment with it and the review's dialogs — which are
  `position: fixed` — live further down the same column
  ([QcReviewPanel.js](../../frontend/src/components/image-selection/album-proofing/QcReviewPanel.js)).
- **Album Proofing no longer offers a pin: a correction is circled or drawn, never dropped as a
  bare point.** The Pin tool is gone from the marking toolbar in **both** review rooms — the QC
  Lead's and the client's — and the double-click/double-tap that dropped a point goes with it, so
  the gesture cannot leave a mark the toolbar no longer names. A bare point said *where* something
  was wrong without saying *what* or *how much*, and the studio was left reading a comment against
  a dot; the circle states the area and the pen traces it, and both already carried the comment.
  Each room now opens on **Circle** rather than Pin. Nothing already marked is affected: corrections
  pinned as points before this still show on the spread, still open their comment, and are still
  the reviewer's own to edit or remove — only the making of a new one is closed. The pen is
  untouched, including the amber marker it drops at the middle of a drawing while the comment card
  is open ([ProofToolbar.js](../../frontend/src/components/image-selection/album-proofing/ProofToolbar.js),
  [ProofSpreadCanvas.js](../../frontend/src/components/image-selection/album-proofing/ProofSpreadCanvas.js),
  [QcReviewPanel.js](../../frontend/src/components/image-selection/album-proofing/QcReviewPanel.js),
  [ClientProofViewer.js](../../frontend/src/components/image-selection/album-proofing/client/ClientProofViewer.js)).
- **The designer fixing corrections can open the proof they are pinned to.** *View Proof* was
  offered only while the album sat in QC review, or to somebody holding the QC grant. A designer
  holds `Album Proofing` and not `Album Proofing QC`, and an album waiting to be fixed is
  `QC_CHANGES_REQUESTED` — so both tests failed and the one screen that shows a correction *where it
  is*, pinned on the spread, was shut to the person it was addressed to. They were left reading the
  Corrections tab's list and guessing at the placement. The version and its corrections have always
  been `READ_ANY` on the server, so the data was theirs to read the whole time and only the button
  was missing; a version existing is now the whole condition. It still opens read-only for them —
  the ruling stays with the QC grant, and the button says *View Proof* rather than *Open QC Review*
  ([AlbumProofWorkspace.js](../../frontend/src/components/image-selection/album-proofing/AlbumProofWorkspace.js)).
- **The marking toolbar opens sideways, in a pale wash, and carries a button for the corrections.**
  Three changes to the bar that sits on the proof, in both review rooms, since it is one shared
  component. It **unfolds to the left** along the top of the spread rather than downward: a proof is
  taller than it is wide far more often than the other way about, so height is the scarce direction
  and a column of controls growing down the picture ate the thing being judged — closed it is one
  chevron pointing left, and the chevron stays at the right-hand end so opening and closing never
  moves the control that does it. It is **translucent** now — a pale wash over the photograph rather
  than a solid slab, with `backdrop-blur` keeping the icons legible over a busy picture — because it
  sits on top of the very thing being reviewed. And it gains a **message button that shows and hides
  the corrections rail**, which no longer stands open by default: most of a review is looking at the
  spread, and a 320px column of words beside it is worth the room only while the words are what is
  being read. Once opened it stays open as the album is paged, so a reader turning spreads is not
  asked for the same click on every one of them. The button appears only from `lg`, where the rail
  exists at all — below that the spread's corrections are listed under the proof as before, so a
  button promising to show them would promise nothing.

  **The bar renders for a reader who may not mark, carrying the corrections button alone.** Hanging
  the rail off a button inside the marking toolbar had taken the corrections away from everyone the
  toolbar is withheld from — a version not in QC review, a reader without the QC grant, a client
  whose round is closed — all of whom could see a spread and had no way left to read what was
  written on it. `canMark` now decides only whether the marking tools are drawn; the corrections
  button is not one of them. A bar with nothing in it but that button stays hidden below `lg`, where
  the button is hidden too. The wash is fainter as well — 35% white at rest, firming to 75% under
  the pointer, so the bar is barely there while the reviewer is looking and solid while they use it.
  The chosen tool is a **pale blue chip rather than a solid blue disc**, and every button in the bar
  is 28px rather than 32px: a saturated fill on a translucent bar lying over a photograph was the
  loudest thing on the spread, louder than the picture it was drawn on, and it read as a heavier
  control than the ones beside it when it is only the chosen one. `aria-pressed` is what states the
  choice; the colour only has to show it. **The black ring around a chosen tool is gone**: it was the
  browser's own focus ring — `outline: auto 1px rgb(35,40,48)`, drawn as a circle because the buttons
  are round — which on a pale bar over a photograph read as part of the icon rather than as focus,
  and on the circle tool as a second, wrong-coloured ring around the right one. The bar states its
  own `focus-visible` ring in the application's blue instead of leaving it to the browser; it stays
  `focus-visible` rather than `focus`, so it answers the keyboard and does not ring every button
  that is clicked
  ([ProofToolbar.js](../../frontend/src/components/image-selection/album-proofing/ProofToolbar.js),
  [QcReviewPanel.js](../../frontend/src/components/image-selection/album-proofing/QcReviewPanel.js),
  [ClientProofViewer.js](../../frontend/src/components/image-selection/album-proofing/client/ClientProofViewer.js)).
- **The QC review's corrections rail is bounded by the two bars instead of running past them.** The
  rail listing a spread's corrections was a sibling of the whole review column, so it ran the full
  height and sat *beside* the bar naming the album and the bar the album is ruled from, rather than
  between them — the two bars stopped short at its edge and read as though they belonged to the
  proof alone. The review is now a column of three: the album bar, a band holding the proof and its
  corrections side by side, then the paging and ruling bar. Both bars span the full width (1670px
  rather than 1350px on a 1920px screen) and the rail is bounded by both. Nothing about the rail
  itself changed — still 320px, still from `lg` up, with the phone's own list under the proof below
  that ([QcReviewPanel.js](../../frontend/src/components/image-selection/album-proofing/QcReviewPanel.js)).
- **The QC review's left rail is gone; the proof has the width it was using.** The review opened
  with a 270px sidebar carrying the version, its status, and the review progress — all three of
  which are now on the two bars above the proof, so the rail was a column repeating what the reader
  had just passed. Removing it hands the pane back to the thing being judged: the workspace bar
  goes from about 440px to about 710px on a 1280px screen and to about 1350px on a 1920px one, and
  on a phone the proof is no longer pushed down by a stacked card before it starts. **The one control the rail held that is not
  written anywhere else — Changed spreads only, which narrows a revision round to the spreads that
  moved — moved with it**, to sit under the progress it filters rather than being lost with the
  column ([QcReviewPanel.js](../../frontend/src/components/image-selection/album-proofing/QcReviewPanel.js)).
- **Both proofing rooms stopped telling people to double-click the proof to add a pin.** The
  corrections list beside the spread still read "Double-click the proof to add a pin, or drag to
  circle an area" after the pin tool was removed — an instruction for a gesture that no longer does
  anything, in the QC room and the client's alike. It names the two tools that exist: drag to
  circle an area, or take the pen and draw. The read-only wording follows ("Corrections on this
  spread, as recorded", where it said "Pins")
  ([ClientProofViewer.js](../../frontend/src/components/image-selection/album-proofing/client/ClientProofViewer.js),
  [QcReviewPanel.js](../../frontend/src/components/image-selection/album-proofing/QcReviewPanel.js)).
- **A QC review says at the top of the page which version it is reviewing.** The Album Proofing
  header now reads `Manual Album Proof Test / V1 · Round 1` with the album's status beside it while
  a QC review is open, where it named the project alone — so the round being ruled on is legible
  without reading down into the panel for it. The round is the part nothing above the review could
  already work out: it belongs to the QC detail rather than to the album, so
  [QcReviewPanel.js](../../frontend/src/components/image-selection/album-proofing/QcReviewPanel.js)
  reports it up as the detail arrives and withdraws it on the way out. The trail therefore cannot
  outlive the review that put it there — leaving QC, closing the album and switching project all
  unmount that panel, and the same cleanup clears the header in every one of those cases. It is
  **only** QC review: the albums list and the album workspace are untouched, as is the `Created:`
  line, which keeps its own row below.

  **Which spread is open is left to the arrows that turn it**, between them in the footer, and is
  printed on no bar at all: the review panel's own header gave it up, and so did this one. Three
  places counting the same album to the same number is three chances for one of them to look stale,
  and the count means most beside the control that changes it. That count no longer hides on a
  narrow screen either — it was hidden there while a bar above carried it, which none now does, and
  a phone paging an album with nothing saying where in it you are is the worse trade. The title bar
  reports **only** the version and its status, which also means a page-turn no longer sets state on
  the page above and re-renders it, sidebar and all, for a number it does not show — it re-renders
  when a review opens and closes, not while one is read. At a phone's width the project name gives
  up characters before the version and status do, so the thing being reviewed stays readable
  ([album-proofing/page.js](../../frontend/src/app/(app)/image-selection/album-proofing/page.js),
  [AlbumProofWorkspace.js](../../frontend/src/components/image-selection/album-proofing/AlbumProofWorkspace.js),
  [ProofSpreadNav.js](../../frontend/src/components/image-selection/album-proofing/ProofSpreadNav.js)).
- **The Calendar is open to everybody in the studio, not only to the CRM.** The topbar Calendar
  icon and `/api/schedule` behind it answered for `SALES / CRM Leads`, which described the folder
  the page sits in rather than what it holds — so the person most likely to need to know the studio
  is shut on the 20th was the one who could not open it. Signing in is now the whole of the guard on
  *opening* it. **Nothing is widened by that:** what is drawn is still decided source by source
  against each module's own Read permission, so a viewer who may not open Project Management still
  sees no shoot, and somebody holding no module permission at all gets the holidays and an
  otherwise empty month rather than a refusal. Scheduling is unchanged and still asks for
  `SALES / CRM Leads` write by name — without it there is no Add Meet, days are not clickable, and
  the List view, which lists a source, is not offered to somebody with no source to list
  ([calendar/routes.js](../../backend/src/modules/schedule/calendar/routes.js),
  [MeetingsPage.js](../../frontend/src/components/sales/MeetingsPage.js),
  [AppTopbar.js](../../frontend/src/components/app-shell/AppTopbar.js),
  [modulePermissions.js](../../frontend/src/constants/modulePermissions.js)).
- **Yet to connect now holds every lead nobody has spoken to, however long ago the enquiry came
  in.** The label expired: a lead with no status read Yet to connect on the day of its own Enquiry
  Date and **Inactive** from the next morning, so the queue emptied itself overnight while the calls
  in it went unmade — an enquiry nobody rang on Monday was out of the worklist on Tuesday, chased by
  nobody, and reachable only by filtering the Status column to Inactive. Two exemptions had already
  been bolted on for exactly that reason — a Qua Qua lead inside a newborn or birthday window, and
  anything a Google Sheet link had put here — which is the rule telling on itself: a lead nobody has
  rung is a lead nobody has rung on day two as much as on day one. The ageing is gone rather than
  exempted from a third way, and with it the derived Inactive; Inactive is still shown for a row that
  genuinely stores the word, which is what an import writes when the file's own Status column said
  so. Setting any status is what takes a lead out of the queue, so it still empties — by being
  worked. **The tab will open much longer than it did**, holding the untouched backlog rather than
  the day's arrivals; Today New Leads is the tab that is about the day. Same on Dum Dum, Corp and
  Qua Qua, and the Status column, the Status filter and the tab all still say one thing
  ([leadQueryService.js](../../backend/src/modules/sales/crm-leads/leadQueryService.js),
  [CRMPage.js](../../frontend/src/components/sales/CRMPage.js)).
- **The Yet to connect tab also picks up the leads that carry the words themselves.** "Yet to
  connect" arrives two ways — derived, and as stored text an import wrote because the file's own
  Status column said it — and the tab held only the first. Those rows read Yet to connect in the
  Status column, answered a Status filter of Yet to connect, and were counted in Today Pending
  Followups, which had been moved onto the pair; the tab actually named for the label was left
  behind. It now reads `readsYetToConnectSql`, the same expression Today Pending does
  ([leadQueryService.js](../../backend/src/modules/sales/crm-leads/leadQueryService.js)).

  Access is unchanged: both are the existing `SALES` / `Leads & Followups` reads, guarded as before,
  and no new route, screen or permission is involved.

- **Deleting a lead a Google Sheet is syncing now sticks.** The sync worked out what a row was by
  looking for its lead — found, an edit; not found, new — which is two answers to three questions.
  A row whose lead had been **deleted** looked exactly like a row nobody had ever imported, so the
  poller put it back two minutes later, under a fresh id, on the board that had just been cleared.
  Under the soft delete this replaced it was worse: the upsert ends `deleted_at = NULL`, so where the
  sheet carried the lead's own Enquiry ID the hidden row was silently brought back to life under its
  original id. Deleting a synced lead was not an act a person could complete. A new
  `sales_google_sheet_row` ledger remembers every row ever imported, kept whether or not its lead
  survives, so the three cases separate: a lead that still exists is **updated** (spreadsheet edits
  reach the CRM exactly as before), a known row with no lead is **left deleted**, and an unknown row
  is **imported**. Rows held back are counted as `skippedDeleted` in the sync summary rather than
  dropped quietly, and an unreadable ledger withholds every new row rather than risk re-importing a
  whole sheet over a board somebody has just cleared. To bring a deleted row back: remove it from the
  sheet and add it again, or delete the link, which takes the ledger with it
  ([googleSheetsService.js](../../backend/src/modules/sales/crm-leads/googleSheetsService.js),
  migration `20260912120000_google_sheet_row_ledger`).
- **Deleting a CRM lead now removes it from the database.** It stamped `deleted_at` and left the row
  where it was — every screen honours that column, so the lead vanished and the studio reasonably
  read it as gone, while the table kept growing. One dev database was holding **92,452 hidden rows
  against 20,094 live ones**. Delete is now a `DELETE`. Nothing in this schema is joined by a foreign
  key, so what goes with the lead is decided explicitly: its **own** rows go with it (activity trail,
  newborn/birthday occurrences, Watchers entry, meetings); its **calls and transcripts are unlinked,
  never deleted** — a call happened, and it returns to the Calls board as a caller nobody has added,
  the same move `retireCallyzerCallLeads.js` makes; and a lead carrying a **project, quotation or CRD
  document is refused**, because deleting it would leave a booking with money against it naming an
  enquiry that no longer exists. A refused lead is named in the reply with the reason rather than
  reported as a smaller number
  ([crmLeadService.js](../../backend/src/modules/sales/crm-leads/crmLeadService.js),
  [crm-leads/controller.js](../../backend/src/modules/sales/crm-leads/controller.js)).

### Added
- **`npm run report:whatsapp-gate` says why an account is, or is not, being asked to link its
  WhatsApp.** Four separate things decide it, set on three different screens, and a gate that has
  decided not to hold you renders nothing at all — so from the browser a missed **Allow WhatsApp**
  tick, a switched-off studio switch and an administrator's exemption are indistinguishable, and
  "it never asks me for the QR" has no answer on screen. Nor does the most common reason of all,
  which is that the account is **already linked** and there is nothing to ask for — the gate
  working rather than failing. The script prints all five for an account and names the first one
  that said no, which is the one to go and change; the pairing it reads is the credentials on disk,
  so it answers from outside a running server. Reads only, so it is safe against production:
  `npm run report:whatsapp-gate -- vijay`
  ([reportWhatsappGate.js](../../backend/scripts/reportWhatsappGate.js)).

### Changed
- **Every full-screen form now refuses a missing required field, reddens it, and scrolls to it.**
  Each form already declared which fields were required and drew the asterisk, but acting on it was
  left to the form and most did not: the save was refused by a sentence at the top naming the field,
  while the field itself looked exactly like one that had been answered — usually on a part of a
  form long enough to have a section rail, and often scrolled off screen. The reader was told what
  was wrong and not where. `SchemaFormPanel` now does all three itself for every form drawn from it,
  working out which fields and which section from the schema it is already rendering, so no form
  keeps a second list that can drift out of step with what it asks
  ([requiredProblems.js](../../frontend/src/components/schema-form/requiredProblems.js)). Only fields
  actually on screen count, nothing is red until the first refused submit, and a form's own error
  message still wins over the generic mark. Four field types could not show a refusal at all — a
  toggle, a checkbox, a time and a file upload took no `error` — so a required agreement, tick, time
  or proof document was the one kind of unanswered question that stayed invisible; all four now
  redden and say so. A panel that means its `required` marks as a guide rather than a rule passes
  `validateRequired={false}`.

### Fixed
- **The WhatsApp pairing code is no longer shown after it has expired, and Refresh code no longer
  does nothing when pressed.** Two clocks on the pairing dialog disagreed with the two things they
  have to match. WhatsApp gives the first QR sixty seconds and every rotation after it twenty, and
  the dialog re-read the session every twenty — so a code could be drawn at the moment WhatsApp
  replaced it and refuse to scan, with nothing on screen saying why. It now re-reads every five
  seconds, which is a status call rather than a pairing attempt and costs the account nothing, so a
  displayed code has at least fifteen seconds of life left. Separately, the button re-enabled five
  seconds after a pairing request while the server drops any request inside fifteen and answers with
  the state it already had, so a press in that ten-second window did nothing at all and said nothing
  — which reads as broken and invites the run of presses that gets a number blocked. The countdown is
  now the server's own gap, so when it says Refresh code the press reaches WhatsApp
  ([WhatsappRequiredGate.js](../../frontend/src/components/sales/WhatsappRequiredGate.js)). Both
  constants are held to their source of truth by
  [whatsapp-gate-pacing.test.mjs](../../frontend/test/whatsapp-gate-pacing.test.mjs), which reads
  Baileys' own QR timeout and the backend's `MIN_CONNECT_GAP_MS` rather than restating either.
- **A refused dropdown now turns red, on both onboarding forms.** The message appeared under the
  field and the border stayed grey, which on a form where everything else had reddened reads as the
  one field that is fine. Two separate causes. `Dropdown` has two triggers — the standard one is a
  button that takes the error colours from `triggerClassName`, and the **searchable** one is a
  wrapper around a text input with classes of its own that hard-coded `border-slate-300`. That is
  every searchable dropdown: on the employee form, Designation, Vertical, Reports To, Referred By and
  both work locations — the fields HR must now answer before an account can be created. It reddens
  with the same colours, through `focus-within` rather than `focus`, since the focus lands on the
  input inside the wrapper ([Dropdown.js](../../frontend/src/components/shared/Dropdown.js)). Separately,
  `SchemaFormFields` never passed `error` to the combo box or the radio group at all, so those showed
  neither border nor message — the employee panel draws its own fields and had always passed it,
  which is why it only showed on the candidate's onboarding form, on Emergency Contact Relationship
  ([SchemaFormFields.js](../../frontend/src/components/schema-form/SchemaFormFields.js)). **Department**
  is the third: it takes several answers, so it is drawn by `MultiSelectField`, which had no `error`
  prop at all. It stays required and at least one department still has to be picked — an empty
  selection is stored as `""` and refused like any other blank — but the control now reddens and says
  so instead of being the one unmarked field on a page of marked ones
  ([MultiSelectField.js](../../frontend/src/components/shared/MultiSelectField.js)).
- **A refused onboarding submission now goes to the problem.** The candidate's form is eight sections
  long and is filled in on a phone. It marked the offending fields and raised "Please fill in all
  required fields" from a notice at the top, then left the reader standing at the Submit button they
  had just pressed — which restates the complaint rather than answering it, since somebody who cannot
  see the marked field is being told to go and find it. All five refusals — the agreement, a missing
  required field, a bad format, the age rule, an unfinished row in one of the repeating lists — go
  through one `refuse` helper that marks, says why and **scrolls to the section holding the first
  problem**, so the scroll cannot be added to some of them and forgotten on others. It finds the
  heading by `data-schema-section`, the same anchor the signed-in forms use, so both sides of
  onboarding refuse the same way. The agreement check is the one exception to the helper and
  deliberately so: it runs first and has no field of its own, so passing it an empty set would clear
  marks a previous press had left on the form
  ([PublicOnboardingForm.js](../../frontend/src/components/onboarding/PublicOnboardingForm.js)).
- **A login can no longer be created for an employee whose Employment Details are blank.** The ten
  fields moved from Personal Details to the Login Credentials page — Designation, Department, Reports
  To, Vertical, the two work locations, Joining Salary, Referred By, Date of Joining, Next Review
  Date — stayed marked required, and the split was meant to be that each page validates its own:
  **Approve & Next** stopped refusing over fields it was not showing, and the press that creates the
  account took them on instead. Only the first half was wired up. Nothing on the credentials page's
  submit read the validation, so the asterisks were decoration — **Create User & Send Email** wrote
  the account regardless. It was quietest in the ordinary case: the record is only re-saved when
  something on it has changed, so leaving all ten untouched skipped the record write entirely and
  went straight to creating the login. That press now refuses, marks the fields and goes to the first
  one. A refusal can also **change page** to reach it — `showFirstProblem` scrolled without switching
  pages, which did nothing at all when the field was on the other one, now reachable since a
  malformed Bank Details entry can be refused from a page that does not show it. The refusal names
  what is missing in the same sentence the record page uses — `useCredentialForm` hands back
  `showError` for it, since that page's message has no caller to go out through. Save Draft is
  untouched, on both pages: a draft is deliberately unfinished
  ([EmployeeFormPanel.js](../../frontend/src/components/employees/EmployeeFormPanel.js),
  [useCredentialForm.js](../../frontend/src/components/employees/useCredentialForm.js)).
- **Next Review Date fills itself in on a record that arrives with a joining date.** The six-month
  clock was applied when the joining date was *edited* and when the last review row was deleted, but
  never when a record was loaded — which went unnoticed while HR typed the joining date themselves.
  Once the candidate began supplying it on the onboarding form the date was already stored by the
  time Review Employee opened, nothing changed it, and Next Review Date sat empty on the Login
  Credentials page beside a joining date that was right there: empty on open, filled the moment you
  touched the date next to it. It is now derived on load as well, into an empty field only — a stored
  date is either HR's own answer or the latest review's, and both still outrank the default
  ([EmployeeFormPanel.js](../../frontend/src/components/employees/EmployeeFormPanel.js)).
- **Referred By is optional again.** It started optional, was made required, and is back at the
  studio's asking: most hires are not referred, so a required field meant answering it with somebody
  who had not referred them. Nothing downstream needed it — the endpoint already took it as nullable,
  and `syncEmployeeReferral` reads an empty one as "no referral", removing any row that was there
  unless an instalment against it has already been paid
  ([EmployeeFormPanel.js](../../frontend/src/components/employees/EmployeeFormPanel.js)).
- **The onboarding review form no longer pre-answers Designation, Department and Vertical.** A
  candidate's submission wrote the words "Not Allocated" into all three, standing for "the studio has
  not chosen yet" — but that is on none of the three dropdowns, and a select adds a stored value it
  cannot find among its options back into the list rather than losing it, so it arrived on Review
  Employee as a *selected* answer in a required field. HR could approve a record without ever
  picking, and the register then held a designation no dropdown offers. The same line also ran on
  **resubmission**, so a candidate sent back for corrections reset whatever department or vertical HR
  had already settled by pressing Submit. None of the three is the candidate's answer, so the
  submission no longer writes any of them: the columns stay empty, the review form shows its own
  *Select Department* placeholder, and the required field refuses to save until HR answers it.
  Designation alone is seeded once from the invite's Intended Designation — required to send an
  invite at all — on the first submission only
  ([onboarding-invites/controller.js](../../backend/src/modules/hrm/onboarding-invites/controller.js)).
  `20260912090000_clear_not_allocated_employee_placeholders` clears the sentinel from records already
  written, so the candidates currently waiting to be reviewed behave the same way.
